# Changelog

## Unreleased

- **New plugin: DamSafety Finder 0.1.0 (`DamSafety_Finder/`, provider `ndfind`): structures that are not on the lists (Nick, 2026-11-11: "Can the plugins currently find dams, ponds, and culverts that are not listed?").** No other plugin changes; RELEASE and damsuite unchanged.
  - **Found.** Only Screening tool 16 found anything unlisted (ponds and dugouts in a water-body layer), and it checked them against DWR dugouts only, never DWR dams or the NID, so it could not say which ponds were new (and tool 18 merges without removing duplicates). Nothing looked for a dam in the terrain: a dry or detention dam, or one whose pool is not in the water-body layer, was never found. Culverts were only per-dam guesses inside a Runner run (`core/culvert_guess.py`). Screening's hydro-conditioning (`core/hydrocond.py`) cuts any barrier up to 8 m high / 150 m long on a 3DHP line that is not a DWR dam line - triage passes no road layer - so an unlisted small dam is cut as if it were a road fill (not changed here; the Finder's list can protect them in a follow-up).
  - **Change.** Three Processing tools (Find structures). *Fills across streams* (`core/embankments.py`, new): walks each stream line over the bare-earth DEM; every hump in the profile is tested in 2-D - the spill level is the highest water level at which the ground above the hump and the toe below it are still separate pools (bisection on connected components), so a bank the line wanders onto is not a fill; height = spill - toe (foot of the face, `face_deg`) and crest height = crest on the line - toe (the Screening record uses it), head = upstream level - toe less the valley's own fall, storage and pool area at the spill level, a flat pool surface (flat ground whose rim is all higher), on a road / rail or not -> dam / dry embankment / road fill (ponding) / road fill. Tiled (5 km with a pad) so a county runs in bounded memory. *Ponds and dugouts*: Screening tool 16's engine (`core/ponds.py` + `core/storage.py`, byte-identical, added to `tools/ci_checks.py` IDENTICAL_ACROSS_PLUGINS) over a water-body layer. *Road and rail crossings of streams* (`core/crossings.py`): every crossing, bucket-indexed, with the nearest fill from the first tool. Every candidate is checked against the known-structure layers given (`core/listed.py`: listed within MATCH_FT, unsure within 2x, new; a known point anywhere in a pond outline is listed); outputs a GeoPackage + CSV per tool, the not-listed dams / ponds also as DWR-export dam records (`screening_dams` / `screening_ponds`, status Candidate) for Screening tool 1, and a multi-direction review hillshade (`gdaldem hillshade -multidirectional`). DEM unit from the band (feet scaled), no overviews, no vertical shift (`core/warp_opts.py`, the shared copy). The same files ship as DamIT Finder in the damit repository (only `brand.py` differs). Packaging: `tools/build_release.py`, `tools/mac/make_plugin_zips.py`, `install_dwr_suite_mac.sh`, README, CI (both suite legs).
  - **Tests.** `DamSafety_Finder/tests/test_core.py` (15: dam / dry embankment / road fill on a synthetic valley, a road fill holding water, line direction, a wandering line is not a fill, the height threshold, two lines one fill, the spill level at the low end of a fill, no data; listed / unsure / new, a pond outline, holes; crossings), `tests/test_finder.py` (GDAL end to end: CRS changes, tiles give the same answer, a DEM in feet, ponds, crossings with fills, the Screening layer, the hillshade), `tests/test_qgis.py` (the three tools through `processing.run` in real QGIS; a memory layer is refused). Real LiDAR (1 m 3DEP, about 20 x 9 km round Absaraka, pack layers): 20 fills in 55 s, Absaraka found and matched to ND00199 (crest height 21.3 ft vs 27.5 ft listed: the toe is read at the foot of the face), every other fill a road / rail crossing (checked on the hillshade); 85 crossings, 10 on NBI / SARP.
  - **Open water from aerial imagery (fourth tool, `ndfind:water`; Nick: "aerial website has some higher resolution imagery").** `core/water.py`: NDWI (green, near-infrared) at least `threshold` (0.15, YOUR value) and dark in the near-infrared (at most `nir_frac` 0.25 of the land's median: rail cars, metal roofs, gravel and shadows have a positive NDWI but are not dark), opened / closed by a cell, `min_ac` and `min_width` (10 m: road and rail ditches); each patch `mapped` when half of it is on the water-body layer. `finder.run_water` reads a file / VRT or an ArcGIS ImageServer (2000-px GeoTIFF tiles cached beside the output; 3000 px gets HTTP 500 from the ND GIS Hub). Presets: ND GIS Hub 2018 30 cm 4-band and 2017 / 2019 / 2020 NAIP colour-infrared (the 2021-2024 statewide NAIP there is natural colour, no near-infrared; aerial.dwr.nd.gov is not reachable from the cloud test machine). Tests: the filters on a synthetic image, a file run, tiles, mapped share, a mocked image service (tiles reused), the tool in real QGIS. Absaraka box (2018 30 cm at 1 m, 12.5 min with the download): 17 patches, 13 not on 3DHP_waterbodies including Absaraka's own 16.8 ac pool; through the pond tool: Absaraka listed (2801), 1 new embankment pond, 1 new dugout, 13 natural depressions.

- **Hazard 2.24.2, Runner 3.17.2, Screening 0.19.2, Data 1.10.1: the owner's window-simplification plan (docs/Simplifying_the_four_plugin_windows.md, audit "usability windows").** RELEASE ndhazard 2.24.2 / ndbreach 3.17.2 / ndscreen 0.19.2; damsuite `loss.py` changed (two strings; the one copy is now in `DamSafety_Core/damsuite/`).
  - **Found.** The plan is dated 2026-10-13 (release 1.22); checked against this main, changes 2 (YOUR values), 5 (Runner tabs), 6 (one Run in Hazard), 8 (result tools in ToolRows) and 9 (updater reads the suite folder) and the home's "Data…" dead end were already done; the rest were not (the status table is in the PR).
  - **Change (damsuite loss.py).** `rate_table_status()` told the engineer to sign the fatality rate table off in "Suite settings > Loss of life", a tab no window has; the two texts now say to supply a signed rate table (signed_off_by, source) to the loss-of-life tool. No number changes.
  - **Change (Data auto check).** `auto_check` was already off by default; it now also waits until a data folder is set (Data's own or the suite folder) or the default folder already holds downloads (`settings.auto_check_blocked`, logged once), so a fresh install never downloads into a folder the plugins do not read. A check started by hand is never blocked.
  - **Change (Screening quick screen - MAINTAINER DECISION).** `core/workflow.DEFAULT_SETTINGS["chain"]` was `[]` while the guide and five of the six presets have the quick screen (6) on, so "Screen this county" on a new window gave every dam a full 2D flood. It is now `["6"]`; a saved project or settings file keeps its own value; untick "also the quick screen (6)" to simulate every dam. Revert: set the default back to `[]`.
  - **Not changed (Screening tool defaults - Nick, 2026-11-09).** Tools 7, 9 and 12 (`runbreachsim`, `sensitivity`, `estimateruntime`) keep LISFLOOD-FP ACC (ENGINE 0) as their toolbox default; the Screening window and tool 14 stay on the built-in engine. A model or script that names ENGINE is unchanged.
  - **Change (settings grids, plan change 2).** `shared/ui/suite_settings_tools.py` `PER_RUN_KEYS`: parameters that name one run (Dam ID / name, NID ID, scenario, project file, reviewer, report titles and signatures) are left out of the catalog the three grids show - they are not settings and "Dam id" sat three times on Hazard's Other tab. They stay in the tool dialogs; a stored value for one in an old file is kept and reported as unknown. Runner grid 900 -> 855 rows.
  - **Change (toolbox, plan change 3).** `suite_settings_tools.settings_aware(hidden=True)` adds `FlagHideFromToolbox` (Qgis 3.36+ / 4 enum or the legacy flag; 0 on the test doubles). Hidden: Runner `selftest`, `verification`, `classify` (the hand-off the Results button opens); Screening 14 self-test, 15 headless chain, 17 preflight (the window's Check button); Hazard `savedamstate` (the batch chain's hand-off). Every one stays registered and runnable from scripts, models, qgis_process and the windows; 77 toolbox entries become 70.
  - **Change (Plugins menu, plan change 4 - a real bug).** The Dam suite home window had no menu entry in any plugin (Screening's `_add_suite_menu` was written for a separate "ND Dam Suite" menu and never called; Hazard and the Runner had none), and no window opens it, so the suite's front door was unreachable. `shared/ui/suite_home.py` `SUITE_MENU` is now "Dam safety" (the one menu every plugin already uses) and `add_home_action(plugin, iface)` adds the one "Dam suite home…" entry once per QGIS; each of the three plugins calls it from `initGui` and removes it on unload. The test fakes' QAction gained `text()` / `objectName()`.
  - **Change (Download a DEM, plan change 7).** The Runner's 13-control box and the Screening's two copies of the same form passed every value to `nddam:downloaddem` and then opened its full dialog anyway. Each window now has one row: a resolution and one button ("Download DEM for this dam…" in the Runner, with the dam's point from dam.json, its ID and <dam folder>/terrain filled in; "Download DEM for this county…" in Screening, with the county chosen above and the DEM folder); source, area and the ND LiDAR package are chosen in the tool, which is unchanged. About 30 controls fewer; the Runner box's overlapping rows (1.22 screenshot) cannot recur. The Runner's facts line ("Local copy: source USGS 3DEP...") is on its own row below the button (beside it, it was clipped at 1280x800 on Qt5: `realqt_dem_rows.py`). Screening's county box keeps a short ND DWR units note on its facts line (DEM_Imagine .img in UTM metres; ASCII_Grids_ft in State Plane feet, converted); the Download DEM tool's help has the long form (`test_dem_units_once.py` reads `settings_tab.py`).
  - **Change (Hazard tool launchers, plan change 8).** The Classify view's nine "Dam suite" tool buttons (a row wider than the window: the whole tab scrolled sideways) are the gear's "Dam suite tools" submenu; each action opens the same Processing dialog with the dam's folders filled in. The model-gate ticks and the dam.json line stay on the view. The Results tools were already ToolRows on the Results view.
  - **Change (checks, gui-grammar "CheckPanel, never a message box").** Screening: the check list and every refusal of a county / one-dam / selected-dams run (no work folder, nothing to run, not ready, a required input empty, a step that did not finish) go to the shared `CheckPanel` above the action bar (FAIL red and the primary button blocked until the next check; WARN amber; passes folded); "Check inputs" sits beside Screen this county. Hazard: an unreadable raster at Classify goes to the panel; failed runs report on the red status line; finished creep watch / dams-in-series open their report without a summary pop-up. The run confirmations (`QMessageBox.question`) stay. `tools/gui_lint.py` gets a source rule, `message_boxes_in_run_handlers` (no `QMessageBox.warning` / `.critical` inside `_on_run*` / `_run_*` / `_launch_*` / `_start_*` of a window file), which `tools/ci_checks.py` also runs; `tools/test_gui_lint.py` tests the rule. The test fakes' buttons gained `text()` / `setText()` / `styleSheet()`.
  - **Change (Screening window split, plan change 5/8-style clean-up).** `DamSafety_Screening/dialog.py` (2,600 lines) is split by tab as Hazard's window is: `settings_tab.py` (`SettingsTabMixin`: Run options, the county DEM box, the settings grid, file and presets), `review_tab.py`, `results_tab.py` and `dialog_consts.py` (the constants, re-exported by `dialog.py` so `from .dialog import TITLE` still works). Every method moved unchanged (the split was checked AST-identical method by method); the new files are in the CI colour, Qt6-enum and message-box lints.
  - **Tests.** `DamSafety_Data/tests/test_suite_folder.py` `automatic_check_waits_for_a_folder`; `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_gui_simplify.py` `per_run_parameters_stay_out_of_the_settings_grid`; `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_popup.py` (the county chain includes step 6; the box round-trips).

- **DaMiT suite mark as the Plugin Manager icon (all plugins; artwork only, no code or default changes).** Each plugin gets `damit.svg` (the DaMiT mark, light colour mode: a "D" standing in water with the orange i-dot of the wordmark) and `icon=damit.svg` in metadata.txt, so the Plugin Manager and the `plugins.xml` index show the suite brand. Menu entries and Processing providers keep each plugin's own `icon.svg`, so the tools stay apart there. `docs/brand/` holds the wordmark and mark SVGs in both colour modes (plus a simplified 16/24 px mark). `release.yml` copies the icon that `icon=` names to `gh-pages` instead of a fixed `icon.svg`. `docs/gui-grammar.md` Plugin icon row updated. `tools/gui_lint.py rule_icons` already checks that the named icon exists.

- **DamSafety Core 1.0.0: one shared damsuite (Hazard 2.24.0, Runner 3.17.0, Screening 0.19.0, Data 1.10.0, Setup 1.1.0, PMP 0.5.0, Hazard Lite 0.2.0; consolidation).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.24.0, ndbreach 3.17.0, ndscreen 0.19.0; damsuite content unchanged (1.18.9), now in `DamSafety_Core/damsuite/` (git mv from `shared/damsuite/`); the pin and the sync tools are retired.
  - **Found.** `shared/damsuite/` (48 modules, about 19,500 lines) was vendored byte-identically into DamSafety Data, Hazard, Runner and Screening (three modules into Setup) by `tools/sync_damsuite.py` against `tools/damsuite_pin.json`, and `shared/ui/` was copied into each plugin by `tools/sync_ui.py`: five copies of every shared line in the repo and in each QGIS profile, two tools and a pin to keep them equal, and a `FILES.json` per copy.
  - **Change.** New plugin `DamSafety_Core` (`metadata.txt`, a library plugin object with one About entry) holds the one copy: `damsuite/` (with the one `FILES.json`, written by `tools/damsuite_files.py`), `ui/` (the shared window files) and `ui/styles/` (the QML layer styles the three plugins shipped identically). `DamSafety_Core/alias.py` is a `MetaPathFinder`: each plugin's `damsuite/__init__.py` is a two-line shim (`from DamSafety_Core import alias; alias.install(__name__)`) after which `<Plugin>.damsuite` and every `<Plugin>.damsuite.<sub>` are the very module objects of `DamSafety_Core.damsuite` (no second copy; `from .damsuite import x` and `from ..damsuite.x import y` keep working unchanged; `importlib.reload` reloads the core module in place). Each shared window file in a plugin (`tokens.py`, `ui_widgets.py`, `ui_fit.py`, `suite_settings.py`, `suite_settings_tools.py`, `suite_styles.py`, `suite_home.py`, `tool_dialog.py`; Setup's `suite_config.py` / `suite_fill.py` / `pack_convert.py`) is a three-line shim (`import sys; from DamSafety_Core.ui import x as _m; sys.modules[__name__] = _m`). Every other plugin's metadata names `plugin_dependencies=DamSafety Core`, so the Plugin Manager offers it; each plugin calls `damsuite.log_selfcheck()` at start-up (the Core's files vs its `FILES.json`, to the message log). The suite home's diagnostics name the plugin that opened the window (`opened_from`). Hazard's transform guard loads from the Core plugin's folder. `tools/ci_checks.py`: "no vendored copy" (every plugin's `damsuite/` is exactly the shim, every ui copy the three-line form, the Core's `FILES.json` current) replaces the drift and pin checks; `IDENTICAL_ACROSS_PLUGINS` stays. Retired: `tools/sync_damsuite.py`, `tools/damsuite_pin.json`, `tools/tag_damsuite_pin.sh`, `tools/sync_ui.py`, `tools/test_sync_damsuite.py`. `tools/mac/make_plugin_zips.py` and `install_dwr_suite_mac.sh` zip and install DamSafety_Core first. Users: install DamSafety Core before (or when prompted by) the other plugins; a profile with the old plugins keeps working until they are updated, and an updated plugin without the Core fails to load with a message naming "DamSafety Core". The archived nd_hazard fork is not updated (it keeps its own vendored copies; the shared pin it mirrored is no longer produced).
  - **Tests.** `DamSafety_Core/tests/test_alias.py` (both import forms, identity, a module `__init__` does not import, reload, the ImportError when the Core is absent), `DamSafety_Core/tests/test_ui_shim.py` (the import-time `sys.modules` replacement under `from .x import y`, every real shim checked), `tools/test_ci_checks.py` rewritten for the new checks; the Runner and Screening suites and the Hazard / Data / Setup tests that import without QGIS pass through the shims. Tests that read a vendored copy by path now read `DamSafety_Core/`.

- **Runner 3.16.15 / Screening 0.18.21 / Hazard 2.23.19: one zone rule and one sampling footprint across the three plugins, matching Hazard, the classifier of record (audit P1).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.16.15 / ndscreen 0.18.21 / ndhazard 2.23.19; damsuite content unchanged (pin rewritten for versions.py).
  - **Found.** The same structure on the same rasters could get a different ACER TM 11 zone from DamSafety_Hazard than from DamSafety_Runner / DamSafety_Screening. (a) Sampling footprint: Hazard reads the outline plus a 5 ft edge ring (`constants.OSM_EDGE_BUFFER_FT`) and a point structure as a 20 ft disc plus the ring (`DEFAULT_POINT_RADIUS_FT`); the Runner's `algorithms_ndhazard.Sampler`, `core/nd_hazard.sample_footprint` and the Screening's `core/hazard.Grid` read the bare footprint and the point's own cell, so a house 3 ft outside the wet edge was flooded in Hazard and dry in the other two. (b) Zone rule: Hazard reads the digitized REG_05.2026 table (`damsuite.tm11_curves.zone`, `helpers.classify_values`: a depth ON a boundary takes the higher zone); `core/nd_hazard.DangerChart.zone` tested the chart-file polygons and then a distance-to-polygon band of 0.01 of each axis (`acer_tm11_charts.json` "tolerance": about 0.10 ft / 0.25 ft/s on the house chart, 0.03 ft / 0.16 ft/s on the mobile chart), putting a point that close BELOW a boundary into the higher zone. On a 251 x 201 (d, v) grid over each chart the old rule gave a higher zone at 1,028 of 50,451 house points and 998 of 50,451 mobile points, never a lower one (largest depth gap 0.128 ft / 0.031 ft).
  - **Change.** `core/nd_hazard.py` (MODULE_VERSION 1.4.0; byte-identical in both plugins, now in `tools/ci_checks.py` IDENTICAL_ACROSS_PLUGINS with `acer_tm11_charts.json`): `DangerChart.zone` delegates to `damsuite.tm11_curves.zone` (the vendored damsuite, loaded by path when the module is read on its own); the polygons stay for the review figures (`chart_svg`) and the axes check, the "beyond the axes -> high danger" note stays; the old rule is kept as `DangerChart.polygon_zone` (comparison only). Hazard's borderline option replaces the band with Hazard's defaults - `Settings.near_ft` 0.5 ft (= `DEFAULT_NEAR_THRESHOLD_FT`) flags a structure that close below the next boundary (`Finding.near` / `margin_ft`, CSV columns, `counts.near_boundary`, a note on the finding); `Settings.borderline_higher` (off, as in Hazard) moves it into that zone (ND 5.3(f), borderline). The report's Notes say which zone rule applied ("Zone rule: the digitized REG_05.2026 table ... the classifier of record" and whether near-boundary structures were moved); a chart file whose polygons lie more than the table's stated 0.06 ft off it is noted too. Runner tool parameters `NEAR_FT` / `BORDERLINE_HIGHER` (advanced); Screening `classify_nd(near_ft=, borderline_higher=)`. Sampling: `nd_hazard.EDGE_RING_FT` 5 / `POINT_RADIUS_FT` 20, `sample_footprint(..., edge)` (cells whose centre is inside the ring or within `edge` of its wall, exact point-to-segment distance) and `sample_disc`; Runner `Sampler.polygon` / `points(structure=True)` with `unit_m` (metres per grid unit from the rasters' CRS, so the feet are right on a State Plane grid) and the Screening's `Grid.sample(structure=True)` (metres) use them for habitable structures; lifelines, dams, roads and railroads are read as before. The `how` / `sampled` text names the ring.
  - **Direction.** Zone rule: a Runner / Screening structure within about 0.1 ft (house) / 0.03 ft (mobile) below a curve DROPS a zone - said plainly: matching Hazard, the classifier of record; it is now flagged "near" that boundary instead of silently promoted. Sampling footprint: more cells are read, so a zone can only RISE; a structure just outside the wet edge becomes inundated as in Hazard. The Hazard's own results do not change.
  - **Tests.** `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_classification_parity.py` (new, stub Qt, numpy only): one synthetic 1 m flood and one structure set - outlines, points, a structure 3 ft outside the wet edge, one 0.05 ft below the high boundary, one exactly on the judgment boundary, a peak-pair case - through (i) Hazard's array-level rules (`fastsample.structure_cells` cell selection, `peak_pair`, the table), (ii) the Runner `Sampler` + `nd_hazard.classify`, (iii) the Screening `Grid` + `classify_nd`: identical zones, depths and velocities; plus the zone-rule grid test (every point including those on and either side of each boundary) that also quantifies the old rule. `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_classification_parity.py` (the Screening leg in its own suite) and `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_classification_parity.py` (the whole-plugin leg: GeoTIFFs, QgsGeometry, `RasterSampler`, `evaluate_structures` GEOS and fast paths; needs QGIS, SKIP otherwise) mirror it. `test_nd_hazard.py`, `test_ndhazard_tool.py`, `test_v341.py`, `test_nd_hazard_adapter.py` updated to the table rule (the invented test polygons no longer decide zones).
  - **Golden regression harness (no data yet).** `tools/freeze_golden_dam.py` writes `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/data/golden_dams/<dam_id>/` from a finished Runner dam folder with the Hazard classification in it: depth / velocity / arrival rasters clipped to the structures' box (+100 ft) and downsampled only above 5 MB, `structures.gpkg` / `.csv` (the Hazard's Habitable_Structures) and `expected.json` (ND class, TM 11 class, lives in jeopardy, inundated / cut-off counts, every structure's zone, depth, velocity). `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_golden_dams.py` re-classifies every fixture with the current code and compares; with no fixture (this branch) it prints a notice and passes. The maintainer runs the freeze tool on DWR-2801 / DWR-3161 / DWR-8278 and commits the fixtures. `ci.yml` is not touched: the Hazard suite step already runs every `tests/test_*.py`.

- **Runner 3.16.13, Hazard 2.23.18, Screening 0.18.20, Data 1.9.7, PMP 0.4.5, HazardLite 0.1.8: reliability and engine guards (audit R1, R2, R3, R6, R11; Grok audit 2026-11-07 findings 3.1 and 3.2).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.16.13 / ndhazard 2.23.18 / ndscreen 0.18.20; damsuite content CHANGED (updatecore.py: download_file / ensure_free_space / remote_size - the pin label must be bumped by the maintainer). RESULTS-CHANGING: Grok 3.1 moves flood extents (longer) where a shallow sheet leaves the model; Grok 3.2 can only raise storage / extents on a mixed-CRS or feet local DEM folder. Growth-cap follow-up: a run that stops at a cap (domain-run / maximum-extent / wall-clock) with OUTFLOW_WARN_PCT% or more still leaving is flagged report['extent_is_minimum'] beside cut_off, so a cap-truncated flood on flat terrain is not read as final; named predicates hit_run_cap / hit_extent_cap and a GDAL-free tests/test_growth_caps.py.
  - **Found.** (R1) numpy 2 removed `np.cross` on 2-vectors; the HEC-RAS crest walk and the weir-gap merge raised `ValueError` under a QGIS shipping numpy 2 (three Runner tests failed on numpy 2.5), and `np.array(dem, copy=False)` in `core/proposed.py` (Runner + Screening) raises on numpy 2. (R2) a Hazard structure whose evaluation raised kept `max_depth=None` and read as `Not inundated` - out of the ND / TM 11 counts, no report line, a QGIS-log warning only; a LOW could rest on a house the plugin never looked at. (R3) HazardLite `core.py`, PMP `pmp.py` / `gridutil.py` / `pothole.py` called `gdal.UseExceptions()` at import and Data `pack_build.py` / `http_qgis.py` inside functions without restoring - process-wide, against `damsuite/gdalsafe.py`'s policy. (R6) a HEC-RAS compute / Wine step with no wall-clock limit held an unattended batch or chain for ever (a TCU dialog, a diverged solver); the USER_GUIDE's "known issue" about the hidden `TypeError` was stale (fixed in 3.10.57). (R11) Data downloads were held whole in RAM (up to the 2 GB cap) with no disk-space check and no `.part` staging. (Grok 3.1, signed off by Nick 2026-11-07) the Runner grew the domain only on an edge deeper than the edge tolerance; a sheet under 1 ft carried 36-40% of the breach volume out at Absaraka (DWR-2801) with the domain never growing; 3.16.7's H08 only made the loss visible. (Grok 3.2) three Screening tools mosaicked a local DEM folder with a bare `gdal.BuildVRT`, which drops tiles in another CRS and mosaics feet beside metres, and reused one fixed VRT name across folders.
  - **Change.** (R1) new `DamSafety_Runner/core/geom2d.cross2`, used in `hecras_crest.py`, `hecras_structures.py` and the offcrest tests; `np.asarray` in both `proposed.py`; a repo sweep found no other numpy-2-removed API. (R2) `structures.OUT_FAILED` ("Evaluation failed - verify"), `rec["eval_failed"]` (in `EVAL_KEYS`, so it survives the snapshot and the evaluation cache), Notes "EVALUATION FAILED - verify: ...", `ACER_Zone_Label` FAILED, `evaluation_failures()`, a per-structure depth read that raises marks that structure only; the pipeline logs `>>> WARNING: N structure(s) could not be evaluated` per scenario and `classify_nd(..., structs_failed=N)` marks the class INCOMPLETE ("structures (N could not be evaluated)" - on the summary headline line, in the portfolio row and the snapshot), a PROVISIONAL reason first, a judgment item, "; N not evaluated" on the structure criteria rows. (R3) module-level calls removed; HazardLite gets a local `_checked()`, PMP a local `core/gdalsafe.py` (checked / open_raster / open_vector / layer / `gdal_exceptions()` save-restore context), Data uses the vendored `damsuite.gdalsafe` and checks `VectorTranslate` / `BuildVRT` / `Translate` returns in both modes. (R6) `hecras_engine.BATCH_TIMEOUT_S` = 12 h per compute and per preprocess step for an unattended run (`workflow.run_hecras_stage(..., unattended=True)` from the batch tool and the CLI batch; the chain's Windows launcher `hecras_windows.run_plan(unattended=True)`), when `NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S` gives none; a number replaces it, `none` / `off` / `no` / `unlimited` removes it; logged at the start and recorded in `hecras_time_limit` ("unattended batch default: ..."); a single interactive run keeps no limit unless set; USER_GUIDE and Runner README rewritten. (R11) `updatecore.ensure_free_space` / `remote_size` / `download_file` (HEAD for the size, free-space check with a 256 MB headroom and x2 for an extracted zip, body to `<file>.part`, rename when complete, md5 / SHA-256 unchanged) used by every HTTP source and a folder check in `_update_one`; `http_qgis.download` streams a body over 32 MB through `QgsNetworkAccessManager` (`readyRead` in a `QEventLoop` on the task thread), keeps the blocking get for small bodies and falls back to the urllib client through `damsuite.netproxy` when the API is missing. (Grok 3.1) ported from the ND Runner 3.26.5 on top of H08's `boundary_outflow_acft` / `_pct` and `terrain.edge_flux_shares`: `OUTFLOW_EDGE_SHARE` = 0.10, `RunSettings.outflow_extend_pct` (parameter `OUTFLOW_EXTEND_PCT`, advanced; CLI `model.outflow_extend_pct`); in the extend loop, once that share has left, every edge not already over the tolerance, wet past the arrival depth and carrying at least 10% of the outflow (depth^(5/3) weights) moves out too (`edges_over_outflow` in the run record); the run report names the rule and carries a "Breach volume that left the model" row; a run that still loses 5% or more when it stops gets a WARNING that the flood area and consequences are minimums. (Grok 3.2) `TerrainLoader`, `propose_dam._source` (with a per-run `_mosaics` cache) and `find_ponds` go through `terrain_cache.local_mosaic` with `_common.mosaic_key(tiles)`-keyed VRT names, as the ND copies do; the 3DEP mosaic is unchanged.
  - **Tests.** Runner `tests/test_geom2d.py` (cross2, the crest junction stop and `merge_gap` under the running numpy), `test_hecras_no_default_limit.py` (+4 unattended cases), `test_hecras_batch.py` (the batch is unattended, the single run is not), `test_grok31_outflow_extend.py` (ND `test_v3265` ported, + the Qout accounting); Hazard `tests/test_eval_failed_not_dry.py` (stand-in qgis; the failed structure, apply_type from a snapshot, classify_nd incomplete / provisional, the summary headline, a clean run unchanged) and `test_m1m2_classify_nd.py` (stub gains OUT_FAILED); Screening `tests/test_local_mosaic_mixed_crs.py` (mosaic_key, the mixed-CRS warp with GDAL doubles, TerrainLoader naming and reuse, the refused CRS-less folder, no bare BuildVRT left on the local paths); Data `tests/test_download_guards.py` (a local HTTP server: `.part` rename, a cut download, unknown size, the free-space refusal before the request, the updater's error row). The three numpy-2 crest failures of the Runner baseline are gone.

- **Hazard 2.23.15: every long job of the Hazard window runs in a background task (audit, GUI responsiveness).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.23.15; damsuite content unchanged (pin rewritten for versions.py). `DamSafety_Data/constants.py` kept byte-identical to Hazard's (PLUGIN_VERSION only; no Data change).
  - **Found.** The classification has run in a `QgsTask` since v2.6 (`runner.start_task`, `dialog._start_background`), but five jobs still ran on the GUI thread behind a modal `QProgressDialog` whose tick handler called `QApplication.processEvents()`: the HEC-RAS plan import (`rasimport_tab._ras_import` - `RI.import_plan` reads the whole HDF, writes the cell GeoPackage and runs `gdal.Grid`, minutes on a 1 M-cell plan), the DSS-WISE Lite import (`dsswise_tab._dsw_import`, `DW.convert`), Check inputs with the service probes (`dialog._on_check_inputs`; each probe waits on the network), the creep watch (`classify_tab._run_watch`, `WT.run_watch`) and the self-test (`dialog._on_run_selftest`). QGIS froze for the duration, the canvas could not be panned, and Cancel was noticed only between steps.
  - **Change.** All five go through `_start_background()` exactly as the classification: the file / network work runs in the task with the job's `progress` callback mapped to the task's progress (cancel honoured at every tick); everything that touches the project or the widgets moves into the task's done callback on the main thread (`rasimport_tab._ras_imported`, `dsswise_tab._dsw_imported`, the check panel, the watch's message box, `selftest.finish_self_test` for the print layout / PDF and Processing-provider checks that need the main thread - `run_self_test(deferred=True)` hands them back as callables; the synchronous call is unchanged). The worker never scans the project: Check inputs resolves the building-outline layer on the main thread first (`dialog._check_job` / `_check_work`; `checks.check_all(outline=...)`, `checks.outline_text`), the creep watch the project's NSI / address point layers (detached copies into `point_sources`; `watch._unsupported_settings` lets an empty list keep the parallel mode). GDAL / OGR objects are made in the task; `QgsRasterLayer` / `QgsVectorLayer` are made on the main thread from the paths the worker returns. New `runner.release_folder` takes an earlier import's layers out of the project before the files are rewritten (the worker's `prepare_output_path` cannot, and a loaded layer locks the GeoTIFF on Windows). `_BUSY_WIDGETS` now also covers the import options, the DSS-WISE table, "Use this plan for the classification" and the creep-watch worker count, so nothing the done step reads changes under a running task; the Import / watch handlers carry a busy guard. `_progress_tick` keeps its `processEvents` for the main-thread steps that still use it (`_finish_deferred`, Classify's inline file checks), as does `_task_progress`'s synchronous fallback. No classification change.
  - **Tests.** `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_background_jobs.py` (new, stub QGIS: each moved job goes through `runner.start_task`, its main-thread part runs in the done callback after the work, cancel / error paths, busy guard, the resolved outline and point layers, the source has no modal bar left in the moved jobs). The real-Qt tests `test_window_shell`, `test_ui_polish` and `test_dam_folder_rasters` pump the event loop until the check task's done callback has run (`_wait_idle`).
- **Hazard 2.23.14: HEC-RAS plan import reads results as float32; VectorTranslate fallback (audit P6).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.23.14; damsuite content unchanged (pin rewritten for versions.py).
  - **Found.** `rasimport.read_area` cast every per-cell array (`Maximum Water Surface`, face velocity, times) and every water-surface series block to float64, four to five full-plan 8-byte arrays on a plan HEC-RAS stores in single precision; when the CSV-as-points path failed, the GeoPackage fallback wrote millions of features in a Python loop.
  - **Change.** `RESULT_DTYPE = np.float32` for `wse` / `vel` / `peak` / `arr` and the series blocks (`arrival_days` and `_pixel_first_reach` subtract the float64 ground, so the depth test is still done in double); cell centres, the cell minimum and the cell-id grid stay float64. `_translate_points` writes the fallback GeoPackage with one `gdal.VectorTranslate` of the CSV and verifies the layer; `_write_points` (the loop) is the last resort. The code digest covers the new function.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_rasimport_float32.py` (stand-in reader; dtypes, wet / dry parity with a float64 read, `arrival_days` on a float32 series). `test_rasimport_terrain_arrival.py` needs GDAL + QGIS and could not run here.

- **PMP 0.4.3: vectorised storm step, cached grid selection, FileGDBs opened once (audit P3-PMP).** RELEASE unchanged (PMP is not in versions.py); damsuite content unchanged.
  - **Found.** `core/pmp.py` ran a per-storm x per-point Python loop with a `Decimal` quantize per value for every duration; `basin_average` / `weighted_weights` re-ran `cells_intersecting` + `points_in_cells` (a spatial filter over 68,277 cells and a union) for every duration and subbasin; `read_dad` / `read_storm_factors` re-opened the FileGDBs per storm; `tests/test_light.py` could not run without GDAL because `core/__init__` imported `pmp`.
  - **Change.** `storm_step` / `factor_arrays`: TAF and TRANS as arrays once per storm, `adj = where(trans == 1, py2round_array(y * taf, 1), 0)`, strict `>` keeps the first storm at a tie as before. `py2round_array` is bit-exact with `py2round`: nearest integer of `x * 10**n` where that is farther than 2 ulp from a half-integer, the scalar Decimal rounding at and near ties. `Grid.selection` / `Grid.weights` cache per basin geometry (WKB key). `_Sources` opens each geodatabase once per run. GDAL imported lazily; `core/__init__` resolves its names on first use.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_vector_round.py` (10,518 values incl. exact .x5 ties, n = 0..3, bit-exact; `storm_step` vs the 0.4.1 loop on 12 random trials with null factors and a storm with no zone), added to the light list. No PMP data here: `run_core.py` / `verify.py` need the AWA geodatabases.

- **Runner 3.16.10: LISFLOOD-FP wall-clock limits, cheaper progress polling, cached DEM warps and float32 results (audit P2 / P3 / P4).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.16.10; damsuite content unchanged (pin rewritten for versions.py).
  - **Found.** (P4) `lisflood._run` re-parsed every row of the `.mass` file once a second for the progress bar - a multi-thousand-row file on a long run. (P2) The LISFLOOD-FP child had no wall-clock limit and the domain-growth loop no cumulative budget, so a hung or unstable solver held the run for ever; the solver's time step was never looked at. (P3) `GdalTerrain.read` warped the same DEM window from disk on every call (the crest window, the inflow window, the planning grid, the model domain, each again on a clock extension or a resume), and `read_results` loaded `.max`, `.maxVc`, `.inittm` and `.mxe` as float64 (four full-domain 8-byte arrays, each 0.001 m / 0.01 ft quantities).
  - **Change.** `lisflood.read_mass_tail` seeks to the last 4 KB and returns the last complete row (a partial trailing line is ignored); the final full parse is unchanged. New advanced parameters `LISFLOOD_MAX_WALL_S` (one run; 0 = automatic: 6 x the `core/estimate.py` run-time estimate when the session or the CLI's `model.lisflood_rate` knows this computer's `calibrate()` speed, else 24 h, floor 1 h) and `LISFLOOD_MAX_TOTAL_WALL_S` (the growth loop; 0 = Most LISFLOOD-FP runs x the per-run limit), CLI `model.max_wall_s` / `max_total_wall_s` / `lisflood_rate`, `RunSettings.max_wall_s` / `max_total_wall_s` / `lisflood_rate`; on expiry the child is killed and the run stops with `ModelError` naming the limit and the setting; the loop stops with a plain warning (`cut_off` "wall-clock budget", report `lisflood_wall`) and keeps the last finished pass. The solver's dt is logged once a minute and warned about once when it falls below the CFL step of 100 m of water on the model cell (`dt_collapse_s`). `GdalTerrain` keeps a 3-entry LRU of warped DEM windows keyed by (bounds snapped to the cell, cell, resample, z factor), invalidated when the file's path, size or mtime changes; `read_results` returns float32 depth / velocity / arrival / WSE grids (elevation grids stay float64). Neither limit changes a run that finishes, so both stay out of the run key.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_wall_limits.py` (tail read on a 5,000-row file with a partial trailing line; a stand-in child that sleeps is killed at a 2 s limit; dt collapse warned once; the growth loop stops at a 1.5 s budget; the tool / CLI / settings catalog carry the settings), `tests/test_dem_cache.py` (the LRU hit / miss / invalidation rules on a fake warp), `tests/test_results_float32.py` (dtypes and the stats unchanged to 1e-3 against the float64 read). The golden built-in-engine baseline is unchanged.

- **Hazard 2.23.16 / Runner 3.16.11 / Screening 0.18.18 / Data 1.9.5 / Setup 1.0.21 / PMP 0.4.4 / Hazard Lite 0.1.6: usability, docs and release (audit U6; repository metadata; changelog trim; GitHub Release + QGIS plugin repository).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.23.16 / ndbreach 3.16.11 / ndscreen 0.18.18; damsuite content unchanged (pin rewritten for versions.py).
  - **Found.** The root README.md was empty and `DamSafety_Data/README.md` still described the "ND Data Updater" zip on a Mac. Four plugins carried Plugin Builder placeholders (`author=Your Name`, `example.com` links, empty `tracker=` / `repository=`), which the Plugin Manager shows and which would have gone into `plugins.xml`. The Runner's `metadata.txt` `changelog=` was 149 KB (Hazard 86 KB, Screening 40 KB) and QGIS's Plugin Manager parses and renders all of it for every installed plugin. There was no release: no zips, no plugin repository, no binaries, and the Setup dock's "Build LISFLOOD-FP…" (`status_dock.py`) started a bash script with a bare `Popen` and a "Started…" box, so no result, error or path ever reached the user (U6); the Oct-15 audit's M15 (Setup writes `ndsuite/lisflood_exe`, the Runner read only its own key) was fixed on the Runner's side, so Setup keeps writing the suite key.
  - **Change.** Root `README.md` (the seven plugins, install order Setup → Data → Runner → Hazard, the three install routes, first run, LISFLOOD-FP / ANUGA per OS, links to the docs); `DamSafety_Data/README.md` rewritten from `plugin.py` / `core.py` / `sources.py`. Every `metadata.txt` names `repository=` / `tracker=` / `homepage=` (https://github.com/nickdbendickson/dAmIt_Plugins) and a real author; `tools/ci_checks.py` "metadata format" rejects `example.com` / `example.invalid`, an empty `repository=` / `tracker=` / `homepage=` and `author=Your Name`. Each `changelog=` keeps its newest 7 entries; the older ones are below, verbatim, under `## Archived metadata changelog — <plugin>`; the "vs main" rule accepts an entry that left `metadata.txt` when its version is still named here (bullet title or archived section) and fails a `changelog=` over 12 KB (`CHANGELOG_MAX_BYTES`). New `.github/workflows/release.yml` (tag `v*` or by hand): LISFLOOD-FP 8.3 from Zenodo with the Runner's patch for linux-x64 (`build_lisflood_linux.sh`, new `LISFLOOD_STATIC=1`), macos-arm64 (`macos-14`, `build_lisflood_macos.sh`, `libomp.dylib` bundled with `@executable_path`) and windows-x64 (new `scripts/build_lisflood_windows_cross.sh`, MinGW-w64 posix, `-static`; the MinGW `_aligned_malloc` edits of the nd_hazard fork are now in `scripts/patch_lisflood.py` and the Mac 8.3 diff); new `tools/build_release.py` zips the committed files (no `tests/`, caches, dotfiles) with the binaries under `DamSafety_Runner/bin/<platform>/`; the GitHub Release gets the zips, `SHA256SUMS`, each binary, the patched source and `plugins.xml`; new `tools/make_plugins_xml.py` writes the QGIS plugin repository index, published to `gh-pages` (`peaceiris/actions-gh-pages@v4`, `keep_files`) at https://nickdbendickson.github.io/dAmIt_Plugins/plugins.xml. Runner `core/lisflood.bundled_platform` / `bundled_exe` know `macos-arm64` and `macos-x64` besides `linux-x64` / `windows-x64`, and `nd_data.lisflood_candidates` lists the bundled build after every installed one (never the built-in engine). Setup `status_dock.py`: `QProcess`, output streamed into a ▸ Log fold-out, `lisflood_exe` written to the suite store on success, both build buttons off on Windows with a why-tooltip; the Linux build script (identical in Runner and Setup) and the macOS one print "DamSafety Runner" as the provider. `.gitignore` covers `DamSafety_Runner/bin/`, `bin/`, `dist/`. `docs/CI.md` documents the changelog policy and the release workflow.
  - **Tests.** `tools/test_ci_checks.py`: a trimmed entry kept in the archived section or a bullet title passes, an entry in neither still fails, the 12 KB cap, the placeholder rule. `DamSafety_Setup/tests/test_status_dock_u6.py` (new), `DamSafety_Runner/tests/realqt_dock_area.py` (Log fold-out, button state), `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_v3110.py` (`bundled_platform`). The release workflow itself cannot run here: YAML parsed; the Linux static build and `tools/build_release.py` / `tools/make_plugins_xml.py` were run locally on the nd_hazard 8.2 source.

- **Runner 3.16.7 / Hazard 2.23.11 / Screening 0.18.15: tri-repo audit (correctness) batch 2.** RELEASE ndbreach 3.16.7, ndhazard 2.23.11, ndscreen 0.18.15; damsuite content unchanged (versions.py is fork-specific, pin unchanged). Six confirmed high-severity findings, each verified by an adversarial review panel; the ones that move a published number move it in the conservative direction and are documented here.
  - **Runner H06 — skip only a finished run.** The batch queue and the CLI skipped a dam on `Depth (Max).tif` alone, but every LISFLOOD-FP pass writes the final rasters so a cancel does not lose work; a dam cancelled in its second pass was reported "already run" and skipped with its first-pass (truncated) rasters left as the result. `workflow.run_finished()` now also requires the run report with a truthy `run_finished` stamp, so interrupted dams are re-run to completion. A truncated or unreadable report is not finished (the report used to be treated as done on any parse error, so a kill during `json.dump` still skipped the dam). The report is replaced atomically. No value a finished run computes changes.
  - **Runner H08 — boundary outflow surfaced (diagnostic).** The domain-extension loop grows the model only while an edge is deeper than the 1 ft tolerance, so a wide shallow sheet can carry a large share of the breach volume off the model while every edge stays under tolerance — understating the flood area and consequences with no signal. The `.mass` Qout is now integrated into `boundary_outflow_acft`/`_pct` and, at ≥ 5 %, warns the result is a minimum. Value-neutral: the domain is not grown (the golden baseline is unchanged); the correction that grows it moves an issued classification and is held for sign-off.
  - **Runner H09 — resume from the domain-run counter.** The 3.13.2 while-loop made clock-extension and 3DEP-refill passes append a checkpoint without advancing the domain-run index, so `start_it` overshot on resume: a run could resume past `max_iterations` (lf_env None → AttributeError, un-resumable) or silently perform fewer domain runs than a cold start (understated extent / acres / receptors). The counter (`next_it`) is now persisted and read back (old checkpoints fall back), a desync raises a clear error, and a resumed extent / acreage can grow to match a cold start (conservative). DWR-only (nd's for-range loop already held the invariant).
  - **Hazard H19 — refuse a mixed-velocity multi-breach run.** The "no velocity raster → 1D criterion" fallback was decided from `breaches[0]` only. On a multi-breach run where one scenario has Vmax and another has depth only, the data-less scenario was classified at velocity 0 (the ACER zone read off the curves at v = 0), lowering the governing class. The whole-run 1D fallback now fires only when every breach lacks velocity; a mixed set is refused with a clear error (fail-closed: supply Vmax for every scenario or choose 1D). No published number is lowered.
  - **Screening H10 — LiDAR mosaic across CRSs.** `build_vrts` used a bare `gdal.BuildVRT`, which keeps the first tile's CRS and silently drops tiles in another; the ND LiDAR index spans UTM 13N / 14N and State Plane N / S, so a reservoir straddling a CRS boundary lost tiles and understated storage → breach volume → hazard class. It now warps minority-CRS tiles into the majority native CRS before the mosaic (single-CRS downloads unchanged). Conservative (restored tiles only add storage); county / state and boundary-area downloads that spanned CRSs should be re-run.
  - **Screening H23 — triage samples the polygon interior.** Tool 6 tested a polygon receptor (habitable structure footprint, lifeline parcel) only at its ring vertices and their mean, while tool 8 — the 2D tool triage is a conservative shortcut for — classifies the same footprint on the deepest of every wet cell centre inside the ring. A footprint whose interior or edge cells were wet while all its vertices landed on dry cells read `clear` and the dam could be stamped Low. The receptor test now also applies `hz.nh.sample_footprint` for polygons, after the vertex/mean test. Monotone and conservative: a receptor can only move clear → needs-2D, never the reverse; no threshold or chart is touched.
- **CI matrix: an LTR / Qt5 leg, numpy 2 legs, GUI lint on a 1080p display, the Runner fast suite in the fast gate (audit CI matrix 2026-11-07; tests and CI only, no plugin version change).** Runner stays 3.16.6; damsuite untouched.
  - **Found.** `metadata.txt` promises `qgisMinimumVersion=3.28` and the owner runs QGIS 3.28 LTR, but `plugin-suites` and `model-tests` install an unpinned conda `qgis` that resolves to QGIS 4.x / Qt6, so no Qt5 line was tested. Every job pinned `numpy<2`, which hid that `np.cross` on 2-vectors raises under numpy 2 (`core/hecras_structures.py:212`, `core/hecras_crest.py:552/555`; fixed on `claude/reliability-engine-guards`). `tools/gui_lint.py` ran offscreen only, where docs/gui-grammar.md's "no page scrolls sideways" rule returns early (800x600 screen). The fast gate skipped the Runner's stub-Qt suite because its 32 GDAL-backed tests failed, not skipped, without `osgeo`.
  - **Change.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: new `plugin-suites-ltr` job runs the same suites in the official `qgis/qgis:release-3_34` container (Ubuntu 22.04 system `python3`, Qt5, GDAL 3.4, pip `numpy<2`, scipy and pytest; nothing from conda); `plugin-suites` drops its `numpy<2` pin (conda-forge scipy resolves with numpy 2) and is the Qt6 / numpy 2 leg; `fast-checks` installs `numpy>=2` + scipy and runs `python3 -m nd_breach_runner.tests.run_tests`; both suite legs probe an xvfb 1920x1080 display and run `gui_lint.py` under `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=xcb` there, falling back to the offscreen run with a warning annotation when xvfb is unusable. `model-tests` unchanged (`numpy<2`). `docs/CI.md`: the table, the matrix section, the four required checks and the local recipe.
  - **Tests.** `DamSafety_Runner/tests/registry.py` gets `Skip` and `need_osgeo()`; `run_tests.py` counts a `Skip` as skipped (`N tests, F failed, S skipped`) instead of failed. The GDAL helpers / tests of `test_hecras_structures.py`, `test_hecras_terrain_crs.py`, `test_warp_no_overviews.py`, `test_qa_basemap.py`, `test_hecras_line_bands.py`, `test_hecras_breaklines.py`, `test_hecras_crest_offcrest.py` and `test_h8_z_units.py` call `need_osgeo()` first; with GDAL present every one of them runs as before. Plain python 3.13 + numpy 2.5 + scipy: 1161 tests, 32 skipped, 4 failed (the three `np.cross` hecras_crest tests, red on the numpy 2 legs until the engine-guards branch merges, and the 2-core `anuga_threads_job_file_gets_the_resolved_count`).

- **Runner 3.16.6 / Screening 0.18.14: classifying velocity over all wet cells (audit H07; Nick's call).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.16.6, ndscreen 0.18.14; damsuite content unchanged (versions.py is fork-specific, pin unchanged). The ND hazard sampler (`core/nd_hazard.sample_footprint` and the Runner's `algorithms_ndhazard.Sampler`) took the peak velocity only over cells at or above the 0.1 ft `wet_ft` floor, while `DamSafety_Hazard` classifies on the peak velocity over every wet (depth > 0) cell (`structures.peak_pair`). The same rasters and footprint could then read HIGH in Hazard and JUDGMENT in the Runner/Screening when a sub-0.1 ft wetting-front cell carried a high velocity (reachable via the HEC-RAS import or an external velocity raster). The sampler now uses the (depth > 0) cell-set, matching Hazard. The 0.1 ft "is the structure flooded" dry-gate is unchanged; peak depth and vel_at_max are unchanged. This can raise a structure's zone and never lowers one - the conservative resolution of the divergence. Test `tests/test_nd_hazard.py` updated (the trace-depth fringe cell now contributes the classifying velocity).

- **Hazard 2.23.10 / Runner 3.16.5 / Screening 0.18.13: tri-repo audit (correctness) fixes.** RELEASE ndhazard 2.23.10, ndbreach 3.16.5, ndscreen 0.18.13; damsuite loss.py changed (pin unchanged - versions.py is fork-specific). These move published safety numbers in the conservative direction; the changes are documented here and were verified by an adversarial review panel.
  - **Loss of life / PAR (shared damsuite `loss.py`).** A flooded structure with no known arrival time was filed in the longest warning band (">60 min"), which carries the smallest fatality rate, so the population-at-risk warning-time split and, with a signed rate table, the loss-of-life total were understated - the direction the suite's own rule forbids. Unknown arrivals now count as no warning (UNKNOWN_WARNING_BAND = "under15", matching the Runner's consequences.warning_class), and the count is reported (arrival_unknown / par_arrival_unknown, surfaced in estimate_text). `warning_band()` now returns "" for NaN as well as None. WARNING_BANDS, the rate-table format and the DV/depth/time thresholds are untouched. Affects every plugin that vendors damsuite. (audit H12)
  - **Sensitivity sweep (`DamSafety_Hazard/sensitivity.py`).** `_scale_structures` reduced per cell (the pre-2.22.3 same-cell rule) while the unscaled base row classifies on the peak depth / peak wet-cell velocity pair (option C), so every scaled row was compared against a differently-paired base - producing spurious optimistic zone flips, and classifying a structure with no velocity raster as LOW while its run zone was higher. It now builds the peak pair with reduceat exactly as `peak_pair` does and takes the more severe of that and the per-cell minimum. Flip counts, per-factor classes and the verdict line move conservatively; the classification is unchanged. Test `tests/test_sensitivity_peak_pair.py`. (audit H10/H08)
  - **Screening storage leak guard (`DamSafety_Screening/core/storage.py`).** A strict `d < stage` made the pool-level reference footprint empty, so the pool-level prism branch was dead, the "already at the border" exemption was defeated, and the bisection floor was 0 - collapsing the above-pool curve to one point and reporting zero above-pool storage for a DEM pool that reaches the clip border or has a notch. The top-of-dam storage fed to the breach then missed the above-pool volume and a dam could read "clear" on triage. The reference footprints use a tiny epsilon and the pool-level prism test is edge-only; no-leak curves are unchanged. (audit H17)
  - **Screening triage road rule (`DamSafety_Screening/algorithms/triage.py`).** Tool 6 never passed the road name / NG9-1-1 shield to `nd_road`, so a US / State highway identified only by name/shield with AADT < 500 fell to Low and was dropped from the triage hit list. It now reads the name/shield as tool 8 does; conservative widening (5.2.1.2(b)). (audit H19)
- **Runner 3.15.0: crest burn and the automatic finer grid, both on by default (ports of ND Runner 3.27.7 + 3.28.0; Nick: "do 2 and 3" as defaults in both plugin sets).**
  - **Crest burn** (`core/crestburn.py`, byte-identical to the ND Runner's): every model cell a road or railway crosses is raised to the highest 1 m ground inside it (never lowered), so the embankment crest the cell average flattens still holds water back. 1 m source: CREST_BURN_DEM / provider setting, else the run's DEM when 2 m or finer, else the suite data folder's DEM (the statewide 1 m VRT), else USGS 3DEP 1 m through the terrain cache. Traced main channel, bridges and raises over 5 m left alone; inflow search / trace / planning reads unburned. Toggle: CREST_BURN, window check box, CLI `model.crest_burn`.
  - **Grid-spread check + finer grid**: the ND 3.27.7 signature (cell > 10 m, flood >= 1,000 ac, median wet depth <= 1.0 ft) now re-runs the dam on the same domain at 10 m (the record) and optionally 5 m (`Grid check 5 m`; off by default since a 5 m check of 2801 took ~14 h, set GRID_CHECK_CELL = 5 to opt in, convergence check). Toggle: GRID_REFINE / GRID_CHECK_CELL, window check box, CLI `model.grid_refine` / `grid_check_cell_m`.
  - Evidence (DWR-2801): 10 m + 1 m crest burn took the extent from 1.46x to 1.33x of DSS-WISE at > 0.5 ft; 20/10/5 m CSI 0.36/0.43/0.57. Tests: `tests/test_crest_burn.py`, `tests/test_grid_spread_advisory.py`.
- **Runner 3.14.7: terrain-aware grid default on flat section-road plains.** A coarse DEM window (~4 km across, half 2 km; was ~8 km, which missed Absaraka at 32.25 m) around the dam is classified by relief (p95-p5 <= 25 m) and mean neighbour slope (<= 1%). Flat plains start at 10 m, with **5 m (finer, slower)** offered in the Advanced hint and CELL / grid_m tooltips; a pre-run WARNING fires when the cell is coarser than 10 m on a flat plain (report `terrain_grid`). Other terrain keeps the existing 10 m start; setting keys stay numeric metres. Evidence: DWR-2801 (Absaraka) Claude Runner pack vs DSS-WISE wet at >=0.5 ft scored CSI 0.36 / 0.43 / 0.57 at 20 / 10 / 5 m (Docs & QA compare; Nick "Do that"). Module `core/grid_default.py`; test `tests/test_grid_default.py`. RELEASE ndbreach 3.14.7; damsuite content otherwise unchanged (pin rewritten for versions.py).
- **Screening 0.18.7: DEM download works when the PROJ grid server cannot be reached (GUI check follow-up; Nick: "Fix").** RELEASE ndscreen 0.18.7; damsuite content otherwise unchanged (pin rewritten for versions.py).
  - **Found.** With `PROJ_NETWORK=ON` and cdn.proj.org unreachable, PROJ picks a network grid for a datum step (e.g. NAD83 -> HARN via `us_noaa_ndhpgn.tif`) and building or using the transform raises `RuntimeError`. Four area boxes hit it, so a DEM download stopped before it began: `dem_download.to_5070` (USGS 3DEP request), `dem_download._box_2266` (ND DWR tile index), `lidar_tnm._bounds_in` (USGS 1 m lidar VRT: "building the VRT failed: PROJ: Cannot open https://cdn.proj.org/us_noaa_ndhpgn.tif") and `lidar_tnm.box_5070_to_ll` (a dam folder's Runner domain).
  - **Change.** New `core/proj_offline.py`: `box(fn)` runs the box once as-is and, on `RuntimeError` or a non-finite result while the network is on, again with the PROJ network off, then restores the setting. A download / tile box needs no grid (a few metres either way are inside its buffer). The four boxes go through it. Heights, DEM values and vertical datums never pass through it. `find_ponds` already falls back to reading every water body and is unchanged.
  - Tests: `test_dem_units_review.area_box_works_when_the_proj_grid_server_is_unreachable` (simulated unreachable server, all four boxes; fails on 0.18.6). `test_dem_units_once` and `test_7b_dem_download_gdal` build their made-up data with the PROJ network off (fixtures only). The Screening suite passes with `PROJ_NETWORK` on (no server reachable) and off; the DEM-unit suites also pass with the NOAA grids installed.
- **Test only: `test_dem_z_once` with a geoid grid (second GUI check of 2026-11-04).** No plugin change, no version change.
  - **Found.** Still failed with `PROJ_NETWORK=ON` on a machine that reaches cdn.proj.org. The test makes a deliberately bad warp (no `-novshift`) to check that the run refuses it. With a geoid grid, GDAL also turns that warp's NAVD88 heights into ellipsoidal ones (396.2 -> 370.7, about -25 m in ND), and the test expected the no-grid number 396.2 in the stop message. The run still stopped.
  - **Change.** The expected numbers are read from the warped file, so the test passes with or without the grid. Reproduced with the NOAA grids installed locally (proj-data 1.9): failed before, passes with `PROJ_NETWORK` ON and OFF, with and without grids.
  - **Real runs.** With the geoid grid present, the Runner's own reads of a compound-CRS DEM keep its heights (`dem_unit_is_applied_once_through_a_compound_crs`: GdalTerrain, read_like, the HEC-RAS terrain, all at 396.24 m from 1300 ftUS). The other DEM-unit and vertical-datum suites also pass with the grid.
- **Runner 3.14.6 / Hazard 2.23.4 / Screening 0.18.6 / Setup 1.0.20 / Hazard Lite 0.1.4: GUI check fixes (GUI + test check of 2026-11-04 on main 395c277; Nick: "Yes").** RELEASE ndbreach 3.14.6, ndhazard 2.23.4, ndscreen 0.18.6; damsuite content otherwise unchanged (pin rewritten for versions.py).
  - **Runner window.** Minimum width 760 (was 820). `_fit_body_width` measured the frame around the body as window width minus the scroll area's viewport, before the first show, when the viewport still has its default size: about 340 px of "frame", so the window could not shrink below 829-867 px. It now adds the window margins and the vertical scroll bar to the body's minimum (about 40 px).
  - **Dam suite home** (`shared/ui/suite_home.py`, synced): no fixed 900 px minimum; `fit_window` opens it at 960 x 600 where the screen has room and lets it shrink to the screen, never below its button rows.
  - **Docks at 420 px.** Breach results: the five layer buttons sit on two rows (Depth / Velocity / Arrival, then the two vector layers); it needed 529 px. Setup's Suite status: the four buttons sit two to a row; it needed 500 px.
  - **Hazard Lite.** `core.VERSION` (window title, report) is read from metadata.txt; it said 0.1.2 while metadata.txt said 0.1.3.
  - **Test, environment only.** `test_dem_z_once` failed with `PROJ_NETWORK=ON` where cdn.proj.org cannot be reached: NAD83(2011) -> NAD83 then picks a NADCON5 grid, the made-up DEM got an inf geotransform and read 0.0. The made-up DEMs are now built with PROJ's network grids off; the reads (the code under test) are unchanged and pass with the network on or off.
  - **Processing panels at 800 px.** Short tick-box labels, the detail moved to the parameter help (names and defaults unchanged): Runner `sensitivity` SENS_METHODS / SENS_CELL (panel about 1400 px), `probabilistic` PROB_RUN_RASTERS (about 1100 px); Screening `hazardclassification` INCREMENTAL (950 px), `runnerhandoff` REFINE_NEAR (765 px). Every DamSafety Processing panel now needs at most 640 px (measured under Xvfb with QGIS 3.34).
  - **Lint.** `tools/gui_lint.py`: every window must shrink to 800 px wide (was 1400).
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/realqt_dock_area.py` (both docks fit 420 px), `DamSafety_HazardLite/tests/test_core.py` (VERSION = metadata.txt).
- **Screening 0.18.5: the self-test takes the LISFLOOD-FP executable (fork parity with nd Screening 0.17.0; Nick: "Yes fix").** RELEASE ndscreen 0.18.5; damsuite content otherwise unchanged.
  - **Found.** Running tool 14 headless on a Mac (`qgis_process run nddam:selftest -- ENGINE=0 EXE=...`) failed at step 7: DamSafety's self-test had no `EXE` parameter and passed none to tool 7, so a LISFLOOD-FP engine worked only when tool 7 found the binary on its own (`NDBREACH_LISFLOOD` or PATH). The nd copy fixed this in 0.17.0.
  - **Change.** `algorithms/selftest.py`: optional `EXE` file parameter (filter `run_breach_sim.EXE_FILTER`, "All files (*)", since the binary has no extension on macOS / Linux), passed to `core.selftest.run_selftest(..., exe=)` and on to step 7's `EXE`. Empty leaves `EXE` out of step 7, so tool 7 falls back as before. Tool 7 itself is unchanged.
  - Test: `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_selftest_exe.py` (new).
- **Runner 3.14.5 / Hazard 2.23.3 / Screening 0.18.5 / Data 1.5.4: GUI fit fixes and 3DHP labels (QGIS Dev's 800x600 review; Nick approved Nov 3).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.14.5, ndhazard 2.23.3, ndscreen 0.18.5; damsuite `servers.py` changed (below), pin `tools/damsuite_pin.json` rewritten.
  - **Narrower Processing panels.** Long labels on `rundam` (STOP_RULE, REFINE_NEAR, RESUME, ANIMATE, PRODUCTS, ALLOW_FLAGGED_STRUCTURE, CULVERT_BURN_*, S_INVERT_TOL_FT, BASELINE, FORCE_ANUGA, HECRAS_*) and `runnerculverts` (SEARCH_FT, OSM_SEARCH_FT, USE_NBI_WIDTH, ALLOW_NO_EMBANKMENT, PASS_ELEVATIONS) are shortened; the detail moved to the parameter help. The Settings window grid shows the help (`suite_settings_tools.param_defs` carries `help`).
  - **Persistence.** CULVERT_BURN_GUESSES and OSM_SEARCH_FT are already rows in the Settings window (Dam / Hazard tabs), so a saved value is the default when the tool is reopened; the help now says so, and a test proves the round trip.
  - **OSM_SEARCH_FT** shows 1 decimal and has a 3280.8 ft (1 km) maximum (SEARCH_FT too).
  - **Settings Dam tab.** The S_INVERT_TOL_FT rows (rundam / batch / estimate) were truncated to look like duplicates; the label is shorter, and the settings grid (`shared/ui/suite_settings.py`, synced) now adds the tool name to any label that several tools share, e.g. "... to the LiDAR bed — Run breach model for one dam".
  - **Classify.** The four tick boxes have short labels with the detail in tooltips, so they fit 640x480.
  - **Data windows.** DamSafety Data's Settings window (needed 841 px of height) and Build offline pack (645 px) scroll their body; the buttons stay under it.
  - **Naming.** "ND Dam Breach Runner" is now "DamSafety Runner" in the reviewed tool labels; "NHD flowlines" wording is 3DHP where it means the current source (suite export, Screening's data package / Run breach sim / package check / workflow slot; setting keys and the NHD_flowlines data set name unchanged).
  - **Screening county package flowlines.** `damsuite/servers.py` VECTOR_SOURCES `flowlines` is "3DHP flowlines" from `3DHP_all/MapServer/50` (was "NHD flowlines" from `nhd/MapServer/6`); `core/offline.py` adds the NHD-style fields with `hydro3dhp.normaliser_for_url`, as the Hazard downloads do.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_guifix_20261103.py` (new).
- **Runner 3.14.4 / Hazard 2.23.2: OpenStreetMap culverts in the structures layer (Nick: "do culvert ideas", from the DWR-10554 run on main 33a93c0).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.14.4, ndhazard 2.23.2 (numbered above the 3.14.0 guessed-culvert PR); damsuite content otherwise unchanged.
  - **Found.** OSM way/492688522 (Culvert under the BNSF ZAP SPUR, near EPSG:5070 -440541, 2717772) was never modelled: the structures layer was built without OSM, and Culvert prep had no OSM input.
  - **Hazard `runnerculverts`.** New `osm_search_m` / `OSM_SEARCH_FT` (default 60 m): an OSM culvert or ford is placed only on a stream x road / rail crossing that close (bridges keep `search_m`, 150 m; the smaller wins). On DWR-10554 the example is 3.6 m off the rail, 15.5 m off the 3DHP line and 46.3 m from the 3DHP x rail crossing; 60 m places it and 39 of the 79 OSM records as culverts (30 m: 34 and not the example; 150 m: 43). An OSM culvert with no railway tag takes the nearest crossing of any road or rail line (was: a road first). `source` OSM, `src_id` the way; the notes say mapped location, size not surveyed.
  - **Runner.** Culvert prep's build step has a `Build: OpenStreetMap crossings` input; a STRUCTURES feature with `source` OSM is labelled `OSM way/...`, not surveyed, in the run report (`core/structures.label_source`, Processing tool and `ndbreach_cli`). No size in OSM: the default culvert size (`S_DEFAULT_WIDTH/HEIGHT`, 4 ft).
  - **Class.** On DWR-10554 the crossings are overtopped by 1.6-3.4 ft; a 4 ft culvert passes a small fraction of that flow, so the class is not expected to change (no model re-run in this PR).
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_runnerculverts_osm.py`, `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_osm_structures.py` (new).
- **Runner 3.14.3 (built as 3.14.0): guessed culverts are cut into the DEM, marked unverified (Nick: "do culvert ideas", from the DWR-10554 run on main 33a93c0).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.14.3; damsuite content otherwise unchanged.
  - **Found.** The 7 road x channel guesses on DWR-10554 (one 1.2 x 0.9 m box each, `culvert_candidates.csv`) were listed but "not cut into the DEM", so the model saw a solid embankment where a culvert almost certainly is.
  - **Change.** `culvert_guess.guess_structures` turns each unmatched guess into a culvert with the same cut a matched guess gets (`burn_line` along the channel, `BURN_MIN_CELLS`, `lidar_inverts`); `source` guess, `surveyed` false, the name starts with `guess:`, the CSV `note` says it was cut, and `run_report` gets `culvert_guesses_cut` and a "Guessed culverts cut into the DEM (unverified)" section. Matched guesses still follow `culvert_burn_matched` (off).
  - **Setting.** `culvert_burn_guesses` / `CULVERT_BURN_GUESSES` (default on, advanced); off lists them only, as before.
  - **Class.** On DWR-10554 the crossings are overtopped by 1.6-3.4 ft; a 1.2 x 0.9 m box passes a small fraction of that flow, so the class is not expected to change (no model re-run in this PR).
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_culvert_match.py` (two new).
- **Hazard 2.23.1, Runner 3.14.2, Screening 0.18.4, HazardLite 0.1.3: lint and the Runner window width (2026-11-03 audit, fix E).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.23.1, ndbreach 3.14.2, ndscreen 0.18.4; damsuite unchanged.
  - **Runner window.** "Guided wizard…" on its own row under the four Run-tab result buttons; `_fit_body_width` sets the window's minimum width to the scrolled body's minimum plus the frame (capped at the screen). At 800 x 600 the body needed about 800 px and Browse… / File… / Guided wizard… were clipped (audit shot DamSafety_Runner__002); now about 660 px.
  - **Screening storage.py.** `conf_leak` was set and never read, and both branches gave "medium". Kept the behaviour: low when a value was defaulted / not found or the storage-leak guard held the storage (its note contains "storage leak"), else medium - as the nd copy. Code simplified, result unchanged.
  - **Lint.** hecras_model.py weir-cell note F522 (unused `u`); HazardLite dialog.py unused `v`, `QWidget`, `T`; Runner tests/run_tests.py second `fake_qgis as fq` (F811); unused imports: QCheckBox in seven Hazard tabs, lone911 QgsGeometry, hecras_fine math, hecras_windows shutil. Left on purpose: compat / qtcompat / ui_check_panel / PMP core re-exports and the optional-dependency probes (h5py, cairosvg, scipy Voronoi, contourpy, osgeo.gdal, win32com, matplotlib), and local F841s whose right-hand side does work.
- **Hazard 2.22.6, Runner 3.13.10, Screening 0.18.3, Data 1.5.3, PMP 0.4.1: wording sync (2026-11-03 audit, fix D).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.22.6, ndbreach 3.13.10, ndscreen 0.18.3; damsuite loss.py docstring only.
  - **3DHP flowlines (Nick: "3DHP").** `constants.NHD_FLOWLINE_URL` (Hazard and Data, the shared constants.py) is the USGS 3DHP service `3DHP_all/MapServer/50` (was `nhd/MapServer/6`), as the nd Hazard Helper 1.19.0; `download._fetch_arcgis_live` gives each 3DHP feature the NHD properties (`damsuite.hydro3dhp.normaliser_for_url`). Hazard labels and messages say "3DHP flowlines" (pipeline.py, pipeline_extras, classify_tab, data_tab, datafetch, estimate, processing_provider, traveltime, termination, datafolder, suite_algorithms, runnerculverts). Data set names (`NHD_flowlines`) and identifiers unchanged; waterbodies stay on NHD.
  - **Spelling.** judgement -> judgment (Runner report_docx, damsuite loss.py; Nick's quotes unchanged); meters -> metres in prose (Hazard helpers docstring, Screening handoff comment) - the "Meters" DEM-unit choice labels and unit-name lists are left, the Screening -> Runner hand-off matches the label; fps -> ft/s (Hazard helpers.py dz log).
  - **PMP name.** metadata `name=DamSafety PMP (prototype)` (was "ND PMP Tool (prototype)"), as its window title.
- **Hazard 2.22.5: DV is peak depth x peak wet-cell velocity; missing velocity filled with the structure's peak (2026-11-03 audit, fix C; option C).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.22.5; damsuite content otherwise unchanged.
  - **DV.** `structures.evaluate_structures`: `dv_max` = peak depth x peak wet-cell velocity (`peak_pair`, the pair the zone is classified on), never sampled from a DV raster (a DV raster only marks where the worst cell is). With the velocity unknown at every wet cell `dv_max` is None, `consequences.flood_severity` says "velocity unknown - no DV" (depth only) and Notes say "velocity unknown at every wet cell - depth only, no DV - verify".
  - **Missing velocity.** A NaN velocity on a wet cell is filled with the structure's peak velocity, else 0 (`_fill_missing_velocity`, as the nd Hazard Helper 2.35.x); was 0, which read the structure on the optimistic side. Notes: "velocity missing on some wet cells (filled with the structure's peak velocity, else 0) - verify".
  - Test: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_dv_peak_pair.py` (new).
- **HazardLite 0.1.2: the judgment zone is ND MEDIUM by rule by default (audit 2026-11-03, Nick: "Fix all").** No damsuite change; no other plugin changes.
  - `judgment_high` defaults to False in `core.structures/classify/run`, the window tick and the Processing `JUDGMENT_HIGH` parameter (label: "Count the judgment zone as High (old option; off = ND MEDIUM by rule)"). Off, the 5.2.1.1 reason is Medium and reads "judgment zone - ND MEDIUM by rule"; houses above the high line still drive High.
  - Docs & QA nits: `velocity_unknown` is in `S_COLS` (CSV and gpkg), `vmax_fps` is `nanmax` (blank when all NaN), README covers velocity unknown.
  - Test: `DamSafety_HazardLite/tests/test_core.py`.

- **Hazard 2.22.4: DSS-WISE result names with units after the quantity are autodetected (Nick: "Do the DSS-WISE autodetect fix PR").** RELEASE ndhazard 2.22.4; damsuite content otherwise unchanged.
  - **Found.** `autodetect._is_time_step` took the text after "Depth"/"Velocity" for an output time, so `Depth_ft.tif`, `Velocity_ftps.tif`, `Depth_m.asc` ... were skipped in the dam folder (any case, any depth of sub-folder). A label made only of unit words is no longer a time step; `Depth_0100`, `Depth (01JAN2020 12:00:00)` still are.
  - **Units.** `dsswise.detect_units` reads units right after the quantity word: `Velocity_ft_s` / `Velocity_ft_per_s` are ft/s and `Depth_m` / `Velocity_m_s` are m, m/s (were "assumed ft / ft/s"). `MaxDepth_in_Ft` style names are unchanged.
  - **Metric in a dam folder.** Dam-folder rasters are read as ft, ft/s; a depth or velocity whose name says m or m/s now gets a WARNING note pointing to the DSS-WISE import, which converts.
  - Test: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_dsswise_unit_names.py` (new).

- **Runner 3.13.9 (built as 3.13.6; #248/#249 took 3.13.7/3.13.8): Breach results dock shows the hazard class (Docs & QA dock test, main db9c665, DWR-2801 r4; Nick: "Yes").** RELEASE ndbreach 3.13.9; damsuite content otherwise unchanged. Reading is `DamSafety_Runner/core/results_info.py` (no Qt).
  - **Hazard line.** ND / TM 11 class, provisional flag and Hazard version from `Hazard_Classification_Summary.txt` (a `Hazard_Classification_Summary.json` wins key by key if one exists) in the output folder dam.json's classified status names; status and `dam_record.next_action` from dam.json. 2801 r4 reads "Hazard 2.19.1: ND High (provisional), TM 11 Significant".
  - **Layer buttons open their data read-only (Docs & QA re-test of 70ed432).** The Wet Hazard structures button had opened Habitable_Structures.gpkg read-write (change counter, md5 and mtime changed). All five buttons now open read-only: vector layers with `forceReadOnly` (GeoPackage also with the GDAL `IMMUTABLE=YES` open option; receptors fall back to OGR's read-only CSV driver when the delimitedtext provider is absent), rasters through the read-only gdal provider. The flooded/wet filter is a provider subset string and writes nothing. Tested: loading all five from a copy leaves every md5, mtime and the folder listing unchanged.
  - **Class of record: `review.json` in the dam folder (Nick, 2026-11-01).** `core/review_record.py`, schema `damsafety-review-v1`: `nd_class_of_record` (Low/Medium/High), `tm11_class_of_record` (Low/Significant/High), `decided_by`, `decided_on` (YYYY-MM-DD), `note`, `overrides` {`nd_class`, `tm11_class`?, `hazard_version`}, `applies_to` {`run_folder` and/or `run_id` = run_report `run_started`}. Read defensively; missing or invalid means "class of record: not recorded". The dock shows it beside the printed class and flags a review for another run folder / run id, or one whose overridden printed class or Hazard version differs from the current summary. Only `sign_off_review()` (an explicit engineer sign-off; refuses to replace without `replace=True`) writes it; no run, rerun or classify code calls it (tested).
  - **Stale summary warning** when the summary's Hazard version is older than the installed Hazard (QGIS, else the Hazard folder beside the Runner) or the file is older than run_report.json / the final rasters.
  - **"37 structures"** is now "37 Runner receptor structure(s) ... (Hazard: 45 flooded habitable)".
  - **Layer buttons** Depth, Velocity, Arrival, Flooded receptors (receptors_flooded.csv as points in the run's work CRS), Hazard structures (outputs/Classification/Habitable_Structures.gpkg); files in the dam folder first, then the paths run_report.json recorded.
  - **Stale summary: the Next line says "Re-classify with the installed Hazard <version>"**, never "Nothing to do".
  - **Vector layers load filtered:** "Flooded receptors (N)" with `"depth_ft" > 0`, "Wet Hazard structures (N)" with `"Max_Depth_ft" > 0` (2801 r4: 673 of 673 receptors, 79 of 3,809 Hazard structures, 53 of them habitable; Hazard's 5.2.2 count of 45 also applies its outcome rules, said in the tooltip).
  - **Rasters open through `/vsisubfile/` (3.13.9, CI of 48e366b):** QGIS's statistics were saved as `<raster>.aux.xml` beside Depth/Velocity/Arrival (GDAL PAM) when the layer closed; the read-only byte-range path lets GDAL write no sidecar, so the run folder listing stays unchanged.
  - **"140 thing(s) to check"** is a toggle that lists the run warnings.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_results_dock_hazard.py` (new), `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_review_record.py` (new), `tests/realqt_dock_area.py` (results dock checks).
- **Runner 3.13.8 / Setup 1.0.19 (built as 3.13.6; #248 took 3.13.7): Style A wizard polish (Crest build pack brief, 2026-11-02; adapted to the one-page Setup of 1.0.14, Nick's choice).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.13.8; damsuite content otherwise unchanged. No parameter the run tool gets changes (`wizard_logic.build_rundam_params` untouched).
  - **Setup: scan as a status list.** Each layer is a green `✓ <layer> — found` or a red `✕ <layer> — <what it is for>` ("required — the dam list comes from here", "optional — locates crests precisely", "optional — improves receptor mapping", "optional — road-crossing impacts", "optional — rail-crossing impacts"), then `✓ Enough to continue — missing layers only reduce detail.` once the DWR dams are found. Missing optional layers were grey circles.
  - **Steps line.** Setup's page and each Runner wizard page start with the Start here… steps (Data folder → Dam → Terrain → Scenario → Models → Review → Run tool; ✓ done, ▶ this page, ○ later), one wrapping line so a narrow window never scrolls sideways. `WIZARD_STEPS` / `steps_html` are the same in both plugins (test).
  - **Tooltips.** DEM elevation units, the download resolution and the cell size carry the brief's user-tested wording (`wizard.FIELD_TIPS`; also Shift+F1 What's This for the keyboard); the Terrain page says "(i) Hover any field for what it means.". The brief's crest / inflow-drop / run-length / EAP fields are no longer in the wizard (crest from the record, run length and EAP in the tool's dialog), so they have no tooltip here.
  - **No silent failures.** Setup: an installed Runner whose wizard does not import, and a Dams folder that cannot be created, push a message-bar note (they were swallowed). Runner wizard: the busy cursor, model settings, loaded-DEM lookup, button label and window fit log to the DamSafety Runner message-log tab instead of `pass`.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Setup/tests/test_style_a.py` (new), `test_wizard_live.py` and `DamSafety_Runner/tests/realqt_wizard.py` (updated).
- **Hazard 2.22.3 / Runner 3.13.7 / Screening 0.18.2 (PR #248, option C, Nick 2026-11-01): "TM 11 practice uses the absolute peak velocity and peak depth regardless of whether they coincide."** RELEASE ndhazard 2.22.3, ndbreach 3.13.7, ndscreen 0.18.2 (Runner 3.13.6 is #247). Every structure, ND and TM 11, is classified on its peak depth and the peak velocity over its wet cells, even from different cells.
  - Runner / Screening `core/nd_hazard.sample_footprint`: velocity = max over the wet cells (reverts db9c665's deepest-cell velocity). Runner Sampler multipoint: `vel_fts` = max over the wet points; `vel_at_max_fts` stays as info. Polygon was already max-over-wet.
  - Screening `core/hazard.py` Grid polygon: peak velocity across parts and dv = footprint max (reverts db9c665).
  - Hazard `structures.peak_pair`: the zone is the ACER class of (Dmax, Vmax over wet cells), never less severe than the worst cell; margins from the same pair; METHOD_LABEL updated. Roads (`linear.py`) and scenarios are not structures and are unchanged. HazardLite already used separate hmax / vmax.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_nd_hazard.py` parity test (every tool's classifying velocity = max over wet cells, hand-built 3x3 case), `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_peak_depth_peak_velocity.py` (new).
