- **Docs only (Docs & QA): USER_GUIDE sections for the toe-inflow rule (Runner 3.13.0+), the judgment-zone MEDIUM rule (#241: Hazard 2.22.0, Runner 3.13.0, Screening 0.18.0) and the Oct 29 offline pack.** Plain-language how the Runner picks the breach inflow (lowest 1 m DEM cell from 5 m out to H x (DS slope + 1), DS slope from the settings, DS_SLOPE / F_DS, the DWR record, else 3:1; outer limit 15-150 m; 5-30 m with no dam height; widening when the strip is still the dam face) with the DWR-4031 and DWR-2801 examples; the judgment-zone rule and the old option (`nd_judgment_as_probable` / `ND_JUDGMENT_HIGH`, off by default) with the DWR-4031 class of record (MEDIUM, engineer's judgment call on house 16775's corner cell); offline runs use `offline_packs_20261029_full` - the 20261021 pack no longer exists. Guide header now main 1e3e2f7. No code or version change.
- **Runner 3.13.5: DWR-6141 Taskers Coulee findings (main 1e3e2f7, Runner 3.13.4).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.13.5; damsuite content otherwise unchanged.
  - **False CHECK THE DAM POINT.** `terrain.inflow_side_check` check (b) ("the inflow point is behind the dam relative to the flow trace's direction") measures the inflow from the dam point along the trace's first 200 m. On 6141 the toe-band inflow (-453152.9, 2862253.2) EPSG:5070 is 33.3 m NE (downstream) of the bent DWR dam line, but west of mid-crest, and the trace first runs east along the toe (0.99, 0.14), so (b) said "behind". With a downstream side or a dam line, (b) is now a `notes` entry; (c) upstream side, (d) trace vs downstream (0.59) and (f) trace vs the line normal (0.59) decide. With neither, (b) is still a problem. The same weakness showed on DWR-2801 from the mid-crest point (#238 review). The point the run picks is unchanged.
  - **AADT siblings.** `aadt_join.siblings` also matches the per-dam pack clips (`pack_HPMS_ND_<year>_<dam>.gpkg`, `pack_ND_county_roads_<dam>.gpkg`, `pack_NDDOT_traffic_counts_<dam>.gpkg`) next to the roads layer. DWR-6141's run gave ROADS = pack_ND_roads_NG911_6141.gpkg and no ROADS_MORE / AADT_LAYERS, so the Runner's ND classification had no AADT source while the Hazard plugin used the county roads.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_sidecheck_6141.py` (new), `test_inflow_point.py`, `test_report_text_4031.py` (updated).

- **Hazard 2.22.2 / Runner 3.13.4 / Screening 0.18.1 / Setup 1.0.18 (PR #242; Runner built as 3.13.3, released as 3.13.4 after #238 took 3.13.3): fixes from the 2026-10-30 full test (QGIS 3.34 and 4.2).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.22.2, ndbreach 3.13.4, ndscreen 0.18.1 (on main 2.22.0 / 3.13.0 / 0.18.0 from #241; Hazard 2.22.1 / Runner 3.13.2 from #244; Runner 3.13.3 from #238). damsuite content otherwise unchanged.
  - **Hazard: no-arrival road / rail stretches are counted.** `crown.line_check` no longer returns `DECISION_NO_ARRIVAL`; a wet stretch with no arrival value goes through the depth and crown checks and, if it passes, is counted with no warning time. `crown.arrival_unknown` / `NOTE_ARRIVAL_UNKNOWN`: its Note and QA Note start "arrival unknown". On the full-test inputs ND00199 went from Low (123 roads + 2 rail skipped) to High.
  - **Hazard: downloads off downloads nothing.** `processing_provider.no_download_overrides()` also turns off the OSM mobile-home parks, Microsoft footprints and NAIP dates when all nine `DOWNLOAD_*` parameters are false (classify, batch, watch); `CONFIG_JSON` still applies after it.
  - **Hazard: QGIS 4 legends.** `compat.legend_manual` (setSyncMode(Manual), else setAutoUpdateModel(False)) in report.py and mapbook.py.
  - **Runner / Setup: QGIS 4 docks.** The results dock and the Suite status dock use `Qt.DockWidgetArea.*` (PyQt6 has no `Qt.RightDockWidgetArea`).
  - **Screening: tool 1 / inventory with the default DWR field names.** `inventory.resolve_fields`: a chosen field is matched ignoring case; one the layer lacks falls back to the alias it has (`structure_` -> `structure_id`, ...) with a warning, else blank. No more `KeyError: 'structure_'` on the suite GeoPackage.
  - **Screening: the one-dam chain (Crest ND00199 hand-off).** `workflow.defaults('1')` takes DWR / NID from `slot_source` (Inputs choice, else the suite folder / pack), the preflight's resolution; with only `suite_folder` set the preflight passed and step 1 failed with "Provide a DWR layer, an NID layer, or both". The preflight disk estimate counts the `only_ids` dams (`preflight.only_ids_count`), not the whole layer (10088). Tool 15 (`runchain`) writes its LOG line by line (a killed run leaves its log; `_finish` still rewrites it whole) and has an optional `DAM_ID` that sets `only_ids`, as the ND copy. Tool 6 (`triage`) matches `ONLY_IDS` against `dam_id` or `nid_id`, as tool 1 does (Crest sends ND00199, tool 1 names the dam DWR-2801, so tool 6 skipped it). Test: `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_onedam_chain.py`.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_line_crown_check.py` (updated), `test_classify_no_downloads.py`, `test_legend_sync.py`, `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_dock_area_enum.py`, `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_dwr_default_fields.py`, `test_onedam_chain.py`.
  - Not in this change: the Runner's bundled LISFLOOD-FP binary is missing from the install (out of scope; no binaries added).

- **Runner 3.13.3 (PR #238, issue #237; built as 3.12.10 and 3.13.1, never released): breach inflow placement on DWR-4031 (Washek, main cc9adc2, Runner 3.12.9).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.13.3 (after #241 3.13.0 and #244 3.13.2; #242 re-syncs if it merges after this). The run put the inflow at (-406905.97, 2823086.89) EPSG:5070, 393 m NNE of the dam in a farmyard draw, with the downstream side turned round, and the side check said ok. Four defects in `core/terrain.py`:
  - **Flattened pool.** `pool_mask` needed more than max(0.5 m, 15 % of H) of fill (3.1 ft at DWR-4031); the hydro-flattened pond has 1.1 ft. With `dam_xy` and `top_m` it now adds a shallow pool: fill > `POOL_SHALLOW_M` (0.1 m), ground between the top of dam and H/2 below it, in a part that reaches within `POOL_TOUCH_M` (60 m, at least 3 cells) of the dam line or point (`pool_at_dam`). The old call (no dam) is unchanged.
  - **Decoy depression.** `downstream_direction` used the centroid of any pool cell within 600 m: a closed low 450 m ESE. It now uses only the pool that reaches the dam line (`pool_at_dam`); with a dam line and no such pool, the clearly lower side of the line (`_lower_side`: median ground 20-80 m off each side, at least max(0.5 m, 10 % of H) apart); then the trace as before. New `top_m` argument (workflow and reservoir_carve pass it).
  - **No distance limit.** `_pick_inflow`'s 400 m search took the nearest low-enough cell however far. `find_inflow_point(crest_line=, max_from_dam_m=)` now never picks further than `INFLOW_MAX_FROM_DAM_M` (150 m) from the dam line (`dam_distance`; the dam point without one). When only farther ground qualifies, the lowest ground within the limit (on the downstream side) is used and the note starts with `INFLOW_FAR` ("CHECK THE INFLOW POINT"), a warning at the top of the run's list. `RunSettings.inflow_max_from_dam_m` overrides the limit.
  - **Circular side check.** `inflow_side_check` tested the inflow against the same downstream vector that picked it. New: the trace direction against `downstream` (cosine < 0 is a problem; DWR-4031: -0.80), against the dam line's normal on the inflow's side (`crest_line`), and `far` when the inflow is more than the limit from the dam line (a separate `INFLOW_FAR` warning from `_check_inflow_site`, not a side problem). New report keys `trace_vs_downstream`, `trace_vs_dam_line_normal`, `inflow_from_dam_line_m`, `far`.
  - Local check on DWR-4031's DEM (1 m composite read only, clipped and averaged in scratch; no model run): downstream is now (0.946, -0.325) "away from the pool" and the inflow is 64-72 m from the dam line on the east side at 1600.5-1604.0 ft (20, 10 and 5 m cells), side check ok; the 3.12.9 point now fails the side check (trace cosine -0.74 at a 10 m planning cell) and is reported 374 m from the dam line. The explicit toe that worked, (-406986.0, 2822699.9), passes.
  - **Inflow at the dam toe (Nick: "can we make the inflow point at the dam toe at the lowest elevation?").** New `terrain.toe_band_mask` / `toe_band_inflow`: with a dam line (DAM_LINES, else the batch CREST_LINE) and a downstream side, `workflow._downstream_inflow` reads the DEM at 1 m (or its native cell if coarser) round the line and takes the lowest cell of a toe band downstream of the line along its full length, not the coarse search cell (`RunSettings.inflow_toe_cell_m`).
  - **Toe band width (Nick: "would do 2": the outer limit is H x (downstream slope + 1)).** New `terrain.toe_band_for_dam`: the band runs from 5 m (`INFLOW_TOE_INNER_M`) out to H x (slope + 1), H the dam height in metres and the slope H:1V - the dam's `ds_slope_h` (single dam DS_SLOPE, batch / CLI F_DS, as `hecras_model` uses for the toe), else the dam's DWR record (below), else 3:1 (`INFLOW_TOE_DS_SLOPE_DEFAULT`, so 4H) with the warning "Breach inflow: the dam's downstream slope is not known (...); the toe band uses 3:1 ...". The outer limit is at least 15 m (`INFLOW_TOE_MIN_OUTER_M`, always past the inner limit) and never past the 150 m limit (`inflow_max_from_dam_m`). With no dam height: 5-30 m (`INFLOW_TOE_BAND_M`) and the warning "Breach inflow: toe band 5-30 m (dam height not known ...)". The run logs the band and how it was found. RunSettings: `inflow_toe_band_m` (a given band, as is), `inflow_toe_ds_slope_h` (the slope, over the dam's), `inflow_toe_min_outer_m`. Examples: DWR-4031 (20.7 ft = 6.31 m, DWR 2:1) 5-18.9 m; DWR-2801 (27.5 ft = 8.38 m, DWR 2.5:1) 5-29.3 m; a 70 ft dam 85 m (3:1) to 107 m (4:1).
  - **Downstream slope from the DWR record (Nick: "downstream slope data is in dwr data").** New `core/dwr_slope.py`: when DS_SLOPE / F_DS is empty, the single-dam run, the batch, the cascade and the CLI read the dam's DWR_dams `downstream_slope` (text, H of H:1V) by its DWR structure number (dam.json `dwr_structure_id`, else the digits of a `DWR-2801` ID), first in the dam folder's DWR dams (Data's "Get data for this dam", dam.json data section), then the suite's (`suitedata.dwr_layers`: the dwr/ drop-in, else the offline pack); read-only. `parse_ds_slope`: "3", "3.0", "2:1", "3`", "4 STEP" are read as given; several values ("2.5;3", "3,3.5,") take the flattest, so the band reaches the toe; empty, "0", "varies", or anything outside 1:1-10:1 ("37.5", ".6", "3:5") is not known -> 3:1 and the warning. Order (`workflow._toe_slope`): RunSettings `inflow_toe_ds_slope_h`, DS_SLOPE / F_DS, the DWR record, 3:1; the band note says which ("slope: DWR record downstream_slope '2' = 2:1 (DWR_dams.gpkg)"). Only the toe band uses the DWR value; the breach parameters and the HEC-RAS embankment keep DS_SLOPE / F_DS. Read-only recompute on the 1 m DEMs: DWR-4031 (DWR "2") band 5-18.9 m, inflow (-406985.9, 2822700.4) EPSG:5070, 1607.48 ft NAVD88, 18.4 m from the dam line, 0.5 m from the hand-picked toe (-406986.0, 2822699.9), side check ok (3:1 gave 5-25.2 m, 16.5 m from it); DWR-2801 (DWR "2.5") with the pack dam line, from the NID point or mid-crest: band 5-29.3 m, (-106258.7, 2666643.7), 1042.97 ft, 28.9 m from the line, side check ok. This replaces the fixed 5-15 m strip first built here (Nick: "use a 5-15 m toe strip").
  - A cell counts only on the downstream side of the line segment it is nearest (within 60 degrees of that segment's own normal, turned downstream), so a bent line keeps its local side and the band does not wrap round the ends; a segment that runs with the flow has no side. Cells below the top of dam only, and not in the pool behind the dam (`_downstream_inflow` now passes its `pool_mask` grid to `_toe_inflow` - it passed None in 6258133; pool cells within half a pool cell's diagonal of the line still count, since a coarse cell can straddle it). Ties: nearest the line, then the dam point. If the pick is not 75 % of the dam height below the top of dam (`INFLOW_TOE_DROP`; the band is the dam's face), the band widens 10 m at a time up to 60 m more, never past the 150 m limit; if it never gets there, the first band reaching half the height, else the first, with a note - a safeguard past H x (slope + 1) for a given band or a wrong slope.
  - Grok Bot's review of 6258133 (DWR-2801 with the pack dam line): the 5-15 m strip of the 27.5 ft dam is all face (13-15 ft below the top along the whole line, the toe being ~20-30 m out), and its lowest cell was a pad ~20 m from the south end, 14.9 ft down, which passed the old half-height test; from the mid-crest dam point the side check failed (the pad is behind the dam seen from mid-crest). Fixed by the band reaching the toe (H x (slope + 1)), the 75 % face test and the per-segment side; `inflow_side_check` is unchanged and still runs on the trace. The 0.2 m difference in the 2801 line: PROJ's default EPSG:4326 to 5070 operation without the PROJ network (the null NAD83/WGS 84 step, what QGIS and every conda env here use) puts the line's first vertex exactly on the NID dam point (-106343.83, 2666591.50); the review line came from PROJ_NETWORK=ON ("NAD83 to WGS 84 (33)", the ND HPGN grid, 2 m accuracy). The default is kept: it is what the Runner gets and keeps the line on the dam point; 0.2 m is within the datum's accuracy.
  - No dam line, no downstream side or no valid cell: `find_inflow_point` as before (150 m limit) with the warning "Breach inflow: not placed at the downstream toe (...)".
  - Local check (DEMs read only, no model run, slope not given so 3:1): DWR-4031 (1 m composite), band 5-25.2 m: (-406970.2, 2822704.7) EPSG:5070 at 1605.36 ft NAVD88, 25.1 ft below the top of dam, 25.2 m from the dam line (just past its east end), 16.5 m from the hand-placed toe (-406986.0, 2822699.9; 1607.55 ft on the DEM) and 2.2 ft lower; side check ok. DWR-2801 r4 inputs have no dam line, so the fallback gives the r4 point (-106263.8, 2666571.5), 1044.41 ft, with the warning. With the DWR pack line for 2801, band 5-33.5 m: (-106255.9, 2666640.8), 1042.85 ft, 29.4 ft below the top of dam, 32.9 m from the line, 95 m along it (the bend is at 121 m, mid-crest at 168 m); side check ok from the NID point and from mid-crest.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_inflow_toe_band.py` (15, synthetic: the H x (slope + 1) limit, the 3:1 default, the 15 m minimum, the cap, no height; the dam's slope and the RunSettings overrides through the workflow; a long bent line with a high pad near one end reproduces the 2801 pick and side-check failure under the old 5-15 m half-height strip; the pool passed to the band); `test_inflow_point.py`'s workflow helper test (no dam line) now expects the fallback warning.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_inflow_4031.py` (10; a synthetic DEM built like DWR-4031 that reproduces 3.12.9's flip and 393 m pick on main, plus the side check on the run's numbers). No DWR data in the repo.

- **Hazard 2.22.1 / Runner 3.13.2: report text from the DWR-4031 toe10m run (Runner 3.12.9, Hazard 2.20.4) and the QA re-test of #241.** RELEASE ndhazard 2.22.1, ndbreach 3.13.2 (3.13.1 is PR #238, `fix/runner-inflow-4031`; whichever merges second re-syncs `versions.py`, FILES.json and the pin). No class changes on the default settings.
  - **Hazard: depth cell size.** The road-crossing note said "Max_Depth_ft is the 30 m depth cell" on a 10 m grid (hard-coded). It now gives the depth raster's cell in metres (`pipeline.depth_cell_label`, from `res["cell_size_m"]`; "depth-grid" when unknown).
  - **Hazard: 5.3(f) count (old option only).** With `nd_judgment_as_probable` / `ND_JUDGMENT_HIGH` on, the "borderline rule 5.3(f)" citation and the PROVISIONAL count included borderline structures that were not moved to a higher class (DWR-2801 house 4026, from #240). They now count only structures actually moved up (`borderline_higher`, or `near_raised` on the 1D path); `nd["borderline_moved"]` holds the count. The 5.3(f) criteria row still lists every borderline structure. `ND_JUDGMENT_HIGH` is still not wired through the Runner chain or Screening tool 8 (on hold pending Nick).
  - **Hazard: DWR-4031 house id.** The judgment-zone house in the toe-run folder is Struct_ID 9953 (2.111 ft at 6.95 ft/s); tests and this changelog called it 16775, a dry house there (Struct_IDs differ by footprint set).
  - **Runner: peak inflow.** The LISFLOOD-FP report gave 1,570 cfs, the largest Qin at the `.mass` output samples (every `massint` = 300 s; the breach peak at 283 s falls between samples), while the inflow file peaks at 1,696 cfs. The report now gives the breach hydrograph's peak (`peak_inflow_input_cfs`) and labels the `.mass` value ("at the .mass output samples"); `peak_inflow_cfs` is unchanged.
  - **Runner: passes.** A clock extension keeps the same domain run by design, so both passes read "run 1". The run report and the Word report now have Pass and Domain run columns ("1 (clock extended)"); old run reports are numbered by position.
  - **Runner: AADT.** The ND hazard module's AADT join read only HPMS / NDDOT counts (the Mac driver's `AADT_LAYERS`) and ignored `ROADS_MORE`, so 87TH ST NW was "AADT unknown (assumed Medium)" while the Hazard summary used AADT 60 from ND county roads. `aadt_join.join_sources` adds the `ROADS_MORE` GeoPackages; `tools/mac/nickreview_mac.py` lists ND county roads in `AADT_LAYERS`.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_depth_cell_label.py`, `test_summary_judgment_4031.py` (house 4026 not moved: no 5.3(f)), `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_report_text_4031.py`. Synthetic values only.

- **Hazard 2.22.0: a house in the ACER judgment zone is ND MEDIUM by rule (Nick, 10/30: "the rule needs to be any house in the judgement zone should be automatically labeled as medium hazard, for all plugin sets and crest").** RELEASE ndhazard 2.22.0. Stacked on 2.21.1 (PR #240).
  - **Rule.** A habitable structure between the ACER judgment and high-danger boundaries makes the dam ND MEDIUM [5.2.1.1]: reason "MEDIUM - n habitable structure(s) in the ACER judgment zone - classed MEDIUM by rule (judgment-zone houses are Medium, not probable loss of life)", criteria row result "Medium (by rule)". It is not probable loss of life and no longer makes a provisional HIGH. High-danger-zone houses still make the dam HIGH. `classify_nd(judgment_as_probable=False)` is the default and the result dict gets `judgment_rule` ("medium" / "probable").
  - **Old option kept, off by default (Nick decided).** New setting `nd_judgment_as_probable` (Classify "ND: count ACER judgment-zone houses as probable loss of life (HIGH) - old option", Processing `ND_JUDGMENT_HIGH`, dam state / history / dialog settings). On, it gives the 2.21.1 behaviour (HIGH, provisional when it rests on judgment-zone houses within 1 ft, 5.3(f) cited only when counted).
  - **TM 11 unchanged.** The existing `judgment_as_probable` / Processing `JUDGMENT` / "judgment zone" check box now applies to ACER TM 11 lives in jeopardy only (default on). A new key was used for ND so saved dam states and run parameters with JUDGMENT=true do not bring back HIGH.
  - Defaults follow the rule in robustness (margins to the high-danger boundary), sensitivity, rulecompare, devscreen (future development) and the lone-911 / multi-structure judgment notes (TM 11 still counted as before there).
  - The Runner chain (`algorithms_chain.CLASSIFY_STATE_KEYS`) does not pass `ND_JUDGMENT_HIGH`, so a chained classification always uses the default (MEDIUM by rule).
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_judgment_zone_medium_rule.py` (synthetic houses at the depth / velocity of DWR-2801 house 4010, 3.10 ft at 0.11 ft/s, and DWR-4031 house 9953 in the toe-run folder (was cited as 16775; Struct_IDs differ by footprint set), 2.11 ft at 6.95 ft/s: ND MEDIUM, TM 11 Significant; old option HIGH; high-zone house HIGH).
  - **Runner 3.13.0 / Screening 0.18.0: the same rule in the shared `nd_hazard` core (1.3.0).** RELEASE ndbreach 3.13.0, ndscreen 0.18.0. A habitable structure in the judgment zone is a MEDIUM finding (section "5.2.1.1 Fig. 5.x", reason "... - MEDIUM by rule (judgment-zone houses are Medium, not potentially lethal)"), not a 5.3 borderline HIGH; it no longer counts in `habitable_lethal` / `n_bldg_lethal`. New `Settings.judgment_zone_high` (default False) keeps the old behaviour: Runner ND hazard classification advanced parameter `JUDGMENT_HIGH`, Screening `core.hazard.classify_nd(judgment_zone_high=True)`. Screening tool 8 `hz_lethal` reads "no (judgment zone, MEDIUM by rule)". Tests: `test_nd_hazard.py`, `test_nd_hazard_adapter.py` updated (default MEDIUM, option HIGH).

- **Hazard 2.21.1: summary wording from the DWR-4031 review (Washek, main cc9adc2).** RELEASE ndhazard 2.21.1. No class changes.
  - **ND class without judgment-zone items.** The summary's "Without judgment-zone items: Low" was the ACER TM 11 Table 1 class; the ND class without the judgment-zone house was MEDIUM (5.2.2 economic loss, 5.2.1.2 cut-off). New `classification.nd_class_without_judgment` (classify_nd with `judgment_as_probable=False` + extra_criteria, inputs unchanged), stored as `nd["cls_without_judgment"]` by the pipeline when judgment-zone dwellings are counted. The line now reads `Without judgment-zone items: Low (TM 11 Table 1); ND class: MEDIUM`; the summary table adds "ND classification without judgment-zone items"; formfill reads both (`TM11_WITHOUT_JUDGMENT` unchanged, new `ND_WITHOUT_JUDGMENT`, older summaries still read).
  - **5.3(f) only when counted.** The judgment-zone reason said "(conservative, borderline rule 5.3(f))" and the PROVISIONAL note "borderline goes to the higher class, 5.3(f)" while the 5.3(f) row reported 0 borderline structures. The reason now says "(conservative judgment-zone setting)", with "; borderline rule 5.3(f)" added only when the borderline count is above 0; the PROVISIONAL note cites 5.3(f) with the count when it is above 0, else "the judgment zone is counted as probable loss of life". The 5.3(f) criteria row and the CLASSIFICATION MARGIN (5.3(f)) heading are unchanged.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_summary_judgment_4031.py` (4).

- **Hazard 2.21.0: name a dam and add its model results (Nick: "make it so that i have the option to name the dam, and if it doesn't have a current folder in dams it would create it and put the rasters in it").** RELEASE ndhazard 2.21.0 (damsuite content otherwise unchanged; Data's `constants.py` kept identical).
  - New `DamSafety_Hazard/adddam.py` (no Qt), `add_results_dialog.py` (Classify > Setup "Add model results to a dam…"; "Use this dam folder" fills the Setup box) and the Processing algorithm `ndhazard:adddamresults` (DAM, SOURCE / SOURCE_FOLDER, MODEL, SCENARIO, DAMS_FOLDER, CRS, TERRAIN, ACCEPT_ASSUMED, CSV; outputs DAM_FOLDER, RESULTS_FOLDER, SUMMARY).
  - The dam folder is matched in the Dams folder (as `watch.dam_folders` lists it) by folder name or dam.json `dam_id` / `name` / `nid_id` / `dwr_structure_id`, ignoring case, spaces and punctuation (IDs also leading zeros and the DWR / NID prefix); several matches stop. None: `<Dams>/<typed name>` with a dam_record dam.json.
  - Results go to `<dam>/Hazard_inputs/Breach_<DSSWISE|HECRAS|LISFLOOD>` (`No_Breach_<MODEL>` for the baseline), found by `autodetect.find_rasters` unchanged. DSS-WISE Lite: `dsswise.convert`, outputs renamed `*_Max_*` (find_rasters reads `Depth_ft` as a time step). HEC-RAS: RAS Mapper rasters copied (SI converted to feet from the band / tile unit or the project), a `.p##.hdf` through `rasimport.import_plan`. LISFLOOD-FP: `.max` / `.maxVc` / `.Vmax` / `.inittm` / `.mxe` ESRI ASCII to GeoTIFF in ft / ft/s / h; CRS from the parameter, a `.prj`, or the dam's DEM, else it stops.
  - Never overwrites (next free `_n`; same sha256 + scenario is "already added"), staged in `.adding_*` and renamed; the source is only read. `results_provenance.json` (source, sha256, model, scenario, time, plugin version, outputs, notes); dam.json status `modelled`. Warns when find_rasters would still pick another raster. No classification or batch change.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_add_dam_results.py` (20; QGIS 3.34 and 4).

- **Runner 3.12.9 / Screening 0.17.58 / Hazard 2.20.4 / damsuite 1.18.4: the unconditional local band is 0-500 ft (was 0-300).** 301-499 ft is below `ND_MIN_FT` (500), so it is not a North Dakota ground elevation, but 1.18.2 still VERTCON-shifted it unless another local rule ran. `LOCAL_MAX_FT` is 500 inclusive: the range test is `v < 500`, so 500.00 was the round value that slipped through. A 0-500 ft elevation is never shifted and is tied to the DEM whatever datum is stated (culvert inverts too). A top outside the band that is local by another rule still carries its band values on the same offset. No classification change. The hazard summary heading is VERTICAL DATUM OF THE DAM ELEVATIONS. Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_local_midcrest.py`, `test_vertical_datum.py`, `test_v360.py`, `test_culvert_precheck.py`, `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_vertical_datum_summary.py`. The synthetic culvert valley used by the tolerance tests sits above 500 ft, so those tests still cover an invert that matches the LiDAR bed.

- **Hazard 2.20.3 / Runner 3.12.8 / Screening 0.17.57 / Data 1.5.2 / Setup 1.0.17 / damsuite 1.18.3: both offline packs serve every layer the plugins read (format-1 readers, HIFLD lifelines, flood zones; Nick).** The 2026-10-29 audit found all 39 layers in both packs, but three readers missed some of them. RELEASE ndhazard 2.20.3, ndbreach 3.12.8, ndscreen 0.17.57.
  - **Format 1 (nd_offline_pack.sqlite) in suitedata / suite_fill.** `suitedata.pack_file`, `nlcd_raster` and `dwr_layers` (Screening, Runner) and `suite_fill._pack_dwr` / `scan` (Setup, through an installed plugin's damsuite) find DWR, NID and the NLCD land cover in a format-1 pack. Its data sets are compressed blobs OGR cannot read, so each one asked for is written once to a GeoPackage cache outside the pack (`NDSUITE_PACK_CACHE`, else the user cache folder `ndsuite/pack_v1`, keyed by the sqlite's path, size and time); the pack is never written. The NLCD raster comes from the manifest "rasters". `RASTER_EXT` adds `.gpkg`. `find_pack` returns a format-1 folder only after the format-2 subfolder scan; format-2 behaviour is unchanged. A zipped format-1 pack is unpacked by offline_pack with its listed rasters.
  - **Data's pack build.** The format-1 pack gets the NLCD file too (`nd_offline_pack/landcover/NLCD_2021_landcover.gpkg`, the format-2 file copied, listed in its manifest "rasters"; `rasters_not_included` dropped). `pack_build --zip1 ZIP` writes the format-1 pack as one zip (sqlite, manifest, README, rasters); `--verify` checks both packs list the same rasters. The statewide pack was not rebuilt; the Oct 29 format-1 manifest gains "rasters" at the next build.
  - **HIFLD lifelines.** `constants.LIFELINE_PACK_SETS` / `lifeline_names` / `lifeline_sets` / `lifeline_fetches`: Hazard's lifeline_hifld_fire / ems / eoc / police / wastewater resolve to the packs' HIFLD_fire_ems_stations (fire and EMS, read once), HIFLD_local_eoc, HIFLD_local_law_enforcement and HIFLD_wastewater_treatment_plants in both formats, in suiteexport and in the run's lifeline fetch (pipeline, datafetch; reproduce orders them); the old `lifeline_<key>` names still win when a pack has them.
  - **Flood zones.** `damdata.PACK_SETS["flood_zones"]` is empty: no pack holds the FEMA flood zones, so the layer no longer claims a pack layer.
  - Tests: tiny synthetic format-1 / format-2 packs in `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_pack_formats.py` (Screening's and the Runner's damsuite), `DamSafety_Setup/tests/test_suite_fill_v1.py`, `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_pack_lifelines.py`, `DamSafety_Data/tests/test_pack_build_v1_nlcd.py` (added to the CI pytest step); `DamSafety_Data/tests/test_pack_build.py` now expects the NLCD raster in format 1.
- **Runner 3.12.7 / Screening 0.17.56 / damsuite 1.18.2: elevations between 0 and 300 ft are always local; the dam point at mid-crest (Nick, DWR-4031).** 3.12.6 (#231, ANUGA resume) is already on main, so this is Runner 3.12.7 (RELEASE ndhazard stays 2.20.2; ndbreach 3.12.7; ndscreen 0.17.56).
  - **0-300 ft rule.** `damsuite.vdatum.is_local_elev` (0 <= v <= 300 ft, inclusive) is the first unconditional test of #220's `local_check`; `plan(values=...)` makes the record local (LOCAL_ASSUMED, how "detected", `local_rule` "0-300 ft") whatever the stated datum or the engineer's choice; `convert_ft` never shifts such a value. The top of dam is the DEM at mid-crest; the other elevations keep their height below the local top (offset = DEM crest - local crest), labelled "DEM-derived". Logged with original, new, offset and method in run_report.json / .md, dam.json and the Hazard summary (`summary_lines`).
  - **Nick's decisions of 2026-10-29 ("Forget overrid datum. Convert local to navd 88." / breach position: "Ok").** No override path or wording: a 0-300 ft value is local and converted to NAVD88 by the DEM tie whatever datum is stated or chosen (no split, confirmed by Nick on 2026-10-29: the choice is not applied to any value of a local record - every elevation of it, one inside ND's 500-3,600 ft range too, gets the same DEM offset). Mixed records are tied, not dropped: a band value of a dam whose top is local by any rule (e.g. 350 ft) gets the same DEM offset (`local_band_leftovers` only holds it until `apply_local`). No tie is never guessed: no DEM crest, a DEM crest outside 500-3,600 ft or x3.28 the top, no mid-crest (no dam line / no DEM across it: `apply_local(tie_ok=False)`), or no local crest to tie by (a real top, or none) -> `review` / `review_reason` in the datum record, a `REVIEW:` warning, `report["datum_review"]`, and the local values left UNCONVERTED (fields `unconverted`; the model takes the top / pools / breach bottom from the DEM / the spillway rating and uses a trigger pool, rating or outlet as given). `vdatum_run.finish_band` flags a band record whose tie never ran. A breach position keeps the dam point and the crest is still read at mid-crest (unchanged).
  - **Culvert inverts.** `structures.reconcile_inverts`: an inventory invert between 0 and 300 ft is tied to the LiDAR channel bed (one offset for both ends when they agree within 1 ft, else the bed at each end); `invert_local` is logged in the datum record (`structures_local`). Inverts in the normal range (NDDOT) get no datum conversion and the checks as before.
  - **Mid-crest dam point.** New `damsuite/midcrest.py` (pure Python): the dam line's midpoint by length, the perpendicular DEM profile +-25 m (0.1 m steps, bilinear), its highest point. Runner `workflow.place_midcrest` (run_dam, before the datum steps) moves the dam point there from `dam.crest_line_xy` (DAM_LINES) or the batch's CREST_LINE (`st.midcrest_line`, now read without a breach position too) and reads the crest there instead of the 98th percentile; a breach position keeps the point (the crest still from mid-crest); without a line the point is kept with a warning. `report["dam_point"]` and the datum record keep the original point and the move. Screening tool 3 (optional LINES, passed by the workflow) reads the crest at mid-crest; tool 4 writes `mc_x, mc_y, mc_z_m, mc_moved_m`; the 7b hand-off uses them as DAM_POINT.
  - DWR-4031: DEM mid-crest 1630.49 ft NAVD88 at (-407005.516, 2822706.843) EPSG:5070 (moved 27.77 m); offset +1509.79 ft: top 120.7 -> 1630.49, streambed 100 -> 1609.79, spillway 117.7 -> 1627.49 ft (the old Screening crest read 1643.63 ft on higher ground).
  - **Follow-up (2026-10-29, Nick: "Yes, no split is intended").** The mixed-record split is removed from `vdatum.plan`, `apply_local` and `summary_lines` (no `other_datum`, no "in North Dakota's range" treatment): a local record's values are all DEM-tied, or flagged for review and left UNCONVERTED when there is no tie. In a record whose top is not local, a 0-300 ft value is tied by the DEM crest when the top is local by another rule (e.g. 350 ft), else flagged for review; its other values follow the normal NAVD88 / NGVD29 path. ELEV_DATUM does not change a 0-300 ft record. A local record without a 0-300 ft value (#220 rules, e.g. a 350 ft top) and without a dam line is not flagged: it is tied to the DEM crest near the dam point as in 1.18.1 (unless the DEM looks like the wrong units). Re-synced after #227 (Runner 3.12.4) merged: Runner 3.12.7, Screening 0.17.56, damsuite 1.18.2.
  - **Plausibility guard and edge check (2026-10-29, Nick: "change them"; Kentner DWR-11011, Foxholm DWR-7592).** After the DEM tie, a tied value (streambed / breach bottom, normal pool, PMF pool, trigger pool, spillway rating's first row, outlet inverts) more than `vdatum.TIE_MAX_FROM_CREST_FT` (100 ft) above or below the DEM crest is not used: the field is `unconverted` + `implausible` with `tied_ft` / `off_crest_ft` and a note, the record gets `review` / `review_reason`, and one `REVIEW:` warning names each value, its tied result and how far above / below the crest it is (e.g. Kentner streambed 845 -> 2134.33 ft, +745 ft; Foxholm bottom 100 -> 732.09 ft, ~966 ft below). Without the value the run takes the breach bottom / pools from the DEM / the spillway rating as for an unconverted value; a trigger pool falls back to the peak pool, a spillway rating or an outlet is left out of the run (all named in the warning). The top of dam (the DEM crest) and the values within 100 ft are used; `crest_source` stays "DEM (local datum)" (not "NOT tied") when the tie ran. `midcrest.midcrest` flags a highest point at the edge of the +-25 m profile (|offset| >= 24.95 m): `at_edge`, `review` (EDGE_REVIEW), "REVIEW" in `summary_line`; the Runner adds a `REVIEW: Dam point:` warning and `report["crest_pick_review"]`; Screening tool 4 adds a "REVIEW mid-crest" note. The pick method is unchanged.
  - **Log / message fixes (2026-10-29).** A stated Local band record no longer prints an empty "(0 - 300 ft rule: )"; before the tie a local record's values print "local -> waiting for the DEM tie" (record `tie` "pending" -> "done" / "review" / "not applied (...)") instead of "100.00 local -> 100.00 NAVD88 (DEM-derived)"; the pre-tie local warning (incl. the 1.18.1 stated-Local one) is replaced by the tie's warning in the record and in the run's warnings (`vdatum_run.merge_tie_warnings`), so no near-duplicates; a datum stated as Local is "local (stated; ELEV_DATUM NAVD88 not applied to a local record, given as 'Local')" under an NAVD88 / NGVD29 choice, and a detected one "local (assumed) (detected from the values by the 0 - 300 ft rule ...)" with the reason on the "Local datum rule:" line only.
  - **A real top of dam with 0-300 ft values: DEM elevations (2026-10-29, Nick: "in that case, use dem elevations"; Thompson Lake DWR-5930: NAVD 88, top 1718, streambed and outlet invert 100 ft).** Such a record has no local crest to tie its band values by; they are no longer flagged UNCONVERTED and used as given but replaced (`vdatum_run._dem_leftovers`, via `apply_local(dem_low=...)`): the streambed / breach bottom and the outlet inverts become the DEM low point (thalweg) at the downstream toe (`vdatum_run.dem_toe_low`: the lowest DEM cell from the toe - ds slope (3H:1V) x dam height + half the crest width (20 ft), as hecras_model's breach toe - to 30 m past it, beside the dam line on its lower side; a ring around the dam point without a line; read from the unburned DEM), labelled "DEM-derived: ..." (`dem_derived`), with one warning naming each value given and its replacement; the PMF pool and a trigger pool come from the routing (peak pool), a normal pool from a usable spillway rating's crest. A band spillway rating (no DEM source), a normal pool without a usable rating, and a DEM low point that is missing, not below the top of dam or more than 100 ft from the DEM crest (the guard applies to DEM values too) are flagged for review and not used (an outlet is left out). The top of dam and `crest_source` are unchanged; `report["band_dem"]` records the low point; the run's flood settings in the report list the outlets / spillway after the change. No DEM crest (or no top of dam) still flags review. Culvert inverts unchanged. `vdatum.summary_lines` prints such a value as "100.00 (0-300 ft, not used) -> 1695.00 NAVD88 (DEM-derived: ...)" (no damsuite version change).
  - The Runner window's datum line (`popup_logic.datum_info`) shows the 0-300 ft rule whatever the ELEV_DATUM choice.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_local_midcrest.py` (4031 numbers for every datum choice, mixed records, review flags for no DEM / units / no dam line / no local crest, breach position, the 100 ft guard with Kentner / Foxholm-like records, the edge pick, the message fixes, the DEM low point and the DEM-elevation replacement for a real top with 0-300 ft values), `test_vertical_datum.py`, `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_storage_midcrest.py`.

- **Runner 3.12.6 / damsuite RELEASE ndbreach 3.12.6: ANUGA resume keeps its key; resume from a named checkpoint (DWR-2801 r3 was relaunched with resume on and started again at t = 0: the Runner re-meshed with gmsh on 6 threads and got 344,856 triangles instead of 344,866 from a byte-identical `gmsh_mesh_in.json`; the mesh is part of the worker's checkpoint key, so `c835230a18bc32d2` became `ce932027254a09f4`. The dt diagnostic, file times and paths were not the cause: the dt keys are outside the key and no file time is hashed).**
  - `anuga_model.make_gmsh_mesh(..., reuse=)`: a resumed run re-uses `gmsh_mesh.npz` made from the same mesh input (thread count aside) and, when recorded, the same `gmsh_mesher.py` (`gmsh_mesh_key.json`, new); `run_report` `anuga_mesh.reused`. Not resuming: unchanged.
  - Worker: `job_fingerprint` unchanged (existing checkpoints keep their key). `checkpoint.json` adds `job_digests` (per-input sha256). A resume that finds no checkpoint of its own logs the other folders and the inputs that differ (`stats.checkpoint.other_checkpoints`).
  - New `resume_from` / `resume_ref_job` job keys (outside the key) from `NDBREACH_ANUGA_RESUME_FROM` / `NDBREACH_ANUGA_RESUME_REF_JOB` (or `AnugaSettings.resume_from`): resume a named checkpoint folder after a compatibility check that logs every difference; a different sim time, yield step, hydrograph, DEM or output grid stops the run (`NDBREACH_ANUGA_RESUME_FORCE=1`), as do an unloadable folder or uncomparable inputs (`ResumeError`, never a fresh start). The named folder is only read. USER_GUIDE "Resuming an ANUGA run from a named checkpoint".
  - Tests: 5 new in `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_anuga_checkpoint.py` (fake ANUGA, no gmsh).

- **Data 1.5.1 / Hazard 2.20.2 (pack engine build_nd_pack 1.3, damsuite RELEASE ndhazard 2.20.2): hydro filled area by area, honest HIFLD credits, file dates.** The statewide pack of 2026-10-29 had NHD_flowlines entirely from NHD: 3DHP's object-id query timed out (HTTP 504) for part of ND, and 1.5.0 then dropped every 3DHP feature already read and used NHD for the whole layer, with the reason "3DHP incomplete (0 area(s) not read)".
  - New engine kind `arcgis_tiled` (`fetch_arcgis_tiled`): ids listed per 0.25 degree tile; a tile that fails (504, time-out, service error) waits 2 s x level and is quartered, down to 4 levels (`_list_ids_split`); a tile that fails 12 times in a row gives up, 6 tiles in a row with nothing listed = service down. Features read in chunks of 250 (halved on failure). Areas not listed, or holding features not read, are read from the fill service (NHD), listed and split the same way, under their own NetSession. One source per place: a feature belongs to the fill when the centre of its bounding box is in an area NHD read (or outside the whole box and touching one); 3DHP rows there and NHD rows elsewhere are removed, with their chunk records (a later run reads them again). uids are `3DHP:<id>` / `NHD:<id>` (the two services' object ids collide). Areas neither service read are gaps (status partial); both failing everywhere: an error.
  - Data `pack_build`: NHD_flowlines / NHD_waterbodies use it; `hydro_src` = 3DHP / NHD on every feature; the manifest gives `hydro_source` (3DHP, NHD or 3DHP+NHD), `count_3dhp`, `count_nhd`, `areas` (tiles, not_from_3dhp, filled_from_nhd, not_read, failed_3dhp_areas, gap_areas), and `fallback_reason` like "3DHP: 3 area(s) not listed (HTTP Error 504: Gateway Time-out) - 3 of 3 area(s) filled from NHD". Format-2 manifest keeps zero values (count_3dhp 0) as format 1 does.
  - Paged reads (`_fetch_arcgis_pages`): a page refused with HTTP 400 (FEMA USA_Structures) is tried after 10 s with half, quarter ... pages down to 100; then a plain error (features read so far kept; the next run continues). `http_json` errors carry `http_code`.
  - HIFLD credits (`pack_build.HIFLD_KNOWN`): the reference pack's four files are recognised by sha256 - fire / EMS, EOC, law enforcement: the HIFLD national shapefile from hsdl.org (uploaded 2026-10-06, clipped to ND + 0.1 degree); wastewater: DataLumos, with `provenance_check` (the reference manifest names project 239290, which DataLumos lists as the SIC-code table; the points are 239262 - not confirmed). Any other file: "supplied for the build ... where this copy came from is not recorded" and `source_origin: not recorded` (1.5.0 said "Data Rescue Project copy" for every file).
  - Dates: `dwr_files.source_date` = the GeoPackage's own `gpkg_contents.last_change` (else the modified time, and `source_date_from` says which); the DWR note says "file dated <day> (GeoPackage last change)" (1.5.0: the copy day).
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_pack_tiled_fill.py` (fake ArcGIS services: split, fill, dedupe at edges, features not read, gaps, resume, both down, HTTP 400 pages, source_date); `DamSafety_Data/tests/test_pack_build.py` (per-area hydro manifest and hydro_src through the whole build; HIFLD credits by checksum).

- **PMP 0.4.0: Get basin data from StreamStats… (Nick: "would it be possible to pull flow estimates from streamstats for pmp, pmf work").** New menu item Plugins > DamSafety PMP > *Get basin data from StreamStats…* (`streamstats_dialog.py`, stdlib client `core/streamstats.py`, no QGIS in the client).
  - Outlet point picked on the map or from a dam point layer; StreamStats `ss-delineate` 1.2.0 / `ss-hydro` 1.4.0 (the Jan 2026 services) and NSS. Point not on the stream grid: "Not on the stream network … Move the point onto the channel just below the dam" and nothing filled (DWR 4031's mapped point stops after the first request).
  - Fills drainage area (DRNAREA), a **suggested** non-contributing area (ISOLAKESUM) and Tc / lag: Kirpich (10-85 slope, lag = 0.6 Tc) or NRCS lag with the user's CN. CN is never taken from StreamStats. Loads the basin layer.
  - Read-only sanity panel: SIR 2015-5096 region C peaks 50% - 0.2% AEP with ~90% ranges from the average standard errors (51-83%), and the Crippen & Bue envelope (PKMAX_CB_R). DWR 2801: 16.6 mi², 0.2% AEP 1,300 cfs (523 - 3,232), envelope 27,400 cfs.
  - `ndpmp:inflow_hydrograph`: optional `STREAMSTATS_JSON`; after the run the PMF peak is compared with the 0.2% AEP peak and the envelope (and the area used with StreamStats' area), warnings only, `streamstats_pmf_check.json` output.
  - Requests go through QGIS networking (`qgis_transport.py`, `QgsBlockingNetworkRequest` from the QgsTask thread; Nick: "switch to qgis"), so the QGIS proxy / auth / SSL settings and network timeout apply and Cancel aborts the request in flight; urllib (env proxies) only outside QGIS. The transport (`qgis` / `urllib`) is recorded in the provenance. No answer (DNS, proxy, SSL, timeout) gives a plain "Could not reach StreamStats (…) … check the proxy settings in QGIS (Settings > Options > Network)" message; HTTP errors keep their status. QGIS sets its own User-Agent, so the client's identification goes in `X-Client` (a request preprocessor to rewrite the User-Agent crashes QGIS 3.34's bindings).
  - Runs in a QgsTask with progress and Cancel. Requests serial, >= 2 s apart, identified as `DamSafety_PMP/<version>`; sha256-keyed cache (tampered files refused) and a provenance JSON (service versions, query time, region, point, requests with answer hashes, citations) next to the outputs.
  - PMP now ships the suite UI files (`tokens.py`, `ui_widgets.py`, `ui_fit.py`, via `tools/sync_ui.py`), an icon, and is in `tools/gui_lint.py` and the ci_checks window files. CI: new step *PMP light tests + StreamStats (offline, real QGIS)*; no request goes to USGS from CI.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_PMP/tests/test_streamstats.py` (saved responses for DWR 2801 / 4031, guard, cache tamper, mock fetcher pacing, cancel, PMF check), `test_streamstats_dialog.py` (offscreen, mocked client), `test_qgis_transport.py` (mocked QgsBlockingNetworkRequest: QGIS transport used, proxy hint on network errors; real requests to a 127.0.0.1 server: X-Client, HTTP status kept, closed port, QGIS proxy setting followed, QgsTask thread).

- **Runner 3.12.4 / damsuite RELEASE ndbreach 3.12.4: ANUGA host-pause check (DWR-2801 r3 logged 12 "stalls" of 128-2,083 s wall per 300 s of model time at normal step counts; dmesg showed the VM being frozen, and ANUGA's time.time() checkpoint timer saved an extra checkpoint right after each freeze).**
  - `core/anuga_dtdiag.YieldClock` (injectable clocks) appends ` cpu_s= mono_s= paused_s=` to each PROGRESS line (after the existing fields; `wall_s` is still the realtime delta) and ` box paused ~N s` when a pause is found. Two checks: realtime minus monotonic (> 1.5x monotonic; monotonic does not advance while the VM is frozen) and, after 3 unpaused yield steps, monotonic >= 3x the median step at a normal step count with CPU/monotonic below 0.3x the median cores (estimate mono - cpu/cores). Under 10 s is ignored. `cpu_s` is `time.process_time()`, the CPU of all of the worker's threads.
  - End of run: `PROGRESS 100.00 box paused ~N s in total over K yield step(s) (largest ~M s); cpu_s= mono_s= wall_s=` and `stats["host_pauses"]` in the result.
  - `ckpt_every_min` checkpoints are saved by the worker on `anuga_dtdiag.MonoTimer` (monotonic; minus CPU-check pauses), with ANUGA's own pickle name and pickler, atomically; `stats["checkpoint"]["checkpoint_timer"] = "monotonic"`. `ckpt_every_steps` is unchanged. The partial-flush timer is monotonic too.
  - Parsers unchanged (`anuga_detach.parse_progress`, `anuga_slowcheck.parse_log` read old and new lines). Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_anuga_pausecheck.py` (mocked clocks, fake ANUGA; one model test on real ANUGA).
- **Hazard 2.19.2: ND DWR download fixed (`build_nd_pack.py --download-dwr`).** `download_dwr_shapefiles` posted `layer=<index>, format=shapefile, extent=full`; downloadShape.php does not know those fields and answers HTTP 200 with an empty body, so no DWR layer was ever downloaded (each was logged as "not a zip (0 bytes)"). The request now sends the download form's fields: `qlay` = layer name (Dams, Damsline, Dugouts, DugoutsPoly), `whichextent` all / partial, `srid` 4269, `downloadformat` shp / spat, `mapext` (EPSG:2266 feet, `+`-separated) and `map`. New `--dwr-bbox W,S,E,N` and `--dwr-format`; a non-zip answer is reported and not saved; exit 1 when a layer is missing. `constants.py` falls back to `from damsuite import ...` so `python build_nd_pack.py` runs as a script again. Test: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_dwr_download.py` (mocked HTTP).
- **Runner 3.12.2 / damsuite RELEASE ndbreach 3.12.2: ANUGA runs detached from QGIS (DWR-2801: the GUI run died twice when the box's idle clean-up shut desktop :2 down; the worker's stdout pipe to QGIS broke, so its next PROGRESS print ended the run and the SIGTERM partial flush never ran).**
  - **Own log, own session.** New `core/anuga_detach.py` (stdlib only). `anuga_model._launch` starts the worker with stdout + stderr to `<workdir>/anuga.log` (a file, not a pipe), stdin /dev/null, `start_new_session=True` on POSIX, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP | CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` on Windows (without breakaway when the job forbids it), `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`. QGIS tails the log (`LogTail`) for PROGRESS lines; Cancel stops it as before (SIGTERM + grace with checkpoints, then kill); `NDBREACH_ANUGA_TIMEOUT_S` is an optional wall-clock limit (none by default).
  - **Hardened worker.** `worker_entry` (the worker's `__main__`): SIGPIPE and SIGHUP ignored, stdout / stderr wrapped so a failed write never ends the run, SIGTERM -> SystemExit (the checkpoint handler still flushes `anuga_partial.npz`). The result is written atomically (`savez_atomic`).
  - **Status file.** `<workdir>/anuga_worker.json`: pid, script, job, result, log, host, start / end times, state running / finished / failed / stopped (+ partial), and a tag with the run's checkpoint key and buffer. `worker_state` checks the pid without signalling it (OpenProcess on Windows; /proc zombie + command line on Linux) and reports `died` for a dead pid without a result.
  - **Adoption / finishing.** `anuga_model.run(run_key=...)` (workflow passes its checkpoint key): a worker left in the folder with the same key is waited for (still running) or its result used (finished, not yet built into products, or Resume on); a worker for other settings stops the new run. The rasters, products and report come from the usual post-processing. `rundam` saves its parameters to `_breach_model/run_params.json` (`core/run_params.py`); new Processing tool **Finish an ANUGA run after QGIS closed** (`ndbreach:finishanuga`, `qgis_process run ndbreach:finishanuga -- DAM_FOLDER=...`) runs the dam again from them with Resume on. The window shows the waiting worker and suggests the tool; CLI `anuga-status DAM_FOLDER` (exit 4 when one is waiting), `run CONFIG --resume` finishes it.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_anuga_detach.py` (fake worker; no ANUGA).
  - `tools/sync_damsuite.py` also brought `DamSafety_Data/damsuite` up to the master (it had missed the VERTCON vendoring on main; the same re-sync is in #223).

- **Runner 3.12.3: culvert prep - a corrected copy of the structures layer with big changes flagged (DWR-2801's manual invert fixes made automatic; Nick: "big changes flagged for my review").** New `core/culvert_prep.py`, Processing tool *Culvert prep: corrected copy of the structures layer (review list)* (`ndbreach:culvertprep`), CLI `ndbreach_cli.py culvert-prep`. It runs the #166 pre-check on the run's fine DEM. A HIGH end's proposed invert is the lowest fine ground within max(apron, 5 m). The change is applied in a NEW GeoPackage (the input is never written; its sha256 is checked before and after) when it is <= the limit (1 ft by default) on native ground (the top source, <= 1 m cells) on a single-part line. Larger changes, fill / coarser ground and multi-part lines are flagged (unchanged, listed); FALLBACK ends are listed. Line straightening (`SIMPLIFY_RATIO`, default off) is always flagged. Each changed feature gets a "PREP …" note; the copy's `runner_culverts_info` gets a `culvert_prep` row. Change log `culvert_prep_<dam>.md/.csv/.json` with the input and output sha256. Accept flow: fill the CSV's `accept` column (or a list of ids) and run again from the same input; stale accepts are not applied. Without a structures layer the tool builds one first with ND Dam Hazard's `runnerculverts` (NBI / NDDOT inventories). The run is unchanged (no new Run option); #210's bed floor and #218's channel-bed check still apply. Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_culvert_prep.py` (DWR-2801 fixture: 8 culverts, the invfix2 values; synthetic 1 m tiles from the recorded ground).
- **Docs (Docs & QA): Runner 3.11.0 / Hazard 2.19.0 in the user guide; changelog and metadata clean-up.** USER_GUIDE.md: new sections for the ANUGA first-step inflow cap (3.10.94, #208), culvert inverts against the 1 m channel bed with the opt-in bed floor (3.10.96, #210), the offline-only Microsoft footprint message (2.18.68, #211), crossing depth in the 1 m channel (2.18.69, #212) and the aerial basemap (2.19.0 / 3.11.0, #213); "Not verified in this guide" updated. docs/CHANGELOG.md: conflict-marker lines from the 2026-10-28 merges removed (no entry dropped or retitled), shipped entries moved out of Unreleased into dated sections (UTC merge date), #213 / #215 / #216 entries added. `DamSafety_Hazard/README.txt`: conflict markers removed (version line 2.19.0; the 2.18.67 / 68 / 69 notes kept), 2.19.0 maps note. metadata.txt changelogs (text only): Runner 3.10.93 - 3.10.96 version lines restored and a 3.11.0 entry written; Hazard 2.19.0 entry and the 2.18.66 entry moved back under `changelog=` (they had ended up inside the Runner `icon=` and the Hazard `hasProcessingProvider=` values). Brought up to date with main after #218 (merge of main b8e4bc1, no force-push): the guide, the Hazard README and the metadata changelogs now give Hazard 2.19.1 / Runner 3.11.1 as the current versions (2.19.0 / 3.11.0 stay as the versions that brought the aerial basemap); #218's metadata.txt key lines, `versions.py`, `constants.py` and code are kept as merged; in the metadata.txt changelogs #217's detailed 2.19.0 / 3.11.0 bullets replace #218's one-line summaries (both CHANGELOG entries kept).

## 2026-10-28

- **Screening 0.17.55: tool 7b (Detailed runs with DamSafety Runner) can download the Runner's DEM for the chosen dams (Nick, Mac, QGIS 3.34.6: the 'DEM for the runner' box was empty and 7b had no way to get tiles).**
  - **Parameters.** `DEM` is optional. `DEM_SOURCE` 'Runner DEM source…': Use the DEM above (default) / Download USGS 3DEP 10 m covering the chosen dams / Download USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles (native) covering the chosen dams. Advanced: `DEM_BUFFER_MI` (10 mi = half the Runner's 20 mi default extent), `DEM_FOLDER` (empty = `<Dams folder>/_runner_dem`; the dam listings skip `_` folders), `DEM_MAX_GB` (10), `DEM_TILE_FOLDER` (empty = the suite's shared `dem/lidar_1m`). A download choice overrides a DEM picked above (warning). The settings sheet picks them up from the definitions.
  - **Extent.** The box of the chosen dams' breach points (EPSG:5070) plus the buffer, snapped to 10 m. File names carry a hash of the box (`7b_dem_<n>dams_<key>_10m_5070.tif`, `..._1m_lidar.vrt`), so the same dams reuse the files.
  - **10 m.** New `core/runner_dem.py` calls `dem_download.download` (ImageServer, 12 km pieces, 3 tries per piece on any error incl. 502, 120 s timeout) and `write_local_dem` (one EPSG:5070 GeoTIFF, band unit m, `_dem.txt` facts). **1 m.** `lidar_tnm` make_plan / fetch (native 10 km TNM tiles, resume, retries) / build_vrt (10 m file underneath when there is one; VRT in the tiles' UTM CRS, band unit m). Either way rundam gets `DEM` = the file and `DEM_Z` = Meters (`handoff.RUNDAM_KEYS` + `DEM_Z`); the manifest records `runner_dem`.
  - **Before downloading.** The log gives the requests / tiles and the size; over `DEM_MAX_GB` the run stops and says what to change. Validate only plans and fetches nothing. Cancel: `dem_download.Cancelled` at the next request (Download DEM logs 'Cancelled. Nothing was written.').
  - **No DEM, no download.** The error names 'Download DEM (dam, county, or state)' and the new option.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_7b_dem_download.py` (mock QGIS, fake downloads), `test_7b_dem_download_gdal.py` (real GDAL, fake download; SKIP without GDAL).
- **Runner 3.12.1 / Hazard 2.20.1 / Screening 0.17.55 / damsuite 1.18.1: local (assumed) datums taken from the DEM (Nick: "be assumed ngvd29, if elevation is 100 or similar, they would be local and would have to take actual elevations from dem").** Unstated datums stay NGVD29. In Auto, a top of dam (or pool) outside 500 - 3,600 ft, more than 50 ft from the DEM crest, or a round 100 ft multiple more than 10 ft from it is a local datum: not converted; the top of dam is the DEM crest (along the dam line, else near the point) and the other elevations keep their height below the local top. New choice "Local (use DEM)" (window, ELEV_DATUM, --elev-datum local). Recorded as "local (assumed)", method DEM, with the original values and a warning, in run_report.json, the run report, dam.json and the Hazard summary.

Add this to the plugins for all dams that do not state the elevations as NAVD88").** Inventory / dam.json / user elevations not stated as NAVD88 are shifted NGVD29 -> NAVD88 at the dam (bundled ND clip of NOAA VERTCON `us_noaa_vertconc.tif`, pyproj fallback, offline) once, with the source datum, shift and method recorded in run_report.json, the run report, dam.json and the Hazard summary; NGVD29 assumed when unstated, with a warning. Vertical datum override (Auto / NAVD88 / NGVD29) in the Runner window, Run one dam, Batch and the CLI. DWR-2801: 1071.15 + 1.09 = 1072.24 ft NAVD88.
- **Hazard 2.19.1 / Runner 3.11.1: fixes from QGIS Dev's smoke test of main 7feee1f (PR #218).**
  - **Map labels checked first (Hazard).** `basemap.label_checks` asks for one label tile per service (3 s, `LABEL_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`, cached; a failed host is not asked twice) and `layout_basemap` adds only the label layers that answered. With the label host unreachable, `layout_imagery` + one layout export took 2.3 s + 122.8 s and the credit still said "labels: Esri reference"; now the export does not wait on labels and the credit / DATA SOURCES name labels only when they are drawn (a failed label service is listed under "not used").
  - **Service checks (Hazard).** A check that ran to its timeout says "did not answer within N s (timed out)"; the check cache key is the URL plus the WMS request's layer / format / CRS (`probe_key`); the DATA SOURCES line lists the unused sources once (a hillshade base map listed them twice).
  - **Offline flag (Hazard).** `ND_DAM_OFFLINE` counts only when 1 / true / yes / on (the Runner's `_truthy`); `0` / `false` no longer turns the base map and the downloads off. `ND_DAM_SUITE` set still does.
  - **Hillshade (Hazard).** z factor = 2x exaggeration on a projected DEM, converted from degrees on a geographic DEM (was a fixed 2.0). `constants.PLUGIN_VERSION` once (it was repeated five times).
  - **Culvert channel bed (Runner).** `structures.channel_bed` took the lowest ground 0-30 m beyond each end, which on a sloping channel read the downstream bed low by up to slope x 30 m (2 % -> 0.6 m = 2 ft) and rejected correct inventory inverts (smoke repro: 2 % slope, inverts = the true bed, "disagree ... +2.0 ft: LiDAR inverts used"). Now `thalweg_profile` + `channel_slope` (Theil-Sen over the 30 m) carry each thalweg point back to the end, and the bed is the 10th percentile over the first 10 m: the same repro gives "inventory inverts used (+0.0 ft / -0.0 ft)" at 0, 0.5, 1 and 2 %. ANUGA culvert bed notes give feet as well as metres.
  - **ANUGA volume note wording (#209, Runner).** The fractional-step integral is every operator's (the breach inlet and any culvert / bridge operators); the note names the inlet alone only when the culverts' net (`operator_volumes`) is known and small, otherwise it splits or says the culverts' part was not logged (`structures_net`). It recommends the first-step inflow cap only when the run did not use it (`step_cap_used`, `volume_balance.inflow_step_cap`).
  - **metadata.txt.** Hazard's 2.18.66 entry sat after `hasProcessingProvider=yes` (value 'yes' + the entry, so `qgis_process` skipped the Hazard provider) and a Runner line sat after `icon=`; both fixed. A bare `%` makes QGIS's configparser read (BasicInterpolation, `qgis.utils` / Plugin Manager) of the changelog fail, so it showed empty; every `%` is `%%` now.
  - **CI (`tools/ci_checks.py`).** The marker check uses `^(<<<<<<<|=======$|>>>>>>>)` (a bare `=======` separator too); new "metadata format" check: QGIS's own read of every metadata.txt must succeed, an indented continuation only under changelog / description / about, one Hazard `PLUGIN_VERSION`. Tests: `tools/test_ci_checks.py`, Hazard `tests/test_basemap_smoke_fixes.py`, Runner `tests/test_smoke_7feee1f_fixes.py`.

Source chain: ND DWR NAIP 2025 (WMS `aerial.dwr.nd.gov/dwrwms`, layer `NorthDakota_NAIP_2025_col_air`, requested in the map CRS), then Esri World Imagery, then USGS imagery - the first that answers; a timeout, an HTML / XML page or a blank picture skips to the next. Esri street / town labels are drawn above the flood, only over online imagery. A credit line under each map gives the source, resolution, flight date(s), labels, CRS and plugin version.

"imagery" warns when it falls back, auto is silent. Offline (`ND_DAM_OFFLINE`, `NDBREACH_BASEMAP_OFFLINE`, `ND_DAM_HAZARD_OFFLINE_ONLY`, or `ND_DAM_SUITE` set): pack imagery, else the DEM hillshade. 30 s network budget per map; a map never fails a run. Tile cache in the user cache folder (`DamSafety/basemap_cache`; `NDBREACH_BASEMAP_CACHE` = folder or off; 90 days; `NDBREACH_BASEMAP_CACHE_MB`, default 200), never in a run or input folder. `NDBREACH_BASEMAP_ORDER` / `NDBREACH_BASEMAP_SOURCES` override the chain. The basemap keys are left out of the run / ensemble cache keys.

- **Hazard 2.18.69: DWR-3034 REVIEW 9.8 item 8 - crossing depth in the 1 m channel.** The crossing depth was the deepest 30 m `Depth (Max)` cell within 50 ft (3034's ford: 3.24 ft, while WSE minus the 1 m channel bed is ~9.4 ft: cell bed 1713.68 ft vs 1 m min 1707.48 ft). New `crown.channel_depth` reads the run's 1 m DEM on a 1 m grid within `CROSSING_RADIUS_FT` and takes the deepest WSE (Max) - ground; `facilities.channel_depths` adds it to every wet crossing in the breach scenario (the crown source is opened when there are wet crossings too). `Bridges_Culverts_Crossings` gains `Channel_Depth_1m_ft`, `Channel_Bed_ft`, `Channel_Basis` (after `Source`); the summary and run log name it. `Max_Depth_ft` / Flooded / Charged unchanged (the grid), so the classification does not move. No WSE / 1 m DEM: the basis says why. Test: Hazard `tests/test_crossing_channel_depth.py`.
- **Hazard 2.18.68: DWR-3034 REVIEW 9.8 item 7.** The 3034 Hazard run was offline-only with the ND offline pack; Microsoft footprints are never in the pack and offline-only makes no internet request, so they could not be read - not a download or code failure. `pipeline.building_geometry_note` (new `offline` argument, default `_offline_now()`) and the run-log NOTE now give that cause and the fix (`ND_DAM_HAZARD_OFFLINE_ONLY=false` for the run) instead of "the download failed ... (offline?)"; the NAIP fallback likewise. Test: Hazard `tests/test_footprint_geometry.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.96: DWR-3034 REVIEW 9.8 items 3, 4.** (3) `structures.reconcile_inverts` checks inventory inverts against the LiDAR channel bed: `channel_bed` (lowest 1 m ground up to 30 m beyond each end) replaces the end disc when it reads ≥ 0.15 m lower (`end_bed`); DWR-3034's inverts (527.30 / 526.39 m) are now used as given instead of a −3.4 ft datum shift. The ANUGA bed floor (`NDBREACH_CULVERT_BED_FLOOR=enquiry|channel`, default off) clamps to the enquiry bed or the channel LiDAR, never the exchange-triangle minimum. (4) Upstreamed from the 3034 scratch patches: the structures layer's `losses` field (`losses` / `loss_coef` / `k_loss`), the bed floor, and the per-operator volume log (`operator_volumes.csv`, `anuga_worker.install_operator_volume_log`). Tests: `tests/test_culvert_channel_bed.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.95 / Hazard 2.18.67: DWR-3034 REVIEW 9.8 items 2, 5, 6.** (2) `anuga_model.volume_note` gives the inflow operators' share of a gain (`operator_share`) and the first-step cause (`anuga_worker.FirstStep` -> `volume_balance.first_step`, `first_step_cause`): on DWR-3034 4,065 m³ = 100% of the gain is the inlet's 0.5·(Q(t)+Q(t+dt))·dt over one 300 s first step (9,994 m³ booked for 5,929 m³); it no longer names the mesh edge or an instability for that. (5) Hazard `facilities.dedupe` merges two OpenStreetMap crossings of one source that share a node, or lie within 5 m with the same category or changeset (`osm_same_crossing`); `osm_parse` asks Overpass for `out center meta` and writes `osm_nodes` / `osm_changeset`. DWR-3034's ford node/11121114805 + way/1198845155 is now one row. (6) `bridge_snap.snap_counts`: kept_verified / kept_unverified / none / moved by road and rail in run_report.json, the run report and the log (DWR-3034: 49/6/2/1 = 24+25 / 5+1 / 2+0 / 1+0). Tests: Runner `tests/test_g5_message_snap_counts.py`, Hazard `tests/test_osm_crossing_dedupe.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.94: ANUGA's first dry-domain step capped at the hydrograph interval (DWR-3034 REVIEW 9.2, G5).** A dry domain has no CFL limit, so ANUGA's first step was the whole 300 s yield step and `Inlet_operator` booked 0.5·Q(300 s)·300 = 9,994 m³ for the first 300 s against 5,929 m³ in the hydrograph (+4,065 m³, ratio 1.0461 on DWR-3034; 1.0263 on the 3.10.73 run). `anuga_worker.inflow_max_timestep` / `InflowStepCap`: `domain.evolve_max_timestep` = the hydrograph's median spacing (at most 1/100 of its duration and the yield step, at least 0.5 s) until the first yield step after the hydrograph ends, then the old limit. `NDBREACH_ANUGA_INFLOW_MAX_DT` overrides it (`0` = off). Volume balance gets `inflow_step_cap`. Tests: `tests/test_anuga_inflow_step_cap.py` (real-ANUGA bowl: ratio 1.0000 capped vs 1.2000 uncapped). DWR-2801 / 2865 / 3034 G5 need a rerun to re-gate (Grok Bot).
- **Screening 0.17.54, Hazard 2.18.66, Runner 3.10.93, Setup 1.0.16, damsuite 1.17.7: the ND DWR dugouts in the plugins, and the five DWR GeoPackages in the offline pack.**
  - **Screening tool 1 (Prepare dams).** Optional DWR dugout points / polygons (suite slots `dugouts` / `dugout_polygons`, auto-filled). A dam inside a dugout polygon or within `DUGOUT_BUF_FT` (default 100 ft = 30.48 m; true metres in every CRS) of a dugout point is left out, logged `DWR dugout <structure_id>` and listed in `1_dugouts.csv`; a dam with a real DWR hazard class or an NID id is kept with a warning. Empty inputs: no change. Why 100 ft: DWR's dugout points sit on the dugout (1,608 of 1,767 inside their own polygon); DWR dams match 5 / 6 / 9 / 14 at 10 / 30 / 50 / 100 m, unknown structures 0 up to 50 m - 30 m catches the dam records on a dugout without reaching the next structure.
  - **Screening tool 16 (Find ponds).** A water body that is a DWR dugout gets `dwr_dugout = structure_id` and is classed a dugout (only the water above the natural ground is released, from the LiDAR); `storage_above_grade_acft` goes in the notes, a clearly larger DWR value is a warning.
  - **Hazard `build_nd_pack --dwr-folder PATH`** (new `dwr_files.py`, kind `local_gpkg`): DWR_dams, DWR_dam_lines, DWR_unknown_structures, DWR_dugouts, DWR_dugout_polygons (group `dwr`), reprojected to lon / lat (DWR's EPSG:2266 / 2265 are international feet), every attribute kept, source file / layer / CRS / date / rows in the manifest. Without the option the build is unchanged.
  - **damsuite 1.17.7.** `suite_fill`, `suitedata.dwr_layers` and `damdata` read the DWR layers from the active format-2 pack when the suite folder has no DWR file; the engineer's own file always wins; Download mode reads none.
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_dwr_dugouts.py`, `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_dwr_pack_files.py` (synthetic, mock QGIS).
- **Screening 0.17.54: tool 16 (Find ponds and dugouts) on a statewide water-body layer no longer stalls or comes back empty without saying why (Nick, Absaraka DWR-2801: "tool 16 stalled out and didn't find ponds").**
  - **Local DEM.** Only the water bodies over the DEM mosaic's extent are read (`raster_box`, a filter rectangle on the layer). A statewide NHD layer with the one-dam DEM gave a 'no terrain data' error for every water body of the state: 94,979 errors in 120 s on the box (about 360,000 in all) before; now 672 of 369,435 are read and the run ends in 95 s (37 written).
  - **County.** The county's box is read first, then the county test runs on the lon / lat of each water body. It used the centroid in the layer's CRS, so a projected layer (EPSG:5070, UTM) had none in any county: 0 analysed in 0.1 s before, 652 analysed and 33 written now. The summary always gives the count outside the county; a county the local DEM does not cover stops with a message.
  - **USGS 3DEP.** Each water body is one or two exportImage requests of 1.7 to 10.7 s (measured), and the progress bar's step was the statewide count: Cass County moved 0.06 % in 220 s. Above 200 water bodies a warning gives the time (Cass box: 1,982, 1.7 to 5.5 h). A line every 30 s gives water body i of n, analysed / written / failed, seconds each and the time left. Cancel says where it stopped.
  - **Nothing written.** A warning gives the reasons (natural depressions in 'Water bodies not written' and the 'Also write natural depressions' option, failures, size limits, nothing in the county). In the Absaraka area about 92 % of the NHD water bodies are natural depressions.
  - **Not done.** No EXTENT parameter. Statewide on 3DEP still runs (with the warning). `_dem_window` / `_write_clip` still block for up to 3 x 120 s on a hung request (`fetch_3dep_tile`).
  - Tests: `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_findponds_stall.py` (new, simulated), `test_findponds.py` (a pond outside the local DEM is not read now).

## 2026-10-27

- **Runner 3.10.92: HEC-RAS export depth = max WSE minus the actual terrain under each output pixel by default again (the run's 1 m DEM where it has one); reverses #189's (3.10.84) cell-minimum default; cell minimum stays selectable; three more bases (`terrain_mean`, `terrain_max`, `fine`); vertical datum / unit check.**
  - **Default.** `hecras.DEPTH_BASIS_DEFAULT` is `terrain` again (#173's basis). Depth, wet mask and the 0.1 ft arrival use the same depth. On sloped cells the depth is shallower than cell minimum (never deeper). DWR-2801 re-exported from the same HDF: wet (>0.1 ft) 44,376 grid cells on terrain vs 83,690 on cell minimum. Cell minimum: `NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS=cell_min`, `RunSettings.hecras_depth_basis`, the import tool's Depth basis, `ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport --depth-basis cell_min`.
  - **Ground.** `hecras_engine.fine_terrain_on_grid`: the run's own DEM (`run_report.json` DEM, its unit, less the reservoir carve) when finer than the model terrain by more than 1.5x (DWR-2801: 1 m vs ~3 m), else the model terrain; `NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_TERRAIN=auto|dem|model`. Sampled at each grid cell's centre (nearest): the WSE is the nearest HEC-RAS cell's to that centre, so depth and WSE refer to the same point (3.10.79 averaged over the 30 m cell).
  - **Datum / unit.** `hecras.datum_check` compares the ground with the HDF's cell minimum elevations (the model terrain is written from the run's DEM with no vertical shift; DWR-2801 NAVD88). Median more than 1 ft below, or a feet / metres ratio: `DatumMismatch` - the stage keeps the cell minimum with a WARNING, the re-export / import tool refuse; `NDBREACH_HECRAS_DATUM_CHECK=warn` keeps the terrain. Above everywhere: a warning. Recorded in `hecras_stage.json` / `reexport.json` (`depth_terrain`, `terrain_sample`, `datum_check`, `datum_stop`, `depth_warnings`) and the run report.
  - **Chain.** The Hazard import adopts the stage's own terrain-basis rasters (no TERRAIN) when the stage recorded its terrain; the default with no terrain found uses the cell minimum with a WARNING.
  - **Three more depth bases (review of #204), terrain still the default.** `NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS` / `RunSettings.hecras_depth_basis` / the import tool's Depth basis / `hecras-reexport --depth-basis` take `terrain`, `cell_min`, `terrain_mean`, `terrain_max`, `fine`. `terrain_mean`: max WSE minus the mean ground over the whole 30 m pixel (3.10.79's sampling). `terrain_max`: at every terrain point in the pixel (1 m on the run's DEM: 30 x 30), the WSE of the nearest HEC-RAS cell minus that point's ground; the pixel keeps the deepest point, and its arrival is the earliest time any point reaches 0.1 ft - conservative: the deepest spot and the earliest arrival in the pixel (a narrow channel inside a pixel wets the whole pixel). `fine` (new `core/hecras_fine.py`): a full-resolution depth in the spirit of RAS Mapper's - an approximation, not RAS Mapper's own algorithm - the WSE interpolated barycentrically (Delaunay of the wet cell centres) when all three cells of the triangle are wet (a cell is wet when its max WSE is above its minimum elevation); next to a dry cell the nearest wet cell's WSE is held flat, and a point whose nearest cell is dry stays dry, so the surface never crosses a dry cell; minus the 1 m ground (no depth where the ground is above the surface); written as `Depth_fine (Max).tif` / `WSE_fine (Max).tif` (tiled DEFLATE Float32, ft; `NDBREACH_HECRAS_FINE_RASTER=0` / `hecras-reexport --no-fine-raster` skips them) and aggregated to the 30 m grid by `max` (default, as `terrain_max`) or `mean` of the in-mesh points, dry counted as 0 (`NDBREACH_HECRAS_FINE_AGG`); arrival from the deepest point's interpolated WSE series. Both fine bases work in 512 x 512 point tiles, single-threaded, only over the pixels a wet cell can reach, and stop above `NDBREACH_HECRAS_FINE_MAX_MPX` million points (default 1500): the stage then keeps `terrain` with a WARNING (`fine_stop`), the re-export refuses. All bases run the datum / unit check; `hecras_stage.json`, `reexport.json` and the run report record `depth_basis`, `terrain_sample` and, for the fine bases, `depth_fine` (points per side, aggregate, points, tiles, raster paths / bytes, seconds). The batch chain's Hazard import (Hazard 2.18.63 knows `terrain` and `cell_min`) takes the three new bases as `terrain`, with a WARNING. DWR-2801 (read-only re-export of `batch_new/DWR-2801`, 1 m DEM, datum ok on all): wet > 0.1 ft 44,376 grid cells `terrain` (mean 0.90 ft), 83,690 `cell_min` (1.17), 41,416 `terrain_mean` (0.85), 75,131 `terrain_max` (1.12), 69,921 `fine` max (1.16), 48,557 `fine` mean (0.81); `fine` 116.9 M points, 16,410 x 22,860 1 m rasters (118 MB depth + 24 MB WSE), 231 s for the fine step (315 s in all), about 1.3 GB memory. Tests: `tests/test_hecras_depth_bases.py` (6, synthetic: sloped cell, narrow channel, wet / dry edge, arrival, datum check and point limit for every new basis, re-export records).
  - **Not done.** Hazard's texts (`rasimport.py`, `processing_provider.py`, metadata) still call cell minimum the Runner's default (no Hazard version bump here).
  - Tests: `tests/test_hecras_depth_terrain_default.py` (8, synthetic), `test_hecras_depth_cellmin`, `test_hecras_terrain_depth`, `test_chain_batch` updated.
- **Hazard 2.18.65, Hazard Lite 0.1.1: roads and rail counted as overtopped only over the 1 m centreline crown (DWR-2801 review).**
  - **Hazard (`linear.py`, new `crown.py`, `road_profile.py` copied from the Runner 3.10.90).** A wet road or rail stretch (Roads_Inundated, Rail_Inundated) counts only where the max WSE (`WSE (Max).tif` beside the depth raster) is above the centreline crown read from the Runner's 1 m DEM (`_breach_model/run_report.json`); structure cuts in `structures.json` (bridge / notch) are masked as in the Runner. Stretches with no arrival time or under 0.1 ft are skipped. New fields Depth_Basis, Grid_Depth_ft, Crown_ft, WSE_ft, Freeboard_ft (the basis note in Notes); Max_Depth_ft is the overtopping over the crown. `outputs/QA/Line_Crown_Check` lists every wet stretch and the decision (also in the submittal). No WSE / DEM: raster depth, noted. Setting `line_crown_check` (default on); the evaluation key includes the WSE, run report and structures files.
  - **Hazard Lite (`core.py`).** The same check on `roads.csv` / `railroads.csv`, `line_crown_check.csv`.
  - **DWR-2801 (`tools/check_dwr2801_line_crown.py`, read-only).** Rail: 9 BNSF KO stretches counted before, 0 after (8 below the crown by 1.97 - 8.56 ft, 1 under 0.1 ft). Roads: 209 before, 22 after (154 below the crown, 33 under 0.1 ft / no arrival).
  - **A run folder from another computer (follows #198's `road_dem_of`).** When the DEM path recorded in `run_report.json` does not exist here (a Windows path, a moved or copied run folder), `crown.open_source` and Hazard Lite's `open_crown` look for it under the dam folder (the longest tail of the recorded path, as the Runner's `relocate_in_folder`) and the crown note says which file was read; with no DEM found the depth raster is used as before.
