- **Runner 3.10.91: the ANUGA gmsh mesh holds road and rail crests at their 1 m DEM elevation (DWR-2801 review).** The gmsh mesh sampled the bed at its vertices / centroids, so the DWR-2801 Hwy 18 embankment read 938.80 ft in the Oct 24 mesh (true 1 m pour about 941.5 ft, pond max WSE 939.80 ft) and the pond leaked east through it. Road and rail centrelines inside the mesh and within 500 ft of the LISFLOOD-FP wet area are now crest lines (new `core/anuga_crest.py`): embedded in the gmsh mesh (`gmsh_mesher.crest_edges`, the nodes on them returned as `crest_nodes`), and each such node held at the 1 m crest (the highest fine-DEM reading every 1 m along the line within the mesh's smallest edge plus 3 m); the ANUGA worker raises those vertices only (`crest_vertex_values`, never lowers). Bridge / notch cut corridors (`road_profile.cut_corridors`, as #198) and 50 m around the inlet are not held. `NDBREACH_ANUGA_CREST` (on by default; off / no / false / 0), `NDBREACH_ANUGA_CREST_PAD_M` (152.4), `_HALF_WIDTH_M` (3), `_CELL_M` (1); separate from `NDBREACH_ANUGA_BAND` (off by default). `run_report.json` `anuga_mesh.crest`. DWR-2801, mesh only (no solve; `tools/check_dwr2801_anuga_crest.py`, edge-aware / centroid pour level from the W pond east across Hwy 18): Oct 24 mesh 939.07 / 938.85 ft -> 941.34 / 940.04 ft (crest lines only, no hold: 939.07 / 938.89); cc5892e mesh 941.31 / 940.03 -> 941.34 / 940.04 ft; +33,000 triangles (314,130 -> 347,386), gmsh 17 s on 1 thread. The HEC-RAS export is unchanged: its default road / rail bands (3.10.76) put cell faces on the centrelines and the subgrid face tables come from the terrain. Not done: a crest sag narrower than about one crest edge (~20 m) is not resolved; no ANUGA solve was run. Tests: `tests/test_anuga_crest.py` (9), `tests/test_gmsh_gate.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.90: road overtopping depth over the road crown on the centreline, not in a structure-cut trench; NBI bridge cuts snapped to the road's opening (DWR-3034 Docs & QA review).**
  - **(a) Road overtopping (ND hazard tool, `algorithms_ndhazard.py`, new `core/road_profile.py`).** A road's overtopping depth was the deepest 30 m depth cell under the road, so the trench the Runner cuts for a bridge or culvert counted as road overtopping: DWR-3034 Hwy 25 read 3.99 ft at (-384307.9, 2675129.3), in the trench of the misplaced Square Butte Creek bridge cut. Now it is the water surface (`WSE (Max).tif`) minus the road surface along the centreline every 1 m (metres in any CRS: on a State Plane feet folder the profile constants are converted with the rasters' CRS unit). The road surface is the run's fine DEM (`run_report.json` settings `input_files.DEM`) in the unit the run recorded (`dem_z_to_m`, else `dem_elevation_units` as `core/hecras_model._z_factor`, else the raster's band unit or vertical CRS; nothing recorded: the depth raster with a Check, never a metres default), or the advanced `Road surface DEM` in its own unit (new advanced `Road surface DEM elevation units`: Detect / metres / feet / US survey feet), used only when its median offset from the run's own ground (the run's DEM at the wet centreline points, else the model ground WSE - depth of the wet cells under them) is within 1 m; an unknown unit or a larger offset sends every road to the depth raster with a Check naming why. Culverts never mask (over a buried culvert the fine DEM is the road surface, and the trench cell's water surface reads dry against it unless the road is overtopped). Bridge and notch cuts (`_breach_model/structures.json`, the cut corridor only, no buffer; the lines moved from the run's `work_crs` into the rasters' CRS) do not drop points: they are read on a deck stand-in, the lower approach's road surface just outside the corridor with a sag's approach grade (at most 10 %) carried in, so the stand-in is never above either approach; a point whose own surface is lower but within 1.5 m reads on its own surface. A bare-earth opening (>= 1.5 m below both 40 m shoulders, its steep side measured over max(5 m, 2 DEM pixels)) takes the same stand-in only where it lines up (within 15 m) with a bridge or notch corridor; any other opening is read as it is (the tool has no mapped stream input, and a road sag over a culvert looks the same). Wet points with no road surface (DEM no-data, outside it, a refilled area) or no water surface take the depth raster there (the road depth is the larger); a road whose DEM block cannot be read (a VRT tile gone) keeps the depth raster with a Check naming it; no wet point with both surfaces -> the depth raster. Every road the depth raster had overtopped and the centreline reads lower gets a Check and a row in the report's Inputs and in `road_depth.dropped` (no longer overtopped) / `road_depth.lowered` (shallower): id, raster depth and x/y, centreline depth and x/y, freeboard, points on the stand-in / an opening / with no road or water surface, and the reason; roads read from the depth raster are in `road_depth.raster_roads`. The report text says how many bridge / notch cuts matched a road (it no longer claims cuts were left out when none matched). `Road overtopping depth` = 'Model depth raster' keeps the old basis; the 3.10.90 draft's `ROAD_CUT_BUFFER_FT` (50 ft) is gone. Synthetic DWR-3034-like cases (bridge snapped onto the creek's bare-earth opening, or misplaced on solid fill; water 6 m below Hwy 25, 1 m in the cut's trench): legacy 3.28 ft, centreline not overtopped (19.7 ft freeboard), listed as dropped. DWR-3034 Hwy 25 (read-only check of the real folder with the first 3.10.90 draft, not re-run after this review): legacy 3.99 ft; centreline 0.0 ft (all 30 wet points in the cut corridor; without the mask 19.2 ft freeboard).
  - **(b) NBI bridge cuts (Runner `core/workflow.py`, new `core/bridge_snap.py`).** Before the inventory inverts are read, every inventory bridge is checked against the road or rail centreline it carries: the one its line crosses (a line crossing none matches one only when its midpoint is on that line's crown, within 30 m), road / rail pieces whose end points touch (1 m) merged into one line first (a layer split at the bridge or at an intersection), and a rail bridge (NBI item 42A = 2, NDDOT "service on" 2, Hazard runner_culverts' placement or a "no rail line crosses" note, rail words in the facility carried; new `Structure.carries`, read by the QGIS tools and the command-line runner, not in the run key) only against rail lines - with none in the run it is not checked. The bare DEM is read along that line within the radius (1 m steps) and beside it (10-50 m each side). Main's place stays unless the evidence says it is solid embankment, and the keep tests run before any target is looked at: an opening in the DEM at the cut, a channel line (`CHANNEL_LINES`) there or the ground beside the road at least 0.5 m lower there than along the road (a channel under a deck or water surface that is in the LiDAR) keep it (verified); the road dipping 0.3 m or more at the cut below both sides, within the shoulder window (40 m plus half the span: wide openings) or the grade line (150 m each way) - its low point, a sag, an opening the opening rule cannot size -, no DEM data at the cut or beside it (water, a gap), or a road line that ends within the shoulder window keep it too (not verified). Only from solid embankment does a cut move, and only to the NEAREST crossing along the road within the radius, never one past it: a DEM opening of at least 1.5 m (depth only breaks a tie) whose bare-earth bed is at least 1 m below the ground beside the road at the cut, or a channel line crossing; with channel lines on that road an opening counts only with one within 50 m (else a grade separation), and never one where another road or rail line passes under. A crossing another structure's line already cuts (within 30 m) is not taken, and crossings are handed out once, nearest bridge first, so the inventory's order does not matter (twin bridges of a divided highway each get their own carriageway's opening). A moved cut is centred on the crossing, oriented along the channel line there (else across the road), keeps its length across the road, and its inverts are cleared (NBI / NDDOT bridges carry none; the cut then goes to the lowest ground near its new ends, as for any bridge without inverts - not re-read). Every move is a run warning (name, from, to, distance, method); the bridges kept without an opening or a channel seen at the cut are named, with the reason, in one warning; `run_report.json` `bridge_snap` holds the radius and a row per bridge (method, verified, moved, from, to, why) and `run_report.md` a table of them. `NDBREACH_BRIDGE_SNAP_M` sets the radius (m, default 300), read like the other `NDBREACH_*` numbers: 0, off, no or false = off (main's behaviour), a value that is not a number is a warning. Moved bridges change the run key. Synthetic checks: the DWR-3034-like case (the cut 190 m off on solid fill, the valley falling to the creek) moves onto the creek's opening (built-in engine, 20 m cells: creek depth downstream 0.71 -> 0.90 m, ponding upstream 1.75 -> 1.26 m, as the first draft); correct bridges with the deck in the LiDAR, a weak, wide or no-data opening, at the road's low point, on a channel line, on a road split at the bridge, rail bridges beside a road and a bridge 40 m from its opening with a deeper one 200 m away are no longer moved elsewhere (the first draft moved them 150-250 m); 300 random valley layouts: no correct bridge moved off its creek (the first draft: 22 of 339). DWR-3034 with the first draft (read-only check, 58 bridges): NBI 0025008224 moved 187 m north to the 6.6 m deep, 30 m wide opening at Square Butte Creek (-384305.7, 2675306.5); 0000BISM03 (Hay Creek) 24 m; 28 kept; 28 not checked (mostly OSM rail bridges, no rail lines in the check). That folder was not re-run with this review's rules (`python tools/check_dwr3034_road_bridge.py DAM_FOLDER` does it read-only); a bridge the evidence does not support stays at the inventory location, with the warning.
  - **(c) Command-line runner (`scripts/ndbreach_cli.py`).** `st.road_lines` / `st.rail_lines` now come from the kept `layers.receptor_roads` / `receptor_rail` lines and `input_files.DEM` is recorded, as the QGIS tools do, so the bridge check (b) and the ND tool's centreline basis (a) also work for CLI runs; a structure's facility kind (b) comes from the layer's properties. As in QGIS, the road lines also turn on the culvert guesses at road crossings (listed, cut only when matched and `culvert_burn_matched`), gmsh embankment lines, `dam_point_checks.road_m` and the HEC-RAS road bands, and a CLI run's key changes.
  - **Review (QGIS Dev, PR #198).** (1) *Dip rule on a sagging road* (`bridge_snap._judge`): a road sagging into a coulee put a misplaced cut on the approach grade below the far side within the 150 m grade line, so the dip rule kept it (2 % grade, 60 m off: kept, 21.3 ft of Hwy-25-like overtopping on the deck stand-in). A dip now counts in a window (shoulder or grade line) only when the cut is the road's low point there (the lowest crown on each side within it is not more than 0.3 m below the cut); grades 1-4 % with the cut 40-80 m off now move to the opening (crown 0.0 ft, 16.4 ft freeboard), and a true sag's low point is still kept. (2) *Streets named after the railway* (`bridge_snap.name_kind`): the facility carried is split on `/ \ ; & + ,` and 'and / over / under / at'; a part is rail when it has a rail word with no street word after the last one (AVE, ST, STREET, RD, DR, BLVD, LN, WAY, PL, CT, PKWY, HWY, ... - 'RAILROAD AVE', 'Soo Street', 'SOO LINE RD' are streets), road when it has a street word or a road mark (CO RD 12, US 2, ND 25, I-94, county / township road); the name says rail only when some part is rail and none is road. A compound road / rail name ('CO RD 12 / BNSF RY', 'ND 25 OVER BNSF') is ambiguous - which one the bridge carries is not in the name - so it falls to the embankment Hazard's runner_culverts placed it on, else unknown (checked against roads and rails as before); the NBI / NDDOT service code still wins. Known limit: 'BNSF RY ST PAUL' reads ST as a street word (not rail by name). (3) *Moved bridges in sub-runs*: `_move` edits the structure in place and sensitivity / probabilistic / cascade / nested / refine sub-runs share it through shallow copies, so a sub-run's report showed the moved bridge 'kept, verified'. The original line, inverts, distance and method are kept on the structure (`Structure.snap_from`, not in `to_dict` or the run key); a bridge with it is reported as moved from its original location (`snapped_earlier`), keeps its crossing, and is not judged or moved again (idempotent). (4) *Paths* (`algorithms_ndhazard.py`): the advanced Road surface DEM is compared with the run's DEM by `same_path` (`samefile`, else `normcase(normpath(abspath))`: Windows case and `/` vs `\`), and `road_dem_of` reads a recorded DEM that does not exist here (a folder copied from another machine, a Windows path on Linux) from the same relative path under the dam folder, saying so in the basis text. DWR-3034 read-only check after these fixes (`tools/check_dwr3034_road_bridge.py`, which now passes the DEM unit it decided to `GdalTerrain`: since 3.10.89 'Detect' stops on the composite VRT, which records no unit): the same as the PR head before them - NBI 0025008224 kept, verified (the ground beside the road 2.3 m lower at the cut: a channel under the deck), Hwy 25 0.00 ft on the centreline (18.7 ft freeboard with the cut on the deck stand-in, 19.25 ft without; legacy raster 3.99 ft); all 58 bridges: 31 kept, 27 not checked (26 OSM rail bridges with no rail line in the check, 1 with no opening within 300 m), none moved. Tests: 4 added (sagging road over 1-4 % and 40-80 m, street / compound names, a sub-run copy, paths and the relocated DEM), the snap-again assert of the first bridge test updated.
  - **Follow-up (PR #198, the 'lower beside the road' keep of review commit 7c27c79).** That keep held DWR-3034's NBI 0025008224 'kept, verified' 187 m south of its creek - the case that motivated (b). Root cause (read-only, the raw 1 m composite VRT, so not the burned trench; 3DHP flowlines): the cut is on a smooth embankment grade (crown 523.7 m, no opening); the 2.3 m comes from low ground 28-50 m off the centreline (the side window reaches 50 m) that runs ALONG Hwy 25 - about 515.2 m on the east side for ~105 m (s = -90 to +15), 515.8-516.2 m on the west for ~55 m - Square Butte Creek's floodplain / ponds at the embankment toe (3DHP waterbody connectors), compared with a 120 m along-road median of 518.5 m. Nothing crosses the road there (the 3DHP 'Square Butte Creek' connector stub at the cut runs over the 523.8 m crown); an unnamed 3DHP channel line crosses at the real opening, 6.6 m deep and 30 m wide, its bed (~515.9 m) at the ponds' level. Now (`bridge_snap._judge`): (i) low ground beside the road that stays within 0.5 m of its lowest at the cut for 40 m (`DITCH_M`) past the cut, on either side of the road and either way, is a ditch, borrow area or pond along the road, not a crossing, and keeps nothing; (ii) low ground across the road at the cut is still a keep, kept, verified (a channel under a deck in the LiDAR; see the next item: nothing about the nearest crossing overturns it). After a ditch the cut moves when the nearest crossing is clearly better - a DEM opening at least 3 m (`CLEAR_DEPTH_M`) and 2 x (`CLEAR_FACTOR`) the dip deep, at least half the span wide (15 m cap), its bed within 0.5 m of the ground beside the cut - or by the usual rules (the bed 1 m below the cut's side ground, a channel line). DWR-3034 read-only (`tools/check_dwr3034_road_bridge.py`, all 58 bridges): NBI 0025008224 moved 186.7 m north to (-384305.7, 2675306.5), the 6.6 m x 30 m opening (the first draft's place); Hwy 25 0.00 ft on the centreline (18.70 ft freeboard with the moved cut on the deck stand-in, 19.25 ft without; legacy raster 3.99 ft); 29 kept (21 at an opening in the DEM, 3 on low ground across the road - 0.7, 2.3 and 2.4 m, no channel lines in the check -, 5 at the road's low point incl. 0000BISM03), 1 moved, 28 not checked or unverified - 26 OSM rail bridges (no rail line in the check), NBI 000000008111250 (41ST ST NE, no opening within 300 m), and NBI 000000008110210 (201ST AVE NW over Burnt Creek, was 'kept, verified' on a 0.5 m dip: the ground beside the road is flat at ~586 m on both sides for 260 m, a road on fill across a flat wetland; now 'ditch along the road', no crossing within 300 m, the cut stays and is named in the stay warning). No other bridge moved (0000BISM03, moved 24 m by the first draft, stays kept). Test r4 (fails on the previous head): a 3034-like ditch / borrow strip 2.3 m deep with and without channel lines moves 190 m to the creek, local low ground across the road stays kept, verified (a shallow opening, a deep one with or without a channel line there, a taken one), and the ditch with no opening stays unverified.
  - **Review of 9c646d6 (QGIS Dev, probe R4).** 9c646d6 also let low ground across the road at the cut give way to a clearly better opening when a channel line crossed there and none at the cut (`not keep_weak or t["channel"]`). That read a missing line as proof the cut is misplaced, but `CHANNEL_LINES` is often only the main stem clipped to the run radius (`algorithms.py`): a correct bridge whose deck shows in the LiDAR over an unmapped 1.5 m creek moved 145 m to the main stem's opening, 'verified' (kept on fdab2c0). Now low ground across the road at the cut is never overturned by the nearest crossing: kept, verified, with the crossing named in the note. Whether the lines cover the cut's surroundings (tributaries near the cut, lines reaching past it) cannot be judged reliably from a clipped, partial line set, so the safer rule was chosen: a missing line is never evidence. `_weak_fallback` (the keep when the clearly better target was taken) is gone with it. DWR-3034 is unchanged (read-only, all 58 bridges identical to 9c646d6): 0025008224's low ground runs along the road, so the ditch rule drops the keep and the move (186.7 m N) never used channel lines (the check has none); 8110210 stays unverified and named. Test r5: probe R4 at x = 150 / 155 / 160 stays kept, verified, not moved (moves 145 m on 9c646d6); with the creek mapped it is kept by the line. Random layouts with a channel line only at the other creek: 0 of 900 moved (and 0 of 900 deck-in-LiDAR, 0 of 600 with roadside ditches).

Read-only check of a finished run: `python tools/check_dwr3034_road_bridge.py DAM_FOLDER [--nbi 0025008224]` (old / new cut location, overtopping with the bridge / notch cuts on the deck stand-in and without them, the legacy raster value; writes nothing).
- **Runner 3.10.89, Screening 0.17.50: a one-dam run on a downloaded DEM - 'Detect from the raster' reads the Download DEM record of a USGS 3DEP download; Feet on a metre DEM says to choose Meters; Download DEM records the unit of what it writes (Nick, Absaraka DWR-2801; on top of 3.10.88).**
  - **Detect.** Downloads from Screening 0.17.49 and earlier have no band unit (and a later download of the same dam reuses the file), so 3.10.88's detection still stopped on them. `gdal_io.recorded_z_unit` also reads the tool's own record of a USGS 3DEP download: the dataset metadata `SOURCE` / `CRS` / `UNITS` / `FILE_TYPE` / `CELL_M` of `write_local_dem` (all versions) together with `<stem>_dem.txt` beside it, the two agreeing and describing the raster (file type, CRS, cell size) - the whole-record rule of review round 2 below. Not trusted: an ND DWR LiDAR record (older Download DEM could write ND DWR feet grids labelled metres), another tool's `SOURCE` / `UNITS`, a facts file of another raster. Also stopped, whatever the choice (review round 3): a band with a scale / offset, and a 1 m lidar VRT built before Screening 0.17.50 over a feet or untagged fallback. The rules are `core/dem_zunit.py`, byte-identical in Runner and Screening (`tools/ci_checks.py`). The units note says how ("metres (detected: raster unit 'm')"; it said "metres (chosen)") in the log, `report.json` and the docx.
  - **Feet.** `gdal_io.median_unit_problem` replaces the median check's text ("DEM median 320.2 with units 'feet' is outside the North Dakota ground range (about 250-600 m). Check the elevation unit."): it names the reading that fits and says which setting to choose; a feet reading below 200 m now stops when metres fit (it passed between 100 and 200 m).
  - **Download DEM (Screening 0.17.50).** `write_local_dem` / `warp_to` tag the unit the source records (metres for the ImageServer), never a blind 'm'. The 1 m lidar VRT converts a feet / US survey feet `FALLBACK_DEM` before it goes under the tiles and leaves out one that records no unit (a feet fallback showed through as metres). `build_vrts` decides feet vs metres on the path below the download folder ('/gft' anywhere in the path, e.g. an output folder `D:/gft_dems`, made metre DEM_Imagine tiles feet). A reused USGS 3DEP download from before 0.17.50 is tagged metres; an ND DWR one is left with a warning. The request note for a county or the state says 'W x H km box', a smaller buffer only for a dam, 10 m or a county / box for the state.
  - **Window and guidance.** The Runner's Download a DEM box passes County as the enum's index (the tool dialog opened every county as Adams, also before 3.10.88) and refuses the native 1 m tiles for the state. DEM_Imagine is UTM metres (EPSG:3721 is UTM 14N) and only ASCII_Grids_ft is State Plane feet, in the Runner facts line and wizard, `DEM_Z_HELP`, the Screening help, IMAGINE label, facts file and window (they said 'feet, often EPSG:3721'). Runner README: 'DEM elevation units' describes Detect. Suite check: Screening 0.17.50 is the Runner's recommended minimum.
  - **Tests.** Runner `tests/test_dem_z_download_record.py`, `tests/test_dem_z_trust.py`, `tests/test_dem_z_detect.py`, `tests/realqt_dem_rows.py` (real Processing checks the box's parameters), `test_v320`; Screening `tests/test_dem_units_review.py`, `tests/test_dem_download_units_chunks.py`, `tests/test_lidar_tnm_1m.py` (its fallback records metres).
  - **Review round 2: the unit applied once.** GDAL's default vertical shift converts the values in a warp from or to a compound CRS, so a feet compound-CRS DEM warped to the 2D UTM model CRS came out in metres and `GdalTerrain.read` converted it again (1300 ftUS read as 120.8 m, not 396.2 m), and the 1 m VRT's feet fallback was converted twice (951 ft read as 88.4 m) when its CRS or the tiles' was compound. Every warp of DEM, Manning / NLCD, depth and output values keeps them as stored (`core/warp_opts.no_vshift`, gdalwarp `-novshift`, both plugins), and the US-feet scale VRT keeps all the digits of 1200/3937.
  - **Review round 2: the unit checked.** `gdal_io.resolve_dem_z` checks an explicit Feet / US feet / Meters against the unit the DEM records (a metre download run as Feet passed above 656 m); on a contradiction the DEM's median decides (review round 3, below). The ND ground check samples the whole DEM (`gdal_io.sample_median`; the top-left 200 x 200 cells were nodata on a clipped 1 m DEM and the check was skipped), says in the run report when it finds no valid cell, and stops a feet DEM read as metres at 1100-1200 m too. `z_to_m_factor('auto')` is the run's rule, so the culvert pre-check, the CLI (an ESRI ASCII grid with `auto` stops), the self-test and (round 3) the wizard's DEM estimate read the unit the run reads; the tools with no 'DEM elevation units' to stop on read the recorded unit else assume and warn (round 3, below). The units warning is a structured flag (`z_check`, `workflow.units_warning`). Refused whatever the choice: an ND DWR download from Screening before 0.17.50 in a gft* folder (`dem_zunit.data_problem`; Download DEM records `WRITER` / `written_by` now and will not reuse such a file); refused by every choice (Detect only in round 2): a warped VRT whose vertical shift converted the values. A Download DEM record is trusted only whole (`dem_zunit.download_record`): the in-file metadata and the facts file, with the same source, describing the raster, the raster not rewritten after them - either half alone also describes copies (a `gdal_translate -scale` feet copy keeps the metadata; a file re-exported over the download keeps the facts). Tests: Runner `tests/test_dem_z_once.py`, Screening `tests/test_dem_units_once.py`.
  - **Review round 3: every tool can read the unit, or says how to.** Import HEC-RAS results and `ndbreach_cli hecras-reexport` stopped on a terrain that records no unit (a RAS Mapper terrain in State Plane feet or UTM metres), naming a 'DEM elevation units' they do not have; they read it in the Runner's own record of its HEC-RAS terrain (`hecras_stage.json`; written in the model's project unit), else in its CRS's linear unit as before 3.10.88, with a warning naming the unit and their new 'Terrain elevation units' / `--terrain-z` (`hecras_engine.terrain_z_factor`, `gdal_io.z_factor_or_guess`); the animation hillshade (it assumed metres) and the HEC-RAS export of a pre-3.10.16 run report do the same. An explicit unit stops on a band with a scale or offset (it ran on the raw values) and on a vertical-shift VRT, as Detect does, and Detect's message there names QGIS Translate `-unscale` / Warp `-novshift` instead of a unit choice. An explicit unit the record contradicts is decided by the DEM's median: North Dakota ground only in the chosen unit runs with a warning in the run report; only in the recorded unit stops; unclear stops and names 'Feet / US survey feet / Meters (the raster's unit tag is wrong)', appended to the `DEM_Z` enum after Meters (indices 0-3 unchanged in rundam.json, the wizard and the culvert pre-check; CLI `feet_tag_wrong` ...), which run with the warning. The wizard's DEM estimate uses the run's rule. Run outputs are written in the horizontal part of a compound DEM CRS (a later warp without `-novshift`, e.g. DamSafety Hazard's `reproject_raster`, rescaled them). Screening's reuse warning names the unit by the file's source. Tests: Runner `tests/test_dem_z_round3.py` (also the CLI's explicit check, `-novshift` in the carve and the refill, `z_check` through the terrain wrappers and `run_dam`, the decimated median fallback, the metadata-only gft* refusal, the facts file-type rule, QGIS '|' sources, the exact chosen-unit guidance, `_z_factor` of an old report), `tests/suite_dams/test_dem_estimate.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.88, Screening 0.17.50: 'Detect from the raster' reads the DEM's unit; Download DEM tags metres and explains the 1 m request count (Nick, Absaraka DWR-2801).**
  - **Units.** The Runner's `_Base.gis()` stopped every run with "Set the DEM elevation unit (feet or metres)..." whenever 'DEM elevation units' was 'Detect from the raster' (the default), even for a DEM that records its unit. New `gdal_io.recorded_z_unit` / `resolve_dem_z` read the band unit (m / metre / ft / US survey foot) or a compound vertical CRS; a DEM with no recorded unit still stops, naming the file and the setting ("Set 'DEM elevation units' to Meters or Feet", with which ND / USGS DEMs are which). Screening's `write_local_dem` and `warp_to` now tag band 1 (the unit the source records, see 3.10.89 above): the ImageServer tiles and `BuildVRT` carry none, so the downloaded GeoTIFF had none.
  - **729.** USGS 3DEP (ImageServer) is fetched in 1200 x 1200 px export requests: 1.2 km at 1 m, so the default 10 mi buffer (a 32 km square) is 27 x 27 = 729 requests - not lidar tiles. The log, the tool help and the Runner's Download a DEM box now say so (`imageserver_chunks`, `chunk_note`, `dialog.dem_facts_text`), and the Runner box offers the native source 'USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles (native, TNM)' (Screening SOURCE 2; about 20 10 km lidar tiles for the same box, listed before downloading).

- **Runner 3.10.87: HEC-RAS export, the crest trace finds DWR-2801's embankment, and a dam line input.** Run in real HEC-RAS 6.6, DWR-2801 Absaraka's export fell back to a straight 8,188 ft dam line through the pool. (1) The old rule decides wherever it finds a crest (the highest ridge across the direction to the run's inflow point, at the top of dam on record): its line and breach are used unchanged. (2) Only where it finds none, with the run's flooded area (`Depth (Max).tif`), with the walk's step (the DEM's cell, at least 5 ft, coarser for a very long dam) no coarser than the crest width on record and with `breach_at` one of its options, a line off that rule is tried: the crest at the DEM's level, up to max(5 ft, 25% of H) above the record, when it stands above the ground on both sides there (another vertical datum: DWR-2801's DEM crest is 3-6 ft above its record) - not when the ground at the dam point is within the tolerance of the record, nor with a ridge at the record's level across the section within the toe distance; the dam point's own ridge at the record's level over a higher one that the datum rule would take; else, from a dam point on no ridge, not on the face of another ridge and with no level top within the toe distance, the nearest crest-high ground within the toe distance plus half the crest width and half a step that the ground climbs to from the dam point (`hecras_crest.snap`). (3) Those walks take a side of the ridge test that runs into no data (a pool) as falling, drop crest found past a stretch stepped over at their end that is shorter than that stretch (a knoll on the abutment), keep to the middle of a level top only while it is no wider than about 1.5 crest widths on record (a wider one is a junction, or the abutment when it runs into higher ground: the walk stops), and stop where the heading turns more than 20 degrees from the one it settled on while the crest also goes on straight past the bend (a road crossing it) - DWR-2801's 41-degree bend is followed - and where another crest at their level stands beside them within the toe distance, above the ground on its far side (a road joining or crossing at a small angle, even one abutting the dam), back to where they left its line. (4) The breach of such a line is at its deepest section (the toe also looked for along normals turned up to 30 degrees at a bend), which must be clear: no other place more than the breach's top width away with toe ground within max(1 ft, 4% of H) of the lowest. (5) The line is used only with positive evidence that it is the dam (`hecras_crest.evidence`): the dam point within the toe distance plus half the crest width and half a step of it, the ground climbing from the dam point to it; the run's flooded cells on its downstream side (`flow_check`, with no ridge or other embankment between it and the flood in more than a quarter of its sections); the run's flow at the valley's scale across it - from no dam line, the direction from the dam point to the centroid of all the flooded cells (`valley_axis`) no more than 45 degrees off square to it; near it, the directions from its middle and from the dam point's place on it to the flood (`valley_flow`, within max(1,000 ft, 1.5 line lengths)) and the flow path from its toe at the breach (as long, never cut short by the DEM's edge) each less than 40 degrees off square, the flow path and the flood's direction no more than 40 degrees apart; the pool on its upstream side (water, level as LiDAR flattens it, or no data inside the DEM, along three quarters of it), or else the flood's long axis clear and square to it; it is the only embankment there - no other ridge as high as the line's own ground less the tolerance and the datum allowance, nor another embankment at any level (`_tops`: falling half the reference height - the lower of H and the line's own height - on one side and half the crest prominence on the other within 1.5 toe distances, so a dam that reads far below a road is still seen), across the section through the dam point, at its ends, beside it, or between it and the pool (looked for along its normal and up the valley max(1,500 ft, 1.5 line lengths, past the flood's upstream end) back and as far again, to the pool, on a read of the DEM of their own; a DEM that ends short of both is no evidence), and no crest-high ground behind it, nor a bench at about the crest's level on its upstream side (the dam's crest beside a higher road on its downstream face), in more than a quarter of its sections; where it dips below its level, no higher ground beside it off its own crest and the walk it was made from (a gap in a road beside the dam); and it is not a part of the embankment (at least half the crest length on record, its crest not going on at its level past its ends for longer than itself). No data inside the DEM's footprint and the read counts as a fall (a pool left out of the DEM), no data beyond either does not. Otherwise the straight line with its WARNING, which keeps the old rule's reason and says what was tried (`snap_tried`). (6) Every such line has one WARNING: how it was traced, its distance from the dam point and its angle at the breach to the run's flow at the valley's scale (from the dam point to the centroid of the flooded area, `flow_angle_deg`), and to the flooded area near it (`flow_angle_local_deg`) when the two disagree by more than 15 degrees; traced at the DEM's level, the weir follows the higher of the terrain and the record, and the breach elevations use the record. The cut, the pool outline and the storage check follow the line (the dam point moves to its nearest point on it); on a crest traced at the DEM's level the breach is looked for where the crest is within the tolerance of the record or of that level, or between. (7) `hecrasexport` has an optional `DAM_LINE` line layer (any CRS, new option `dam_line_crs`); the dam point (moved to its nearest point on that line), the pool outline and the storage check then follow it. The site check is unchanged: DWR-2801 still stops on its breach bottom 9 ft below the toe unless overridden. Tests: `tests/test_hecras_crest_offcrest.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.85: tighter HEC-RAS solver and plan settings in the generated model (`core/hecras_solver.py`, new).** Every setting comes from the build option `solver`, else `NDBREACH_HECRAS_<NAME>`, else the default; bad values stop the build naming the variable; the README and `info["solver"]` list the settings, where each came from and what changed. Defaults (before → now): 2D and 1D max iterations 20 → 40 (HEC-RAS accepts at most 40, so the requested 50 cannot be written); Computation Interval = the Courant estimate capped at 3 s (`DT_MODE=cap`; `fixed` = exactly `DT_S`, `courant` = the old uncapped rule); the variable time step block stays on (Use Courant -1, max Courant 1.0 / min 0.45 for SWE, count to double 4, max halving 3, max doubling limited so the step never doubles above the cap; `ADAPTIVE`, `COURANT_MAX`, `COURANT_MIN`, `COUNT_TO_DOUBLE`, `MAX_HALVING`); 1D/2D (storage area) iterations 0 → 3; dam and breach connection rows settable (`CONN_SPACING`, x cell; default auto = 0.5 as before, because 0.35 and 0.4 left cells with more than 8 faces in the grid test builds); `UNET D2 Cores` all (0) → by mesh size: under 10,000 cells 4 (`CORES_SMALL`, 4-6), else the build host's physical performance cores (hyper-threads and E-cores left out; `CORES` sets a number or `all`); 2D initial-condition ramp-up off → 0.5 h with ramp fraction 0.1 → 0.5 (`IC_TIME_HR`, `IC_RAMP_FRAC`); breach progression linear → HEC's sine-wave curve, same formation time (`BREACH_PROGRESSION=linear` for the old table); weir 2.6 and culvert Ke 0.5 / 0.4, exit 1.0 unchanged but now settable; outflow: the 2D area is clipped to the ANUGA run's mesh outline when one is found, with normal-depth outflow lines where the run's flood reaches the edge (`OUTFLOW=single` = the old single boundary line). Tests: `tests/test_hecras_solver.py` (8 tests, the written `.p01` / `.u01` / `.g01` on synthetic data); `hecras_timing_tolerances_default_unchanged_and_tight` follows the new 40-iteration default.

## 2026-10-26

- **Screening 0.17.49: tool 7b sends the DWR inventory max-pool area when no curve folder is given; tool 1's normal-pool area (inv_area_m2) no longer reads max_pool_area (follow-up to #188).** (1) Tool 7b with CURVES empty: AREA_AC = the dam's inventory maximum-pool area (`inv_maxa_m2`, DWR `max_pool_area`, acres), with a per-dam warning; `surface_area_source = inventory_max_pool_area` in `rn_area_src`, the dam.json `surface_area_ac` note, the log tally and the run manifest (`extra.surface_area_source`). No inventory value = no area as before, plus a warning. Dams with a usable tool-3 curve are unchanged. The OLD_TOOL1 warning (layer without `inv_maxa_m2`) now fires with or without curves. With no curve folder (and in #188's shallow-curve fallback) `AREA_AC` is the DWR max-pool area; where tool 3 put the max pool below the DEM crest (`stor_anchor_m` below `est_crest_m`, Screening 0.17.47), the storage sent includes the DEM fill above it, so the Runner's storage exponent can come out somewhat low (a lower routed peak) - give tool 7b the tool-3 curve folder to use the curve's area at the crest. (2) Bug: `core/inventory.match_fields` took the last alias a layer has, and the DWR surface-area aliases were `normal_area, normal_are, max_pool_area, surface_area`, so on the suite GeoPackage (`dwr/DWR_dams.gpkg`: `normal_area` and `max_pool_area`; 2,367 of 10,088 dams have both, different and > 0) `inv_area_m2` held the maximum-pool area. `max_pool_area` is no longer a surface-area alias, and the SA / MA roles match by priority (first alias); the other roles keep the last-match rule. The DWR shapefile export (`normal_are`, `max_pool_a`) was not affected. The chain's step 1 replaces a saved `DWR_SA` / `NID_SA` = `max_pool_area` (`1_fields.json` from 0.17.44-0.17.46); a hand-picked max-pool field in tool 1 is used but warned. Impact: `inv_area_m2` is not sent to the Runner; its only reader is dam.json's null `surface_area_ac` note, which quoted the max-pool value as the normal-pool area. Rerun tool 1 to correct the field. Tests: `tests/test_inv_area_fields.py` (real `DWR_dams.gpkg` column names, read-only when present; shapefile names; chain field map; tool 1), `tests/test_7b_area_fallback_watermask.py` (no-curve runs).
- **Hazard 2.18.64, Runner 3.10.86, Screening 0.17.48, damsuite 1.17.6: the offline pack check never raises; a broken pack blocks where layers are required (#171 follow-up).**
  - **`damsuite/packcheck.py`.** `check_pack` never raises: a `manifest.json` that cannot be read (not UTF-8, not JSON - a half-written file -, not an object, `datasets` / `rasters` / `left_out` not objects, a UTF-8 byte-order mark: the pack readers decode plain UTF-8, so a BOM is named - folder and zip alike - instead of passing the check) is a **FAILED** result (`PackCheck.failed`, summary `FAILED - <why>`), and every required layer counts as missing. Before, a broken `manifest.json` either raised (a list, a section of the wrong type) or made the folder silently "not an offline data pack". One bad entry (not an object, a non-text `file`) is a named `manifest` warning for that layer (an error when the layer is required), not a FAILED result. New: `manifest_problem(path)`, `failed_result`, `sqlite_file_problem` (SQLite header, page size, and a file shorter than its header says: a half-written GeoPackage is "cannot be read"; `integrity=True` adds `PRAGMA quick_check`), `pack_signature(path)` (size + mtime of the manifest and every listed file), and zipped format-2 packs are checked by their listing. `PackCheck.summary()` names a problem of the pack itself (no layer name: a format-1 pack's broken `manifest.json`) by its text instead of an empty `missing: `. `detect_format` / `pack_folder` are unchanged.
  - **Hazard.** An Offline-only run stops with a clear error when the pack's check FAILED, when the pack cannot be opened whatever the check says (`_log_pack_check(opened=False)`: a zip member with a bad CRC, a format-1 zip without `nd_offline_pack.sqlite`, a `format_version` the reader refuses - the check passed on those and the run went on without NSI; the log no longer calls such a pack OK; the log and the error give the reader's reason - `download.pack_reader(errors=[])`, after the check's own when the check FAILED (`_pack_open_why`: a format-2 folder whose `manifest.json` is broken or has a BOM is read as format 1, and the reader only says `nd_offline_pack.sqlite` is not found) - and a path with no pack at all, moved or deleted or an empty folder, is named by the check's own "not an offline data pack" and "cannot be opened", not a check that "found no problem against manifest.json"; the log says what a passing check looked at (`_pack_checked_what`: a format-1 `.sqlite` with no `manifest.json` beside it is not said to be checked against one)), or when no check result could be made (`NSI_structures` is required); online it warns and downloads. The run signature (`_offline_pack_sig`) includes `pack_signature`, so a GeoPackage replaced in place is a new evaluation. Tests: `tests/test_packcheck_malformed.py` (QGIS-free), `tests/test_pack_offline_block.py` (real QGIS).
  - **Runner.** The water-body mask does not use the suite pack's water-body file when the check FAILED or names that layer's file missing or unreadable, and says why in the run log; `pack_gap_notes` / `build_mask` never raise. Test: `report_metrics_suite_water_file_not_used_when_the_pack_check_fails`.
  - **Screening.** The preflight row 'Offline pack vs manifest.json' shows a broken `manifest.json` (also one with a UTF-8 byte-order mark) as WARN / FAILED instead of no row. Test: `tests/test_preflight_packcheck.py`.
  - **Not done.** No content hashes in the signature (size + mtime only: a hash would read gigabytes at every signature); the SQLite `quick_check` is opt-in (`integrity=True`, a full read) - the default catches cut-short and garbage files, not a corrupt page inside a full-length file; a zip is checked by its listing only (the full check runs on the folder Hazard unpacks it to).
- **Screening 0.17.47: each inventory storage at its own elevation (DWR-2865 / DWR-3034 NickReview QA).** Tool 3 with Max storage (the default, kept per the 2026-10-19 decision) used to put the inventory "normal" storage at the lidar water surface and add the DEM fill to the spill or crest on top. NickReview's maxstor field map read DWR `max_pool_vol` into the normal-storage box, so DWR-2865's maximum (167 ac-ft, DWR at 1874 ft) sat at the 1870 ft pool and the run of record got 237.6 ac-ft at the 1875.9 ft spill (DWR normal 141 ac-ft; QA ~198 ac-ft at the 1875.6 ft saddle); DWR-3034 got 97.5 vs `max_pool_vol` 71.6. Now (`core/storage.anchor_inventory_storage`) the maximum sits at the max pool, (DWR `max_pool_elev` - `normal_elev`) above the water surface (a difference, so local datums such as DWR-3034's work), the normal storage at the normal pool, and only the DEM fill above the max pool is added on top. A max pool above the DEM crest puts the maximum at the crest (MAXPOOL-ABOVE-CREST, the fill from the pool scaled to reach it), so the storage at the top of dam (tool 5's Vw, `td_vw_m3`) is never below the inventory maximum, as the old `ensure_top_of_dam` guaranteed; it is set to the maximum, which can be less than the normal storage plus the DEM fill to the crest (the inventory difference replaces that fill, as it does between the two pools). Without a usable normal storage, a DEM fill to the max pool larger than the maximum keeps the DEM curve (FILL-EXCEEDS-MAX: the below-pool estimate at the pool, never 0, which failed tool 5's sunny day; the storage at the top of dam is then above the maximum); with a normal storage below the maximum the DEM fill between the two pools is scaled to fit between them (no flag). A max pool at the normal pool with a larger maximum is MAXPOOL-ELEV-BAD (the fallback). A normal equal to the maximum is flagged NORMAL-EQUALS-MAX and not used at the pool; records without pool elevations (NID-only, or a tool-1 layer older than 0.17.47) keep the old placement at the top of dam, flagged MAXPOOL-ELEV-MISSING / FALLBACK, where only a normal equal to the maximum is dropped below the pool (a normal above the maximum stays at the pool as before, flagged NORMAL-NOT-BELOW-MAX). Tool 7b's stage-storage `AREA_AC` (0.17.46: dV/dz over the top 0.3 m of the curve) changes where tool 3 uses the maximum. With pool elevations and the max pool more than ~0.3 m below the crest (or no usable normal storage) the top of the curve is DEM fill, so `AREA_AC` is about the DEM's area at the crest (before, inflated where the old logic raised the last curve point to the maximum); with a usable normal and the max pool within ~0.3 m of the crest, or above it (MAXPOOL-ABOVE-CREST), the 0.3 m window takes in the scaled segment, so `AREA_AC` is the DEM's area scaled like the fill, by (maximum - normal) / (DEM fill to the max pool or crest), and can be much smaller, also for dams the old logic did not raise. Fallback dams (no usable pool elevations) with NORMAL-EQUALS-MAX (e.g. NID-only records with `NORMAL_STORAGE` = `MAX_STORAGE`) no longer use the normal at the pool, so where the DEM curve tops out below the maximum `ensure_top_of_dam` now raises the last point to it and their `AREA_AC` grows (synthetic curve: 15.8 -> 36.9 ac at normal = maximum = 250 ac-ft, 15.8 -> 87.7 ac at 300); other fallback dams keep their 0.17.46 area. Tool 1 gets normal / max pool elevation boxes and `inv_norm_elev_m` / `inv_max_elev_m`; tool 3 writes `stor_anchor` / `stor_anchor_m`. Read-only `tools/recompute_storage_anchor.py` gives old vs new for an existing work folder. Tests: `tests/test_storage_anchor.py` (also tool 1's output values by field name and tool 7b's area on an anchored curve). No rerun made.
- **Runner 3.10.85: HEC-RAS export, the normal-depth BC line lies just outside the 2D perimeter.** Run in real HEC-RAS 6.6 (Ras.exe -c under Wine with .NET 4.8) the DWR-2801 Absaraka package stopped in the geometry writer: "BC Line 'DS Normal Depth' intersects the perimeter of 2D Flow Area 'Downstream' ... it must be entirely outside the 2D Area Perimeter". The line was the perimeter's own points; it is now drawn a quarter cell (`BC_OFFSET_CELLS`) outside along the outward normal, as in nd_hazard Runner 3.26.4. With it the package passes geometry processing and solves. Test: `tests/test_hecras_bc_offset.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.84, Hazard 2.18.63: HEC-RAS export depth is the cell's max WSE minus the cell's minimum terrain elevation by default again; reverses #173's (Runner 3.10.79) per-pixel default.** Depth = max WSE of the HEC-RAS cell minus that cell's `Cells Minimum Elevation`, for every grid cell inside it (`core/hecras.terrain_depth(basis=)`, new `depth_basis()` / `DEPTH_BASIS_ENV`). Cell-minimum depth is never smaller than per-pixel depth and is usually larger on sloped cells. #173's DWR-2801 table: >=1 ft 5,060 ac cell minimum vs 1,923 ac per pixel (ANUGA 1,960); >=0.1 ft 17,420 vs 8,843 (ANUGA 6,718); wet 18,727 vs 10,657 (ANUGA 8,290). Under per-pixel depth DWR-2801's HIGH homes went from 18 to 0. Per pixel stays selectable: `RunSettings.hecras_depth_basis` or `NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS=terrain` (`cell_min` default; an unknown value is an error), the HEC-RAS import tool's new *Depth basis* option, and `ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport --depth-basis cell_min|terrain`. The wet mask and the ARRIVAL_FT (0.1 ft) arrival use the same depth as the basis written. Recorded: `_hecras/hecras_stage.json` `depth_basis` / `depth_basis_requested` / `depth_basis_text` / `depth_basis_compare` (areas on both bases when a terrain is there), `reexport.json` `areas_by_basis`, `run_report.json` / `.md` *HEC-RAS depth basis*. An unknown basis stops the HEC-RAS stage before the model is built (`hecras_engine.run_stage`, a `HecRasEngineError`; it was found only after the compute, as an uncaught `HecRasError`). **Batch chain:** its Hazard import (`algorithms_chain.QgisOps.run_hazard_import`) passed `TERRAIN` every time, so the chain classified on per-pixel depth while the Runner's rasters said cell minimum; it now uses the basis the HEC-RAS stage used (`import_depth_basis`: the Linux stage's `hecras_stage.json` for its own plan, else `NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS`) and looks for (and needs) a terrain only for `terrain`; for `cell_min` Hazard makes its own cell-minimum depth from the plan, unless the newest current Runner re-export of the plan (or a fresh terrain stage) is on the terrain basis, which Hazard then adopts. The log names the basis and how to get per-pixel depth (`NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS=terrain` before the Runner's HEC-RAS stage runs, or `hecras-reexport --depth-basis terrain`, when the stage's record decides; else the variable, read when the import runs). A resumed chain keeps the import as already done, so a re-export is used only when the import runs again: when `NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS` or `HEC_TERRAIN` changes (both in its resume check); unticking *Skip stages already done* also runs the HEC-RAS stage again, which replaces the results the re-export was made from. A Linux stage that was asked for `terrain` but found no terrain (`depth_basis_requested` `terrain`, `depth_basis` `cell_min`) is named as such in the log, with the re-export and `--terrain`. The stage outputs add `depth_basis` and `hazard_depth_basis` (Hazard's `depth_basis.json`); the import's resume fingerprint includes `NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS` (a resumed chain re-imports and re-classifies once after this update). `reexport.json` records the HDF it read (`results_hdf_stat`). **Hazard 2.18.63** (`rasimport.find_runner_rasters`): without a terrain raster it took the newest re-export that said `terrain`, so an older 3.10.79-3.10.83 terrain re-export won over a newer 3.10.84 `cell_min` one, and a re-export of a previous model run was taken after the model ran again into the same `_hecras/<plan>.hdf`. Now only re-exports of the plan's current results count (`_reexport_fresh`: `results_hdf_stat`, else the HDF not newer than `reexport.json`, 5 s) and the newest decides (`terrain`: used; else no older one; then the stage's rasters when it says `terrain` and they are current, else the cell minimum). `CELL_MIN_WARNING` now says the cell minimum is the Runner's default (conservative) and how to get per-pixel depth (the terrain raster, or `ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport --depth-basis terrain`). `damsuite/versions.py`: the Runner's recommended (soft) minimum of Hazard is 2.18.63 (was 2.11.0), as the chain's cell-minimum Hazard import relies on Hazard choosing the newest current Runner re-export; the hard minimum stays 2.9.1. Tests: `tests/test_hecras_depth_cellmin.py`; `tests/test_hecras_terrain_depth.py` now asks for the terrain basis explicitly; `tests/test_chain_batch.py` (`chain_qgis_ops_hec_wait_and_import` now expects no `TERRAIN` by default); `tests/test_v320.py` (Hazard 2.18.63 is current); Hazard `tests/test_rasimport_terrain_arrival.py`.
- **Screening 0.17.46: tool 7b falls back to the DWR max-pool area; optional Runner water-body mask (#188).** With a curve folder, a dam whose tool-3 stage-storage curve is too shallow (spans less than half the dam height), missing or gives no area now sends the DWR inventory's maximum-pool area as AREA_AC (DWR records max pool at the top of dam) instead of nothing, with a warning; no inventory value = no area, warned. The source (`stage_storage` / `inventory_max_pool_area` / `none`) is in the new field `rn_area_src`, the dam.json note, the log and the run manifest. Tool 1 writes the new field `inv_maxa_m2` (from `max_pool_area` / `max_pool_a`; empty for NID dams); an older tool-1 layer is warned. New optional water-body mask (Settings, step 7b, off by default) sends the Inputs NHD waterbodies to the Runner's `WATER_MASK` (Runner 3.10.27+), report figures only. Test: `tests/test_7b_area_fallback_watermask.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.83: the real-Qt DEM-rows test finds DamSafety_Screening through a symlinked Runner (tests only).** `dem_download_rows_keep_their_height_with_real_qt_offscreen` failed with `AssertionError: 1` when the Runner suite ran from a `nd_breach_runner -> DamSafety_Runner` link outside the repo (or a Runner-only copy): `tests/realqt_dem_rows.py` looked for `DamSafety_Screening` only beside the unresolved path, so the Runner built the "Download a DEM (not available)" stub and the test counted 1 row. It looked QGIS 4 only because QGIS 3.34 skipped the test in the same runs. The test now also looks beside the symlink-resolved plugin folder; with no Screening at all it prints SKIP locally and fails under CI. No plugin code changed.
- **DamSafety_PMP 0.3.1: declared QGIS 4 (Qt6) ready (metadata/compat, plus a fast-gate check).** `metadata.txt` had no `supportsQt6` / `qgisMaximumVersion`, so QGIS 4 treated PMP as QGIS 3 only. It now has `supportsQt6=True` and `qgisMaximumVersion=4.99`, the same as the other DamSafety plugins. No code change was needed: PMP has no Qt imports, and its QGIS enums resolve on PyQt6. Checked on QGIS 4.2.3 (Qt 6.11, GDAL 3.13): the plugin loads, the 6 `ndpmp` algorithms register, the light tests pass, and `pmp`, `inflow_hydrograph`, `spatial_distribution`, `snowmelt` and `export_hms` run on a 38 sq mi test basin with the same outputs as QGIS 3.34 (only file paths differ). `pothole_storage` wasn't run because whitebox_tools isn't on the box. New `tests/test_qt6.py` (in the light tests) checks the metadata and scans for Qt5-only spellings. No CI job runs PMP's tests, so `tools/ci_checks.py` gets a "qt6 metadata" check: every plugin's `metadata.txt` must declare `supportsQt6=True` and a `qgisMaximumVersion` of 4.99 or later, and the fast gate fails if either key is dropped. The ArcGIS PMP tool port is a separate piece of work. No result changes.
- **Hazard 2.18.62: reproduce / data folder - every file of every role is loaded, or a loud warning.** `datafolder.py`: `local_items` (one normaliser for every recorded shape) and `open_config_all` (every file; a missing / unreadable / malformed entry is a note); `open_config` no longer wraps a list twice (one-file roles were skipped silently, multi-file roles opened only the first path). `pipeline.py`: AADT, bridges and the other multi-source roles read every data-folder file; notes are run-log WARNINGs; the record lists traffic as read. `reproduce.py` / `ensemble.py`: `local_data` is `[[path, category], ...]` for old and new packages; Input rows `Loaded` / `Not loaded` in the check. Records carry `"inputs_format": 2` (`reproduce.INPUTS_FORMAT`, both forks; `_used_inputs` writes traffic, NBI / OSM crossings and data-folder / agency / OSM lifelines in the order the run reads them); a record of either fork without it (not a version cutoff: an nd 2.34.4 record is old too) is put in run order from the whole record (`_old_inputs_as_run`), so a newest HPMS year that is gone is missing, not replaced by an older one. `reviewpkg` C13 names `not_loaded`. Tests: `tests/test_reproduce_local_data_shapes.py` (real QGIS, now also `_collect_inputs` / `_collect_facilities` / `_used_inputs` / `ensemble.dam_settings`), `tests/test_reproduce_inputs_as_run.py` (QGIS-free).
- **Runner 3.10.82: HEC-RAS line bands pass the 8-face check where bands overlap or cross (#170).** With the default bands (3 rows x 20 ft each side of road, rail, structure and breakline lines), every band point was protected from `fix_faces`, so where the bands of nearby streets overlapped (DWR-2801, Absaraka) a protected point among only protected neighbours kept more than 8 faces and the HEC-RAS build stopped (4 cells at 3 x 20 ft, 1 at 2 x 50 ft). Now: band seeds closer than 0.7 x the band cell (was 0.5) to a kept one are dropped, inner rows first, so crossing and close parallel bands leave no near-duplicate seeds (`core/hecras_bands.py`); only the first row each side (the points whose shared faces lie on the line) is protected, and only where no other banded line is within 1.5 rows, so the outer and graded rows and the first rows where bands meet may be moved or dropped; `fix_faces` drops the neighbour across an over-faced cell's shortest face rather than the cell itself, and splits a big coarse cell ringed by small ones (`core/hecras_breaklines.py`); and the Voronoi pre-check, `fix_faces` and `hecras_model.mesh_check` run qhull on centred coordinates (`centred_voronoi`), because at state-plane feet its round-off decides near-cocircular faces of thousandths of a foot (the DWR-2801 build passed 8 faces while its written `.g01` had a 9-face cell). The `fix_faces` changes apply to every HEC-RAS build, with or without bands: fewer points removed, points added where a cell is split, and the build note now counts the added points. DWR-2801 dry build at the defaults (3 x 20 ft, all classes, gmsh seeds): 727,255 cells, 0 over 8 faces, 0 face points over the limit; the written `.g01` re-read on its own: 0 cells over 8 faces, smallest seed spacing 14.0 ft, smallest cell 256 sq ft (the structure-only workaround had 522,432 cells). Tests: `hecras_bands_dense_crossing_streets_pass_the_8_face_check`, `hecras_bands_crossing_and_close_parallel_lines_no_near_duplicates_and_8_faces`, `hecras_centred_voronoi_is_the_diagram_at_the_origin`, `hecras_voronoi_precheck_is_stable_at_state_plane_coordinates` (a stability check), `hecras_breaklines_fix_faces_splits_a_big_cell_ringed_by_small_ones`, `hecras_breaklines_fix_faces_drops_the_neighbour_across_the_shortest_face`.
- **Runner 3.10.81: the self-test tests' `no_models()` also hides the remaining model-discovery settings (tests only).** Hardening on top of #180, which already hid `NDBREACH_LISFLOOD` / `NDBREACH_ANUGA_PYTHON` from these tests (`selftest_algorithm_runs_and_writes_the_report` passes with them set since #180; this release does not change that). `tests/test_selftest.no_models()` now also clears `NDBREACH_GMSH_PYTHON` and `NDBREACH_HECRAS_WINEPREFIX` / `_WINDOWS_DIR` / `_LINUX_DIR`; makes the suite store's `lisflood_exe` / `anuga_python` (project override, QgsSettings `ndsuite/<key>`) and the provider's Processing settings `NDBREACH_LISFLOOD_EXE` / `NDBREACH_ANUGA_PYTHON` return no model; makes `shutil.which` miss lisflood / Ras / RasUnsteady; and stubs `core.nd_data`'s candidate lists (home folders, `CONDA_PREFIX`, `conda env list`) besides the detectors. Everything is restored afterwards, also when the block raises. Test `no_models_clears_and_restores_the_model_environment` sets each of these (fake executables on the PATH) and fails against #180's helper. No plugin code changed.

## 2026-10-25

- **Runner 3.10.80: LISFLOOD-FP gauge discharge, the gauge section is clipped to the grid.** `core/lisflood.write_inputs` gave each virtual gauge a section as long as the domain's longest side, across the narrow side, so it ran off the grid. LISFLOOD-FP's own clip compares an E/W section with the column count and a N/S section with the row count (swapped), so it read past the Qx/Qy arrays and the discharge was garbage: 1.8e297 m3/s in the first `model-tests` CI run (#180, LISFLOOD-FP 8.3), a huge negative value clipped to 0 locally with 2 threads, plausible-looking with the 8.1 build. The section is now clipped to the grid, ending one cell short of the far edge (`_gauge_line`). Gauge discharges from earlier runs may be wrong. Test: `run_tests.gauge_section_is_clipped_to_the_grid`; `gauge_records_the_flood_wave` now steady (204.6 m3/s, 3 of 3 runs).
- **Hazard 2.18.61: HEC-RAS plan import - arrival on the same depth basis as the depth (follows Runner 3.10.79, #173).** `rasimport.py`: arrival per pixel from WSE minus the terrain; without a terrain raster the Runner's terrain re-export / terrain-basis rasters of the plan, else the cell minimum with a warning. Basis (`terrain` / `runner_reexport` / `cell_min`) in `depth_basis.json`, the plan cache manifest and the run log; REG_05.2026 workbook unchanged. Test: `tests/test_rasimport_terrain_arrival.py`. *(Prose: Docs & QA.)*
- **Docs: ND_Dam_Suite_Pack has its NHD waterbody layer back (2026-10-24, no plugin change).** `hydro/NHD_waterbodies.gpkg`, which was found missing from the shared pack on 2026-10-24, was restored with Nick's approval. It was rebuilt from the USGS ND state NHD GeoPackage (NHDWaterbody, 2023-12-27) with the pack's own `gpkgpack.write_layer`. It matches the unchanged `manifest.json` entry: 369,435 features; fields gnis_name, ftype, fcode, areasqkm; MULTIPOLYGON; EPSG:4326; same bbox. The only difference is the file size, about 32 KB larger than the manifest's `file_size` (290,148,352 vs 290,115,584 bytes). Docs that said the pack has no waterbody layer are corrected (USER_GUIDE water-body mask, `docs/Run_report_metrics.md`, `docs/Reservoir_carve_from_storage.md`, the #92 entry below). Behaviour to know: the Runner's report-only water-body mask looks for `hydro/NHD_waterbodies.gpkg` in the suite pack when no 3DHP waterbody file is there, so runs that use this pack now get masked figures (`*_masked`, `Water Body Mask.geojson`) by themselves, next to the unchanged unmasked ones. Set `WATER_MASK=off` to keep the old output. The reservoir carve is unchanged: its run hook passes no lake polygon, so the DEM still sets the footprint.
- **Docs: why HEC-RAS results before Runner 3.10.79 were too deep, and how to re-export them (for #173; merge after #173, no plugin change).** New USER_GUIDE section "HEC-RAS results made before Runner 3.10.79 are too deep and too wide: re-export them". It explains in plain language that the old export gave every 30 m pixel of a HEC-RAS cell the depth at the cell's lowest point (a ditch or channel), so depth, the flooded area, the wet structures and the arrival times came out too high. It gives the DWR-2801 example (area at >=1 ft 5,060 -> 1,923 ac, against ANUGA's 1,960 ac; HEC-RAS's 2 lethal and 16 judgment structures came from the old export). It also covers how to read `depth_basis` in `_hecras/hecras_stage.json` (`terrain`, `cell_min`, or missing before 3.10.79), how to re-export with `ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport DAM_FOLDER` or `hecras_engine.reexport_hecras_results(run_dir)` and what each option does, that Hazard should then be rerun on the re-exported rasters, the import tool's new Terrain input, and what is not changed (the installed nd Runner 3.21.0, and Hazard's own `rasimport.py` arrival).
- **Runner 3.10.79: HEC-RAS depth from the terrain under each 30 m grid cell, not the HEC-RAS cell's minimum; re-export of a finished run (DWR-2801 NickReview QA).** The HEC-RAS export took depth = max WSE minus `Cells Minimum Elevation`, so a 150 ft cell took its lowest ditch's depth across its whole area, and the wet mask and arrival used that depth too (`REVIEW_HECRAS.md`, `HEC-DEPTH-CELLMIN`, Critical). Now depth = the nearest cell's max WSE minus the run's terrain averaged onto the output grid, clipped at 0; wet mask, WSE/velocity/time of max and the 0.1 ft arrival (per grid cell, from the cell's WSE series minus that grid cell's terrain; time of the max when it is reached only between output steps) use that depth (`core/hecras.terrain_depth`, `core/hecras_engine.results(terrain=)`). New `hecras_engine.reexport_hecras_results(run_dir)` / `ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport DAM_FOLDER [--out DIR]`: re-export from the existing plan HDF + terrain without rerunning, into `_hecras/reexport_<UTC>` by default, never overwriting unless `--overwrite`. DWR-2801 re-exported (common domain): >=1 ft 5,060 -> 1,923 ac (ANUGA 1,960); >=0.1 ft 17,420 -> 8,843 ac (ANUGA 6,718); wet 18,727 -> 10,657 ac. The installed nd Runner 3.21.0 has the same line (archived `nd_breach_runner`, branch `archive/nd-plugins`); not changed here.
- **Runner 3.10.78: Windows HEC-RAS launcher correctness (two bugs from the #167 review; NOT TESTED ON WINDOWS YET).** `core/hecras_windows.py`. (1) `run_headless` no longer throws away this launch's results when a stale file is in the way: a `<name>.p##.hdf` left by an earlier run used to make the promotion guard delete the fresh, verified `RasUnsteady.exe` output (`.tmp.hdf`), so a re-run over an old result reported "no results" even though RasUnsteady had just succeeded. The fresh verified tmp now always replaces the canonical file (`os.replace` advances the mtime, so the freshness gate still accepts it). Regression test `tests/test_hecras_windows.py::hecras_windows_stale_results_overwritten_by_fresh_rasunsteady`. (2) `run_com` with a timeout runs the blocking `Compute_CurrentPlan` in a watchdog thread; that thread now `CoInitialize`s and receives the controller through `CoMarshalInterThreadInterfaceInStream` rather than calling the main thread's apartment object directly, which fails with `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` on Windows (so the timeout path broke whenever `NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S` was set). `pythoncom` is absent off Windows and under the test mocks, where the marshalling stream stays `None` and the worker calls the object directly as before. Windows verification still pending.
- **Hazard 2.18.60, Runner 3.10.77, Screening 0.17.45, damsuite 1.17.5: format-2 offline packs in Hazard, and every pack checked against its manifest.json (#171; merge after #171).**
  - **Hazard reads format-2 packs.** The Offline pack box now takes a folder of GeoPackages with `manifest.json` (for example nd_Pack_20261014 or ND_Dam_Suite_Pack) as well as a format-1 `nd_offline_pack.sqlite`, `.zip` or unpacked folder. "Browse..." accepts `manifest.json` and puts its folder in the box. Layers are read under the same names as format 1, and `NHD_flowlines` / `NHD_waterbodies` also take a newer pack's `3DHP_*` layers. Before this, DWR Hazard read format 1 only, and a format-2 folder worked only as the "Data folder" (which still works).
  - **Suite folder shortcut.** Choosing a suite data folder that holds a format-2 pack also fills the Offline pack box, but only when the box is empty. *(Pending decision: Nick to confirm this behaviour.)*
  - **Pack check (`damsuite/packcheck.py`, read-only).** Each layer in `manifest.json` is checked for its file and table, feature count, fields and types, geometry type and CRS. Vectors are expected in EPSG:4326 because the manifest has no CRS key. Format-1 packs get a lighter table and row-count check. Before this, a layer whose file was gone was skipped without a word (October 2026: `NHD_waterbodies.gpkg`).
  - **Where the check shows.** Hazard: a quick check when a pack is chosen (QGIS log, message bar, `CHECK:` on the Status line), a full check in the run log, and the Check inputs row. Screening: a new preflight row, "Offline pack vs manifest.json". Runner: when no water-body file is used and the suite pack's manifest lists a water-body layer that is missing or unreadable, the run log says why the mask is missing. The suite's per-dam data step also logs the check.
  - **Required layer.** Only `NSI_structures` is required. With "Offline only" ticked, a run stops with a clear error if it's missing; online, the run warns and downloads it. *(Pending decisions: more required layers, such as `USA_Structures` or `NDGISHUB_address_points`; and whether a missing required layer should also stop an online run.)*
  - **Not changed.** Hazard's automatic pack search still doesn't find format-2 folders, so nobody is switched to a different pack silently. *(Pending decision: auto-discovery of format-2 folders.)*
  - An updated format-2 pack (a changed `manifest.json`) now counts as a new Hazard evaluation.
  - SWE's check of the real packs: ND_Dam_Suite_Pack (format 2, 32 layers), nd_Pack_20261014 (format 2, 34) and DamSafety_Pack_20261014 (format 1, 21) all OK.
  - Docs: USER_GUIDE "Offline pack formats and the pack check"; the Hazard README's offline section now covers format 2.
- **Runner 3.10.76: HEC-RAS bands of small cells along roads, rail, structures and breaklines; optional ANUGA line band (Nick: "3 rows of 20 ft grid alongside both sides").** New `core/hecras_bands.py`. `build_model` puts rows of `band_cell_ft` (20 ft) cells, `band_rows` (3) each side, with cell faces on the line, along the run's road centrelines (`road_lines`), rail (`run_report.json` `rail_lines_xy`, new), the culvert / bridge / weir connection lines and extra breakline files (`band_lines`); the flow path is class `channel` (off by default). Beyond the band the spacing grows at `band_grading` (1.5, at most 2:1) to the 2D cell. Crossings: the higher-rank line (structure > road > rail > breakline > channel) keeps its rows; parallel lines closer than the band share one set of rows; pieces stop short of the perimeter, the dam line and the breach cut (their rows are unchanged). By default only in the run's wet area + 500 ft (`band_extent` `wet` | `all`). Each banded line is a `.g` BreakLine (`Band road 1` ..., CellSize Min = the band cell, Near Repeats = rows); a banded structure keeps its Connection line and its 3.10.75 face check. Options / variables: `line_bands` / `NDBREACH_BAND` (`on` default, `off` = the 3.10.75 geometry, byte-identical but for timestamps), `band_cell_ft` / `NDBREACH_BAND_CELL_FT`, `band_rows` / `NDBREACH_BAND_ROWS`, `band_grading` / `NDBREACH_BAND_GRADING`, `band_classes` / `NDBREACH_BAND_CLASSES`, `band_extent` / `NDBREACH_BAND_EXTENT`, `band_wet_pad_ft` / `NDBREACH_BAND_WET_PAD_FT`, `band_lines` / `NDBREACH_BAND_LINES` (`;;`-separated). The 2 rows x 50 ft option: `NDBREACH_BAND_CELL_FT=50 NDBREACH_BAND_ROWS=2`. Cost (analytic, real lines): DWR-2801 (421,317 cells) default wet roads + rail + structures 3x20 +52 % (643k cells), 2x50 +12 % (472k); Absaraka (65,478) +121 % / +28 %. ANUGA: `NDBREACH_ANUGA_BAND=on` (gmsh meshes only; `NDBREACH_ANUGA_BAND_CELL_M` 6.1, `NDBREACH_ANUGA_BAND_DIST_M` 18.3) adds gmsh `size_lines` along the embankment lines, breaklines and culverts; **off by default** (DWR-2801: about +184k triangles, +58 %, smallest inradius ~3x smaller, run time ~x2.4). The gmsh quality gate follows the smallest size line (`gmsh_mesher.gate_area`). Tests: `tests/test_hecras_line_bands.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.75: HEC-RAS RasUnsteady stack limit, structure connection lines on cell faces, connection names without '/' (DWR-2801 NickReview).** Three bugs that stopped the DWR-2801 HEC-RAS run (Runner 3.10.68). (1) `core/hecras_engine.compute()` raises RasUnsteady's `RLIMIT_STACK` (soft to unlimited, else to the hard limit) in the child before exec (`preexec_fn`, so through `nice`, a wrapper or Wine); before, the Linux RasUnsteady died with exit 174, SIGSEGV in `GridGeometry.f90:151` ("Reading 2D Area(s)") under the default 8 MB stack. The log line `HEC-RAS compute: stack limit for RasUnsteady ...` and `run_report.json` `hecras_time_limit.stack_limit` record it; no change on Windows. With the stack unlimited DWR-2801 then died again (forrtl 174) at PROGRESS 0.50 in the Intel OpenMP worker threads (4 MB default stacks, not covered by `ulimit -s`; by hand `KMP_STACKSIZE=OMP_STACKSIZE=1g` stepped on): `KMP_STACKSIZE` and `OMP_STACKSIZE` are now `1g` in RasUnsteady's env, in `wine_env` (Ras.exe -c and the RasUnsteady.exe it starts) and in the Windows launcher's child env; a value already set is kept, `NDBREACH_HECRAS_OMP_STACKSIZE` sets another default (`off` = unset); logged as `HEC-RAS compute: OpenMP thread stacks for RasUnsteady ...` and in `run_report.json` `hecras_time_limit.omp_stacksize`. (2) `core/hecras_structures.connection_breaklines`: point pairs along each connection line at most half its length apart (`structure_spacing`, floor max(20 ft, 0.2 cell)) and one spacing past both ends, so a short line gets 2+ faces with facepoints at its ends; a line shorter than the cell used to get ONE pair, and HEC-RAS turned that face off the line ("The SA 2D Connection at OSM way_254643 2 has an error with the connection. Check that the From: is set correctly."). The gmsh seeds are graded to that spacing along the short lines (`core/hecras_seeds.spec_for(structures=)`, new `core/gmsh_mesher` spec key `size_lines`, consistent with #160's line sizing; ANUGA meshes unchanged). New check `aligned_share` / `alignment` (Voronoi faces with HEC-RAS's merge of faces shorter than 0.1 spacing, 0.5 ft tolerance): under 90 % of a line on faces is a warning (log, STRUCTURES_README), under 1 % stops the build (`structure_align_check` = `warn` to write it anyway; warn-only with `structure_breaklines` off). Checked against the DWR-2801 HEC-RAS geometry: mean error 0.017 against the share measured on HEC-RAS's own faces (126 ft 'OSM way/254643 2': 0.100 vs 0.102); the old `share_on_faces` read 1.0 for every line. (3) Connection names: characters HEC-RAS refuses ('/', ',', '=' ...) become '_', at most 16 characters, unique without regard to case (' 2', ' 3'), one name in geometry, plan and report, and STRUCTURES_README maps each renamed connection to its original OSM / NBI name; `connection_problems` rejects a name HEC-RAS would refuse. Tests: `tests/test_hecras_stack_limit.py`, `tests/test_hecras_2801_structures.py`. A live geometry built before 3.10.75 has to be rebuilt to get (2) and (3).

## 2026-10-24
- **Runner 3.10.73: read-only ANUGA slowdown checker, a host freeze or a real time-step collapse (DWR-2801 NickReview slowdown).** New `core/anuga_slowcheck.py`, standard library only (any python3, no ANUGA environment or QGIS): `python3 DamSafety_Runner/core/anuga_slowcheck.py RUN_FOLDER [--pid N] [--window N|Nm] [--state FILE] [--sample-s S] [--json]` (also `python3 -m nd_breach_runner.core.anuga_slowcheck`). It reads `anuga.log` (the 3.10.70 `utc= steps= dsteps= wall_s= rate=` fields when there), the checkpoint names and times, `anuga_partial.npz`, `timestep_diag.csv` / `timestep_diag_summary.txt`, `/proc/uptime` and `/proc/<pid>/stat`, and prints one line `VERDICT=... confidence=... key=value ...` (`--json`: the whole dict). A freeze shows as wall time without uptime: wall seconds since the worker started (`anuga_job.npz`) minus its uptime age = seconds frozen, from one call; with `--state`, the frozen seconds between calls and the CPU seconds per model second now vs the run's (freeze-proof). 3.10.70 logs: a yield with a long `wall_s` and a normal `dsteps` was frozen, a recent `dsteps` >= 2x the window median is a collapse. Exit codes 0 normal, 1 finished, 3 insufficient_data, 4 host_freeze, 5 timestep_collapse, 6 stalled_unknown, 7 not_running (2 bad arguments). Never writes in the run folder or signals a process. Tests: `tests/test_anuga_slowcheck.py` (synthetic logs and a fake /proc, no model runs).
- **Runner 3.10.72: culvert pre-check before a run.** NickReview queue: before each dam's ANUGA run, list the culvert ends whose invert will be changed by the 3.10.71 rules or that have no fine-grid ground. New `core/culvert_precheck.py` and `python ndbreach_cli.py culvert-precheck` (GDAL/OGR + numpy, no QGIS, no model; only window reads around each end; refuses `--out` = the input folder). Reads the structures layer with the loader's field synonyms and rules (feet, kind, pipe/box, 4 ft defaults), applies `structures.reconcile_inverts` (3 ft, 3 m cells, as `workflow.run_dam`) then `anuga_culverts` + `fine_ground_inverts` against the fine DEM, so the report uses the run's own decisions. Per end: given invert (ft, m), lowest fine ground within max(apron, 5 m), delta, flag `HIGH` (given > ground + 0.3 m: the run caps it), `FALLBACK` (no fine data: the run uses the 30 m model DEM) or `SKIPPED` (shorter than 2 × width, or outside `--mesh-polygon`), the Runner's invert and source, the suggested invert (lowest fine ground, not surveyed), the reconcile note, and which source of a composite VRT carries the end (`on_top_source` = native 1 m or the fill). Dam-folder mode reads rundam.json (DEM, DEM_Z, STRUCTURES|layername, CELL, DEM_RESAMPLE, S_INVERT_TOL_FT). DWR-2801 (282 features, 206 culverts, 29 with NDDOT inverts): 7 HIGH ends on 4 culverts (0.71-2.19 m above the ground), 0 FALLBACK, 184 of 206 culverts on the 5 m fill outside the native 1 m box; about 3 s. Docs: USER_GUIDE "Culvert pre-check". Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_culvert_precheck.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.71: ANUGA culvert inverts on the fine ground ANUGA uses; enquiry points past the inlet triangles.** DWR-2801 review (bridge NBI 9122221): with `ANUGA_ELEVATION` fine the mesh bed is the 1-2 m terrain, but a culvert end with no invert of its own was read bilinearly from the 30 m model DEM, which smears the road embankment into the end cells: the run's 68 culvert ends sat a median 0.69 m above the fine ground (47 > 0.5 m, 23 > 1 m, max 2.30 m; 9122221 1.58 / 1.90 m). And with ANUGA's default `enquiry_gap` 0 every end's enquiry point fell inside its own inlet triangles. `structures.fine_ground_inverts` (called by `anuga_model.run` after `prepare_elevation`, fine bed only): a missing invert = the lowest fine ground within max(apron, 5 m) of the end (`fine_grid`); no fine ground there = the model DEM value as before (`model_dem`, noted); a given invert (structure layer / inventory after `reconcile_inverts`) is kept unless it is more than 0.3 m above that ground, then it is lowered to ground + 0.3 m (`given_capped`) with a WARNING naming both values; a given invert below the ground is never raised. Each job culvert gets `enquiry_gap` = 1.3 × the equilateral edge of `culvert_area_m2` (gmsh's culvert-end size; 9.88 m at 25 m²), raised by the worker (`local_enquiry_gap` / `inlet_clear_gap`) to 0.5 m past the furthest vertex of any triangle over an end's apron rectangle where the actual triangles are larger (seen on a tiny Runner run: a road breakline through the merged culvert region left 89 m² triangles at one end, so 9.88 m still landed in an inlet triangle; 11.23 m clears it), and passed (`anuga_worker.add_boyd_culvert`) only when the installed Boyd operator has the keyword (ANUGA 4.0.1 has) and both enquiry points are inside the mesh; otherwise ANUGA's default is used, with a note. Recorded: `run_report.json["anuga_culvert_inverts"]` (inverts, source per end, fine ground, old model-DEM value, search radius, gap, enquiry point in an inlet triangle), summary `anuga_culverts`, manifest `extra.anuga_culverts`. HEC-RAS packages read the same job inverts (`hecras_structures`). Model-grid bed: inverts unchanged. Synthetic before/after (200 × 100 m, 489 triangles, a 3 m embankment, 30 m 2.0 × 1.5 m box, 1.5 m pond, 600 s, DE0): inverts 100.37 / 100.83 → 99.82 / 99.76 m (ground 99.83 / 99.77); enquiry triangle in the inlet 2 of 2 ends → 0 of 2; peak Q 4.36 → 7.64 m³/s; downstream volume at 600 s 2,597 → 4,157 m³; max velocity 2.78 → 3.35 m/s; steps 2,625 → 2,578; volume conserved in both. Docs: USER_GUIDE "ANUGA culvert inverts and enquiry points". Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_anuga_culvert_inverts.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.70: ANUGA time-step diagnostic (opt-in) and the wall clock on every ANUGA progress line (DWR-2801 NickReview slowdown).** New `core/anuga_dtdiag.py` (numpy only). Off by default; on with the run setting `anuga_dt_diag` (N yield steps between rows; `on` = 1) or `NDBREACH_ANUGA_DT_DIAG=1` (+ `NDBREACH_ANUGA_DT_DIAG_EVERY=N`, default 1; `NDBREACH_ANUGA_DT_DIAG=0` wins). The worker writes a row of `_breach_model/anuga/timestep_diag.csv` every N yield steps: UTC, model time, wall seconds and model s per wall s, internal steps (since the last row and cumulative), dt mean / min / max over the interval (`recorded_min_timestep` / `recorded_max_timestep`), the last `domain.timestep` and its CFL dt, the limiting triangle of the last internal step (argmin `radii / max_speed`; the wet state when every speed is 0) with its centroid in the model CRS and lon/lat, area, inradius, depth, speed and wave speed, and the nearest breach inlet or culvert when it is within max(3 inradii, 50 m). At the end `timestep_diag_summary.txt`, the log and `run_report.json["anuga_dt_diag"]` rank the limiting triangles by share of rows, internal steps and wall time.
