Read-only on the domain (results bit-identical on and off, tested on real ANUGA), not in the checkpoint fingerprint, and a resumed run truncates rows after the checkpoint's time and appends. Every ANUGA `PROGRESS` line now also appends `utc=… steps=… dsteps=… wall_s=… rate=…` (always on), so a host freeze (a long wall gap with a normal step count) can be told from a real slowdown (many small steps); the start of the line, which the progress bar parses, is unchanged. Docs: USER_GUIDE "ANUGA time-step diagnostic". Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_anuga_dt_diag.py`.
- **Screening 0.17.43: tool 7b's dam.json gets surface_area_ac and dam_length_ft (or a null with the reason).** `core/damrecord_io.py`: `dam_length_ft` is the mapped dam line's length (tool 4), else the inventory's dam length (DWR dam_length / NID DAM_LENGTH), the order tool 5 uses; it was never written, so the Runner fell back to its 400 ft crest length. Reruns of existing folders now use the recorded length for the crest-raise strip, the PMF overtopping weir and the HEC-RAS dam connection, so results can change. `surface_area_ac` stays the top-of-dam area tool 7b sends (tool-3 curve); without one it is an explicit null whose source note gives the reason and the inventory's normal-pool area, which is not written (the Runner would take it as AREA_AC). A null never replaces another tool's value. Existing dam.json files are filled on the next tool 7b run. Tests: `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_handoff_record_fields.py`, `test_suite09.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.69: ANUGA gmsh far-field sizing (opt-in; defaults unchanged).** `core/gmsh_mesher.py` reads three new spec keys, each also an environment variable read into the run settings (`anuga_gmsh`; a set variable wins): `grade_dist_m` / `NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_GRADE_DIST_M` (the Threshold fields' DistMax: D from lines, 2 × D from the channel, 0.6 × D from culvert ends; an explicit `*_dist_max_m` still wins), `line_min_area_m2` / `NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_LINE_MIN_AREA_M2` (the line size and the quality gate's base; culvert ends keep `culvert_area_m2`) and `refine_footprint` / `NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_REFINE_FOOTPRINT` (`wet`: lines and the channel are cut to the LISFLOOD-FP wet area plus `NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_REFINE_PAD_M`, default 200 m; culvert points kept), plus `NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_MAX_AREA_M2` for the existing `max_area_m2`. With none set the spec, every gmsh call and the stats are as before: `tests/test_gmsh_sizing.py` pins gmsh_mesh_in.json and the recorded gmsh calls (size fields included) to main ac697d7, so edge-fix rerun 2, which reuses run 1's mesh settings, gets the same mesh. Bad values stop the gmsh mesh before gmsh runs with the setting's and the variable's names (`anuga_model.check_gmsh_options`, `gmsh_mesher.resolve_sizing`). The values used go into the mesh stats (`sizing`), `run_report.json["anuga_mesh"]["sizing"]`, the mesh note and the log. Benchmark estimates (not yet checked for accuracy; DWR-2851 follows): grading 500 m about 1.45×; lines 200 m² alone −31 % triangles; wet-area buffer 1,000 m + lines 200 m² + grading 500 m about 2.3× (−56 % triangles). On the test's 6 km synthetic square: 46,575 triangles by default, 30,380 with grading 500 m, 32,883 with lines 200 m², 21,232 with both. Docs: USER_GUIDE "ANUGA gmsh far-field sizing". Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_gmsh_sizing.py`.

## 2026-10-22
- **Runner 3.10.68: Windows HEC-RAS 6.6 launcher for the batch chain (NOT TESTED ON WINDOWS YET).** `core/hecras_windows.py`: on Windows the chain's HEC-RAS 'Run' tries `Ras.exe -c` / `RasUnsteady.exe` headless, then the `RAS66.HECRASController` COM object (pywin32), and falls back to 'wait for a manual run' (RasMapper terrain) when the terrain is missing or the launcher fails. Mock tests only (`test_hecras_windows.py`).
- **Runner 3.10.67 / Hazard 2.18.59: Batch chain, a list of dams through the whole suite dam by dam.** New Runner 'Batch chain' window (the Runner window's Dam tab) and `ndbreach:chainbatch`: for each dam, Screening (that dam only) -> Runner -> HEC-RAS 2D build -> HEC-RAS run (Run / Wait for a manual run / Skip, per batch and per dam) -> Hazard import -> Hazard classify (ND + TM 11), then the next dam; a failed dam never stops the list. One suite settings file (a section per stage plus `ndbreach|chainbatch`), an overrides CSV (dam_id + the differing columns), a preflight, resume / stop after / keep going / cancel, per-dam `_chain/chain_settings_used.json` (values, versions, checksums), `Chain_Batch_Summary.csv` and `chain_manifest.json`. Culverts and bridges: the Runner's inventory, or an existing ANUGA run's structures (ANUGA is never launched). Hazard: new `ndhazard:savedamstate` saves each dam's Hazard window state as `<dam folder>/Hazard_<ID>.qgz` to reopen (Project > Open, open the Hazard window, Add scenario / Add from a folder…, Classify). Other plugins are called by algorithm ID; a missing one is a clear preflight error.
- **Runner 3.10.66 / Hazard 2.18.58: the batch runs HEC-RAS 2D; the 2D flow area as the Hazard batch's model boundary.** `ndbreach:batch` (and the ndbreach CLI) with Models = 'LISFLOOD-FP then HEC-RAS 2D' silently gave LISFLOOD-FP results only; the HEC-RAS 2D stage is now shared (`core/workflow.run_hecras_stage`) by the one-dam tool, the batch and the CLI, a failed HEC-RAS run is a 'failed' row (the batch goes on) and 'skip finished dams' needs a finished `_hecras/hecras_stage.json` (with `results_hdf`; a timed-out stage's record, status 'timeout', does not count). Hazard: HEC-RAS plan import writes `model_boundary_2d_area.gpkg` (the 2D flow area perimeters; `ndhazard:hecrasplan` output BOUNDARY), and `ndhazard:batch` has a 'Model boundary' option that uses each dam folder's copy for the model edge check (setting `model_boundary_file`; default unchanged). Numbered after #155 (Runner 3.10.65).
- **Runner 3.10.65: HEC-RAS culvert / bridge connections stay 2 cells inside the 2D area; adverse-culvert note; `exclude=` (follow-up to #146, Absaraka KO-01).** Absaraka chain abs2801v2egrcv2 (routing type 1 and `Use RC Family` 0 everywhere, the #146 fix) still stopped at KO-01: "The SA 2D Connection at BNSF KO KO-01 cu has an error with the connection. Check that the \"To:\" is set correctly". Docs & QA found KO-01's connection line had one end 114 ft (about one 100 ft cell) inside the 2D perimeter; every other one was >= 466 ft. `hecras_model.structure_connection` now keeps both ends of every connection line `STRUCT_EDGE_CELLS` = 2 cells inside the perimeter (was 1 cell), and `hecras_structures.connection_problems(..., cell=)` (called by `build_model` and `add_to_package`) stops the build when an end is closer than that ("KO-01: the last end of its line is 114 from the edge of the 2D area (needs 2 cells, 200)"). A culvert whose outlet invert is 0.5 ft or more above its inlet keeps its inverts with an "adverse culvert" note in README_HECRAS.md (KO-01: 953.57 -> 955.31 ft are ANUGA's DEM inverts; the north side is upstream by the terrain and the dam's flood path, and the south end falls on the embankment slope). `add_to_package(..., exclude=[names])` leaves named structures out, for diagnostic packages; an unknown name stops with no folder left behind. A field-by-field check against HEC-RAS 6.6's examples (the BaldEagleDamBrk internal levees g01/g03/g13; culverts in Diamond, OrleansEx and Beaver) and the working Dam connection found no format difference. The internal block matches BaldEagleDamBrk g03's levees, and no HEC example has a culvert on a 2D-to-same-2D connection. Absaraka packages: `v2egr_culverts3` (chain abs2801v2egrcv3, KO-01 ends 686 / 204 ft) and `v2egr_culverts3_noko01` (chain abs2801v2egrcv3noko01, without KO-01). Test: `tests/test_hecras_structures.py::hecras_structures_connection_ends_two_cells_inside_the_perimeter`.
- **Download DEM: optional "Downstream bearing" (Runner 3.10.64 / Screening 0.17.42).** Download DEM 'Downstream bearing' (deg from north, optional): when set, the single-dam DEM box is shifted so the dam sits near the upstream edge and the box reaches downstream instead of being centred on the dam; same box size, so the download is no larger. Blank keeps the centred box. The Runner 'Model one dam' wizard's Terrain page carries the same field and passes it to the download. County and state areas ignore it. core/dem_download.point_box takes an optional bearing and shifts the EPSG:5070 box downstream (dam ~0.12 of the box from the upstream edge); the algorithm adds an optional BEARING_DEG parameter used only for the dam area. tests/test_dem_bearing.py. The ND plugins get the same change.

## 2026-10-21
- **Runner 3.10.63 / Screening 0.17.41 / Hazard 2.18.57: tool dialogs stay in front of the window that opened them.** Tool dialogs opened from the window (DEM download, offline layers, the suite tools) are owned by the window and modal (new shared/ui/tool_dialog.py). Before, QGIS's execAlgorithmDialog owned them from its main window and showed them before exec_(), so they were not modal: a click on the plugin window raised it over the tool, and from the Runner's 'Model one dam' wizard (modal itself) the DEM download could not be clicked at all, the wizard popped up in front of it. `exec_tool_dialog` creates the dialog with `processing.createAlgorithmDialog`, re-parents it to the asking window and runs it with `exec_()` only, then restores the map tool as `execAlgorithmDialog` does. Verified in real QGIS 3.34 under a window manager: the dialog stays above the Runner window, the Screening window and the wizard, and its Close button answers. The ND plugins (nd_hazard) get the same fix.
- **Qt6/PyQt6 enum hygiene + a fast CI lint (Runner 3.10.62; tests/CI across the suite):** three latent PyQt6/QGIS 4.x breakages fixed — the Runner's no-matplotlib QImage fallback (`core/qa_maps.py` `QImage.Format_RGB32`), the `suite_dams` NID reply handler (`QNetworkRequest`/`QNetworkReply` attribute and error enums) and the NID-fill test's `QgsField` type — now resolve the scoped Qt6 enum with a Qt5 `getattr` fallback (unscoped names raise `AttributeError`/`TypeError` on PyQt6; no Qt6 test reached these paths, so they were latent). New fast gate `tools/ci_checks.py` **qt6 enums** (with `tools/test_ci_checks.py`) forbids the unscoped Qt enum spellings and bare-int/`QVariant` `QgsField` field types that broke CI in #131 and #134, suite-wide — strings/comments are masked and `hasattr`-guarded or `# qt6-ok` lines are allowed. `run_all.py` (Hazard, Screening) now prints a failing script's full output instead of a 12/6-line tail that could hide the traceback (the Qt6 NAIP-clip failure showed only passing sub-tests). No plugin result changes; Hazard and Screening versions unchanged.
- **Restored 911 points, model edge with a no-data ring, DWR-8278 inventory follow-ups (Hazard 2.18.56; on 2.18.55; numbered 2.18.50 then 2.18.54 on its branch):** a lone 911 point (H1 default) whose site is already counted - same normalised address on a habitable structure's FEMA parcel record within 1,000 ft, or a habitable NSI RES structure within 150 ft without another 911 address - is the same dwelling (`dwelling_extra`, 0 persons; HEC-RAS tier2 #761 / #733; the self-test PARKING point P is now the same site as house H, 6 + 3 judgment persons, 57 checks); one whose 911 NOTES name a non-residential use, with address number 0 or Placement Parcel, is Unverified. The M3 outbuilding and lone-911 reasons are written to Notes, and a class that rests on lone 911 points is a judgment item (TM 11 notes too; the class is unchanged). `boundary.check` reads the Runner's model domain from `_breach_model` (ANUGA `anuga/mesh_boundary.csv`; LISFLOOD-FP grid = the raster) and reports "Model extent NOT checked" for a no-data ring it cannot see past (Absaraka anuga_structures: 0 edge cells, mesh edge wet to 8.4 ft). Co-located points of different sources (within 30 ft) are snapped together with the group's shortest reach (DWR-8278 911 / NSI split), and an M3 anchor within 3 ft of its 30 ft limit is a judgment item (DWR-8278: 29.4 ft). The lone-911 300 ft rule now reads the USDA NAIP 2025 flight date at each point (ND GIS Hub seamlines; `ms_imagery_assumed_date` only the fallback), flags possibly misplaced 911 points against the NG9-1-1 centerlines (`misplaced_911_m`), and saves small NAIP 2025 clips for the review items (capped, timeout, fail softly). Runner / Screening have their own model-edge checks (not shared code) and are unchanged. Tests: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_restored_911.py`, `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_lone911_naip.py`. Evidence: Docs & QA H1 QA of the 2.18.45 re-baseline and the DWR-8278 hand check. Review (Docs & QA 2026-10-21): a lone 911 point (not Placement Structure) with no Microsoft footprint within 300 ft is Unverified unless its DateUpdate is newer than the footprint imagery (capture_dates_range when present, else an assumed 2022-12-31; Absaraka #721 unverified); Placement=Parcel is counted and flagged instead of unverified; more non-dwelling NOTES words and a "for new <X>" rule (Dairy Queen); co-located groups no longer chain two houses; an always-on judgment line gives the LIJ from 911 points with no building.
- **Screening 0.17.40 (numbered 0.17.33 on its branch): Von Thun & Gillette Cb limits match the Runner.** VTG (1990) breach width Cb now steps at 1,000 / 5,000 / 10,000 ac-ft (a volume on a limit lands in the larger class), the method's limits and the Runner's (`core/breach.py` `vtg_1990`). The rounded m³ limits used before (1.23e6 / 6.17e6 / 1.23e7 m³) mis-classed reservoirs of 997.2–1,000, 5,000–5,002 and 9,972–10,000 ac-ft, so their VTG-E / VTG-R breach was 12.2–24.4 m wider than the Runner's. Other volumes and methods unchanged. `tests/test_vtg_parity.py` compares both plugins on each side of every limit.
- **Runner 3.10.61 (numbered 3.10.41 on its branch): HEC-RAS 2D gmsh points time out on the wall clock.** `core/hecras_seeds.py` ran gmsh in another Python with `subprocess.run(timeout=600)`, which uses the monotonic clock only (a paused VM stretched it) and killed only the interpreter (a child it started kept running). It now goes through `core/wallclock.run` like the HEC-RAS engine and ANUGA mesher (#96): elapsed = max(wall, monotonic), then SIGTERM to the process group, 5 s, SIGKILL. Same 600 s timeout and messages; no mesh or result changes. `tests/test_hecras_seeds_wallclock.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.60 / Screening 0.17.39 (numbered 3.10.43 / 0.17.35 on its branch): `gdal.Warp` without source overviews (`-ovr NONE`).** `gdal.Warp`'s default (`-ovr AUTO`) resampled a coarser model grid from a 1 m tile/VRT's nearest overview instead of its 1 m cells (a 5 m grid from a 4 m overview, 10 m from an 8 m one). A shared `core/warp_opts.py` (byte-identical in Runner and Screening) now passes `overviewLevel='NONE'` (QGIS 3.34's GDAL 3.8.5 keyword; `-ovr NONE` otherwise) everywhere terrain is warped — Runner `gdal_io.GdalTerrain`/`read_like`/`hecras_model`, Screening `TerrainLoader.load`, Find Ponds, the terrain cache and Download DEM's local DEM. Grids from a DEM with overviews shift slightly (≈1–2 cm mean |dz|, up to 5–12 cm for NEAREST; ~0 bias), so baselines may move; a DEM without overviews is identical. Test: `tests/test_warp_no_overviews.py`.
- **Runner 3.10.59 (numbered 3.10.56 on its branch): HEC-RAS package gets the culverts and bridges ANUGA used; terrain-CRS guard.** `core/hecras_structures.py`: culverts and bridges can be taken from another run of the dam (`structures_from` = its dam folder / `run_report.json`; Processing "Culverts and bridges from another run"), e.g. the ANUGA run when the package is built from a LISFLOOD-FP run; culverts take the inverts ANUGA used (`anuga/anuga_job.npz`) when the report leaves them empty; a bridge/notch on the same crossing as a culvert becomes an open gap in that culvert's road weir. The package stops if the terrain GeoTIFF lost its EPSG code / doesn't match `Projection.prj` / `proj.db` is unreachable. 2D-internal culvert connections are routing type 1 (type 3 stopped HEC-RAS). Connection lines are breaklines built into the computation points (`structure_breaklines`, on); each weir face / culvert invert is checked against its cells and raised + 0.01 ft if below (`structure_cell_fit`, on); the terrain is never changed.
- **Runner 3.10.58 (numbered 3.10.55 on its branch): ANUGA outer edges are outflow-only (run queue QA a_culvert_anuga).** ANUGA's outer edges were `anuga.Transmissive_boundary`, which copies the inside momentum to the edge, so water moving inward next to an edge (a pond against a clipped edge, an eddy, flow along the edge) was fed in from outside — the NDDOT culvert run ended with 8.25 M m³ in the domain for 1.99 M m³ of breach inflow (4.15×, a 10 ft edge pond); the 2026-10-15 structures run 1.51×. Now the edges are transmissive where water leaves and mirror the normal momentum where it would enter (zero mass flux), so no edge gains water (`edge_bc`: `outflow` default, `transmissive` = before). ANUGA results change wherever the flood reaches the mesh edge; a run that stayed inside is unchanged. The worker records a volume balance (in domain / (initial + breach inflow)); above 1.02 → a "volume check FAILED" warning.
- **Runner 3.10.57 (numbered 3.10.54 on its branch): HEC-RAS compile errors reach the log; dam-connection weir-cell pre-check (run queue QA c4).** `Ras.exe -c` that exits without writing the run inputs now raises an error carrying HEC-RAS's own Compute Messages (plan HDF Results/Summary/Compute Messages, or `<name>.p01.computeMsgs.txt`; ERROR lines first); before, the "wrote no inputs within {:.0f} s" message could not format the `None` (no-limit) timeout of 3.10.48, so a `TypeError` hid the real error (`v2e_gmsh_r`: a dam SA/2D connection weir below its cells). A `None` time limit reads "no limit" in every HEC-RAS engine message (`hecras_engine.within_text`, `_hm`). New CHECK-ONLY pre-flight of the dam-connection cells where the weir sits below the top of dam, against their lowest ground on the written terrain (`weir_cell_check`: warn (default; note + `run_report.json["hecras_weir_cells"]`) / fail / off); the terrain, weir and notch are never changed. No result changes.
- **Runner 3.10.56 (numbered 3.10.49 on its branch): ANUGA thread-count checks (Docs & QA follow-ups to #132, listed in #135).** `anuga_model.resolve_threads`: (1) `NDBREACH_ANUGA_THREADS=inf` raised `OverflowError` (`int(float('inf'))`, not caught) and stopped the run; inf / nan / text are now ignored with a note and the automatic count is used. (2) Decimals were truncated (2.7 → 2); a count must be a whole number (`6` or `6.0`), anything else is ignored with a note (also for the threads setting). (3) `ndbreach_cli.py --threads` help said "(0 = all)", true only for LISFLOOD-FP; it now gives ANUGA's rule. (4) An explicit or env count above the CPUs this process may use (its affinity mask; e.g. 64 on 8) is clamped to them, with a note. Notes go into the `ANUGA threads: N (rule)` line in the run log and `run_report.json["anuga_threads"]["rule"]`. No result changes. Tests: four new tests in `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_anuga_threads.py`. Numbered after #131 (3.10.45), the reserved 3.10.46 and #137 (3.10.48). Review (Docs & QA): a negative explicit count is ignored with a note as well; the --threads help is wrapped; `cpu_count` says a cgroup CPU quota is not read. Merged current main and renumbered 3.10.49 -> 3.10.56; Hazard and Screening versions as on main.
- **CI (Docs & QA review 2026-10-21): version bookkeeping gates in `tools/ci_checks.py`.** "version bookkeeping": `shared/damsuite/versions.py` RELEASE (and every vendored copy) must equal each plugin's metadata.txt version, Hazard's `constants.PLUGIN_VERSION` and README.txt version line must equal its metadata.txt, and no changelog entry may be newer than version=. "versions and changelogs vs main" (against `CI_BASE_REF`, default origin/main; a notice, not a failure, when it is not fetched): no plugin version or RELEASE entry lower than on main, no metadata.txt changelog version of main missing, no docs/CHANGELOG.md bullet title or date section of main missing. The fast-checks job fetches main first and runs `tools/test_ci_checks.py` (7 tests, throw-away git repos). No plugin code or version changes.
- **Docs only (Docs & QA): `anuga_structures_20261015` flagged UNRELIABLE / do not cite (RESULTS_LOG.md).** SWE's transmissive-edge audit (`/workspace/qa_fixes_20261021/anuga_edge_audit.md`, the bug fixed by #143, Runner 3.10.55) shows the run's final pass ends with 1.51x the injected volume and up to 8.58 ft at the mesh edge, and the 2.18.45 TM 11 Significant -> High call rests on two cropland 911 points (#721, #727) in that edge-ponded water. Flagged in its HEC-RAS comparison row, the 2.18.44 and 2.18.45 re-baseline sections and `results/hazard_rebaseline_2.18.45_20261020.csv` (`qa_note`); neither class is citable until it's rerun on #143 and re-scored after #133/#134. The NDDOT culvert ANUGA run (4.15x, already rejected) is cross-referenced to the audit; the 20x20 48 h and 10-19 refined runs are recorded as not affected. New Open item. No code or version change.
- **Building geometry: Microsoft footprints and the FEMA scene-offset check (Hazard 2.18.53; numbered 2.18.49 on its branch):** Microsoft Global ML Building Footprints (ODbL) are downloaded per study area into `outputs/_downloads` (`download_ms_footprints`, on; never into the offline data pack or ND_Dam_Suite_Pack) and give the building geometry; FEMA USA Structures outlines are matched onto them (overlap, or the nearest footprint of a similar size within 33 ft) and keep supplying the parcel address / occupancy the M3 parcel rule reads (`outline_geometry` = "listed" restores the old order). Each FEMA source scene (IMAGE_NAME) is compared with the footprints or NSI points; a scene offset > 5 m is flagged in Inventory_QA, the log and the judgment items, and moved only with `fema_scene_shift` (off). Points moved > 33 ft from a wet cell onto a dry outline are listed. New `DamSafety_Hazard/footprints.py`; test `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_footprint_geometry.py`. Evidence: Docs & QA DWR-8278 hand check (FEMA scene 1030010045BF2F00 14.7 m SW). Review (Docs & QA): the single-wide (mobile-home) shape is tested on every outline attached to the building, traced FEMA / OSM first, then the Microsoft footprint with a 3 ft width allowance (`mobile_ms_width_allowance_ft`), so a single-wide on a wider ML outline stays a mobile home (9250 56th Ave SW, 505 Square Butte Rd N). FEMA-only geometry (suite folder, offline without a copy, download off) is now a DATA SOURCES line and a judgment item.
- **Docs only (Docs & QA): Hazard zone colours (#126) in USER_GUIDE.md.** New section "Hazard zone colours match the ND Dam Safety Standards": the REG_05.2026 Fig. 5.1 / 5.2 colours (High #EF9897, Judgment #F9F4A3, Low #C4E0AD) and legend labels, where they appear, what didn't change, and a tip on lowering a zone layer's opacity so the basemap shows through (and setting it back to 100 % for figures compared with the standard). No plugin or version change.
- **Docs (Docs & QA): arrival depth (#131) and the HEC-RAS time limit (#137).** USER_GUIDE.md: "Arrival depth: 0.1 ft by default" (what it controls in the Runner and Hazard, where to set it, and how to compare with 1 ft runs), and "HEC-RAS: no time limit unless you set one", rewritten in plain language (settings table, the TCU dialog caution, where the limit is recorded, and the known hidden-error issue when Ras.exe -c exits with no limit set). RESULTS_LOG.md: which logged baselines ran at 1 ft (all of them, including the open #129 and #141 results) and which results stay comparable. No code change.
- **Runner arrival depth 0.1 ft (Runner 3.10.53, Hazard 2.18.52; on #131, numbered Runner 3.10.45 / Hazard 2.18.48 on its branch; user's choice of option 2):** `ARRIVAL_FT` defaults to 0.1 ft (was 1 ft), the ND hazard 'any depth', so every wet structure gets an arrival time (REG_05.2026 5.4(c)). At 1 ft the arrival raster was blank wherever the flood stayed under 1 ft: 9 of the 10 wet structures in the DWR-2801 sample (0.07-0.65 ft deep). One constant, `core/units.ARRIVAL_DEPTH_FT`, feeds Run a dam, the HEC-RAS import, Breach products from rasters, the CLI template, LISFLOOD-FP `inittm_depth` and ANUGA. The same depth is the run summary's 'flooded' depth (flooded_acres_over_arrival_depth, receptors flooded, velocity at structures, flood extent and arrival zones), so those numbers grow; the edge tolerance (1 ft) is unchanged. Also counted at the arrival depth (checked in main's source at 732f597, Docs & QA): the in-run LISFLOOD-FP vs ANUGA `comparison` (areas, fit index F) and the LISFLOOD-FP 'still moving' test (`workflow.py`: latest arrival > 0.9 x the run length lengthens an automatic-length run 1.5x), so some runs now simulate longer than at 1 ft. The ND and TM 11 classes don't use it; Hazard's `First_Arrival_hr`, EAP arrival bands, Graham loss-of-life warning time and evacuation timing read the arrival raster, so they change after a Runner re-run. Hazard's blank-arrival note now names the 0.1 ft default and says an older result needs a re-run. **All 218 saved Runner results (70 dam ids) were run at 1 ft and need re-runs** for the new arrival times. Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_arrival_depth.py` (built-in engine: every cell flooded 0.1-1 ft deep now has an arrival time; none had at 1 ft).
- **Runner 3.10.50 (#132 follow-up): the real OpenMP thread count, and ANUGA environments on Windows.** `anuga.get_omp_num_threads()` (ANUGA 4.0.1) returns a Python variable that `set_omp_num_threads` stores, so `run_report["anuga_threads"]["anuga_reported"]` always equalled the requested count. The worker now asks the loaded OpenMP runtime (`core/omp_probe.py`, ctypes `omp_get_max_threads` of libgomp / libomp / libiomp5 / vcomp; it never loads one itself and never raises), stores `omp_runtime_threads` / `omp_runtime`, and `anuga_reported` uses it (falls back to the old value). A note says when the runtime runs another count or none can be read. `core/nd_data.anuga_python_candidates` finds Windows conda envs (`envs\*anuga*\python.exe`, an active `*anuga*` env, `%USERPROFILE%\miniforge3`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\miniforge3`); Linux and macOS are unchanged. New manual workflow `.github/workflows/anuga-omp-scaling.yml` with `tools/anuga_omp_bench.py`: conda-forge ANUGA at 1/2/4 threads on windows-latest, macos-14, macos-15-intel and ubuntu-latest (time, checksum, runtime count). conda-forge builds checked: win-64 links `libgomp-1.dll` (mingw gcc 15), osx links `@rpath/libomp.dylib`. Tests: `tests/test_anuga_omp_runtime.py`. 3.10.48 is #137 (merged), 3.10.49 is taken by the open #138.
- **Runner 3.10.48: the HEC-RAS 2D compute has no wall-clock limit unless `NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S` is set.** `hecras_engine.compute_timeout` returned max(2 h, 3 × the estimate) capped at 48 h when the variable was unset (`core/hecras_engine.py:169-183` at caaf850), and `compute()` passed it to `_run_logged`, which stopped RasUnsteady (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL) at that wall time, so long runs were killed and the run queue fell back to an older engine. Now: unset, empty or `0` (also `none` / `off`) = no limit on the compute or the preprocess steps (`wallclock.Deadline(None)` never expires); a positive value is a limit as before (SIGTERM, `NDBREACH_HECRAS_KILL_GRACE_S`, SIGKILL; also for each preprocess step); an invalid or negative value is ignored with a note in the log. The Wine preprocess steps (terrain.exe, mesh.exe, drv.exe, Ras.exe -c) lose their 1,800 s per-step cap too (Nick's call): with no value there is no limit anywhere; a Wine dialog waiting for a click then waits until someone deals with it (`NDBREACH_HECRAS_TCU_HOOK` or a positive value for unattended batches). The limit in force (`none` or N s, with the reason and the run-time estimate) is logged and recorded in `run_report.json["hecras_time_limit"]`, `_hecras/hecras_stage.json` and the run report's "HEC-RAS wall-clock limit" row. The gmsh-points timeout (`core/hecras_seeds.py`, #127) is a separate constant and unchanged. No result changes. Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_hecras_no_default_limit.py` (fake Popen and clocks: a 60 h run with the variable unset, empty or 0 finishes with no stop; an explicit 10 h limit stops it with SIGTERM then SIGKILL; invalid values ignored with a note; the report record) and updated `test_hecras_engine.py`. Numbered after the open PRs (#131 3.10.45, 3.10.46 reserved) and main's 3.10.47.
- **Docs only (Docs & QA): ANUGA threads (#132, Runner 3.10.47) in USER_GUIDE.md and RESULTS_LOG.md.** The user guide gets an "ANUGA threads" section. It says what the threads setting does for ANUGA: 0 means `NDBREACH_ANUGA_THREADS` if above 0, otherwise CPUs − 2 between 1 and 4, and an explicit count is used as given. It explains where the setting is (the `THREADS` tool parameter and the Processing setting `NDBREACH_THREADS`), how to override it with `NDBREACH_ANUGA_THREADS`, and where to see the count a run used (run log, `anuga.log`, `run_report.json["anuga_threads"]`, `run_report.md`). It also says the count doesn't change results, only run time. The results log explains how to read `anuga_threads` when comparing baselines, and notes that older ANUGA runs with threads = 0 used one thread. No code or version change.
- **Runner 3.10.47: ANUGA uses several OpenMP threads.** With the threads setting at 0 ("0 = all cores") the Runner passed no count to ANUGA and left `OMP_NUM_THREADS` unset, so ANUGA 4.0.1 ran on one thread (the refined 72 h Absaraka and 20x20 runs used 1 of 8 cores; LISFLOOD-FP's OpenMP already used all cores). `anuga_model.resolve_threads`: 0 → `NDBREACH_ANUGA_THREADS` when > 0, else min(CPUs − 2, 4), at least 1; > 0 as given. The count goes into the job file, `OMP_NUM_THREADS` and `anuga.set_omp_num_threads` (also on checkpoint resume) and is recorded in `runner.log`, `anuga.log`, `run_report.json["anuga_threads"]` and `run_report.md`. No result changes: 1 vs 4 threads bit-identical on real ANUGA (589 and 87,749 triangles; kill at 1 thread + resume at 4 bit-identical too); 87,749 triangles 62 s → 24 s at 4 threads. A build without OpenMP stays at one thread and says so. The envelope operator's per-step update skips full temporary arrays (bit-identical, ~25-30 % less time in that serial step). Help texts for the threads setting fixed. Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_anuga_threads.py` (one model test runs real ANUGA at 1 and 4 threads for seconds). Numbered after the open PRs #126-#131 (3.10.41-3.10.46).

## 2026-10-20
- **Docs only: Hazard re-baselines on 2.18.44 and 2.18.45 logged (RESULTS_LOG.md).** This is Grok Bot's `ndhazard:batch` on copies of 65 saved results (main 0a8344b; 65 classified, 0 failed; DWR-3161 skipped). It records why the old batch numbers aren't comparable: 2.18.0–2.18.34 batch runs had NaN road and rail lengths and outline areas, and PR #94 brought the H5, M3 and H4 changes. It includes the DWR-2801 explanation and the 8 class changes, and marks the older batch hazard figures as superseded. The per-result table is in `docs/results/hazard_rebaseline_20261020.csv`. **Also logged: the repeat batch on Hazard 2.18.45** (main 5b4ed05, #113 H1; 65 classified, 0 failed). Per-result table: `docs/results/hazard_rebaseline_2.18.45_20261020.csv`.
  - **What H1 changed:** it counts 911 address points that have no outline and no NSI record as habitable again. That adds 351 inundated structures (106 distinct points) and 160 cut-off structures across 33 results.
  - **One class change:** Absaraka anuga_structures_20261015 goes from TM 11 Significant to High. QA found it implausible: the two points are cropland and a gas-station site, in water ponded at the mesh edge.
  - **Lives in jeopardy (LIJ) went up** in DWR-2645, DWR-4441 and the three HEC-RAS tier2 variants. Two of these are not real: the tier2 increase is a double count, and the DWR-4441 point is a marina parcel.
  - **Both batches are kept;** 2.18.44 is not superseded.
  - **DWR-8278 (corrected 10-21):** ND Low / TM 11 Significant reflects current conditions, because the single-wide at 9250 56th Ave SW is gone by 2023. The run's "dry" result there is an artifact: FEMA USA Structures scene 1030010045BF2F00 is shifted 14.7 m SW. With the outlines corrected, the home was in the mobile-home High zone, which gives ND High. Open: whether the pad will be reoccupied. Offsets table: `docs/results/dwr8278_footprint_offsets_20261021.csv`. A general caveat that FEMA outline offsets can flip results is added; SWE has been asked for a fix.
  - No plugin or version change.
- **US / State highways at least Medium, REG_05.2026 5.2.1.2(b) (Hazard 2.18.52, Runner 3.10.53, Screening 0.17.38; numbered 2.18.48 / 3.10.45 / 0.17.37 on its branch; on #130):** Runner / Screening `core/nd_hazard.py` 1.2.0 floors an overtopped US or State highway at Medium whatever its AADT (it was Low with a counted AADT < 500); the private / AADT < 500 Low rule is for local, county and private roads. Highways are read from the route type or name (`us_state_highway_from_text`: US / U.S. / ND / State / North Dakota + number, a bare Highway / Hwy + number, not County / Township highways, TIGER U / S, OSM trunk / primary); Hazard's `linear.road_class` reads the same texts and its criteria table has a 5.2.1.2(b) row. A flagged structure (DWR-3161) keeps its Low of record (the Runner's ND hazard tool now says so too). Hazard's Habitable_Structures Notes explain a blank First_Arrival_hr (no arrival-raster value at the wet cells: the arrival depth, 1 ft by default, was never exceeded). Tests: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/test_highway_floor.py`, `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_highway_floor_reg05.py`, `DamSafety_Screening/tests/test_highway_floor.py`.
- **Hazard tables against REG_05.2026 Section 5 (Hazard 2.18.51, Runner 3.10.52, Screening 0.17.36; on #126; numbered Hazard 2.18.47 / Runner 3.10.44 on its branch):** `Habitable_Structures` gains `Danger_Zone` (HIGH DANGER ZONE / JUDGMENT ZONE / LOW DANGER ZONE, Figures 5.1 / 5.2) and `Danger_Chart` (Fig. 5.1 houses, Fig. 5.2 mobile homes, or the 1D depth > 2 ft rule, 5.2.1.1(d)); its XLSX zone cells are filled #EF9897 / #F9F4A3 / #C4E0AD (`DamSafety_Hazard/xlsx_zone_fill.py`); the criteria table lists 5.2.1.2(a) private / AADT < 500 roads (Low); the summary labels the ACER TM 11 Table 1 class a reference, not the ND class. Runner / Screening findings tables gain `danger_zone`, and the chart legend uses the standard's zone names. Existing columns and values are unchanged (styles, rule compare and the Runner's summary reader use them); no class, threshold or default changes. Test: `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_hazard_tables_reg05.py`. Gap list and judgment items: PR description.
- **REG_05.2026 danger-zone colours (Hazard 2.18.46, Runner 3.10.51, Screening 0.17.34; Nick's request via Docs & QA):** every ACER TM 11 zone graphic uses the colours of the ND Dam Safety Standards' Figures 5.1 / 5.2 (pp. 21-22), sampled from the published figures since the text names none: High #EF9897, Judgment #F9F4A3, Low #C4E0AD. Changed: Hazard `helpers.apply_acer11_style` (and its legend now says JUDGMENT ZONE, was JUDGEMENT), `styles.COLORS`, `styles/acer_zone_points.qml` and `styles/acer_zones.qml` in Hazard, Runner and Screening (opacity 1, was 0.75, which lightened the colours), the Hazard chart sign-off overlay (the low zone is now filled; it was white) and the Runner / Screening `nd_hazard.chart_svg` review charts and legend (judgment was peach #fbe7b5, low nearly white #e8f3e8). Breakpoints, classes and stored field values (HIGH / JUDGMENT / LOW) are unchanged; dam hazard-class colours (High / Medium / Low) are not ACER zones and are unchanged. `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_zone_colors_reg05.py` checks every copy (text and rendered SVG) and runs in `tools/ci_checks.py`. Runner 3.10.38-3.10.40 and Hazard 2.18.44-2.18.45 are already on main; Runner 3.10.41 (#127) and Screening 0.17.33 (#125) are claimed by open PRs. Legend labels follow the standard: HIGH DANGER ZONE / JUDGMENT ZONE / LOW DANGER ZONE (QML styles and the chart_svg legend). `helpers.apply_acer11_style` no longer fails on QGIS 3.34 (`invalidateCache()` only when the renderer has it), so the layer repaints.
- **New plugin: DamSafety Hazard Lite 0.1.0 (`DamSafety_HazardLite/`, provider `ndlite`).** One Processing tool, about 700 lines, separate from DamSafety Hazard. From the breach depth and velocity rasters it reports hmax / vmax at each 911-addressed structure (sampled over the FEMA outline the point is in, or the nearest one within 30 ft), at each overtopped road stretch (NG9-1-1 roads, NDDOT AADT from the same road) and at each inundated rail stretch (NARN, FRA trains/day). Then it gives the REG_05.2026 Section 5.2 class with section numbers. FEMA "Agriculture" out-buildings are excluded (5.2.1.1) unless the 911 point or occupancy marks a dwelling (a switch keeps them). It reads the offline pack. It also has a one-window form in the shared GUI grammar (Plugins menu; info banner, stage pills, CheckPanel, bold Classify, ▸ Log, ⚙ gear; checked by `tools/gui_lint.py`). Tests: `DamSafety_HazardLite/tests/test_core.py` (CI plugin-suites job). Absaraka: High, the same roads as Hazard 2.32.0. No change to the other plugins.
- **#114 (merged 02:59Z, main b0c2ad5), tests/CI only:** de-flakes `test_hecras_engine.py::hecras_engine_write_project_hydrograph_inflow`, which compared two `.g01` files byte for byte while `geometry_text` stamps the wall clock into two lines (about 2.5 % of runs failed when the writes straddled a second). No plugin version change (Runner stays 3.10.32). Split out of the open #113.
- **Hazard 2.18.44: Classify no longer scrolls sideways (#108).** Long one-line check boxes set the Classify page's minimum width (1 530 px with the sections open), so Browse…, Zip…, Import, Read plan and *Use this plan for the classification* were cut off at the right. The Classify view's check boxes now wrap (`DamSafety_Hazard/ui_wrap.py`, Hazard only; the shared `ui_fit` / `ui_widgets` copies are unchanged), and long combo boxes and layer lists shrink with the window. The page needs 591 px and fits from the 640 px minimum window up, at 1280x800, 1024x640 and 2x HiDPI. The click area matches a plain check box (box and text only). Layout only, no result changes. New real-Qt test `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_classify_fit_width.py` (Qt5 and Qt6). damsuite `versions.RELEASE` not touched.
- **Runner 3.10.38 (#117; numbered 3.10.35 on its branch until #120 took it): ANUGA checkpoints, partial results and resume, on for long runs.** **Default rule:** a run that simulates ≥ 12 h or is expected to have ≥ 50,000 triangles (gmsh: its count; ANUGA's mesher: 1.5 × area / largest triangle area per region) checkpoints every 30 wall-clock minutes (keep 2) and writes partial results every 10 minutes; a short run is unchanged (same job file, no extra files). `NDBREACH_ANUGA_CHECKPOINT_MIN=0` (or `off`) switches it off; a number > 0 forces it on at that cadence. The hydraulics are unchanged: through `anuga_model.run` on an 839-triangle real-ANUGA domain, the automatic setup (31 checkpoints and 31 partial writes) and `CHECKPOINT_MIN=0` give bit-identical depth, velocity, arrival and WSE. ANUGA 4.0.1's `domain.set_checkpointing` (`NDBREACH_ANUGA_CHECKPOINT_MIN` wall-clock minutes or `NDBREACH_ANUGA_CHECKPOINT_STEPS` yield steps) pickles the domain, with the worker's max depth / velocity / WSE and arrival-time operator, to `anuga/anuga_checkpoints/<job fingerprint>/`; the newest `NDBREACH_ANUGA_CHECKPOINT_KEEP` (2) are kept and `checkpoint.json` lists the complete ones. `anuga/anuga_partial.npz` (the envelope grids so far, written atomically) follows every checkpoint, every `NDBREACH_ANUGA_PARTIAL_MIN` minutes and SIGTERM / Cancel (SIGTERM first when checkpoints are on). `NDBREACH_ANUGA_RESUME=1`, or the run's Resume with checkpoints on, continues the same job from its newest checkpoint that loads; another job starts over. A domain that cannot be pickled, or too little disk for keep + 1 checkpoints plus 256 MB (checked from a trial pickle and at every yield step), runs on without checkpoints and says why. The inlet hydrograph and Inlet_operator's default are picklable functions instead of lambdas (same values). Recorded in `run_report["anuga_checkpoint"]` (with the `rule`) and `run_report.md`. See the Runner README. Tests: `tests/test_anuga_checkpoint.py` (fake ANUGA in `tests/fake_anuga.py`; two model tests run real ANUGA for seconds).
- **Runner 3.10.39: Download a DEM rows no longer collapse (#107).** The main window has an explicit minimum size from `ui_fit.fit_window`, so Qt does not raise it to the layout's minimum. Opening *Download a DEM* in a 1280x800 window therefore squeezed its combo boxes, line edits, buttons and the "Local copy" label to 6-9 px, drawn on top of each other (the minimum is 24-33 px, 47 px for the wrapped label). The dam rows, Download a DEM, Advanced and the Run/Tools tabs are now in a scroll area; the action bar, the Log and Close stay below it. The three sections keep their own height and the Run/Tools tabs take the room that is left (at least 150 px). Opening a section grows the window up to its screen, and whatever still doesn't fit scrolls. Test: `DamSafety_Runner/tests/realqt_dem_rows.py` (real Qt, offscreen, 1280x800, 1366x768, 1024x700 and 1920x1200 screens; run by `test_dem_rows_real_qt.py`). No change to any setting or result. Numbered 3.10.36 on its branch until #121 and #123 took 3.10.36 and 3.10.37; #117 claims 3.10.38.

## 2026-10-19
- **Audit High fixes (2026-10-19 audit, DamSafety suite; Hazard 2.18.45, Setup 1.0.15, Runner 3.10.40; rebased onto current main. Screening unchanged — its F-02 change is tests only.)** F-01 and F-03 from the audit are **not** included: the GUI-grammar rework (#120/#121) moved the dam page into the Runner's dam wizard and made that the suite's one per-dam wizard (`docs/gui-grammar.md`), which already surfaces the NID fill, DEM estimate and source labels — so F-01 (open Setup's own wizard) and F-03 (the storage-source label) are superseded, and the surviving normal-pool fallback is already flagged there.
  - **Hazard H1 (default change back, conservative):** a 911 address point with no building nearby counts as habitable again by the ND site address rule, listed for review. Self-test 56/56 and now in CI (`tests/test_selftest.py`).
  - **Hazard H2:** metre / m/s depth and velocity rasters detected (band unit or metadata, HEC-RAS project SI Units, file name); ERROR in Check inputs and FAIL in the model gate on strong evidence; a run stops on it. The units guard runs before reprojection so a metric secondary scenario in a foreign CRS is caught, not read as feet.
  - **Screening F-02 (tests):** the Screening <-> Runner parity tests find the in-repo Runner and FAIL in CI when it is missing.
  - **Runner R-H1:** `scripts/build_lisflood_linux.sh` (and Setup's copy, now checked byte-identical by `tools/ci_checks.py`) applies `patch_lisflood.py` before cmake and refuses an unpatched build.
- **Hazard 2.18.55: suite hand-off refresh (numbered 2.18.40, then 2.18.47, on its branch).** The Dam suite home's *Classify* fills Hazard's dam folder and then calls the window's `refresh` hook, which Hazard did not have. Handing over the dam the window already showed (opened before the Runner finished, or restored from the last session) therefore left the raster boxes empty. `refresh()` now fills the empty boxes from the Runner's rasters (hand-chosen rasters kept, no auto-run, nothing during a run). No result changes. New real-Qt test `DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_suite_handoff.py`; damsuite `versions.RELEASE` updated (no SUITE_VERSION change).
- **USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles, native (Screening 0.17.29, damsuite 1.17.4; #105, numbered 0.17.28 / 1.17.3 on its branch until #94 took those):** Download DEM (`nddam:downloaddem`) has a third source, *USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles (native, TNM)*, for the lidar project tiles themselves instead of the ImageServer's resampled image. It queries The National Map for a box, a dam folder (the Runner's model bounds) or a dam point, lists the tiles with project, collection and publication dates and sizes, the download total and the free disk space, and downloads only when *download the listed tiles* is ticked (10 GB cap per run, 2 GB must stay free). Tiles go to `<suite data folder>/dem/lidar_1m/` (or a chosen tile folder; never the offline pack or a read-only folder), with resume, retries, a size and GDAL 1 m check, skip-if-present and `lidar_1m_manifest.json` (sha256). The newest collection wins where projects overlap, and the log says so. The result is a per-dam `<DAM_ID>_1m_lidar.vrt` with the coarser DEM underneath and `<DAM_ID>_1m_lidar.json` naming the projects. damsuite's `suitedata` skips `dem/lidar_1m` so the tiles never pass for the user's own DEM. Built in Screening rather than the Data plugin so it reuses Download DEM's areas, sidecar and window. Runner and Hazard versions unchanged.
- **Claude's audit-fix campaign (#94, with the review follow-ups; Runner 3.10.32, Hazard 2.18.39, Screening 0.17.28, Data 1.4.2, Setup 1.0.11, damsuite 1.17.3).** See `docs/Fix_campaign_report.md`. Default-changing items:
  - **Hazard 2.18.39:** **M3** NSI-only homes are not counted as habitable without a parcel (can lower the hazard class); **H4** `collapse_dwellings` removed (PAR can go up); **H5** `transformguard` patches QGIS coordinate transforms for the whole QGIS session.
  - **Runner 3.10.32:** **M6** top of dam from the 98th percentile within 30 m, not the maximum (lower, less conservative); **M5** built-in flood engine, opt-in only (off by default, not validated against LISFLOOD-FP).
  - **Screening 0.17.28:** **M18** a new cap limits the crest to the spillway level; **H11** downstream side judged along the dam with terrain over the waterbody vote (can flip the breach direction on long dams).
  - **Data 1.4.2:** H14 Qt6 fixes, M21-M23, L3, P1; no default changes.
  - **Setup 1.0.11:** M24 status panel, M4 pack_convert; no default changes.
  - Repo: `shared/damsuite` master copy with `tools/sync_damsuite.py --check`, `tools/ci_checks.py` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
- **Runner 3.10.31 (#104, merged 10:23Z, dca3275): ANUGA mesh and bed fixes from the gmsh embankment-line benchmark.**
  - ANUGA's bed now comes from the DEM source at its own cell size, set at the triangle vertices. Before, it was the 40 m model grid at the centroids. You can switch back with `ANUGA_ELEVATION=model_grid`, and `run_report["anuga_elevation_source"]` records which bed was used. With embankment lines, the share of crown height kept went from 13–21 % to 56–63 % (centroids) and 81–82 % (edges).
  - A gmsh embedded edge just longer than the mesh size is no longer cut in two, so DWR-4441 passes the gate. The gate itself is unchanged.
  - gmsh culvert ends get 25 m² triangles, the same as the default mesher.
  - The worker checks the `mesh_filename` signature instead of using a bare `except TypeError`.
  - Tests are in `tests/test_anuga_mesh_elevation.py`. Built on #102 (3.10.30).
- **Runner 3.10.30 (#102, merged 10:09Z, 84e411e): ANUGA default mesher, overlapping culvert-end octagons merged.** The refined octagons around both ends of a short culvert (or twin culverts) crossed each other and ANUGA's mesher made sliver triangles: Absaraka DWR-2801 had 92 triangles under 5 m², the smallest 0.15 m², setting the time step (≈0.02 s). They are now merged into one convex region per cluster: smallest triangle 8.7 m², none under 5 m², time step ≈0.13 s (mesh-only check, no solver). Octagons clear of others are unchanged; DWR-2662 and DWR-4441 (no culverts) mesh identically. Tests: `tests/test_anuga_culvert_regions.py`.
- **HEC-RAS Absaraka fixes and crest dam line (Runner 3.10.28, #90 with #103; numbered 3.10.22 on its branch):** build_model starts the sunny-day breach at a set time and uses HEC's Overtopping mode to match the plugin's breach; an optional spillway notch in the dam weir (spillway flow = total weir flow minus breach flow); tighter solver tolerances (`tolerances="tight"`); the plugin's reference hydrograph recomputed on the HEC-RAS breach; 2D breaklines along the breach and the flow-path strip; optional gmsh computation points (off by default; Runner window Advanced checkbox and `HECRAS_GMSH`) with the 8-sides, perimeter and seed checks; a dam row centred on the breach corners with a breach-cell pre-check (the v2c 38.7 % volume error); and (#103) the HEC-RAS dam line traced along the embankment crest (`dam_axis='crest'`, default; Advanced checkbox 'HEC-RAS dam line follows the crest (recommended)', `HECRAS_CREST`), with the breach cut square to the crest at the deepest crossing by default. LISFLOOD-FP / ANUGA runs are unchanged.
- **Setup 1.0.10 (#88): missing dam height / storage.** On the Dam page a height or storage the DWR record does not have (blank or 0) is filled from the National Inventory of Dams (`nid_fill.py`: the suite pack's NID layer offline, or the NID web service on a "Fill from NID (online)" click), or estimated from the DEM on request ('Estimate from DEM', `dem_estimate.py`); records that are not a dam are hidden from the dam list (`dam_filter.py`), and the dam point comes from the DWR dam line when there is one. See `DamSafety_Setup/CHANGES_1.0.10.txt`. Setup only; no Runner, Hazard or Screening version change.
- **Run report metrics (#92, merged 09:12Z, 31d443f; Runner 3.10.27, numbered 3.10.22 on its branch; closes #91):** reporting only: flooded area at or above 0.1 / 1 / 2 ft, P95 / P99 velocity over wet cells, the depth and position of the max-velocity cell, velocity at flooded structures and overtopped roads, and an optional water-body mask (`WATER_MASK` / `NDBREACH_WATERBODY_MASK` / the suite's hydro waterbodies when present) with masked and unmasked figures side by side. Existing fields unchanged; new CSV columns appended. TIER 2 HEC-RAS goes through `adopt_final_engine`. See `docs/Run_report_metrics.md`. The suite lookup also finds `hydro/3DHP_*waterbod*.gpkg` (after `3DHP_waterbodies.gpkg`, before `NHD_waterbodies.gpkg`).
  - The mask was opt-in in practice at the time because ND_Dam_Suite_Pack had no waterbody file; since 2026-10-24 the pack holds `hydro/NHD_waterbodies.gpkg` again (see 2026-10-24 under Unreleased), so a run that finds the pack turns the mask on by itself. The ND 3DHP waterbodies are on the box at `/home/box/DamSafety/Data/3DHP/3DHP_ND_waterbodies.gpkg` (188,320 polygons, NHD-sourced, 2023 feature dates). At DWR-3161 all of Lake Sakakawea is one polygon about 140 m from the dam, so check the masked area before using it.
- **HEC-RAS timeouts on the wall clock (#96, merged 08:59Z, 09660cf; Runner 3.10.26):** the RasUnsteady compute timeout (and the Wine preprocess steps, the Ras.exe -c loop and the ANUGA gmsh mesher) used `time.monotonic()` through Python's subprocess timeouts. That clock stops while the VM is paused or forked, so a 5 h timeout fired after 9.7 h of wall time and the SIGKILL lost RasUnsteady's end-of-run series. `core/wallclock.py` measures max(wall, monotonic) elapsed, logs clock jumps, and stops a timed-out process group with SIGTERM, `NDBREACH_HECRAS_KILL_GRACE_S` (default 60 s), then SIGKILL. The timeout is logged to `linux_run/run.log` and the Runner log before any cleanup, and recorded in `run_report.json` as `hecras_timeout`; the partial results HDF is kept as `<name>.p01.partial.hdf`. Runner 3.10.22 is claimed by the open #90 and #92 and 3.10.24 was this PR's first number; merged as 3.10.26 after #101 took 3.10.25.
- **Screening 0.17.27: tool 3 honours the storage choice (Nick's decision, #101).** New `STORAGE_CHOICE` enum on tool 3 (Max storage default / Normal storage / Larger of the two). Max raises the top-of-dam storage to the inventory maximum, Normal keeps the DEM above the normal pool, Larger uses the larger inventory value; a missing or zero value falls back to the other with a warning, neither keeps the DEM estimate. `stor_notes` and the log start with the storage used (no new field, tools 16 / 19 write by position). The window sends the choice to tool 3 only (not tool 1); the chain and tool 17 use the same defaults. 7b and the Runner hand-off do not run tool 3; they read its curve (`cal_total_m3`), which follows the choice.
- **Tests that failed on main d217ec2 (#101, merged 08:42Z, 608c58b; Runner 3.10.25):** the gmsh mesher checks the outer polygon before importing gmsh, so a bad mesh spec is reported as such where gmsh is missing (the only plugin-code change). Test fixes, no product change: the default ANUGA job-file pin is checked with the built-in outline tracer (contourpy traces up to one cell differently); the Word report expects its figures embedded exactly when cairosvg renders; the Runner GUI tests resolve the checkout through a symlink; the real-Qt window test skips in a Python without PyQt5; Screening's qgis_mock gained `pushWarning` (QGIS >= 3.16) and `qgis.utils.loadPlugin` / `startPlugin`; the nd_hazard adapter and popup tests follow the one-signer chart rule, the single Dam safety menu entry, the DamSafety labels and the per-computer Dams folder. Runner 3.10.22 / 3.10.24 are taken by the open #90, #92 and #96.
- **Flagged structures (#93, merged 05:23Z, d217ec2; Runner 3.10.23, Hazard 2.18.38, Screening 0.17.26, damsuite 1.17.2):** a repo-shipped list of records that are not a dam to breach (`damsuite/structure_flags.py`, see [Flagged_structures.md](Flagged_structures.md)). The first entry is DWR-3161 / ND00597 (Sanish Bay WPA Dam 1): the ND-23 road fill over backwater, breach analysis not applicable, hazard Low from the DWR record (the 3.10.16 rebaseline's HIGH was a model artifact). Screening lists it with that note and the Low class instead of a breach hazard (new fields `struct_cls`, `flag_note`). The Runner refuses a single-dam breach run of it unless `ALLOW_FLAGGED_STRUCTURE`, and the batch tools skip it (Status `flagged`, new columns `Structure_Flag` and `Hazard_Of_Record`). Hazard refuses to classify it unless allowed. The Dam suite home shows the flag and Low. Runner 3.10.22 is taken by the open #90 and #92, so this is 3.10.23.

- **Damsuite shared pin and recommendations round (#111, merged 18:16Z, 186c926):** `tools/damsuite_pin.json` fingerprints `shared/damsuite/` so each fork's CI checks its vendored copies offline; dead-code sweep and single-sourced unit factors with a `crest_rule` drift guard; CI runs the Hazard suite through `tests/run_all.py`. No plugin version change.
- **Cross-platform review (#100, merged 09:37Z, 0d04108):** child programs (LISFLOOD-FP, gmsh, the ANUGA worker, `conda env list`, …) start with no console window on Windows (`CREATE_NO_WINDOW`; nothing changes on Linux/macOS), and Qt6-safe enum spellings. Static review only; not run on Windows or macOS. No version change.
- **Cleanup (#95, merged 09:21Z, b853ce7; #97, merged 09:24Z, 587dddf):** removed unused `FieldPlan` (Hazard), `compare_result()` (Hazard), `extra_datasets()` (Data) and `NDBreachRunnerPlugin._fill_suite()` (Runner), and three unused imports in `DamSafety_Runner/plugin.py`. No behaviour or version change.
- **README (#98, merged 09:25Z, 622b3c4):** how to run the Setup tests (pytest in the `qgis334` env, offscreen). Docs only.
## 2026-10-18
- **#82 (merged 12:05Z), Setup 1.0.8:** wizard live checks; Next follows the page and the reason it's off shows in red. The Dam page needs height > 0 ft and max storage > 0 ac-ft (42 of the 74 Cass dams in the pack have neither).
- **#76 (merged 12:09Z), Runner (no version bump; 3.10.17), HEC-RAS TIER 2:** the report, polygons and EAP preview are rebuilt from the HEC-RAS rasters (the LISFLOOD-FP figures are kept under `screening_pass_lisflood`). A rejected inflow point is walked downstream. New `NDBREACH_HECRAS_INFLOW_MODE` = `auto` (default) / `hydrograph` / `breach`; `auto` falls back to a breach model when there's no usable inflow point. An inflow point on the wrong side of the dam isn't used: `auto` stops with a "likely dam-point / downstream-side problem" error. The compute timeout scales with the model (max(2 h, 3 × estimate), 48 h cap; `NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S` overrides).
- **#84 (merged 12:11Z), Runner 3.10.18: breach inflow on the downstream side.** The crest fallback and the 8 ft fallback keep every inflow pick on the downstream side. New `run_report.json` `inflow_point.side_check` gives a "CHECK THE DAM POINT" warning, and a road within 30 m of the dam point gives a road-fill warning (`dam_point_checks`) unless `DAM_LINES` was given. At DWR-3161, the inflow moved 242 m north to ground at 1,839.45 ft. See RESULTS_LOG.md.
- **#85 (merged 12:14Z), Runner 3.10.19 (titled 3.10.18; #84 took that number):** gmsh ANUGA mesh gate is 0.45 × the smallest intended triangle (was 0.5 × 100 m², which rejected Absaraka's 49.93 m² mesh and quietly ran the default mesh); `ANUGA_MESHER=gmsh` is strict, `gmsh_or_default` falls back with a warning; road and rail embankment centrelines become mesh lines.
- **#86 (merged 12:17Z), Runner 3.10.20 (titled 3.10.19):** opt-in `RESERVOIR_CARVE` (Off default / Auto / On) carves the reservoir bottom from the storage curve into a copy of the DEM, for LiDAR that shows a water surface. See `docs/Reservoir_carve_from_storage.md`.
- **#87 (merged 12:49Z), Hazard 2.18.36:** UI polish from the Hazard screens review.
- **#83 (merged 12:49Z), docs only:** RESULTS_LOG re-baseline 2026-10-18 (TIER 0, main 8b0601c, culvert burn on) and the HEC-RAS TIER 2 comparison.
- **#89 (merged 12:51Z), Runner 3.10.21, Screening 0.17.25, Data 1.4.1, Setup 1.0.9, Hazard 2.18.37:** monitor-aware window sizing (`ui_fit.py`) and one `…` character in visible text.

## New plugin
- **#60 (merged 10-16), DamSafety_PMP 0.3.0 (internal, experimental):** the nd_pmp prototype is added as `DamSafety_PMP/`. Provider id `ndpmp` and settings keys `nd_pmp/*` are unchanged. The AWA data isn't included. Machine-specific paths were removed. Light tests are in `tests/run_tests.py`, and data-dependent tests skip when their inputs are missing. The OGR memory driver now falls back to "Memory" on GDAL < 3.11. Licensing: for SWC's exclusive use; get permission from SWC and AWA before redistributing.

## Repository
- **#45 (merged 10-15 14:16Z):** Removed the old nd_ plugins (`nd_claude_1.23/`, `nd_installed/`). They are archived on branch `archive/nd-plugins` and tag `archive-nd-plugins-20261015` (18ae1f5). The top-level `nd_breach_runner/` and `nd_dam_screening/` test aliases are kept.

## 2026-10-15 (later)
- **#22 (merged 06:12Z), Screening 7b:** new options are passed to `ndbreach:rundam`:
  - `ENGINE` ("Runner models") → TIER
  - `FORCE_ANUGA` → FORCE_ANUGA
  - `REFINE_NEAR` → REFINE_NEAR
  - `CULVERTS` (line layer) → STRUCTURES
  - `C_KIND`/`C_WIDTH`/`C_HEIGHT`/`C_BARRELS`/`C_SHAPE`/`C_NAME` → `S_*`

  Only values that differ from the Runner defaults are sent, so a default 7b run is unchanged.
- **#32 (merged 10:26Z), cross-platform fixes:**
  - Text files are now read and written as UTF-8.
  - CSVs are written with `newline=""`.
  - On Windows, the conda env python is found at `<env>\python.exe`, which fixes ANUGA env auto-detect.
  - Subprocess output is decoded with `errors="replace"`.
  - No behaviour change on Linux/macOS. The vendored `damsuite/` copies are not yet changed.
- **#38 (merged 14:15Z), Screening 7b follow-ups (Screening 0.17.22):** `ENGINE` is the one Runner engine choice. `FORCE_ANUGA` is folded in and hidden; an old `FORCE_ANUGA = True` still selects "LISFLOOD-FP then ANUGA". The engine is shown on the Run tab and in `rn_engine`, there's a warning when culverts are ignored, and an early check fails if ANUGA is missing.
- **#30 (merged 10:34Z), Runner:** culvert guesses can be sized from the NDDOT/NBI inventory (CULVERT_INVENTORY, CULVERT_MATCH_M 75 m, CULVERT_BURN_MATCHED). See USER_GUIDE.md.

Merged pull requests, 2026-10-14 to 2026-10-15 (main at e2acb76). Merge times are UTC.

## Runner (DamSafety_Runner)
- **#9 (10-14):** Brought back `NDBREACH_DEM_RESAMPLE` with this precedence: tool parameter, then environment variable, then default. Added the `DEM_RESAMPLE` setting. The method used is recorded in `run_manifest.json` (`extra.dem_resample`).
- **#10 (10-14):** **The DEM resample default changed from bilinear to average** (`core/gdal_io.DEFAULT_RESAMPLE`). The precedence stays the same. Bilinear gave flooded areas 3-40% too high compared with the baselines. To keep the old behaviour, set `NDBREACH_DEM_RESAMPLE=bilinear` or pick bilinear in the setting.
- **#11 (10-14):** Security, integrity and performance fixes from the 2026-10-14 review.
- **#14 (10-15):** **The inflow point is now the lowest qualifying cell** (at least half the dam height down) within the usual radius, instead of the nearest one (`core/terrain.find_inflow_point`). The 400 m fallback still uses the nearest cell. This changes results for dams where the nearest and lowest cells differ; see RESULTS_LOG.md. The same PR also regenerated the stale `damsuite/FILES.json`, isolated the tests from `NDSUITE_SETTINGS`, and fixed the test fakes.
- **#20 (10-15):** **Structures crash fix.** `ndbreach:rundam` no longer raises "truth value of an array ... is ambiguous" when it's given a STRUCTURES (culvert/bridge) layer. The same `or []` pattern was also fixed in `culvert_guess` and in the HEC-RAS structure writers.
- **#21 (10-15):** **Per-run QA maps.** Each dam folder now gets `qa_inflow_map.png` and `qa_depth_map.png`. The new `QA_MAPS` setting has the options Off / Inflow map only / Inflow map and depth map, and the default is both maps. A QA-map error never fails a run. Details are recorded under `qa_maps` in `run_manifest.json`.
- **#23 (10-15) + #27 (10-15):** **New tool: Spillway adequacy** (`spillwayadequacy`), also added to nd_breach_runner (the old nd_ copy, now archived; see Repository above). It runs a no-breach routing of each inflow hydrograph against REG_05.2026 residual freeboard. #27 carries the review fixes (start pool, inflow units, ...).

## Screening (DamSafety_Screening)
- **#13 (10-15):** **Shallow-basin storage guard** (ported from nd_ 0.28.3). In tool 3, a mapped water body whose depth from bed to crest is under 0.5 × the dam height gets the full-height inventory curve instead, with a warning. In the hand-off, no AREA_AC is sent when the curve spans under 0.5 × the height, so the Runner uses m = 2.
- **#14:** Tool 7b now sends the package's `structure_points` instead of the structure polygons when no structures layer is named.
- **#15 (10-15):** **Tool 7b treats unknown-like classes as no class.** These values count as no class (any letter case): "", NoData, No data, NULL, None, nan, Unknown, Undetermined, Not determined, Undet, TBD, To be determined, N/A, NA, ?, Unclassified, Not classified, Pending. High, Medium and Low are matched in any letter case. A new mode, "High, Medium, Low and unknown (all classified dams)", was added as option 5; the existing mode numbers 0-4 are unchanged.
- **#19 (10-15):** Fixed test failures on QGIS 3.34 (numpy trapz, test_runner import).

## Shared suite (damsuite, all plugins)
- **#12 (10-15):** **Safe settings saves.** Saving now re-reads the file and merges in only this window's changes, refuses to overwrite a file it couldn't read (unless "Save as" replaces it), skips the write when nothing changed, writes atomically, keeps a `.bak` copy, and uses a `.lock` file (a stale lock is taken over after 30 s; a save gives up after waiting 10 s).
- **#16 (10-15):** First steps to simplify the plugin windows ("your values first", one home, Data plugin fixes).
- **#17 (10-15):** `os.scandir` iterators are now closed (no ResourceWarning).
- **#18 (10-15):** **Standard work-folder layout** (`damsuite/layout.py`). See USER_GUIDE.md.

## Archived metadata changelog — DamSafety_Hazard

Entries moved verbatim out of `DamSafety_Hazard/metadata.txt` `changelog=` on 2026-11-07 (QGIS's Plugin Manager parses and renders the whole `changelog=` value of every installed plugin, so `tools/ci_checks.py` now caps it at 12 KB and keeps the newest entries there). Newest first; the indentation is the metadata.txt continuation indent.

```text
    2.23.4 (2026-11-04; GUI check of 2026-11-04, Nick approved: Dam suite home (shared/ui/suite_home.py) no longer has a fixed 900 px minimum width; it opens at 960 x 600 where the screen has room and shrinks to an 800 x 600 screen, never below its button rows)
    2.23.3 (2026-11-03; GUI fit, QGIS Dev: runnerculverts labels shortened (detail in the help), OSM_SEARCH_FT / SEARCH_FT with 1 decimal and a 3280.8 ft maximum, 'for DamSafety Runner' wording, 'not 3DHP' stream direction; Classify tab's four tick boxes shortened to fit 640 x 480 (detail in the tooltips); the suite settings grid shows the parameters' help and names the tool on a label several tools share; the suite export's flowlines layer is described as USGS 3DHP flowlines (NHD in older packs). Tests: tests/test_guifix_20261103.py)
    2.23.2 (2026-11-03; built as 2.23.0, renumbered after #261 took 2.23.1; DWR-10554 run on main 33a93c0, Nick: "do culvert ideas"): Runner culverts - OpenStreetMap culverts / fords are placed only on a stream x road / rail crossing within the new osm_search_m (OSM_SEARCH_FT, default 60 m = 196.9 ft; bridges keep search_m, the smaller wins); an OSM culvert with no railway tag takes the nearest crossing of any road or rail line (was: a road first); notes name the OSM way and say mapped, size not surveyed. Test: tests/test_runnerculverts_osm.py
    2.23.1 (2026-11-03; audit fix E, lint only: unused imports removed (QCheckBox in seven tabs, lone911 QgsGeometry). No behaviour change.)
    2.22.6 (2026-11-03; audit fix D, wording sync with the nd Hazard Helper. Nick: "3DHP" - the travel-time channel flowlines are downloaded from the USGS 3DHP service (3DHP_all/MapServer/50, was the NHD service nhd/MapServer/6) and get the NHD properties gnis_name, ftype ... (damsuite.hydro3dhp.normaliser_for_url, as the Helper 1.19.0); the labels say '3DHP flowlines'. The dz log line says ft/s (was fps). Waterbodies stay on the NHD service.)
    2.22.5 (2026-11-03; audit fix C, option C: a structure's DV (DV_Max_ft2s, Graham severity) is its peak depth x the peak velocity over its wet cells - the pair its zone is classified on - never a DV raster's value; velocity unknown at every wet cell: DV None (depth-only severity, 'velocity unknown - no DV') and a Notes line. A NaN velocity on a wet cell is filled with the structure's peak velocity (was 0, optimistic), Notes 'filled with the structure's peak velocity, else 0) - verify' - as the nd Hazard Helper. Test tests/test_dv_peak_pair.py.)
    2.22.4 (2026-11-03; DSS-WISE / hand-export names with units after the quantity - Depth_ft, Velocity_ftps, Depth_m, Velocity_ft_per_s - are found in the dam folder (were skipped as time steps) and their units read from the name; metric names in a dam folder are flagged. Test test_dsswise_unit_names.py.)
    2.22.3 (2026-11-01; option C, Nick: a structure's zone is classified on its peak depth with the peak velocity over its wet cells (structures.peak_pair), not cell by cell; can only keep or raise the zone. Margins from the same pair. Test test_peak_depth_peak_velocity.py.)
    2.22.2 (2026-10-30; fixes from the 2026-10-30 full test - Nick. 2.22.1 is #244)
    * Road / rail crown check: a wet stretch with no value on the arrival raster is no longer skipped ("skipped: no arrival time"). It goes through the depth / crown checks like any other and is counted with no warning time (conservative); its Note / QA Note starts "arrival unknown". On the full-test inputs ND00199 was Low because 123 roads and 2 rail stretches were skipped this way; it is now High (7 overtopped stretches on State highways with AADT > 2,000).
    * Classify with every Download box off downloads nothing: the OSM mobile-home parks, Microsoft footprints and NAIP dates were still fetched. CONFIG_JSON can still turn one on. Batch and watch the same.
    * Report and mapbook legends: setAutoUpdateModel is gone in QGIS 4; compat.legend_manual uses setSyncMode(Manual) there and setAutoUpdateModel(False) on QGIS 3.
    * Tests: tests/test_line_crown_check.py (updated), test_classify_no_downloads.py, test_legend_sync.py.
    2.22.1 (2026-10-30; report text from the DWR-4031 toe10m run and the #241 re-test)
    * Summary crossings note: "Max_Depth_ft is the <cell> depth cell" gives the depth grid's actual cell size (10 m on the toe10m run; was a hard-coded "30 m").
    * 5.3(f) is cited (judgment-zone reason, PROVISIONAL note) and counted only for borderline structures actually moved to the higher class. With the old ND judgment-zone option on, DWR-2801 cited "5.3(f): 1 borderline structure(s)" for house 4026, which stayed in the low zone. The 5.3(f) criteria row still lists borderline structures. No class change.
    * Tests and changelog: the DWR-4031 toe-run judgment-zone house is Struct_ID 9953 (2.111 ft at 6.95 ft/s), not 16775 (a dry house in that folder; Struct_IDs differ by footprint set).
    2.22.0 (2026-10-30; a house in the ACER judgment zone is ND MEDIUM by rule - Nick)
    * Nick: "any house in the judgement zone should be automatically labeled as medium hazard". A habitable structure in the ACER judgment zone (between the judgment and high-danger boundaries) makes the dam ND MEDIUM by rule [5.2.1.1]; it is not probable loss of life and no longer makes a provisional HIGH. Houses in the high danger zone still make it HIGH. The summary says "classed MEDIUM by rule".
    * The old setting (judgment zone counted as probable loss of life, HIGH) is kept as an option, off by default (Nick): Classify > "ND: count ACER judgment-zone houses as probable loss of life (HIGH)", Processing ND_JUDGMENT_HIGH, setting nd_judgment_as_probable. The existing JUDGMENT / "judgment zone" setting now applies to ACER TM 11 lives in jeopardy only (default on), so the TM 11 class is unchanged and saved dam states that had it on do not bring back HIGH.
    * Tests: tests/test_judgment_zone_medium_rule.py.
    2.21.1 (2026-10-30; summary wording from the DWR-4031 review - Nick)
    * "Without judgment-zone items" gives the ND class as well as TM 11's, both labelled: "Low (TM 11 Table 1); ND class: MEDIUM" (DWR-4031: the line said Low, which is TM 11; without the judgment-zone house the ND class is MEDIUM from 5.2.2 and 5.2.1.2). Also a summary table row and the form key ND_WITHOUT_JUDGMENT.
    * The 5.3(f) borderline rule is cited in the reasons and the PROVISIONAL note only when the 5.3(f) borderline count is above 0. With 0 the note says High stays because the judgment zone is counted as probable loss of life. No class changes.
    * Tests: tests/test_summary_judgment_4031.py.
    2.21.0 (2026-10-30; name a dam and add its model results - Nick)
    * Classify > Setup: "Add model results to a dam…" and the Processing tool ndhazard:adddamresults (a CSV for many dams). Type the dam's ID or name; its folder in the Dams folder is found by folder name or dam.json ID / name (case, spaces, dashes and an ID's leading zeros ignored) or made with a dam.json. DSS-WISE Lite zips / folders, HEC-RAS RAS Mapper rasters or a .p##.hdf plan, and LISFLOOD-FP .max / .maxVc / .Vmax / .inittm / .mxe grids go to <dam>/Hazard_inputs/Breach_<MODEL> (No_Breach_<MODEL> for the baseline), where the batch finds them. Never overwrites (next free number) and never touches the source; results_provenance.json records source, sha256, model, time and version; dam.json gets the status modelled. No classification change.
    * Tests: tests/test_add_dam_results.py.
    2.20.4 (2026-10-29; the vertical datum section uses the 0-500 ft band)
    * The summary still prints the Runner's record. The local band is 0-500 ft (damsuite 1.18.4, Runner 3.12.9): 301-500 ft is local and tied to the DEM, never VERTCON-shifted, whatever datum is stated. The heading is VERTICAL DATUM OF THE DAM ELEVATIONS. No classification change.
    2.20.3 (2026-10-29; the offline pack's HIFLD lifelines are read - Nick)
    * The lifeline keys with no download address (lifeline_hifld_fire / ems / eoc / police / wastewater) resolve to the offline pack's HIFLD_* layers (constants.LIFELINE_PACK_SETS: HIFLD_fire_ems_stations for fire and EMS - read once, HIFLD_local_eoc, HIFLD_local_law_enforcement, HIFLD_wastewater_treatment_plants), format 1 and format 2; the old lifeline_<key> names still work. Used by the run's lifeline fetch (pipeline, datafetch), suiteexport and the reproduction file order.
    * Vendored damsuite 1.18.3 (format-1 pack readers, flood_zones claims no pack layer); offline_pack unpacks a zipped format-1 pack's rasters (NLCD) beside the sqlite.
    * Tests: tests/test_pack_lifelines.py.
    2.20.2 (2026-10-29; offline pack engine 1.3: area-by-area ArcGIS reads)
    * build_nd_pack 1.3 (shared with DamSafety Data 1.5.1): new data-set kind arcgis_tiled - object ids listed per 0.25 degree tile, a tile that answers HTTP 504 / times out is quartered after a pause (down to 4 levels), and only the areas a service cannot serve are read from a second service (one source per place, tile edges not doubled, the source on every feature). A page refused with HTTP 400 (FEMA USA_Structures) is tried again with smaller pages and fails with a plain message. A DWR GeoPackage note gives the file's own date (GeoPackage last change), not the day it was copied. No classification changes.
    2.20.1 (2026-10-28; local datums in the vertical datum section)
    * Vendored damsuite 1.18.1: the VERTICAL DATUM section shows a local (assumed) datum - e.g. a top of dam of 100.0 ft - as "not converted; elevations taken from the DEM", the DEM top minus the local top, each local value against the NAVD88 value the Runner used, and the warning (Runner 3.12.1).
    2.20.0 (2026-10-28; vertical datum section in the hazard summary)
    * The hazard summary gains VERTICAL DATUM OF THE DAM ELEVATIONS (NGVD29 -> NAVD88): the source datum, whether it was stated or assumed, the VERTCON shift and method, read from the Runner run_report.json or dam.json (Runner 3.12.0). Nothing is shown when no record has it.
    * Tests: tests/test_vertical_datum_summary.py.
    2.19.1 - 2026-10-28 (smoke-test fixes of 2.19.0: map labels checked before use, offline flag, metadata)
      * Map labels on the report / map book are used only when one label tile answers (basemap.label_checks: 3 s, cached like the imagery checks); a dead label host no longer makes each layout export wait ~2 min, and the credit and DATA SOURCES name labels only when they are on the maps (the failed label service is listed under "not used").
      * A service check that ran to its timeout says it timed out; the DATA SOURCES line lists the sources that were not used once (it listed them twice under a hillshade base map); the check cache is keyed by the URL plus the WMS layer / format / CRS.
      * ND_DAM_OFFLINE=0 / false / off is no longer offline (base map and downloads; the Runner's 1 / true / yes / on rule); ND_DAM_SUITE set still is.
      * Offline hillshade: 2x exaggeration on a projected DEM, converted from degrees on a geographic one (it was a fixed z factor of 2).
      * metadata.txt: the 2.18.66 entry was after hasProcessingProvider=yes, which made it 'yes' plus the entry, so qgis_process skipped the Hazard provider - back in the changelog; every percent sign is doubled in the file (QGIS reads metadata.txt with configparser interpolation: a bare one emptied the changelog in the Plugin Manager); constants.PLUGIN_VERSION once.
    2.19.0 - 2026-10-28 (aerial basemap on report and map book)
      * Aerial imagery under the maps of the PDF report and the EAP map book, on by default: ND DWR NAIP 2025 (WMS; skipped outside its seamlines), then Esri World Imagery, then USGS imagery. Street and town labels over the maps (only over online imagery). A credit line under each map (source and flight dates) and a "Map base:" line in DATA SOURCES.
      * Settings tab: "Aerial imagery under the maps of the PDF report and EAP map book", "Street and town labels over the maps" and "Imagery source:" (Automatic (NAIP 2025, then Esri, then USGS) or Only <one source>). Data-source file: AERIAL_SOURCES, AERIAL_SOURCE_ORDER, AERIAL_LABEL_URLS (AERIAL_IMAGERY_URL still read).
      * Offline (a configured or discovered data pack): the pack's imagery (<pack>/imagery/*.tif, *.tiff, *.mbtiles), else a hillshade of the plan DEM, else no base map with a note. Fast runs and ensemble members keep imagery off. The Review tab's zoom imagery is unchanged.
    2.18.69 - 2026-10-28 (crossing depth in the 1 m channel - DWR-3034 review)
      * Every wet bridge / culvert / crossing of a Runner result also gets the depth in the channel on the run's 1 m DEM: WSE (Max) minus the lowest 1 m ground within 50 ft of the crossing (the same WSE (Max).tif and run_report.json DEM as the 2.18.65 crown check). Bridges_Culverts_Crossings carries Channel_Depth_1m_ft, Channel_Bed_ft and Channel_Basis after Source, the summary names it and the run log counts it. For a ford or a culvert road it is the water on the crossing; under a bridge it is the channel below the deck. Max_Depth_ft stays the 30 m cell and still decides Flooded / Charged. DWR-3034: the ford read 3.24 ft on its 30 m cell vs ~9.4 ft over the 1 m channel bed. Test: tests/test_crossing_channel_depth.py.
    2.18.68 - 2026-10-28 (offline-only runs say why Microsoft footprints are missing - DWR-3034 review)
      * In offline-only mode (ND_DAM_HAZARD_OFFLINE_ONLY / the offline-only setting) Microsoft footprints are never fetched: the offline data pack does not carry them and no internet request is made. The building-geometry note (DATA SOURCES / judgment) and the run log now say so and how to get them (turn offline-only off for the run) instead of "the download failed and there is no earlier copy (offline?)"; the NAIP flight-date fallback likewise. DWR-3034's FEMA-only geometry was this case (pack nd_offline_pack_20261021, offline only); DWR-2801's run with ND_DAM_HAZARD_OFFLINE_ONLY=false downloaded them. Test: tests/test_footprint_geometry.py.
    2.18.67 - 2026-10-28 (one OSM crossing mapped as a node and a way counts once - DWR-3034 review)
      * Two OpenStreetMap crossings of one source that share a node, or lie within 5 m with the same category or the same changeset, are one structure (facilities.osm_same_crossing); the kept row lists both ids in Structure_No. DWR-3034's ford node/11121114805 + way/1198845155 (1.6 m apart) was two rows in Bridges_Culverts_Crossings.csv and two crossings in the Summary. Twin structures further apart, NBI / OSM pairs and other sources are unchanged.
      * The OSM download asks Overpass for 'out center meta' and keeps a way's node list and the changeset (osm_nodes / osm_changeset); an older pack file without them is merged by distance + category. Test: tests/test_osm_crossing_dedupe.py.
    2.18.66 - 2026-10-27 (ND DWR GeoPackages in the offline pack: build_nd_pack --dwr-folder)
```
