```text
      * build_nd_pack.py --dwr-folder PATH (and build(..., dwr_folder=)): the ND DWR GeoPackages of that folder (DWR_Dams.gpkg, DWR_Dam_Lines.gpkg, DWR_Unknown_Structures.gpkg, DWR_Dugouts.gpkg, DWR_Dugouts_Polygons.gpkg; the layer each is picked by the suite folder rules) become the data sets DWR_dams, DWR_dam_lines, DWR_unknown_structures, DWR_dugouts and DWR_dugout_polygons (group dwr, kind local_gpkg): reprojected to the pack's lon / lat in pure Python (new dwr_files.py: EPSG:2265 / 2266 with the file's own unit - DWR's files are international feet -, 32120 / 32121, UTM 13 / 14 N, 5070, lon / lat), every attribute kept, the source file, layer, CRS, date and row count in the manifest note; rows with no geometry and features wholly outside North Dakota (kept) are counted. Read again on every run. Without the option the build is the same as before. Run it as python3 -m DamSafety_Hazard.build_nd_pack.
      * The DWR layers are read from the active format-2 pack when the suite folder has no DWR file of its own (damsuite 1.17.7); the engineer's own file always wins.
    2.18.65 - 2026-10-27 (roads and rail counted over the centreline crown - DWR-2801 review)
      * A wet road or rail stretch counts as overtopped only where the maximum WSE is above the centreline crown read from the 1 m DEM (WSE (Max).tif beside the depth raster, the Runner's 1 m DEM from _breach_model/run_report.json); the toe cells beside an embankment no longer count. Stretches with no arrival time and stretches shallower than 0.1 ft are skipped. Structure cuts (bridge / notch in structures.json) are masked the same way as the Runner's 3.10.90 road profile (the same road_profile module).
      * Roads_Inundated and Rail_Inundated carry Depth_Basis, Grid_Depth_ft, Crown_ft, WSE_ft and Freeboard_ft (the basis note in Notes); Max_Depth_ft is the overtopping over the crown. Every wet stretch, counted or not, and why: outputs/QA/Line_Crown_Check (GPKG / CSV, also in the submittal). No WSE or no 1 m DEM: the depth raster as before, with a note in the run log and on every stretch. Setting line_crown_check (on by default).
    2.18.64 - 2026-10-26 (offline pack check never raises; a broken pack stops an offline-only run - #171 follow-up)
      * A manifest.json that cannot be read (half written, not JSON / UTF-8, not an object, a section - datasets / rasters / left_out - of the wrong type, saved with a UTF-8 byte-order mark - Notepad / PowerShell 5 - that the pack readers refuse) is a FAILED check result, never an exception and never a silent "not a pack"; NSI_structures then counts as missing, so an Offline-only run stops with a clear error (online: a warning, NSI is downloaded). One entry of the wrong type (not an object, a non-text file) is a named manifest warning for that layer (an error when the layer is required).
      * An Offline-only run also stops when the pack cannot be opened, whatever its check says (a zip member with a bad CRC, a format-1 zip without nd_offline_pack.sqlite, a format_version the reader refuses: the check passed and the run went on without NSI), and the run log no longer calls such a pack "OK"; and when no check result could be made. The run log and the error give the reader's reason (after the check's when the check FAILED: a broken manifest.json is named first, then the nd_offline_pack.sqlite the reader looked for instead), and a path with no pack at all (moved or deleted, an empty folder) is named "not an offline data pack" and "cannot be opened", not a check that "found no problem"; a format-1 .sqlite with no manifest.json beside it is not said to be checked against one.
      * The check summary names a problem of the pack itself (a format-1 pack's broken manifest.json) instead of "missing: " with no layer name.
      * A cut-short (half-written) or garbage GeoPackage is named "cannot be read" (SQLite header, page size and file length) instead of passing the quick check.
      * A zipped pack is checked by its listing (every listed file in the zip, not empty).
      * The run signature includes each listed layer file's size and time, so a GeoPackage replaced in place is a new evaluation.
      * Suite package 1.17.6 (packcheck). Tests: tests/test_packcheck_malformed.py, tests/test_pack_offline_block.py.
    2.18.63 - 2026-10-26 (HEC-RAS plan import: the newest Runner re-export of the plan's current results decides; the cell-minimum note follows the suite default - with Runner 3.10.84)
      * Without a terrain raster the import looks for the ND Dam Breach Runner's per-pixel ('terrain') rasters of the plan. It took the newest _hecras/reexport_* that said terrain, so an older terrain re-export (Runner 3.10.79 to 3.10.83) still won over a newer Runner 3.10.84 re-export of the same plan on the cell minimum, and a re-export made before the model was run again was taken for the new results (the Runner writes the same _hecras/<plan>.hdf again). Now only re-exports of the plan's current results count (the results HDF's size and time stamp recorded in reexport.json by Runner 3.10.84, else the HDF not newer than reexport.json, 5 s tolerance), and the newest of them decides: 'terrain' is used, anything else stops the search (then the dam folder's rasters when hecras_stage.json says terrain and they are current, else the cell minimum).
      * The cell-minimum note (tool log, plan import tab, run log) no longer calls the cell minimum an overstatement to be re-exported away: it is the Runner's default since 3.10.84 (conservative: a ditch or channel in a cell deepens the whole cell); for per-pixel depth give the terrain raster, or re-export with ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport --depth-basis terrain.
      * Tests: tests/test_rasimport_terrain_arrival.py (newest re-export decides; a re-export of results since replaced is not used).
    2.18.62 - 2026-10-25 (reproduce / data folder: every file of every role is loaded, or a warning)
      * Reproduce: the package's data sets were wrapped twice, so a role recorded as one file (roads, rail, NSI, FEMA USA Structures, ...) was skipped WITHOUT a word and a role with several files (AADT: HPMS + county roads; bridges: NBI + OSM) opened only its first. Now one shape for every role ([[path, category], ...]), old and new packages alike, and every file is handed to the run.
      * AADT, bridges, NSI, address points, USA Structures, outlines and lifelines read every file of the data folder; another role with several files reads the first and says which were not used.
      * Run_Settings.json says its inputs are as the run read them ("inputs_format": 2, written by both forks - ND Hazard 2.34.5 too): traffic, crossings and lifelines in the order the run reads them. A record without it (either fork, e.g. ND Hazard 2.34.4) lists every download in the order it finished: reproduce and the ensemble hand over what the run read - newest HPMS year, else NDDOT counts, then the county roads; NBI before the OSM crossings; data-folder, agency, then OSM lifelines (the first of two duplicates is kept) - decided from the whole record, so a file the run read that is gone is missing, never replaced by an older HPMS year.
      * A missing, unreadable or malformed file is a WARNING in the run log, and in the reproduction log, check (Input rows 'Loaded' / 'Not loaded') and summary - never a silent skip; a one-file role is one warning. The review-package checklist (C13) names the package data not loaded.
    2.18.61 - 2026-10-25 (HEC-RAS plan import: arrival on the same depth basis as the depth)
      * 'Import HEC-RAS plan results' (ndhazard:hecrasplan, plan scenarios): with a terrain raster, the arrival time is per pixel from the cell's water surface minus the terrain (>= the arrival depth, interpolated between output steps; reached only between steps: the time of the maximum), as the Runner 3.10.79 (#173). Before, arrival used the cell-minimum depth.
      * No terrain raster: the Runner's terrain-based rasters of the plan are used (newest _hecras/reexport_*, or the dam folder's rasters when hecras_stage.json says depth_basis terrain); else the cell minimum with a warning.
      * The basis (terrain / runner_reexport / cell_min) is in depth_basis.json, the plan cache manifest, the tool log and the run log. The REG_05.2026 workbook is unchanged.
    2.18.60 - 2026-10-25 (format-2 offline packs; every pack checked against its manifest.json)
      * The Offline pack box now takes a format-2 pack: a folder of GeoPackages (one per layer) with manifest.json and LAYERS.txt, such as nd_Pack_20261014 or ND_Dam_Suite_Pack (Browse accepts its manifest.json). It is read by the suite's reader (damsuite.gpkgpack) under the same data set names as a format-1 pack; NHD_flowlines / NHD_waterbodies also answer to the 3DHP_ layers of newer packs. Format 1 (nd_offline_pack.sqlite / .zip) is read as before. Choosing a suite folder whose pack is format 2 also fills the Offline pack box when it is empty.
      * On load the pack is checked against its manifest.json (damsuite.packcheck, read-only): every listed layer present, with its feature count, fields, geometry type and CRS (EPSG:4326). Missing or different layers are named in the QGIS log and message bar, in the pack status line and Check inputs, and in the run log. A missing NSI_structures (the structure inventory) is an error: offline only, the run stops with a clear message; online, it is downloaded instead. Before, a layer whose file was gone was skipped without a word.
      * The evaluation signature of a format-2 pack folder includes its manifest.json, so an updated pack is a new evaluation.
    2.18.59 - 2026-10-22 (a dam's Hazard state saved to reopen)
      * New Processing tool 'Save a dam's Hazard window state (project to reopen)' (ndhazard:savedamstate; damstate.py), run by the Runner's batch chain after each dam's classification: writes <dam folder>/Hazard_<ID>.qgz (a new project with the dam's flood rasters, its model boundary and the Hazard window's state: scenario table, folders and options) plus a readable Hazard_<ID>.hazard_state.json. To reopen: Project > Open… the .qgz, open the Hazard window (it fills itself from the project), add scenarios with 'Add scenario' / 'Add from a folder…' and press Classify. The open project is never changed. Nothing in the classification changed.
    2.18.58 - 2026-10-22 (HEC-RAS 2D flow area as the batch's model boundary)
      * 'Import HEC-RAS plan results' (and ndhazard:hecrasplan, new output BOUNDARY) writes the plan's 2D flow area perimeter(s) to model_boundary_2d_area.gpkg next to the rasters. ndhazard:batch has a new 'Model boundary for the model edge check' option (BOUNDARY / BOUNDARY_FILE): each dam folder's 2D flow area polygon when present, else the automatic check (default unchanged). New setting model_boundary_file (a file name, relative to the dam folder), also passed to the parallel workers; a missing or unreadable file falls back to the automatic check and says so in the log.
    2.18.57 - 2026-10-21 (tool dialogs stay in front of the window)
      * Tool dialogs opened from the window (the Dam suite tab's tools) are owned by the window and modal (new shared/ui/tool_dialog.py). Before, QGIS's execAlgorithmDialog owned them from its main window and showed them before exec_(), so they were not modal: a click on the plugin window raised it over the tool, and from the Runner's 'Model one dam' wizard (modal itself) the DEM download could not be clicked at all, the wizard popped up in front of it. Nothing in the classification changed.
    2.18.56 - 2026-10-21 (restored 911 points H1, model edge with a no-data ring, DWR-8278 inventory follow-ups; on 2.18.55; numbered 2.18.50 then 2.18.54 on its branch)
      * Review (Docs & QA 2026-10-21): a lone 911 point (Placement not Structure) with no Microsoft footprint within 300 ft is unverified (not counted) - "no building within 300 ft in Microsoft footprints (imagery ...)" - unless its 911 DateUpdate is newer than the footprint imagery (then counted, "possibly built after the footprint imagery"). Only when Microsoft footprints are loaded. Microsoft's US footprints publish no capture dates, so a footprint's capture_dates_range is used when present (now kept on download) and 2022-12-31 is assumed otherwise (MS_IMAGERY_ASSUMED_DATE, screening default). Absaraka #721 (3550 153rd Ave SE, 1,271 ft): unverified.
      * Placement=Parcel alone no longer makes a lone 911 point unverified (28 %% of restored points are parcel centroids): it is counted and flagged "Placement=Parcel (parcel centroid, not the building) - counted; verify"; the 300 ft rule still applies to it.
      * Review 2 (Docs & QA): the Microsoft imagery date the 300 ft rule assumes is a setting (ms_imagery_assumed_date, default 2022-12-31 pending Nick's decision; Hazard window option 'Microsoft imagery assumed date'). Whenever the rule drops points, DATA SOURCES and a judgment item give the count, the assumed date and the 911 record date range; every dropped point wet in a High or Judgment zone is named (structure, address, depth, zone, 911 date) with the ND / TM 11 class and LIJ it would give if counted. The class is not changed.
      * Review 2 nits: the always-on LIJ line says the lone points' share is zone-weighted and gives their persons; the H1 note says both distances (100 ft: no FEMA/NSI building; 300 ft: a Microsoft footprint, so the no-building rule does not apply).
      * Review 3 (Nick): the imagery date of the lone-911 300 ft rule is the USDA NAIP 2025 flight date at each 911 point (ND GIS Hub Imagery/Dates_Imagery seamlines, layer 41; one query by the run's extent, outputs/_downloads, never the offline pack; setting naip_flight_dates). A 911 record newer than that flight keeps the 'possibly built after the imagery' exemption. ms_imagery_assumed_date is now only the fallback (offline, failed download, suite folder, a point outside the seamlines); the fallback and why are said in DATA SOURCES and the judgment items, as with FEMA-only geometry. DATA SOURCES give the seamline count and the flight date range.
      * Review 3: possibly misplaced 911 points - each lone 911 point is matched to its own street in the NG9-1-1 road centerlines (St_Name / St_PosTyp / St_PosDir, the address range holding Add_Number with its parity) and the address location interpolated along it; farther than misplaced_911_m (150 m) is a judgment item ('possibly misplaced: N m from its address on <street>; expected location near <lat,lon>') and a Notes entry. A dropped point with a Microsoft footprint within 300 ft of its address location is a review item, not a silent drop (#721: 531 m from 153RD AVE SE).
      * Review 3: NAIP 2025 clips (about 600 m square, red marker at the 911 point, yellow ring at its address location) of every wet High / Judgment-zone point the rule drops or keeps as newer, in outputs/QA/imagery/<structure id>_naip2025.jpg and named in the judgment item; at most naip_clip_max (25) per run, 20 s timeout per clip, a failure only leaves the clip out (setting naip_clips). download.http_get_bytes takes an optional timeout_s.
      * 911 NOTES non-dwelling words: business, commercial, office, shop, warehouse, church, school, garage, shed, bin(s), elevator, lift station, pump, sign, billboard, cemetery, park, temporary address, and "for new <X>" where X is not a dwelling word (793 19th St S "for new Dairy Queen"; "for new residence" is not caught).
      * Co-located points of different sources: two groups that each already hold a point of the same source are not joined (pairs joined nearest first), so NSI-A / 911 / NSI-B no longer chain two houses into one.
      * Judgment items: "LIJ x includes y from N 911 address point(s) with no building ..." whenever such points add to the LIJ, even when the class does not depend on them.
      * 911 address points with no building (H1 default): one whose site is already counted - a habitable structure with the same (normalised) address on its FEMA parcel record within 1,000 ft, or a habitable NSI residential (RES*) structure within 150 ft that has no other 911 address - is the same dwelling (sampled and listed, not a second household; HEC-RAS tier2 #761 / #733; self-test: the PARKING address point P is now the same site as house H, 57 checks). One whose 911 NOTES name a non-residential use (station, tower, communications, store, restaurant, farm building, substation, utility ...), whose address number is 0 or whose Placement is Parcel is unverified (not counted). The reason is in Notes.
      * Notes: the parcel / site (M3) outbuilding reason and the lone 911 point reason are written to the Notes column. When the ND or TM 11 class rests on lone 911 points the judgment items and the TM 11 notes say so (with the class without them); the class is not changed.
      * Model extent: for a Runner result the model domain is read from the run folder (_breach_model) - the ANUGA mesh boundary (anuga/mesh_boundary.csv) when ANUGA gave the final rasters, the LISFLOOD-FP grid (a no-data edge cell is dry) when LISFLOOD-FP did. A depth raster whose outer ring holds no data and that has no known domain is reported "Model extent NOT checked" (judgment item) instead of "does not reach the edge (0 edge cells checked)". (Absaraka anuga_structures: the mesh edge is wet to 8.4 ft.)
      * Points outside every outline: points of different sources within 30 ft of each other are snapped together, with the shortest reach of the group (DWR-8278: a 911 point 0.2 m from an NSI point is no longer moved 35 ft while the NSI point stays).
      * A parcel whose 911 point was moved within 3 ft of the 30 ft site-anchor limit is a judgment item when a building on it is wet (DWR-8278: 29.4 ft), with a Note.
    2.18.55 - 2026-10-21 (suite hand-off refresh; numbered 2.18.40 and 2.18.47 on its branch; merged with main 2.18.52, after #133 2.18.53 and #134 2.18.54)
      * Suite hand-off: the window has the refresh hook the Dam suite home calls after it hands a dam over (suite_home.point_window_at). Handing over the dam the window already showed (opened before the Runner finished, or restored from the last session) left the raster boxes empty, since the unchanged dam folder fired no textChanged; the empty boxes are now filled from the Runner's Depth (Max) / Velocity (Max) / Arrival Time rasters. Rasters chosen by hand are kept, nothing runs by itself, nothing during a run. No result changes. tests/test_suite_handoff.py.
    2.18.53 - 2026-10-21 (building geometry: Microsoft footprints, FEMA scene offsets; numbered 2.18.49 on its branch)
      * Review (Docs & QA): the single-wide shape is tested on every outline of a building - the traced FEMA / OSM outline first, then the Microsoft footprint with 3 ft more width (setting mobile_ms_width_allowance_ft; ML outlines are wider: 9250 56th Ave SW FEMA 16.4 x 81 ft, Microsoft 20.4 x 78.9 ft). Without it mobile homes on Microsoft geometry were typed houses (non-conservative).
      * Review: when Microsoft footprints are unavailable (suite folder, offline with no earlier copy, download off) DATA SOURCES and the judgment items say 'Building geometry: FEMA only - Microsoft footprints unavailable (why)', not only the log.
      * Building geometry: Microsoft's Global ML Building Footprints (ODbL) are downloaded for the study area into outputs/_downloads (new setting download_ms_footprints, on; never into the offline data pack) and give the building outlines; the FEMA USA Structures outlines are matched onto them (overlap, or the nearest footprint of a similar size within 33 ft) so their parcel address and occupancy still drive the parcel / site (M3) rule. A footprint layer in the data folder or project is recognised by its name and used the same way. setting outline_geometry "listed" keeps the old order. Without footprints the FEMA outlines are used as before.
      * FEMA scene-offset check: the FEMA outlines of each source image (IMAGE_NAME) are compared with the Microsoft footprints, else the NSI points; a scene offset by more than 5 m (spread at most 5 m, at least 5 pairs) is listed in Inventory_QA, the run log and the judgment items. Its outlines are moved by the measured offset only with the new setting fema_scene_shift (off); otherwise another source's outline of the building gives the geometry where one exists. (DWR-8278: scene 1030010045BF2F00 offset 14.6 m SW.)
    2.18.52 - 2026-10-20 (US / State highways, REG_05.2026 5.2.1.2(b); blank arrival times explained; on #130; numbered 2.18.48 on its branch)
      * Roads: a bare 'Highway 23' / 'Hwy 2' (the ND NG9-1-1 names) and 'North Dakota Highway 200' are State highways (Road_Class 'State Highway'); 'County Highway 12' / 'Co Hwy 12' / 'Township Highway' stay local. US / State highways were already at least Medium whatever their AADT; the basis now cites 5.2.1.2(b), and the criteria table has a 5.2.1.2(b) row (US / State highways overtopped - at least Medium whatever the AADT). The same texts as the Runner / Screening nd_hazard 1.2.0 (parity test tests/test_highway_floor_reg05.py).
      * Rule compare passes the US / State highway flag to nd_hazard 1.2.0, so the two rules agree on these roads.
      * Habitable_Structures: a wet structure whose First_Arrival_hr is blank says why in Notes - the arrival raster has no value at its wet cells (an arrival raster records when the depth first exceeds the model's arrival depth: Runner ARRIVAL_FT, 0.1 ft by default from Runner 3.10.53, 1 ft before - so an older result leaves structures flooded 0.1-1 ft deep blank and needs a re-run). No value is invented.
      * A flagged structure (DWR-3161) is still refused before any road rule (test).
    2.18.51 - 2026-10-20 (hazard tables against REG_05.2026 Section 5; numbered 2.18.47 on its branch, which #112 also uses)
      * Habitable_Structures has two new columns after ACER_Zone_Label: Danger_Zone, the zone name of REG_05.2026 Figures 5.1 / 5.2 (HIGH DANGER ZONE / JUDGMENT ZONE / LOW DANGER ZONE; empty when dry or for a 1D model), and Danger_Chart, the relationship that decided it (Fig. 5.1 houses built on foundations, Fig. 5.2 mobile homes, or the 1D depth > 2 ft rule of 5.2.1.1(d)). ACER_Zone_Label keeps its short values (HIGH / JUDGMENT / LOW / DRY).
      * The XLSX tables fill the zone cells (ACER_Zone_Label, Danger_Zone) with the figures' colours: #EF9897 / #F9F4A3 / #C4E0AD (xlsx_zone_fill.py; values unchanged).
      * The criteria table and summary list 5.2.1.2(a): overtopped private roads or roads with AADT < 500 (Low). The summary's TM 11 row says it is the ACER TM 11 Table 1 reference class, not the ND class (REG_05.2026 classes are High / Medium / Low).
      * No class, threshold or default changes. tests/test_hazard_tables_reg05.py checks the headers, zone names, units, fills and labels.
    2.18.46 - 2026-10-20 (REG_05.2026 zone colours)
      * The ACER TM 11 danger zones use the colours of REG_05.2026 Figures 5.1 / 5.2: High #EF9897, Judgment #F9F4A3, Low #C4E0AD (were #F44336 / #FFEB3B / #4CAF50). Zone rasters (apply_acer11_style and styles/acer_zones.qml, now at full opacity: 0.75 lightened them), structure points by ACER zone (styles.COLORS, styles/acer_zone_points.qml) and the chart sign-off overlay, which now fills the low zone too (it was white). The zone raster legend says JUDGMENT ZONE (was JUDGEMENT). Breakpoints, classes and field values are unchanged. tests/test_zone_colors_reg05.py (also in tools/ci_checks.py) checks every copy.
      * Zone legend labels follow the standard: HIGH DANGER ZONE / JUDGMENT ZONE / LOW DANGER ZONE. apply_acer11_style no longer fails on QGIS 3.34 (invalidateCache only when the renderer has it), so the zone layer repaints.
    2.18.45 - 2026-10-20 (fixes of the 2026-10-19 audit, High findings; rebased onto current main)
      * H1 (default change back, conservative): a 911 address point with no building outline nearby and no NSI record counts as habitable again by the ND site address rule (listed for review); the self-test runs in CI (tests/test_selftest.py).
      * H2: metre / m-per-s depth and velocity rasters are detected (band unit or metadata, HEC-RAS SI Units project, file name); Check inputs ERROR and model-gate FAIL on strong evidence, and the units guard runs BEFORE reprojection so a metric secondary scenario in a foreign CRS is caught, not read as feet.
    2.18.44 - 2026-10-20 (#108)
      * Classify no longer scrolls sideways. Long check boxes (the lifeline-facilities one was 1 250 px on one line) set the minimum width of the whole Classify page, so with the sections open it was 1 530 px wide and the Browse…, Zip…, Import, Read plan and 'Use this plan for the classification' buttons were past the right edge. The check boxes of the Classify view now wrap their text (ui_wrap.WrapCheckBox: still a QCheckBox, same settings and signals; the whole text is clickable), and long combo boxes and the layer lists shrink with the window (the full item is in the list and the tooltip). The page now needs 591 px and fits the window down to its 640 px minimum, at 1280x800, 1024x640 and 2x HiDPI. Click area as on a plain check box: an unwrapped box toggles on its box and text only (not the empty space beside them), a wrapped one on its box and its whole text. Layout only: no setting, default or result changes. tests/test_classify_fit_width.py (also runs on Qt6 / QGIS 4: scoped Qt enums).
    2.18.43 - 2026-10-20 (GUI grammar, part 2)
      * The settings window's legend is two lines: the notation (bold, [YOUR value]) and, quieter under it, the units footnote. tools/gui_lint.py also checks that action buttons carry no icon or ▶ and (on a 1080p display) that no page scrolls sideways. No default changes.
    2.18.42 - 2026-10-20 (GUI grammar, part 1)
      * GUI grammar foundation (docs/gui-grammar.md): window colours from the shared tokens.py; the shared window files have one canonical copy in shared/ui/ (tools/sync_ui.py, checked in CI); tools/gui_lint.py opens every window in real QGIS and checks the rules.
      * Settings tabs show 'Review & QA' (was 'Review _QA'); the settings table keeps Value, Unit and Starting value in view (the Parameter column takes the rest and elides, full text in its tooltip).
      * Plugin icon (icon.svg; the menu entry looked for a missing icon.png). Processing provider named DamSafety Hazard. The Run everything button keeps Qt's look (no painted green). No default changes.
    2.18.39 - 2026-10-19 (PR #94, Claude's audit-fix campaign)
      * M3 (default changes): homes that come only from the National Structure Inventory (NSI) are no longer counted as habitable unless a parcel (fema_parcel) supports them; this can lower a dam's hazard class.
      * H4 (default changes): collapse_dwellings removed, so buildings sharing one 911 / street address each keep their occupants (mobile-home parks, apartments, farmsteads); PAR can go up.
      * H5: damsuite/transformguard.py is installed at plugin load and patches QGIS coordinate transforms for the whole QGIS session (worker-thread layers from parallel_map kept their CRS invalid on 3.34, so roads / rail / lifelines emptied out).
      * M1, M2, M4, M14: incomplete-flag surfacing, Classify busy-guard and the other fixes in docs/Fix_campaign_report.md.
    2.18.38 - 2026-10-19
      * Flagged structures (damsuite/structure_flags.py, docs/Flagged_structures.md): a dam
        of the suite's list (DWR-3161 / ND00597, the ND-23 road fill over backwater, hazard
        Low from the DWR record) is not classified from breach rasters: the run stops with
        the reason, unless allow_flagged_structure / ALLOW_FLAGGED_STRUCTURE=1, when the log
        and DATA SOURCES say the hazard of record stays Low and the computed class is for
        information only. The Dam suite home shows the flag and Low.

      * One ellipsis character (U+2026) on the shared Dam suite home window's

    2.18.36 - 2026-10-18
      * Lumo's Hazard screens review. The status line under the tabs no longer repeats
        the Check inputs result ("Input check: N error(s), M warning(s)" / "Inputs need
        fixing ... see the red box"): the red box and the button badge show it once, and
        an empty status line is hidden.
      * The long help text at the top of Review > Structures and Review > Roads &
        facilities is collapsed by default ("How to review structures" and "How to
        review roads and facilities"; click to open).
      * One ellipsis character (U+2026) instead of '...' in the window's labels, tooltips,
        drop-down items, progress messages, the plugin menu entry (Classify…), the dam
        suite tool buttons and the Processing labels.
      * The window is sized for the screen it opens on (QGIS's screen, else the one under
        the mouse), not always the primary screen; it is placed inside that screen, never
        larger than it (the cap follows the window to another monitor), and the 520 x 400
        floor that could exceed a small screen is gone (ui_fit.py, Hazard copy).
    2.18.35 - 2026-10-18
      * The dam folder must be ONE dam's folder: the path is normalised ('//',
        'Dams//2801/') and the file-system root, the suite's Dams folder, a folder
        above it and any folder that holds dam folders (dam.json, run_report.json or
        a DWR / ND ID name) are refused with a red status line (they were accepted
        and the raster search found another dam's rasters).
      * Switching the dam folder replaces the rasters filled from the previous
        folder (they were kept: 2801's rasters with dam folder 9999); a raster typed
        by hand is kept only when it is inside the new folder, otherwise it is
        cleared with a note in the status line and the log.
      * Check inputs fails, and Classify stays off, while a raster is outside the
        dam folder or belongs to another dam (another dam's sub-folder, or another
        DWR / NID ID in its name). A HEC-RAS plan (.hdf) used directly must
        therefore be in the dam folder (or imported).
      * The raster search never enters another dam's folder below the dam folder.
    2.18.34 - 2026-10-17
      * The window has three tabs - Classify, Results, Review - and a gear button
        (Suite settings…, Documentation, Run self-test, Edit data sources…)
        instead of nine tabs. Classify holds the dam folder and rasters, the
        DSS-WISE Lite and HEC-RAS plan imports, Data and output and every
        classification option group as its own section (Report open, the rest
        closed with their ND defaults); 'Show advanced options' is gone (a saved
        value is ignored).
      * Classify is the one run button: 'Run everything' (Data & Output) and 'Run
        Hazard Classification' did the same run. Input errors show in the red box
        above it instead of a pop-up and keep it off until fixed, also after a run.
      * Results: the classes, the criteria table, the summary and the tools for the
        last run; opening another project clears it. Review: Structures and Roads &
        facilities as two sub-tabs (two tables, as before).
    2.18.33 - 2026-10-16
      * The window widgets the DamSafety plugins share (the check panel, disclosure
        sections, stage strip, ...) are in one file, ui_widgets.py, identical in the

    2.18.32 - 2026-10-16
      * Setup and Run: the "Check inputs" results show in a panel under the buttons,

    2.18.31 - 2026-10-15
      * Offline pack builder: a paged download records the service's feature count
        (and last object id / edit date); resumed pages of a changed service are
        fetched again, an unverifiable resume is marked PARTIAL; the age of kept
        chunks is logged; PackWriter.count accessor.
      * Extent clip: failed coordinate transformations are counted, logged once and
        loudly warned about when most fail (e.g. a missing PROJ grid); an invalid
        CRS is warned about.
      * Submittal package: the zip is written whole or not at all (.part + rename);
        files with one name no longer overwrite each other; README asks to keep the
        zip secured.
    2.18.30 - 2026-10-15
      * Submittal package: results tables and the EAP notification list of a
        multi-scenario run (<name>_<scenario>) are packaged (the exact name first);
        evacuation timing, sensitivity and population cross-check tables too; missing
        results tables and every packaging warning are named in the README.
      * Creep watch: a missing / unreadable changes table is flagged (not counted as
        no change).
      * Run comparison: population, speed, occupancy and access distance are recorded;
        an older snapshot without them shows no setting change.
    2.18.29 - 2026-10-15
      * Offline pack: features without an id are keyed by a hash of the whole geometry
        (not its first 400 characters); features without geometry are counted in the
        manifest note; a data set queried without its R*Tree is logged once.
      * OpenStreetMap: semicolon lists ("hospital;clinic") match, locally and in the
        Overpass queries; dotted acronyms ("M.H.P.") are read as mobile-home parks;
        a capacity of 0 or less is unknown.
      * Evacuation: closures layer gains Limits_Failing (limited rows with a negative
        margin; Limits_Structures unchanged and documented); a clear error when
        access.py's helpers are missing.
    2.18.28 - 2026-10-15
      * Downloads: day files written atomically (temporary file + replace); a day file
        that does not open is deleted and fetched again (stale-copy fallback as usual);
        OpenStreetMap downloads honour 'refresh' too; an unreadable ArcGIS layer
        description is logged.
      * Evaluation cache: the offline-pack variables, the QGIS offline-pack settings
        and the pack file itself (size / time) are part of the key.
      * Roads: the AADT of the next-nearest grade crossing is tried when the nearest
        one's street name conflicts; facility review uses the shared motorist formula.
    2.18.27 - 2026-10-15
      * Evacuation (dry-safe distance, opt-in): the dry disk around a node is checked
        densely (ring and inside at cell spacing), and NoData inside the depth raster
        counts as wet; off by default as before.
      * EAP: a reason is logged when the arrival contours are not written; hhmm of a
        negative time keeps the sign ("-1:30").
    2.18.26 - 2026-10-15
      * Data folder: the names borrowed from other modules (and the late classify_tab

        degrades instead of the scan failing; CSV header and row count read with the

    2.18.25 - 2026-10-15

      * Temporal animation: a breach start that cannot be read logs a warning (the

    2.18.23 - 2026-10-15

    2.18.0 - With DamSafety Runner 3.10.0 and DamSafety Screening 0.17.0.

    2.16.0 - With ND Dam Breach Runner 3.8.0 and ND Dam Breach Screening 0.15.0.
      * Pack GeoJSON is written as EPSG:4326. A layer that still has no CRS is assigned one.
      * An inventory that falls entirely outside the study area stops the run. It is not Low.
      * One household per street address. A second footprint is not a second set of persons.
      * Consequences use 1 ft, not 0.1 ft. Thinner water is below the consequence depth.
      * A High that rests on the judgment zone by less than 1 ft is reported provisional.
      * A railroad with blank or zero trains/day stays Medium and is marked unknown.

      * Model gate before classification; model chain check (the classified rasters are the
        Runner's own outputs, SHA-256) in the run and the submittal package
      * Class probability over a Runner ensemble; quick statewide creep screen

      * README: how other tools should hand a dam to ndhazard:classify (DAM_FOLDER only)

    2.8.0 - Faster and more robust downloads; sensitivity, termination point, second
            population source, evacuation timing, reviewer merge, dams in series.
      * Offline pack build: resumes at the chunk that stopped (not the whole data set), slows
        down and recovers when a service answers 429 / 503 / 504, shows every wait and retry
        and keeps the progress message moving
      * Download cache per tile (off by default; on for the creep watch): NSI / ArcGIS tiles
        are kept with their date and only tiles older than N days are fetched again
      * Creep watch: several dams at once in qgis_process worker processes (Workers box)
      * Quick re-run (no PDF report / map book), Data_Vintage table (what data, what date)
      * Sensitivity table: the class with depth / velocity scaled (QA/Sensitivity.csv)
      * Downstream termination point of the incremental hazard (QA/Termination_Point.csv)
      * Second population source (census polygons or a population raster) vs the structures
      * Evacuation timing along the road network (QA/Evacuation_Timing.csv), screening only
      * Review tab: export the decisions and merge another engineer's (backup + report)
      * Dams in series screen for a folder of dams (button and Processing algorithm
        ndhazard:damsinseries)
      * Every screening value is a parameter chosen by the engineer; nothing changes the class
    2.7.1 - Choose where the maximum depth / velocity are read around a structure.
      * Hazard Classification tab, Criteria: "Max depth / velocity read from": whole area
        (outline + 5-ft buffer, as before - the default), buffer ring only, or a band 5 ft
        either side of the wall, so the maximum points lie along the buffer / outline
    2.7.0 - Scenarios in the dialog, cross-checks, creep watch, submittal package, consequences.

      * DSS-WISE HCOM cross-check of the population at risk (QA/DSSWISE_HCOM_CrossCheck.csv)

        changed (dialog button and Processing algorithm ndhazard:creepwatch)
      * Submittal package: report, run log, tables and EAP files copied into one dated
        folder with a SHA-256 manifest, README and cover-letter template (optional zip)
      * Aerial imagery under the map when zooming to a structure on the Review tab
      * Non-residential damage from the engineer's own depth-damage curves (CSV)
      * Population at risk for night and day, and seasonal (camp) shares
      * The evaluation is saved to outputs/QA/_evaluation_cache.bin and re-used after QGIS
        is restarted (same inputs and plugin version, at most 7 days old)
      * Real-QGIS continuous-integration kit (separate download; not run by the author)
    2.6.2 - Both 2.6.1 releases combined; offline-pack fixes; faster; timings.
      * Contains the offline data pack and the download-freeze fix (the two 2.6.1s)
      * Offline pack: data sets with features that could not be read are "partial" or
        record the areas missing, and are used only as a fallback, with a warning; NID
        tries both services before an incomplete pack; Check inputs does not unpack the
        pack; a pack path with "#" works; a pack build stops promptly on Cancel and no
        longer multiplies requests when a service is down; building inside QGIS keeps
        the data-source file's addresses
      * Faster: road / rail / sole-access raster reads batched (same values); ACER zone
        rasters and EAP arrival maps re-used when the rasters are unchanged; review
        decisions matched through a grid
      * Run log: time taken by each stage (TIMINGS)
    2.6.1 (offline pack) - Offline data pack: run without internet access.
      * build_nd_pack.py (or Data & Output > Build offline pack...) downloads every data
        set for all of North Dakota into one zip - NSI, ND GIS Hub, FEMA USA Structures,
        NID, rail, traffic, NBI, lifelines, NHD, OpenStreetMap - with the fetch date of each
      * Data & Output > 7. Offline data pack: pick the zip; data sets in it are read for
        the study area instead of downloaded; "Offline only" makes no internet requests
      * The run log, data-source list and Check inputs report what came from the pack
    2.6.1 (freeze fix) - QGIS froze during downloads (Download data now, classification).
      * A finished download thread pool was released while its last jobs were still
        returning; in PyQt that waits holding Python's lock, which the jobs need -
        the download never completed and QGIS stopped responding. Jobs are now kept
        until the pool has been waited for; a cancelled pool is parked until idle
      * Cancel also stops a data set's own tiles / pages; QGIS exit waits for them
    2.6.0 - One run, in the background; one-pass sampling; evaluation areas; re-use.
      * Main Consolidation and the POI tab are gone: the Hazard Classification's structure
        inventory is the one consolidation. Run everything = data sets (optional) + checks
        + classification. The ACER zone rasters (Hazard_Foundations / MobileHomes /
        Vehicles) are now written by the classification (Classification folder, option)
      * The run executes in a QGIS background task: QGIS stays usable, the progress bar's
        Cancel stops it; layers, map book, report and summary follow on the main thread
      * Downloads run side by side (services, ArcGIS pages after a count query, NSI
        tiles; Overpass limited to 2), one cap of 6 requests in flight
      * Structures are sampled in one pass: a coarse wet mask finds the structures near
        the flood, every evaluation area is rasterised at once (colour classes for
        overlapping areas), velocity / arrival / D x V read for all cells together -
        the same cells and values as before, a handful of GDAL calls instead of four per
        structure
      * QA/Structure_Evaluation_Areas: the exact area searched for each structure's max
        depth and velocity (outline + 5-ft edge ring, or the point disc) with the values
        found; QA/Structure_Sampled_Cells: the raster cells read (option)
      * Re-use: when rasters (contents), input layers, download choices and the settings
        that shape the sampling are unchanged since the last run in the session, the
        evaluation is re-used and only review decisions, rules, consequences,
        classification and outputs are redone (Save and re-run, what-if changes of
        persons per dwelling, judgment, borderline, 1D / 2D, loss-of-life settings)
      * Sole access is placed on the network for every structure, so a structure the
        engineer marks habitable afterwards has its answer
      * Removed: the D x V-bound and time-step ACER methods, the time-step folder, the
        All_Consolidated / EdgeBuf_* outputs, the POI workflow (give points of interest
        as the lifeline facilities layer); README.txt is now the one copy of the manual
    2.5.1 - GDAL fix, Dmax / Vmax only, ND addresses habitable, Data & Output tab.
      * FIX: "Cannot call gdal.DontUseExceptions() ... stack of error handlers is: ogr
        gdal" (GDAL 3.7+) - exception settings restored in reverse order
      * ACER zones from max depth with max velocity only; no velocity raster -> 1D
        criterion (D x V kept for flood severity)
      * Hazard Classification tab: Hydraulics group removed (breach model moved to Setup);
        Criteria / Report / Consequences / EAP, advanced groups behind one checkbox
      * ND GIS Hub site-address structures habitable, ACER type "Habitable" (house
        curve; mobile-home evidence keeps the mobile-home curve)
      * FEMA_Category (FEMA USA Structures / NSI Hazus occupancy) for reference replaces
        Category; Review tab "Habitable (now)" / "Set habitable"
      * Data & Output tab first: output options and every download / project layer;
        "Download data now" saves outputs/Data for the study area or the flooded area
      * Self-test expects 5 Processing algorithms
      * Review fixes: only ND GIS Hub points with an address make a structure habitable
        (not any layer with a NENA-like field); ND-addressed occupied buildings without
        NSI population get one dwelling's persons; Run everything continues when saving
        the data sets fails; Download data now needs only the depth raster; flood buffer
        reaches past the raster edge; partly saved downloads never auto-detected
    2.5.0 - EAP map book and travel-time table, HEC-RAS plan import, campgrounds,
            classification margin, change tracking, future development.
      * EAP outputs can each be switched off (Hazard Classification tab, group 6)
      * EAP inundation map book (PDF): index + numbered sheets in flood order, arrival
        bands, closed roads, numbered notification items listed beside each map
      * EAP travel-time table: river miles from the dam, arrival / peak depth / time of
        peak, towns, bridges, road closures; centerline layer or traced USGS NHD flowlines
      * EAP notification list: Priority column (schools, child care, care homes, hospitals)
      * Schools and child care from OpenStreetMap (vulnerable occupants, judgment item)
      * Campgrounds / RV parks from OpenStreetMap as overnight-recreation structures
        (5.2.1.1), mobile-home curve, persons from the number of sites
      * Classification margin (5.3(f)): depth change at the controlling structure that
        would change the class; on the summary, report page 1 and result panel
      * The dam's own NID hazard potential compared with the result
      * Changes since the last run: QA/Changes_Since_Last_Run.csv and a summary section
      * Future development (5.3(b)): vacant parcels in the danger zone (parcel layer)
      * HEC-RAS 6.x plan results (.hdf) import: depth, velocity, arrival, WSE and time of
        peak rasters from the 2D flow areas (tab and Processing algorithm)
      * Downloads: when a service fails, the latest earlier download of the same area is
        used and reported with its date; NDDOT traffic counts tried when HPMS fails
    2.4.1 - Cleanup of 2.4.0.
      * Removed enhanced_units_from_name (read metric DSS-WISE depth / velocity as feet),
        apply_gishub_dwellings (no effect; would have bypassed the Place_Type rules) and
        vel_at_max_depth (unused) - the 2.4.0 changelog described code that never ran
      * DSS-WISE junk filter narrowed to ._*, .DS_Store, __MACOSX, Thumbs.db,
        desktop.ini (a file starting with "__" is no longer skipped); also applied when
        the zip is extracted and to folders
      * HTTP user agent follows the plugin version
    2.4.0 - DSS-WISE packages: macOS metadata files (._*, .DS_Store) skipped when scanning;
            NDDOT_AADT_URL data-source constant added (its other helpers were never
            called and were removed in 2.4.1).
    2.3.1 - Data-integrity and stability fixes.
      * Overpass: partial results (timeout / out-of-memory) now raise an error instead
        of silently using truncated data
      * ArcGIS paging: orderByFields added for deterministic resultOffset paging;
        objectIdField and supportsPagination read from the service before paging
      * Arrival time: sampled at wet cells at the facility (was at the buffer minimum)
      * Same-family duplicate-point drop now marks the point as placed so it does not
        become a phantom free-standing structure in the merge step
      * DSS-WISE import: no-breach baseline rasters cleared when a new package is loaded
      * Plugin unload: dialog destruction deferred when a run is in progress
      * Ambiguous engineer-review decisions (one saved row matching multiple structures)
        are now logged and not applied, rather than applied to multiple structures
      * ND criteria and scenarios tables: repeat=True to prevent silent row clipping in PDF
      * Hazard_Classification_Summary: csv/xlsx only (no orphan GPKG in outputs)
      * criteria_text(): Found column widened from 22 to 30 characters
      * _export: per-format legacy-file retirement (was retire-all-or-nothing)
      * _on_check_inputs: generic exception handler with log and dialog
      * _set_busy: defensive getattr for all button widgets; scen_* buttons included
      * Downloads skipped: reason logged (no usable CRS/extent on depth raster)
      * FEMA failure message: distinguishes download vs save failure
      * valid_extent(): scale/offset applied before > 0 test (consistent with any_valid_in_bbox)
      * classify_tab: warns when a scenario requests baseline but no-breach rasters are absent
    2.3.0 - Simpler runs: rasters found, Check inputs, Run everything, downloads.
      * Rasters found in the dam folder by RAS Mapper / DSS-WISE names
      * Check inputs (units, WSE-as-depth, minutes, CRS, services) and Run everything
      * NSI / ND GIS Hub / FEMA_Structures downloaded when missing (no project layers needed)
      * ND roads + HPMS / county AADT downloaded when no road layer is chosen
      * Bridges, culverts, low-water crossings and rail bridges (NBI, OpenStreetMap)
      * Result panel with zoom; advanced options collapsed
      * Output sub-folders (Classification, EAP, QA, Consolidation); spreadsheets optional
      * Editable data-source file; batch classification of a folder of dams
    2.2.0 - FEMA_Structures outlines, dams in series, lifelines, model extent, EAP.
      * Outline file renamed OSM_Outlines -> FEMA_Structures (old name still read);
        FEMA point layers no longer processed
      * Dams in series (5.3(c)) from the National Inventory of Dams or your layer
      * Lifeline facilities (5.2.2) from ND GIS Hub, HIFLD (NASA NCCS copy), EIA,
        OpenStreetMap or your layer; at least ND Medium when inundated (option)
      * Model extent check: flood reaching the raster edge / model boundary
      * EAP notification list sorted by arrival time, arrival-time bands and contours
    2.1.0 - Review, report, layer groups, sample locations.
      * Review tab: reviewed structures are no longer listed (option to show them),
        'automatic result is correct' choice, list every flooded structure, double-click zoom
      * PDF report: ND REG_05.2026 Sec. 5.2 criteria table next to ACER TM 11 Table 1,
        scenarios, roads / rail table, every flooded habitable structure with ND outcome
      * Report / outputs open in another program: written under a new name and reported
      * Layers grouped in the layer panel by dam and category (QGIS 3.28+ layer tree API)
      * *_POI.gpkg loaded after Main Consolidation and never overwritten once it exists
      * OSM_Sample_Points and Structure_Sample_Points: where the values were read
      * Run log per classification; Open PDF report / outputs folder buttons; map tips
      * Accuracy: AADT only from the same road; abandoned rail / trails left out;
        trains/day from the same line; roof outlines join the main building;
        1D borderline rule; D x V raster used in the time-step DxV_Max output
      * Stability: locked CSV / PDF outputs never stop a run; settings kept on
        Project > Save and project change; revised DSS-WISE packages never mixed
    2.0.0 - Same-time ACER zones, scenarios, consequences, review, report, DSS-WISE Lite.
      * ACER zones from Dmax / Vmax / (D x V)max bound or from time-step rasters
      * No-breach baseline: incremental classification; several breach scenarios
      * Economic loss (NSI values, EGM 01-03) and loss-of-life screening (Graham 1999)
      * Borderline (near-threshold) structures; sole-access road screening
      * FEMA USA Structures / extra outlines; inventory QA table
      * Review tab with persistent engineer decisions
      * PDF report from a print layout kept in the project
      * DSS-WISE Lite results import (tab and Processing)
      * Processing provider; settings saved in the project
    1.4.0 - Study area, ND GIS Hub address points, QGIS 3.28 - 4.x on Windows / macOS.
      * Every structure layer and download limited to the depth raster extent + 25 ft
        (adjustable); features inside it are kept even where the rasters have no value
      * ND GIS Hub SiteStructure Address Points: detected under their Hub / service names,
        NENA Place_Type and Placement read, driveway points moved onto the building,
        unit addresses counted as dwellings, optional download
      * Edge-buffer ring built in the raster CRS (correct width for EPSG:4326 outlines)
      * Web Mercator / geographic rasters: distances corrected for latitude
      * Qt6 / QGIS 4 ready: scoped enums, QMetaType field types, QAction location
      * Faster edge buffer (dry outlines skipped); download paging de-duplicated
    1.3.1 - ACER curves.
      * TM 11 Figure 4 passenger-vehicle curves added (vehicle zones, TM 11 motorists)
      * FIX: mobile-home High boundary x^4 coefficient typo (non-conservative above ~6 ft/s)
      * Velocities beyond each figure's range evaluated at the range limit
    1.3.0 - Hazard Classification tab.
      * Habitable structures (OSM + NSI/FEMA/GIS Hub + NSI download), house vs mobile home
      * Worst-cell ACER zone per structure with its own curve
      * Overtopped roads with most recent AADT; inundated rail with trains/day (FRA)
      * ND Dam Safety Standards REG_05.2026 Sec. 5.2 and ACER TM 11 Table 1 classification
      * Summary report with reasons and engineering-judgment items
    1.2.0 - Performance and stability release.
      * Hazard rasters processed in strips (bounded memory), tiled GeoTIFFs with overviews
      * GDAL-based raster sampling with vectorised nearest-cell search
      * Edge-buffer zones fixed (previously always failed) and rasterised per ring
      * All_Consolidated join uses the spatial index only; computed once for both outputs
      * Sampling buffer converted from feet to raster CRS units
      * Correct reprojection when point, OSM and raster CRSs differ
      * Safe re-runs: no duplicate layers, own outputs ignored, locked files handled
      * Cancel works in every stage; modal, throttled progress
      * No global gdal.UseExceptions() side effect
      * Built-in self-test (Readme tab)
    1.1.0 - OSM outlines, All_Consolidated and edge-buffer zones.
    1.0.0 - Initial release.
```

## Archived metadata changelog — DamSafety_Runner

Entries moved verbatim out of `DamSafety_Runner/metadata.txt` `changelog=` on 2026-11-07 (QGIS's Plugin Manager parses and renders the whole `changelog=` value of every installed plugin, so `tools/ci_checks.py` now caps it at 12 KB and keeps the newest entries there). Newest first; the indentation is the metadata.txt continuation indent.

```text
    3.16.4 (2026-11-06; Nick, 8:41 PM CST: for a sunny-day breach the maximum pool from the DWR values is used (unless stated otherwise); PMF runs match what the ND Dam Safety Standards state. Vw in the breach equations (Froehlich 2008 / 1995, MLM, VTG, TR-60, the Froehlich 1995 peak check) is the dam's maximum storage (DWR MAX_STORAGE, or the storage you give) for a sunny-day breach, as in 3.16.2, and the storage at the pool when the breach starts (the elevation-volume curve) for a PMF / flood-inflow breach, as before 3.16.2. Report hydrograph vw_source. The HEC-RAS plan's Froehlich (2008) piping takes the run's Vw. Fixes from the review of 3.16.1 / 3.16.2: the 'curve vs maximum storage' warning is a plain note (the curve is anchored at the maximum storage, so it only said the pool starts below the crest); a local-datum dam tied to the NAVD88 DEM no longer gets 'elevations were not shifted to NAVD88' in the HEC-RAS notes; hec_breach_mode 'match_plugin' with no breach_f2008 option only fills missing breach values (it wrote an Overtopping plan with piping widths and slopes). Test: tests/test_v3164.py.)
    3.16.5 (2026-11-07; audit, shared damsuite loss.py: a flooded structure with no known arrival time is counted as no warning, not the longest/best warning band. The population-at-risk warning-time split and, with a signed fatality-rate table, the loss-of-life total are no longer understated on runs with a missing or partial arrival raster; the unknown-arrival count is reported. No depth, velocity, breach or hazard number changes. Test in DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_loss_unknown_arrival.py.)
    3.16.3 (2026-11-06; security audit pass 3, Nick: bad input gives a plain message instead of a freeze or a raw Python error. (1) damsuite 1.18.9 / xlsx_lite 1.5.0 (security audit pass 3, Nick 2026-11-06): the shared .xlsx reader is bounded. A 704-byte damsuite_settings.xlsx naming a cell at row 50,000,000 made read() build a 50-million-row table and freeze QGIS; a sheet past Excel's limits (1,048,576 rows, 16,384 columns), a padded table over 2,000,000 cells or a part that unpacks to more than 64 MB now raises XlsxTooLarge (a ValueError), so the settings file is refused with a plain message (SettingsError) before anything large is allocated. (2) core/consequences: a fatality or damage-curve CSV row with more values than the header has columns stopped the consequences tool with AttributeError ('list' object has no attribute 'strip'); it is now a ValueError naming the file, the line and the column counts, and text the csv module refuses is one too. (3) core/animation: a damaged or cut-short depth_snapshots.npz (bad magic, short header, not 3-D, negative or non-number array, more data declared than the zip holds, bad CRC / deflate stream) raises SnapshotError (a ValueError) with 'run the breach model again', checked before any frame is read, so nothing huge is allocated; the animation tool and the run report show that message. (4) core/builtin_lisflood: a truncated or damaged DEM, Manning's n, start-depth, .bci, .bdy or gauge file (or a .par number that is not a number, an ASCII grid whose values do not match its header or the DEM, a .bdy series with no values) is an EngineError naming the file, so the run stops with 'built-in engine: ...' (ModelError) instead of IndexError / ValueError. Test: tests/test_input_hardening.py.)
    3.16.2 (2026-11-06; Nick, 1:17 PM: the HEC-RAS breach plan always takes Froehlich (2008) piping for the bottom width, side slopes and formation time, also over the run's MLM or Froehlich 1995 values (hecras_model option breach_f2008 = 'overwrite', the default; 'fill_missing' is the opt-in old 3.16.1 behaviour). The breach equations (Froehlich 2008 / 1995, MLM, VTG, TR-60, the Froehlich 1995 peak check) use Vw = the dam's maximum storage (DWR MAX_STORAGE, storage_top_acft) instead of the storage at the starting pool; the elevation-volume curve at the starting pool is a warning check only (more than 5%% off) and still sets the routed reservoir. NOTE: breach hydrograph peaks and widths change for runs that start below the crest (a PMF maximum level lower than the top of dam); runs starting at the crest are unchanged. Report hydrograph vw_acft. Test: tests/test_v3162.py.)
    3.16.1 (2026-11-06; Nick: the HEC-RAS dam breach plan defaults to piping with Froehlich (2008) values. core/hecras.breach_parameter_rows and core/hecras_model.breach_values default a missing failure mode (TR-60, a user hydrograph, older run reports) to piping, so Breach Geom field 6 is True; Froehlich (2008) piping (K0 1.0, side slope 0.7) fills a missing bottom width, side slope or formation time (row 'Froehlich (2008) piping, auto'); the piping coefficient (0.5 unless the run's pipe_cd) and the initial piping elevation (pipe centre, else half way up the breach) are always written. hec_breach_mode now defaults to 'failure_mode'; 'match_plugin' still writes Overtopping for the plugin's routed weir methods. Only an explicit overtopping failure mode writes False. The Breach Calculator Data= line is not written. Test: tests/test_v3161.py.)
    3.16.0 (2026-11-06; port of ND Runner 3.29.0. The main run's LISFLOOD-FP solver is a choice; ACC stays the default and nothing changes unless you pick another (Nick, 2026-11-06). Only solvers that write the maximum velocity and honour the arrival-depth patch (inittm_depth) are offered: ACC, FV2 and MWDG2. New Solver parameter (SOLVER, advanced: Default / ACC / FV2 / MWDG2) on the run, batch and cascade tools; Default = the NDBREACH_LISFLOOD_SOLVER environment variable, else the provider setting 'Default LISFLOOD-FP solver', else ACC; CLI model.lisflood_solver and model.lisflood_limit_slopes. FV2 and MWDG2 run with LISFLOOD-FP's slope limiter (limitslopes). Before the first FV2 / MWDG2 run the LISFLOOD-FP build is checked on the 20-minute synthetic channel (check_lisflood with that solver); a build that does not write the maximum velocity or ignores inittm_depth, the built-in engine, or a refused name (FV1: no maximum velocity or arrival time; DG2: needs slope-coefficient DEMs; any other) stops the run with the reason - it never falls back to another solver. A non-ACC run that writes no maximum velocity also stops. The ACC stability re-run and the near-dam refine apply to ACC runs only. Report summary lisflood_solver; run stats solver. New tests/test_v3160.py.)
    3.15.1 (2026-11-06; port of ND Runner 3.28.1. LISFLOOD-FP stability re-run: a LISFLOOD-FP ACC run whose volume error is over 1%% of the injected volume is run again with theta 0.7 and CFL 0.5 (core/lisflood.py STABLE_THETA / STABLE_CFL / STABILITY_RETRY_PCT), and every later run of that dam uses them; a run that balances is not changed. With LISFLOOD-FP's defaults (theta 1.0, CFL 0.7) the ACC scheme made water on the default 10 m grid: DWR-2865 +567%% of the breach volume after 8.3 h (water level checkerboarding in a deep pond 2 km down, flood 838 ac instead of 213 ac), DWR-4031 +36%% (fast shallow flow at the breach); theta 0.7 + CFL 0.5 gives 0.0%% / -0.2%%. Report lisflood_stability and a warning; run stats acc_theta / acc_cfl; NDBREACH_STABILITY_RETRY=0 turns it off.)
    3.15.0 (2026-11-05; ports of ND Runner 3.27.7 + 3.28.0 from the DWR-2801 grid and breakline tests (Nick: do 2 and 3, as defaults in both plugin sets). Grid-spread check (report grid_spread_check): after an automatic-domain run, a cell coarser than 10 m with a flood of at least 1,000 ac and a median wet depth of 1.0 ft or less is the coarse-grid over-spread signature. Finer grid automatically (GRID_REFINE on by default, GRID_CHECK_CELL 5 m; window check box): such a run is repeated on the same domain, inflow and clock at 10 m, which becomes the record in the dam folder (the coarse report kept as _breach_model/coarse_<cell>m_run_report.json), then at optionally (GRID_CHECK_CELL, off by default; set 5 to opt in, it can take many hours) at 5 m into 'Grid check 5 m' as a convergence check with a warning when its area differs by more than 25%%; report grid_refine. Sensitivity, probabilistic, nested and fixed-plan runs keep their own cell. Crest burn (core/crestburn.py, same file as the ND Runner; CREST_BURN on by default, CREST_BURN_DEM, provider setting 'Crest burn: 1 m DEM or VRT', window check box): every model cell a road or railway crosses is raised to the highest 1 m ground inside it, never lowered; the 1 m ground is the named DEM, else the run's DEM when 2 m or finer, else the suite data folder's DEM (the statewide 1 m VRT), else USGS 3DEP 1 m downloaded once into the terrain cache; GDAL max resampling with overviews off; the traced main channel, bridges and raises over 5 m are left alone, and the inflow search, flow trace and planning grid read the plain DEM; report crest_burn. CLI model keys crest_burn, crest_burn_dem, grid_refine, grid_check_cell_m.)
    3.14.11 - Shared damsuite 1.18.8 (the data updaters' engine and source list are shared; vendored only into DamSafety Data). This plugin is unchanged.
    3.14.10 - Shared damsuite 1.18.7: the pack readers answer to the old ND barrier names (SARP_dams / SARP_surveyed_crossings / SARP_unsurveyed_crossings) with the ABI_* layers of the one shared pack layer list (hydro3dhp.ALIASES); nothing else changed.
    3.14.9 - Shared damsuite 1.18.6: the offline pack builder is one engine for both suites (damsuite/packengine.py); this plugin does not build packs and is unchanged otherwise.
    3.14.8 - Browser connections: the suite's map services (WMS/WMTS, XYZ Tiles, ArcGIS REST Servers: USGS 3DEP / 3DHP / NHD / topo / imagery, NLCD, ND GIS Hub, DWR MapService, NID, NTAD rail / crossings / NBI, FEMA NFHL and USA Structures, SARP, USGS stream crossings, NAIP and the services the user added) are added to the QGIS Browser as 'DamSafety: ...' connections when QGIS starts, each once (a deleted or edited one is left alone); setting ndsuite/register_connections = false turns it off (shared damsuite 1.18.5, connections.py, pin v3). Test: DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_browser_connections.py.
    3.14.7 (2026-11-05; Terrain-aware grid default on flat section-road plains (report terrain_grid, pre-run WARNING when the cell is coarser than 10 m on a flat plain): a coarse DEM window around the dam is classified by relief (p95-p5 <= 25 m) and mean neighbour slope (<= 1%%). Flat plains start at 10 m, with 5 m (finer, slower) offered in the Advanced section hint and the CELL / grid_m tooltips when the engineer can afford the run time; other terrain keeps the existing 10 m start. Setting keys stay numeric metres; the run's cell is not changed by the classifier. Evidence: DWR-2801 (Absaraka) Claude Runner pack vs DSS-WISE wet at >=0.5 ft scored CSI 0.36 / 0.43 / 0.57 at 20 / 10 / 5 m (Docs & QA compare 2026-11-04; Nick 'Do that'). Module core/grid_default.py; test tests/test_grid_default.py.)
    3.14.6 (2026-11-04; GUI check of 2026-11-04, Nick approved: the main window's minimum width is 760 (was 820) and the body-width fit measures the margins and the scroll bar, not width minus the viewport before the first show (that read about 340 px of frame, so the window could not shrink below 829-867 px); the Breach results dock puts its five layer buttons on two rows (rasters, then vectors) so it fits a 420 px dock (needed 529); Dam suite home shrinks to the screen; Sensitivity (SENS_METHODS, SENS_CELL; was about 1400 px) and Probabilistic (PROB_RUN_RASTERS; about 1100 px) have short Processing labels with the detail in the parameter help, so the panels fit 800 px (names and defaults unchanged). Tests: tests/realqt_dock_area.py (dock widths), tests/test_dem_z_once.py builds its made-up DEMs with PROJ's network grids off (failed with PROJ_NETWORK=ON where cdn.proj.org is unreachable), tools/gui_lint.py limits every window to 800 px wide)
    3.14.5 (2026-11-03; GUI fit, QGIS Dev: Processing labels of rundam shortened, the detail moved into the parameter help - Class-stop rule, Near-dam refine, Resume, Animation, products, flagged structure, culvert guesses, invert tolerance (the Settings window's Dam rows #41/#112/#233 no longer cut off, and each names its tool); CULVERT_BURN_GUESSES help says it is kept in the Settings window; 'for DamSafety Runner' wording; Culvert prep 'Streams (3DHP or NHD flowlines)'. Tests: tests/test_guifix_20261103.py)
    3.14.4 (2026-11-03; built as 3.14.1, renumbered after #261/#256 took 3.14.2/3.14.3; numbered above 3.14.0, the guessed-culvert cut PR; DWR-10554 run on main 33a93c0, Nick: "do culvert ideas"): OpenStreetMap culverts reach the structures layer - Culvert prep's build step passes an OSM crossings layer to ND Dam Hazard (Build: OpenStreetMap crossings), and a STRUCTURES feature with source OSM is read as a mapped, not surveyed structure labelled with its OSM way (core/structures.label_source, in the Processing tool and ndbreach_cli); with no size it takes the default culvert size. Tests: tests/test_osm_structures.py
    3.14.3 (2026-11-03; built as 3.14.0, renumbered after #261 took 3.14.2; DWR-10554 run on main 33a93c0, Nick: "do culvert ideas"): road x channel culvert guesses with no inventory record are cut into the DEM as GUESSED, UNVERIFIED 1.2 x 0.9 m boxes - same cut as a matched guess (along the channel, at least 2 model cells, inverts from the LiDAR), source guess, not surveyed, name starts with guess: - and listed in a new run-report section; Settings.culvert_burn_guesses / Processing CULVERT_BURN_GUESSES (default on, advanced) turns it off to list them only, as before. Tests: tests/test_culvert_match.py
    3.14.2 (2026-11-03; audit fix E. Window: 'Guided wizard…' sits on its own row under the four result buttons of the Run tab, and the window's minimum width is the scrolled body's minimum plus its frame (capped at the screen) - at 800 x 600 the five buttons in one row made the body about 800 px wide, so the dam rows' Browse… / File… and the wizard button were clipped behind a horizontal scroll bar (now about 660 px). Lint: hecras_model weir-cell note: unused format argument u (F522); hecras_fine unused math, hecras_windows unused shutil; tests: second fake_qgis import as fq removed (F811).)
    3.13.10 (2026-11-03; audit fix D: the Word report says "reviewer's judgment" (was judgement); damsuite loss.py docstring judgment.)
    3.13.9 (2026-11-03; built as 3.13.6, renumbered after #248/#249 took 3.13.7/3.13.8; Breach results dock, from the Docs & QA dock test on main db9c665 against DWR-2801 r4, Nick: "Yes": a Hazard line read from DamSafety Hazard's Hazard_Classification_Summary.txt (or .json) and dam.json - ND / TM 11 class, provisional, Hazard version, status, next action - with the engineer's class of record from review.json in the dam folder (Nick, 2026-11-01; core/review_record.py: validated, read defensively, flagged when it names another run or a printed class the summary no longer shows; written only by the explicit sign_off_review(), which no run or classify code calls); a warning when the summary is older than the installed Hazard or the run outputs; "37 structures" labelled as the Runner's receptor count with Hazard's flooded habitable count beside it; the Next line says re-classify when the summary is stale; Depth / Velocity / Arrival / Flooded receptors (N) / Wet Hazard structures (N) layer buttons, the vector layers filtered to depth > 0, all five opened read-only (nothing written to the data); the run warnings as an expandable list; rasters open through /vsisubfile/ so GDAL writes no .aux.xml into the run folder. Tests: tests/test_results_dock_hazard.py, tests/test_review_record.py, tests/realqt_dock_area.py)
    3.13.8 (2026-11-02; built as 3.13.6, renumbered after #248 took 3.13.7; Style A wizard polish, Crest build pack): the dam wizard shows the Start here… steps (Data folder, Dam, Terrain, Scenario, Models, Review, Run tool) as one wrapping step line on every page; plain-language tooltips (also Shift+F1) on DEM elevation units, the download resolution and the cell size, with a "(i) Hover any field for what it means." line on the Terrain page; the wizard's fallback steps (busy cursor, model settings, DEM lookup, button label, window fit) log to the DamSafety Runner message-log tab instead of passing silently. No parameter the tool gets changes. Tests: tests/realqt_wizard.py

    3.13.7 (2026-11-01; option C, Nick: TM 11 practice uses the absolute peak velocity and peak depth regardless of whether they coincide. core/nd_hazard.sample_footprint and the Sampler multipoint classify on the peak depth with the peak velocity over the wet cells; reverts db9c665's deepest-cell velocity. vel_at_max_fts stays as an info field. Parity test: every tool's classifying velocity = max over wet cells.)
    3.13.5 (2026-10-31; two findings from the DWR-6141 Taskers Coulee run, main 1e3e2f7: (1) CHECK THE DAM POINT was a false alarm - the inflow (-453152.9, 2862253.2) is 33 m downstream (NE) of the bent DWR dam line, but the trace's first 200 m runs east along the toe (0.99, 0.14) and the point is west of mid-crest, so the "behind the dam relative to the flow trace" test (inflow vs the dam point) fired. With a downstream side or a dam line that test is now a note in side_check["notes"]; the upstream-side, trace-vs-downstream and trace-vs-line-normal checks decide (cos 0.59 on 6141). With neither it is still a problem. (2) The ND hazard AADT join finds the per-dam pack clips next to the roads layer (pack_HPMS_ND_2024_<dam>.gpkg, pack_ND_county_roads_<dam>.gpkg, pack_NDDOT_traffic_counts_<dam>.gpkg) with no AADT_LAYERS / ROADS_MORE; DWR-6141 had none and found none. Tests: tests/test_sidecheck_6141.py)
    3.13.4 (2026-10-30; PR #242, built as 3.13.3, released as 3.13.4 after #238 took 3.13.3; QGIS 4: the breach results dock is added with Qt.DockWidgetArea.RightDockWidgetArea (PyQt6 has no Qt.RightDockWidgetArea); RELEASE set Hazard 2.22.2 / Screening 0.18.1. Tests: tests/test_dock_area_enum.py. 3.13.3 is #238)
    3.13.3 (2026-10-30; breach inflow at the downstream dam toe, the band from the DWR downstream slope - PR #238, issue #237; Nick: "merge pr")
    * Breach inflow placement (PR #238, built as 3.12.10 and 3.13.1, never released; released as 3.13.3, after #241 3.13.0 and #244 3.13.2): DWR-4031 Washek put the inflow 393 m away in a farmyard draw on the wrong side.
    * pool_mask also finds a shallow (hydro-flattened) reservoir against the dam: more than 0.1 m of fill, ground between the top of dam and half the dam height below it, in a part that reaches within 60 m (3 cells) of the dam line or point. DWR-4031's pond had 1.1 ft of fill against the 3.1 ft the depth rule needs.
    * downstream_direction uses only the pool that reaches the dam line (not any depression within 600 m: a closed low 450 m ESE turned DWR-4031 round); with a dam line and no such pool, the clearly lower side of the line (ground 20-80 m off it), then the trace.
    * find_inflow_point never picks further than 150 m from the dam line (else the dam point); RunSettings.inflow_max_from_dam_m changes it. When only farther ground is low enough, the lowest ground within the limit is used and the run warns 'CHECK THE INFLOW POINT'.
    * inflow_side_check compares the flow trace with the downstream side (and the dam line's normal on the inflow's side) and reports an inflow more than the limit from the dam line ('far', a separate CHECK THE INFLOW POINT warning). DWR-4031's run passed with a cosine of -0.80.
    * Nick: the inflow is at the dam toe at its lowest elevation. With a dam line (DAM_LINES or CREST_LINE) and a downstream side, the inflow is the lowest cell of a toe band downstream of the line along its full length, read on the 1 m DEM (terrain.toe_band_inflow). The band runs from 5 m out to H x (downstream slope + 1) (Nick, "would do 2"; H the dam height in m, the slope H:1V from DS_SLOPE / F_DS, else the dam's DWR record downstream_slope (Nick: "downstream slope data is in dwr data"; core/dwr_slope.py, read by its DWR number from the dam folder's or the suite's DWR dams; "2.5;3" takes the flattest; empty, "0", "varies" or outside 1-10 is not known), else 3:1 - i.e. 4H - with a warning), at least 15 m and never past the 150 m limit; with no dam height, 5-30 m and a warning (terrain.toe_band_for_dam; RunSettings.inflow_toe_band_m overrides the band, inflow_toe_ds_slope_h the slope, inflow_toe_min_outer_m the 15 m, inflow_toe_cell_m the 1 m). Each cell is tested against the downstream side of its nearest line segment (a bent line keeps its local side; no wrapping round the ends), cells in the pool behind the dam do not count, and when the band is all dam face (its lowest ground not 75 per cent of the dam height below the top) it widens 10 m at a time (never past the 150 m limit). DWR-4031 (DWR 2:1): band 5-19 m, 0.5 m from the hand-picked toe; DWR-2801 (DWR 2.5:1) with the pack dam line: band 5-29 m, the toe near the bend (a 5-15 m strip was all face and picked a pad near the south end). No dam line, no downstream side or no valid cell: the previous search, with a warning. The side check still runs.
    3.13.2 (2026-10-30; run report text from the DWR-4031 toe10m run. 3.13.1 is taken by #238)
    * Peak inflow: the report gives the breach hydrograph's peak (1,696 cfs on DWR-4031 toe10m) and labels the .mass value as the largest Qin at the .mass output samples (1,570 cfs, sampled every 300 s, missed the short peak). New stats peak_inflow_input_cfs and massint_s; peak_inflow_cfs is unchanged.
    * LISFLOOD-FP passes are numbered (Pass 1, 2, ...) next to the domain run; a clock extension keeps its domain run number (by design) and is marked "(clock extended)". Both passes on DWR-4031 toe10m were labelled "run 1". New per-pass keys pass and clock_extension.
    * ND hazard classification: the AADT join also reads the ROADS_MORE GeoPackages (ND county roads), as the Hazard plugin does. DWR-4031 87TH ST NW: AADT 60 (county roads), was "AADT unknown (assumed Medium)". tools/mac/nickreview_mac.py lists county roads in AADT_LAYERS.
    3.13.0 (2026-10-30; Nick: a habitable structure in the ACER judgment zone is ND MEDIUM by rule, not potentially lethal and not HIGH under 5.3. Above the high line is still HIGH. The old behaviour is an advanced option of ND hazard classification (JUDGMENT_HIGH, off). Shared nd_hazard 1.3.0 (Settings.judgment_zone_high, default False). TM 11 unchanged)
    3.12.9 (2026-10-29; Nick: elevations from 0 through 500 ft are always local, not 0 through 300. 301-500 ft is never VERTCON-shifted and is tied to the DEM whatever datum is stated. damsuite 1.18.4)
    3.12.8 (2026-10-29; the offline pack in format 1 is read too - Nick)
    * Vendored damsuite 1.18.3: the DWR dams / lines, NID and the NLCD land cover are found in a format-1 pack (nd_offline_pack.sqlite: data sets copied once to a GeoPackage cache outside the pack, NLCD from its manifest "rasters") as in a format-2 pack; format 2 is unchanged. FEMA flood zones claim no pack layer.
    3.12.7 (2026-10-28 / 10-29; Nick: an elevation between 0 and 300 ft is always local and converted to NAVD88 by the DEM tie whatever datum is chosen (no override) - never VERTCON-shifted, the crest from the DEM at mid-crest and the others tied by the offset (DEM-derived); mixed records tied, not dropped; no tie (no DEM, DEM units, no dam line, no local crest) flagged for review and left unconverted; local culvert inverts tied to the LiDAR bed; the dam point is moved to mid-crest of the dam line (damsuite.midcrest: midpoint by length, highest DEM point +-25 m across it), original point and move logged in run_report / dam.json / the Hazard summary; CREST_LINE also places the dam point without a breach position; after the tie a value more than 100 ft from the DEM crest is flagged REVIEW and not used (left unconverted, named with how far off), a mid-crest pick at the edge of the +-25 m profile is flagged REVIEW; clearer datum log lines (no empty rule text, no pre-tie NAVD88 line, no duplicate local warnings, stated Local shown as stated). 3.12.6 is #231, already on main)
    3.12.6 (2026-10-29; ANUGA resume keeps its checkpoint key; resume from a named checkpoint - DWR-2801 r3 - Nick)
    * A resumed ANUGA run re-uses the gmsh mesh made from the same input instead of meshing again: gmsh on several threads does not repeat its mesh, and the mesh is part of the checkpoint key (DWR-2801 r3 restarted at t = 0).
    * NDBREACH_ANUGA_RESUME_FROM names a checkpoint folder to resume; the worker compares the inputs, logs every difference and stops rather than start over when it cannot resume safely (NDBREACH_ANUGA_RESUME_REF_JOB for checkpoints written before 3.12.6, NDBREACH_ANUGA_RESUME_FORCE=1 to override).
    * checkpoint.json records a digest per job input; a resume that finds no checkpoint of its own names the others and what differs.
    3.12.4 (2026-10-28; ANUGA host-pause check - DWR-2801 r3's 12 'stalls' were VM freezes - Nick)
    * Each ANUGA PROGRESS line adds cpu_s (process CPU of all threads), mono_s (monotonic wall) and paused_s at the end; 'box paused ~N s' when the realtime clock ran ahead of monotonic (a VM freeze) or wall time was far above CPU at a normal step count. wall_s stays on the realtime clock, so old and new lines parse the same.
    * End of run: a 'box paused ~N s in total' summary line and stats host_pauses in the result.
```
