    * Checkpoints by minutes (ckpt_every_min) are timed by the worker on the monotonic clock (same file name and pickler as ANUGA's, written atomically), so the first step after a freeze no longer saves an extra checkpoint.
    3.12.3 (2026-10-28; culvert prep: corrected copy of the structures layer, big changes flagged - Nick)
    * New Processing tool 'Culvert prep: corrected copy of the structures layer (review list)' and CLI 'culvert-prep': the culvert pre-check's HIGH ends get the lowest native fine ground within max(apron, 5 m) as their invert in a NEW GeoPackage; the input is never written (sha256 checked).
    * Applied only when the change is at most the limit (1 ft to start) on native ground (top source, 1 m cells or finer) on a single-part line; larger changes, fill or coarser ground and multi-part lines are flagged (unchanged, listed); ends with no fine ground are listed. Line straightening is off by default and always flagged.
    * Change log culvert_prep_<dam>.md / .csv / .json with the input and output sha256; a PREP note on each changed feature. Accept flagged items in the CSV's accept column and run again from the same input.
    * No structures layer: built first with ND Dam Hazard's runnerculverts from the NBI / NDDOT inventories. The run itself is unchanged.
    3.12.2 (2026-10-28; ANUGA runs detached from QGIS - DWR-2801's GUI run died twice when the desktop was shut down)
    * The ANUGA worker writes its own anuga.log (stdout + stderr to the file, no pipe to QGIS), starts in its own session (POSIX) / process group without a console, breaking away from a job object when allowed (Windows), ignores SIGPIPE / SIGHUP and never stops on a failed print. SIGTERM (Cancel with checkpoints) still flushes anuga_partial.npz. QGIS tails the log for progress; Cancel works as before; NDBREACH_ANUGA_TIMEOUT_S sets an optional wall-clock limit (none by default).
    * _breach_model/anuga/anuga_worker.json records the worker (pid, state running / finished / failed / stopped, the run's settings key, times). When QGIS dies the worker goes on and writes anuga_result.npz (atomically). Running the dam again with the same settings waits for a worker that is still running or uses its finished result (no second ANUGA run), then builds the rasters, products and report with the usual post-processing; a worker running for other settings stops the new run instead of a second worker in the folder.
    * New tool "Finish an ANUGA run after QGIS closed" (ndbreach:finishanuga; qgis_process run ndbreach:finishanuga -- DAM_FOLDER=...): runs the dam again from the parameters saved in _breach_model/run_params.json with Resume on. The window shows "ANUGA run after QGIS closed" and suggests the tool when a dam folder has one waiting. CLI: anuga-status DAM_FOLDER (exit 4 when one is waiting); run CONFIG --resume finishes it.
    * Tests: tests/test_anuga_detach.py (fake worker: own session and log, parent killed, broken stdout pipe, SIGTERM partial, progress / cancel / timeout, adoption, products from a finished npz, run_params.json).
    3.12.1 (2026-10-28; local / assumed datums: elevations taken from the DEM - Nick)
    * Unstated datums stay NGVD29. A dam whose elevations are on a local datum (Nick: "if elevation is 100 or similar, they would be local and would have to take actual elevations from dem") is detected in Auto - the top of dam (or, without one, a pool) is outside ND's 500 - 3,600 ft, more than 50 ft from the DEM crest after the NGVD29 shift, or a round multiple of 100 ft more than 10 ft from it - and is not datum-converted: the top of dam is the DEM crest (the median of the DEM along the dam line when one is given, else the 98th percentile within 30 m of the dam point) and the PMF pool, breach bottom, normal pool, trigger pool, spillway rating and outlet inverts keep their height below the local top (DEM top - (local top - value)). Without a local top the pools are dropped (from the DEM / rating) and a rating / outlets are left as given, with a warning.
    * New Vertical datum choice "Local (use DEM)" (window, Run one dam / Batch ELEV_DATUM, CLI --elev-datum local); a stated "local"/"assumed" datum does the same. The window line says when the folder's top looks local. An explicit NAVD88 / NGVD29 choice turns detection off.
    * Recorded as datum "local (assumed)" (or "local"), method DEM, the original values, the DEM top - local top offset and a warning in run_report.json / the run report (vertical_datum), dam.json (the vertical_datum record; the plans' values are kept) and the Hazard summary. 1071.15 NGVD29 still becomes 1072.24 NAVD88 (DWR-2801).
    * A DEM crest outside 500 - 3,600 ft or 3.28 x / 1 / 3.28 x the top of dam (DEM units) is never taken as the top: the run's DEM-units check reports it.
    * Tests: 8 new in tests/test_vertical_datum.py.
    3.12.0 (2026-10-28; NGVD29 dam elevations converted to NAVD88 with VERTCON - Nick, DWR-2801)
    * Dam elevations (top of dam, normal pool, flood-input pools) whose datum is not stated as NAVD88 are converted from NGVD29 to NAVD88 at the dam with NOAA VERTCON (bundled ND clip of PROJ-data us_noaa_vertconc.tif, pyproj vgridshift fallback, offline) before they meet the NAVD88 DEM. DWR-2801: 1071.15 ft NGVD29 + 1.09 ft = 1072.24 ft NAVD88.
    * The datum comes from the record (dam.json / DWR / NID datum fields, F_DATUM batch column); when none is stated NGVD29 is assumed and a warning says so. A DEM-read top is NAVD88 already. Conversion is done once (datum tag + per-object marks), never twice.
    * New Vertical datum choice (Auto / NAVD88 / NGVD29) in the window (Advanced, with the detected datum and shift shown), on Run one dam and Batch (ELEV_DATUM) and on the CLI (--elev-datum). Recorded in run_report.json / the run report (vertical_datum: source datum, how, shift ft, method) and in dam.json.
    * Tests: tests/test_vertical_datum.py (DWR-2801 reference within 0.02 ft, NAVD88 unchanged, idempotent, outside-grid warning, batch / cascade per-dam datum).
    3.11.1 (2026-10-28; smoke-test fixes of 3.11.0: culvert channel bed on a sloping channel, volume note wording, metadata)
    * Culvert inverts: the LiDAR channel bed beyond an end (structures.channel_bed) is no longer the lowest ground up to 30 m out, which read a sloping channel's downstream bed up to slope x 30 m low (2 %% -> 2 ft) and rejected correct inventory inverts; the channel's slope is fitted over the 30 m (Theil-Sen), each thalweg point is carried back to the end along it, and the bed is the 10th percentile over the first 10 m. The ANUGA culvert bed notes give feet as well as metres.
    * ANUGA volume note (#209): the fractional-step integral is every operator's (the breach inlet and any culvert / bridge operators); the inlet is named alone only when the culverts' own net (operator_volumes) is known and small, else the note says what each part was or that the culverts' part was not logged. The first-step inflow cap is recommended only when this run did not use it (volume_balance['inflow_step_cap']).
    * metadata.txt: a changelog line after icon= made the icon 'icon.svg' plus that line (removed: it is in the 3.10.93 entry); every percent sign is doubled in the file (QGIS reads metadata.txt with configparser interpolation: a bare one emptied the changelog in the Plugin Manager).
    3.11.0 (2026-10-28; aerial basemap on QA maps)
    * QA maps (qa_inflow_map.png, qa_depth_map.png) over an aerial photo: ND DWR NAIP 2025 (WMS), then Esri World Imagery, then USGS imagery - the first that answers. Esri street / town labels (only over online imagery), a scale bar (m and ft), a true-north arrow and a credit line (imagery, flight dates, CRS, plugin version).
    * QA_BASEMAP / qa_basemap = auto (default) | imagery | hillshade, QA_BASEMAP_LABELS / qa_basemap_labels (on); Processing (advanced), run JSON and CLI. A run JSON without them runs as auto. "imagery" warns when it falls back; auto is silent.
    * Offline (ND_DAM_OFFLINE, NDBREACH_BASEMAP_OFFLINE, ND_DAM_HAZARD_OFFLINE_ONLY, or ND_DAM_SUITE set), or no answer within the map's 30 s network budget: the offline pack's imagery, else the DEM hillshade as before; the credit says which. A map never fails the run.
    * Tile cache in the user cache folder (DamSafety/basemap_cache; NDBREACH_BASEMAP_CACHE = another folder or off; 90 days; NDBREACH_BASEMAP_CACHE_MB, default 200), never in a run or input folder. NDBREACH_BASEMAP_ORDER / NDBREACH_BASEMAP_SOURCES override the sources. The basemap settings are not part of the ensemble cache key.
    3.10.96 (2026-10-28; culvert datum-shift check against the LiDAR channel bed, culvert losses field, opt-in ANUGA culvert bed floor, per-operator volume log (DWR-3034 REVIEW 9.8 items 3, 4))
    * Culvert inverts: the LiDAR bed at an end is the channel beyond it (structures.channel_bed: the lowest ground on a 1 m read up to 30 m out along the line, half the corridor wide) when it reads at least 0.15 m (~0.5 ft) below the disc at the end (structures.end_bed), else the disc as before. DWR-3034's Boyd culvert: the 3 m disc read the toe slope (528.26 / 526.68 m) and the inventory inverts 527.30 / 526.39 m were taken as a -3.4 ft datum shift (moved to 528.33 / 527.41 m); against the channel (527.90 / ~526.6-526.7 m) they are within 3 ft and used as given. A failed 1 m read falls back to the disc.
    * Culvert losses: a structures layer's losses / loss_coef / k_loss field (0-10) sets the ANUGA operator's losses per culvert; out-of-range values are ignored and noted.
    * ANUGA culvert bed floor, opt-in: NDBREACH_CULVERT_BED_FLOOR=enquiry raises an invert below the bed at its enquiry point, =channel below the LiDAR channel bed (default off: no change to the job or the run). Each culvert's culvert_inverts record now has ends (exchange bed min / mean, enquiry bed, invert given / used, floor) and operator_volume_m3; notes flag a phantom head (an invert > 0.3 m below the enquiry bed), a raised end and an operator whose added and removed volumes differ.
    * Per-operator volume log: what each culvert / bridge operator removed and added at each yield (operator_volumes.csv next to the ANUGA results, stats operator_volumes). Read-only; wraps ANUGA's (Parallel_)Structure_operator.__call__.
    * Tests: tests/test_culvert_channel_bed.py (incl. a tiny real-ANUGA culvert with the floor off / enquiry); test_anuga_culvert_inverts updated for the ends record.
    3.10.95 (2026-10-28; ANUGA volume note names the inlet's first step, one bridge-snap breakdown (DWR-3034 REVIEW 9.8))
    * G5 note: when the domain gains water, the note now gives the inflow operators' share of the gain (fractional-step integral minus the hydrograph) and, when ANUGA's first yield was one internal step, that step's booked volume against the hydrograph's (volume_balance first_step). DWR-3034: 92,251 m3 added for 88,186 m3, 4,065 m3 = 100%% of the gain from one 300 s first step (9,994 m3 booked for 5,929 m3); edge 0. It no longer names the mesh edge or an instability for that case; the edge sentence and caution stay for an edge-driven gain.
    * Bridge snap: one breakdown kept_verified / kept_unverified / none / moved, each road + rail (bridge_snap.snap_counts), in run_report.json bridge_snap.counts, the run report and the log. DWR-3034: 49 / 6 / 2 / 1 = 24+25 / 5+1 / 2+0 / 1+0.
    * Tests: tests/test_g5_message_snap_counts.py (incl. a real-ANUGA bowl for the first-step record).
    3.10.94 (2026-10-28; ANUGA: the first dry-domain step no longer over-injects the breach inflow (DWR-3034 G5))
    * On a dry domain ANUGA has no CFL limit, so the first internal step was the whole 300 s yield step and the inlet's 0.5*(Q(t)+Q(t+dt))*dt booked 0.5*Q(300 s)*300 (DWR-3034: 9,994 m3 for 5,929 m3, G5 ratio 1.0461). While the hydrograph flows, domain.evolve_max_timestep is now the hydrograph interval (median spacing, at most 1/100 of its duration and the yield step, at least 0.5 s); the old limit comes back at the first yield step after the hydrograph ends.
    * NDBREACH_ANUGA_INFLOW_MAX_DT = seconds overrides it; 0 / off = the old behaviour. Recorded in the volume balance (inflow_step_cap).
    * Tests: tests/test_anuga_inflow_step_cap.py (rule, release, and a real-ANUGA bowl: volume ratio 1.0000 capped vs 1.2000 uncapped).
    3.10.93 (2026-10-27; damsuite 1.17.7: the ND DWR layers from the offline pack)
    * Slot fill and the dam data read the DWR dams, dam lines, unknown structures and dugouts from the active format-2 pack (dwr/DWR_*.gpkg, built with Hazard 2.18.66 build_nd_pack --dwr-folder) when the suite folder has no DWR file; a DWR file of the engineer's own (suite folder or dwr/) always wins over the pack's copy. No other change.
    3.10.92 (2026-10-27; HEC-RAS export: depth is max WSE minus the actual terrain under each output pixel by default again - the run's 1 m DEM where it has one; reverses 3.10.84's cell-minimum default; cell minimum stays selectable; new bases terrain_mean, terrain_max (conservative) and fine (1 m RAS Mapper-style approximation with Depth_fine / WSE_fine rasters); terrain datum / unit check)
    * Depth (and the wet mask and the 0.1 ft arrival) = the HEC-RAS max water surface of the cell nearest each 30 m grid cell's centre minus the ground at that centre. This reverses the default of 3.10.84 (#189) back to the per-pixel terrain basis of 3.10.79 (#173). On sloped cells the depth is shallower than the cell-minimum depth (never deeper), and the wet / >=0.1 ft areas smaller: DWR-2801 re-exported from the same HDF, wet >0.1 ft 44,376 grid cells on the terrain basis vs 83,690 on cell minimum. Cell minimum stays selectable: RunSettings.hecras_depth_basis / NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS = cell_min, the import tool's Depth basis, ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport --depth-basis cell_min.
    * The ground is the run's own DEM (run_report.json DEM, its elevation unit, less the reservoir carve) when it is finer than the HEC-RAS model terrain by more than 1.5x (DWR-2801: the 1 m composite vs the ~3 m model terrain) or there is no model terrain, else the model terrain (hecras_engine.fine_terrain_on_grid; NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_TERRAIN = auto | dem | model). It is sampled at each grid cell's centre (nearest, TERRAIN_SAMPLE pixel_centre): the WSE is the nearest HEC-RAS cell's to that centre, so depth, wet mask and arrival refer to the same point; 3.10.79 averaged the terrain over the 30 m cell, mixing ground under other cells' water surfaces.
    * Vertical datum / unit check (hecras.datum_check): the ground is compared with the HDF's cell minimum elevations, which come from the model terrain written from the run's DEM with no vertical shift (DWR-2801: NAVD88). A terrain whose median is more than 1 ft below the cell minimum, or whose median is off by more than 10%% (feet vs metres), stops the terrain basis (DatumMismatch): the HEC-RAS stage then keeps its results on the cell minimum with a WARNING, the re-export and the import tool refuse it; NDBREACH_HECRAS_DATUM_CHECK=warn keeps the terrain with a warning. A terrain above the cell minimum everywhere (5th percentile > +1 ft) is a warning. Recorded: hecras_stage.json / reexport.json depth_terrain (source, path, cell size, unit, carve), terrain_sample, datum_check, datum_stop, depth_warnings; run_report.json / .md HEC-RAS depth basis row.
    * Batch chain: the Hazard import adopts the Runner stage's own terrain-basis rasters (no TERRAIN passed) when the stage recorded its terrain; else the terrain basis passes HEC_TERRAIN / the stage's terrain / the package's Terrain folder; the default basis with no terrain found uses the cell minimum with a WARNING (an explicit NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS=terrain still stops the dam). The import tool with no terrain on the terrain basis uses the cell minimum with a WARNING (it refused). Tests: tests/test_hecras_depth_terrain_default.py, test_hecras_depth_cellmin, test_hecras_terrain_depth, test_chain_batch.
    3.10.91 (2026-10-27; ANUGA gmsh mesh holds road / rail crests at their 1 m DEM elevation - DWR-2801 review)
    * ANUGA (gmsh mesh): road and rail centrelines in the wet area + 500 ft are crest lines: embedded in the mesh (no triangle straddles a crest) and their mesh nodes held at the 1 m DEM crest (the highest 1 m reading within the mesh's smallest edge plus 3 m along the line, read from the run's fine DEM). Only raised, never lowered; not in a bridge / notch cut corridor or within 50 m of the inlet. NDBREACH_ANUGA_CREST (on / off), NDBREACH_ANUGA_CREST_PAD_M, _HALF_WIDTH_M, _CELL_M; separate from NDBREACH_ANUGA_BAND (off). run_report.json anuga_mesh.crest. DWR-2801 Hwy 18 (mesh only, Oct 24 run's mesh): pour level 939.07 -> 941.34 ft (edge), above the pond's 939.80 ft max WSE. The HEC-RAS export already holds crests (road / rail bands and terrain subgrid faces): unchanged.
    3.10.90 (2026-10-27; road overtopping depth over the road crown on the centreline, not in a structure-cut trench; NBI bridge cuts snapped to the road's opening - DWR-3034 review)
    * ND hazard tool: a road's overtopping depth is the water surface (WSE (Max).tif) minus the road surface along the centreline every 1 m (metres in a feet CRS too). Road surface: the run's fine DEM in the unit the run recorded (dem_z_to_m, else dem_elevation_units, else the raster's band unit; unknown -> the depth raster), or Road surface DEM in its own unit (new Road surface DEM elevation units) and within 1 m of the run's own ground (else the depth raster, with a Check). Culverts never mask; in a bridge / notch cut corridor (structures.json, no buffer, moved from the work CRS into the rasters' CRS), and a bare-earth opening at one, the road reads on a deck stand-in from the lower approach; other openings read as they are. Wet points with no road surface take the depth raster; a road whose DEM cannot be read keeps it. Every road read lower than the depth raster gets a Check and a road_depth row (dropped / lowered / raster_roads). 'Road overtopping depth' picks the old basis. Synthetic DWR-3034-like trench: 3.28 ft -> not overtopped, 19.7 ft freeboard.
    * Command-line runner: st.road_lines / rail_lines from layers.receptor_roads / receptor_rail and input_files.DEM recorded, as in QGIS (bridge check, ND tool road surface; also the culvert guesses, gmsh embankment lines and HEC-RAS road bands; the run key changes).
    * Runner: each inventory bridge is checked against the road / rail centreline it carries before the inverts are read (pieces whose ends touch merged; a rail bridge - NBI 42A, NDDOT service on, runner_culverts placement, rail in the facility carried - only against rail lines). Its cut stays at the inventory location unless that place is shown to be solid embankment (no DEM opening, channel line or channel beside the road there, the road not dipping there, DEM data there, the road line not ending near); only then it moves to the NEAREST crossing within 300 m: a DEM opening whose bare-earth bed is 1 m or more below the ground beside the road at the cut, or a channel line crossing (with channel lines an opening needs one; none where a road / rail passes under), not one another structure already cuts, each crossing to the nearest bridge whatever the inventory order. A moved cut keeps its length across the road; its inverts are cleared (the cut goes to the lowest ground near its new ends). Every move is a run warning, the bridges kept without an opening or channel seen are named in one; run_report.json bridge_snap (with the radius) and a run_report.md table. NDBREACH_BRIDGE_SNAP_M sets the radius (0 / off / no / false = off). Synthetic DWR-3034-like cut 190 m off on solid fill: moved onto the creek; correct bridges are no longer moved elsewhere. The real DWR-3034 folder was not re-run with these rules.
    3.10.89 (2026-10-26; one-dam run on a downloaded DEM: 'Detect from the raster' reads the Download DEM tool's record of a USGS 3DEP download; a metre DEM run as Feet says to choose Meters; the Download a DEM box passes the county - Nick, Absaraka DWR-2801)
    * 'Detect from the raster' (the default) still stopped on a DEM from 'Download DEM for this dam…' written by Screening 0.17.49 or earlier (and reused by later downloads): those GeoTIFFs have no band unit. gdal_io.recorded_z_unit now also reads the Download DEM tool's own record of a USGS 3DEP download (the ImageServer or the 1 m lidar tiles, metres in every version) - the dataset metadata SOURCE / CRS / UNITS / FILE_TYPE / CELL_M of write_local_dem (every Screening version) together with the <stem>_dem.txt facts file beside it, the two agreeing and describing the raster (file type, CRS, cell size; the whole-record rule below - either half alone also describes a copy). Not trusted: an ND DWR LiDAR record (Download DEM before Screening 0.17.50 could write ND DWR feet grids labelled metres), another tool's SOURCE / UNITS, a facts file of another raster. The rules are core/dem_zunit.py, byte-identical in Screening (tools/ci_checks.py). Tests: dem_z_detect_reads_the_download_dem_record, dem_z_detect_trusts_only_a_usgs_download_record_that_describes_the_raster.
    * 'Detect from the raster' also stops (each was read as metres): a band with a scale / offset (its unit applies to the scaled values, a run reads the raw ones; review round 3: an explicit unit stops on it too - Meters ran a raw-feet band read x 1 - and the message names QGIS Translate with -unscale instead of a unit choice), and a 1 m lidar VRT that Screening built before 0.17.50 over a feet or untagged fallback DEM (the fallback's feet showed through the metre-tagged VRT; such a VRT's json has no fallback_units). Tests: dem_z_detect_stops_on_a_scaled_or_offset_band, dem_z_detect_stops_on_an_old_lidar_vrt_over_a_feet_fallback.
    * The units note of a detected run (the log's 'DEM elevations in ...', report.json dem_elevation_units, the submittal docx) said 'metres (chosen)'; it is now "metres (detected: raster unit 'm')", or the record it came from. Test: rundam_units_note_says_how_the_unit_was_detected.
    * Download a DEM box: County goes to the tool as the Processing enum's index - the tool dialog turned the county name into index 0, so every county download from the Runner window opened as Adams (also before 3.10.88). The ND DWR LiDAR facts line said 'native CRS QL2 feet (often EPSG:3721 ...), native units feet'; DEM_Imagine is UTM metres (EPSG:3721 is UTM 14N), as Screening reads it, and only ASCII_Grids_ft is ND State Plane feet (converted). 'USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles' with Area State is refused in the window (the tool refuses it). The 'DEM elevation units' help, the wizard's DEM line and README say the same. Suite check: Screening 0.17.50 is the recommended minimum (a warning below it). Tests: dem_facts_count_imageserver_chunks_and_offer_the_native_1m_tiles, tests/realqt_dem_rows.py (real Processing checks the window's parameters), test_v320's version check.
    * 'Feet' on a metre DEM stopped with "DEM median 320.2 with units 'feet' is outside the North Dakota ground range (about 250-600 m). Check the elevation unit." - it did not say which unit fits, and a dam on ground above about 328 m was not stopped at all (as feet 100-200 m passed). gdal_io.median_unit_problem: the message gives the metres value and the reading that fits ("Read as metres it is 320 m, which fits: set 'DEM elevation units' to Meters (USGS 3DEP downloads are metres)."), and a feet reading below 200 m now stops when metres fit. The 100-1200 m limits are otherwise unchanged. Tests: median_unit_problem_names_the_unit_that_fits, rundam_runs_an_old_download_with_detect_and_says_meters_for_feet.
    * The DEM's unit is applied once. GDAL's default vertical shift converts the values in a warp from or to a compound CRS: a DEM in ND State Plane (ftUS) + NAVD88 height (ftUS) warped to the 2D UTM model CRS came out in metres, and GdalTerrain.read multiplied by 0.3048 again (1300 ftUS read as 120.8 m instead of 396.2 m, with Detect, Feet or US feet alike); a Manning or NLCD raster warped into a compound feet model CRS was multiplied by 3.28. Every warp of DEM, Manning / NLCD, depth and output values keeps them as stored now (core/warp_opts.no_vshift, gdalwarp -novshift; byte-identical in Screening): GdalTerrain, read_like, nlcd_to_manning_raster, GeoTiffWriter, the HEC-RAS terrain and reads, reservoir carving, the 3DEP refill. Test: dem_unit_is_applied_once_through_a_compound_crs, manning_nlcd_and_outputs_keep_their_values_in_a_compound_crs.
    * An explicit Feet / US survey feet / Meters is checked against the unit the DEM records (gdal_io.resolve_dem_z): a Download DEM output tagged metres run as Feet read the ground x 0.3048 without a stop when it was above 656 m (south-west North Dakota). A trusted record of the other unit: the DEM's median decides (review round 3; round 2 stopped every such run, and the only way through was gdal_edit outside QGIS) - North Dakota ground only in the chosen unit runs, with a warning in the run report to correct the record (gdal_edit -units); only in the recorded unit stops, naming both; when the values cannot tell (both, neither, no valid cell) it stops and names the new choices 'Feet / US survey feet / Meters (the raster's unit tag is wrong)', appended to 'DEM elevation units' after Meters so saved indices keep their meaning (rundam.json, the wizard, the culvert pre-check; the CLI's dem.z_units feet_tag_wrong / us_feet_tag_wrong / meters_tag_wrong), which run in that unit with the warning unless the values fit only the recorded unit. Feet and US survey feet do not stop each other. Tests: an_explicit_unit_the_dem_records_otherwise_stops, an_explicit_unit_against_the_record_runs_when_only_it_fits_the_ground, every_dem_z_enum_keeps_its_indices_and_offers_the_tag_wrong_choices.
    * The North Dakota ground check of the unit reads a sample of the whole DEM (gdal_io.sample_median: 7 x 7 windows, else a decimated read) - it read only the top-left 200 x 200 cells and was skipped without a word when they were nodata (a 1 m DEM clipped to the dam's box or warped to EPSG:5070), so Feet ran a metre DEM x 0.3048. When no valid cell is found the run report says the unit was not checked. The rule works both ways: a feet DEM read as metres at 1100-1200 m (above White Butte) stops when feet fit, and a detected unit is named in the message. Tests: the_nd_ground_check_samples_the_whole_dem_and_says_when_it_cannot, the_nd_ground_check_works_both_ways.
    * Every tool reads the unit the run reads: gdal_io.z_to_m_factor('auto') is resolve_dem_z (it guessed feet or metres from the CRS units and the median), so the culvert pre-check (rundam.json DEM_Z 0), the CLI (dem.z_units auto; an ESRI ASCII grid, which records no unit, now stops), the self-test and (review round 3) the wizard's DEM estimate use the recorded unit and refuse a DEM that records none (the estimate read such a DEM as metres, and let the band unit override the page's choice). The culvert pre-check, the CLI and the estimate also check an explicit unit against the record. The exceptions are the tools with no 'DEM elevation units' to stop on (review round 3: round 2 stopped them naming that setting): Import HEC-RAS results and ndbreach_cli hecras-reexport read a terrain that records no unit in the Runner's own record of its HEC-RAS terrain (hecras_stage.json: written in the model's project unit), else in its CRS's linear unit as before 3.10.88, with a warning naming the unit used and their new 'Terrain elevation units' (CLI --terrain-z; the CLI no longer ends in a traceback on a ValueError); the animation hillshade (it assumed metres) and the HEC-RAS export of a run report older than 3.10.16 do the same, with a warning. Tests: every_tool_reads_the_unit_the_run_reads, an_untagged_hecras_terrain_is_read_in_its_crs_unit_with_a_warning, the_hecras_import_tool_reads_an_untagged_terrain_and_says_how, hecras_reexport_cli_reads_an_untagged_terrain_and_says_how, the_animation_hillshade_reads_the_recorded_unit_else_says_what_it_assumed, the_hecras_export_reads_an_old_run_reports_dem_unit_or_says_what_it_assumed, suite_dams/test_dem_estimate.
    * The run report's 'DEM elevation units' warning comes from a structured flag (the terrain's z_check, workflow.units_warning), not from the word 'check' in the units note, which a detected note's facts-file name could contain. Test: the_units_warning_is_a_flag_not_a_word_in_the_note.
    * A Download DEM record is trusted only whole: the dataset metadata AND the <stem>_dem.txt facts file beside it, with the same source, the facts' file type / CRS / cell size the raster's, and the raster not rewritten more than 10 min after the facts (dem_zunit.download_record). Either half alone describes another raster too: the metadata travels with a copy whatever it did to the values (gdal_translate -scale to feet, a gdalwarp mosaic listing the download first: 1030 ft read as 1030 m), the facts file stays beside a raster re-exported over the download name. A pre-0.17.50 extra-CRS copy (dem_<epsg>.tif, metadata only) now asks for the unit; since 0.17.50 that copy carries a band unit. Test: a_download_record_is_trusted_only_whole.
    * Also refused, whatever the choice (review round 3: an explicit unit ran the VRT): a warped VRT whose GDAL vertical shift converted the values (its band unit is still the source's; the message names QGIS Warp with -novshift), and an ND DWR download written by Screening before 0.17.50 in a folder named gft* (that version scaled DEM_Imagine metres x 0.3048 and tagged them 'm'; core/dem_zunit.data_problem). A GeoTIFF warped that way keeps 'US survey foot' on metres with no trace; the ground check stops it below about 656 m and says the record is wrong, and Meters then runs it with a warning. Tests: a_warp_that_converted_the_values_is_not_read_by_its_band_unit, an_old_nd_dwr_download_in_a_gft_folder_is_refused_whatever_the_choice, a_download_record_is_trusted_only_whole (tests/test_dem_z_once.py).
    * Review round 3: run outputs (depth, velocity, WSE, arrival) are written in the horizontal part of a compound DEM CRS (GeoTiffWriter; also the HEC-RAS import): labelled with the DEM's 'NAVD88 height (ftUS)', a later warp without -novshift (DamSafety Hazard's reproject_raster of a scenario in another CRS, QGIS Warp) rescaled them x 0.3048. The ground check opens a QGIS 'x.tif|option:...' source by its file (it skipped the check without a word), and so does GdalTerrain. The 'record is wrong' guidance says the named choice runs with a warning or the record can be corrected. Tests (tests/test_dem_z_round3.py): outputs_in_a_compound_crs_survive_a_later_warp_without_novshift, a_qgis_source_with_options_is_read_by_its_file, a_chosen_unit_that_fails_the_ground_check_gets_the_chosen_guidance, the_cli_checks_an_explicit_unit_against_the_record, the_carve_and_the_refill_keep_the_values_in_a_compound_crs, the_units_warning_reaches_the_run_report_through_every_terrain_wrapper, the_ground_check_falls_back_to_a_decimated_read_of_a_sparse_dem, an_old_nd_dwr_download_in_a_gft_folder_is_refused_from_its_metadata_alone, a_same_stem_vrt_copy_is_not_described_by_the_facts_file.
    3.10.88 (2026-10-26; DEM elevation units 'Detect from the raster' reads the raster's unit; the Download a DEM box counts the ImageServer requests and offers the native 1 m lidar tiles - Nick, Absaraka DWR-2801)
    * 'DEM elevation units' = 'Detect from the raster' (the default) stopped every run with "Set the DEM elevation unit (feet or metres)..." even when the DEM records its unit. It now reads the unit from the raster (band unit m / metre / ft / US survey foot, or a compound vertical CRS; gdal_io.resolve_dem_z) and runs; only a DEM with no recorded unit (a plain GeoTIFF, a VRT of tiles, an ASCII grid) still stops, now naming the file and the setting: "Set 'DEM elevation units' to Meters or Feet" with which ND / USGS DEMs are which (USGS 3DEP downloads and 1 m lidar tiles, ND DWR .img = metres; ND DWR QL2 ASCII grids = feet). Run dam, Sensitivity, Estimate, Batch and Cascade. Explicit Feet / US feet / Meters are unchanged. Tests: dem_z_detect_reads_the_recorded_unit, rundam_dem_z_detect_runs_a_tagged_dem_and_names_the_setting_otherwise.
    * Download a DEM (Run / Tools): a third source, 'USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles (native, TNM)' (Screening's SOURCE 2: lists the 10 km lidar tiles first, downloads them with '1 m tiles: download the listed tiles', a 1 m VRT tagged metres). For USGS 3DEP the facts line now says how many ImageServer export requests the box takes - 1 m over a dam's 10 mi buffer is 27 x 27 = 729 pieces of 1.2 km (request chunks, not lidar tiles) - and points at the native tiles above 100. Test: dem_facts_count_imageserver_chunks_and_offer_the_native_1m_tiles.
    3.10.87 (2026-10-26; HEC-RAS export: the crest trace finds DWR-2801's embankment; a dam line input in the export tool)
    * Found running the export in real HEC-RAS 6.6 (Ras.exe -c under Wine with .NET 4.8) on DWR-2801 Absaraka, where the trace fell back to a straight 8,188 ft line through the pool. (1) The old rule decides wherever it finds a crest (the highest ridge across the direction to the run's inflow point, at the top of dam on record): its line and breach are used unchanged. (2) Only where it finds none, with the run's flooded area (build_model passes the flooded cells of Depth (Max)), with the walk's step (the DEM's cell, at least 5 ft, coarser for a very long dam) no coarser than the crest width on record and with breach_at one of its options (else the old rule's error, as before), a line off that rule is tried: the crest at the DEM's level, up to max(5 ft, 25%% of the dam height) above the record, when it stands above the ground on both sides there (another vertical datum: DWR-2801's DEM crest is 3-6 ft above its record), the dam point's own ridge first - not when the ground at the dam point is within the tolerance of the record, nor with a ridge at the record's level across the section within the toe distance of it; the dam point's own ridge at the record's level over a higher ridge that the datum rule would take; else, from a dam point on no ridge and with no level top within the toe distance, hecras_crest.snap: the nearest crest-high ground (record - tolerance to record + the datum allowance) within the toe distance plus half the crest width and half a step that the ground climbs to from the dam point, unless the dam point is on the face of another ridge, the trace from there finding its crest within the toe distance. (3) The walks of these traces take a side of the ridge test that runs into no data (a pool left out of the DEM) as falling and drop crest found past a stretch stepped over at their end when it is shorter than that stretch or than two steps (a knoll on the abutment); with a crest width on record at least three times the half-width of the walk's window (1.5 windows) they keep to the middle of a level top that fills the window, but only while that top is no wider than max(1.5 crest widths, the crest width plus a step) - a wider one that falls on both sides is a junction (another embankment joins the crest), one that runs into ground more than the tolerance above the crest is the abutment ("abutment tie-in: ..."), one that runs on at its level past the widest window is named as either - and the walk stops there. A walk also stops where its heading turns more than 20 degrees from the one it settled on while the crest also goes on straight past the bend's footprint for a toe distance (a road crossing it), back to where the turn began - a bend such as DWR-2801's 41-degree one is followed -, and where another crest at the walk's level, standing above the ground on its far side (its near side may be the walk's own crest: a road abutting the dam), lies across the section more than half the crest width on record plus a step beside it within the toe distance (a road joining, leaving or crossing it at a small angle, below the turn limit), back to where it left that crest's line. (4) The breach of such a line is at its deepest section, the toe also looked for along normals turned up to 30 degrees (where the line bends its normals fan out and miss the old channel), and that section must be clear: no other place more than the breach's top width away, along the line and on the ground, with toe ground within max(1 ft, 4%% of H) of the lowest - else the line is not used (two lows about as deep: where the breach goes is not clear). (5) The line is used only with positive evidence that it is the dam (hecras_crest.evidence): the dam point within the toe distance plus half the crest width and half a step of it and the ground climbing from the dam point to it; the run's flooded cells on its downstream side (hecras_crest.flow_check: at least 80%% of those within two line lengths, their centroid within 60 degrees of its downstream normal, no more than one of ten parts more than 65 degrees off it, no ridge near its crest level - nor another embankment at any level, see below - between it and the flood in more than a quarter of its sections: a causeway across the pool, a road below the dam); the run's flow at the valley's scale across it - from no dam line, the direction from the dam point to the centroid of all the run's flooded cells (hecras_crest.valley_axis) no more than 45 degrees off square to its chord (a levee along the valley at the flood's edge is 50-90 degrees off); near it, the directions from its middle and from the dam point's place on it to the run's flooded cells within max(1,000 ft, 1.5 line lengths) each less than 40 degrees off its downstream normal (hecras_crest.valley_flow), the flow path from its downstream toe at the breach (steps to the lowest ground within 60 degrees ahead) running max(1,000 ft, 1.5 line lengths) without reaching the DEM's edge and leaving it less than 40 degrees off square, and that flow path and the flood's direction from the line's middle no more than 40 degrees apart; the pool on its upstream side - water (a level surface as LiDAR flattens it: within 0.01 ft over at least 100 ft, at least the crest prominence below the crest) or no data inside the DEM within twice the toe distance plus the crest width of it, along at least three quarters of it - or, where the DEM shows none there (a dry pool, or the floodplain behind a levee along the valley), the run's flooded cells' long axis clear (their spread along it at least 1.5 times the spread across it) and no more than 45 degrees off square to it; it is the only embankment there - no other ridge at least the line's own ground less the tolerance and the datum allowance high, nor another embankment at any level (hecras_crest._tops: a top whose ground falls at least half the reference height - the lower of H and the line's own height above its downstream toe - on one side and half the crest prominence on the other within 1.5 toe distances, each side only as far as the first ground higher than it; a dam that reads far below a road beside, below or across it is still one), across the section through the dam point (along the line's normal) within three toe distances plus the crest width, at either end within twice the toe distance plus the crest width beyond it, beside it within that distance in more than a quarter of its sections (also a crest-high ridge or level top: a road beside the dam or on the edge of a wide crest), or between it and the pool in more than a quarter of them - looked for along its upstream normal and up the valley (against valley_axis's direction, unless the pool lies just upstream of it) max(1,500 ft, 1.5 line lengths, past the upstream end of the run's flooded area) back and as far again where the DEM goes on, stopping at the pool (water or no data along the normal at least a quarter of that long; up the valley 100 ft), on a read of the DEM of their own (crest_dam_line's window inside it, a wider read outside: they do not stop at the window's edge or take it for a pool left out of the DEM); where the DEM ends short of both U and the pool in more than a quarter of the sections what lies between the line and the pool is not known, so the line is not used; no crest-high ground behind it beyond the toe distance in more than a quarter (it impounds); no bench on its upstream side starting within the toe distance (at least half the crest width on record of ground varying by no more than 40%% of the crest prominence, at least the line's own ground less the tolerance and the datum allowance high, not water: the dam's crest beside a higher road on its downstream face) in more than a quarter; where it dips more than the tolerance below its level (a stretch it stepped over), no ground within the toe distance beside it - off its own crest and the walk it was made from (a corner that smoothing or the straight stretch across the breach cut) - more than the crest prominence above its own ground (it left the dam's crest there: a gap in a road beside the dam); and it is not a part of the embankment: at least half the crest length on record (when one is), its crest not going on at its level (ground within the tolerance of the line's own that stands above the ground on both sides) past its ends for longer than the line itself (a line cut short at junctions). No data inside the DEM's footprint and the read counts as a fall (a pool left out of the DEM), no data beyond either does not. Otherwise the straight line with its WARNING, which keeps the old rule's reason and says what was tried and why it was not used (recorded as snap_tried). (6) Every line off the old rule has one WARNING: how it was traced, its distance from the dam point and its angle at the breach to the run's flow at the valley's scale (the direction from the dam point to the centroid of the run's flooded area, recorded as flow_angle_deg), and its angle to the flooded area near it (from its middle, flow_angle_local_deg) when the two disagree by more than 15 degrees; traced at the DEM's level, the weir follows the higher of the terrain and the record (here the DEM's crest) and the breach elevations use the record. The cut, the pool outline, the storage check and the breach frame follow that line: the dam point moves to its nearest point on it (dam_point_moved_to). On a crest traced at the DEM's level the breach is looked for where the crest is within the tolerance of the record or of that level, or between (breach_detail crest_level_used, crest_band). (7) Export the breach to HEC-RAS has an optional DAM_LINE input (a line layer along the crest, any CRS: the new option dam_line_crs); with it (or any dam line of your own) the dam point (moved to its nearest point on the line), the pool outline and the storage check follow that line. Tests: tests/test_hecras_crest_offcrest.py.
    3.10.86 (2026-10-26; water-body mask: a suite pack that fails its check is not used - #171 follow-up)
    * The suite pack's water-body GeoPackage is no longer used for the mask when the pack check (damsuite 1.17.6) FAILS - a manifest.json that cannot be read (also one saved with a UTF-8 byte-order mark, which the pack readers refuse) - or names that layer's file missing or unreadable (a half-written GeoPackage); the run log says why. pack_gap_notes and build_mask never raise on a broken manifest (before: a broken manifest.json was silently "not a pack" and the file was used unchecked). Test: report_metrics_suite_water_file_not_used_when_the_pack_check_fails.
    3.10.85 (2026-10-26; HEC-RAS export: the normal-depth BC line lies just outside the 2D perimeter)
    * Run in real HEC-RAS 6.6 (Ras.exe -c under Wine with .NET 4.8, DWR-2801 Absaraka), the exported package stopped in the geometry writer: "BC Line 'DS Normal Depth' intersects the perimeter of 2D Flow Area 'Downstream' ... it must be entirely outside the 2D Area Perimeter". The line was the perimeter's own points; it is now drawn a quarter cell (BC_OFFSET_CELLS) outside, along the perimeter's outward normal, the same fix as nd_hazard Runner 3.26.4. With it the package passes geometry processing and solves. Test: tests/test_hecras_bc_offset.py.
    3.10.84 (2026-10-26; HEC-RAS export: depth is the cell's max WSE minus the cell's MINIMUM terrain elevation by default again - reverses 3.10.79's per-pixel default; per pixel stays selectable; the batch chain's Hazard import uses the same basis)
    * Depth = each HEC-RAS cell's max water surface minus that cell's minimum terrain elevation (Cells Minimum Elevation), the same for every grid cell of the HEC-RAS cell. This reverses the default of 3.10.79 (#173), which used the terrain under each grid cell. Cell-minimum depth is never smaller than per-pixel depth and is usually larger on sloped cells: on DWR-2801 (#173's table) >=1 ft 5,060 ac cell minimum vs 1,923 ac per pixel (ANUGA 1,960), >=0.1 ft 17,420 vs 8,843 (ANUGA 6,718), wet 18,727 vs 10,657 (ANUGA 8,290); under per-pixel depth DWR-2801's HIGH homes went from 18 to 0. The per-pixel basis stays selectable: RunSettings.hecras_depth_basis / NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS = terrain (cell_min = default), the import tool's new Depth basis option, ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport --depth-basis cell_min|terrain. The wet mask and the 0.1 ft arrival use the depth of the basis used (pixels the saved steps never reach 0.1 ft get the cell's time of max, as before). The basis is recorded in _hecras/hecras_stage.json (depth_basis, depth_basis_requested, depth_basis_compare with the areas of both bases), reexport.json (areas_by_basis) and run_report.json / .md (HEC-RAS depth basis row). Without a terrain the cell-minimum basis needs none; the terrain basis then falls back to cell minimum with a note (the re-export and the import tool refuse it). Tests: tests/test_hecras_depth_cellmin.py.
    * Batch chain (ndbreach:chainbatch): its Hazard import (ndhazard:hecrasplan) passed the terrain every time (and stopped the dam when none was found), so the chain classified HEC-RAS on per-pixel depth while the Runner's own rasters and run report said cell minimum. It now uses the basis the HEC-RAS stage used: the depth_basis of _hecras/hecras_stage.json when the chain ran the Linux HEC-RAS stage of that plan, else NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS (cell_min default). Only the terrain basis looks for and needs a terrain (HEC_TERRAIN, the Linux stage's terrain_tif, else the package's Terrain folder; none found: the dam stops, as before); for cell_min no TERRAIN is passed and Hazard makes its own cell-minimum depth from the plan, unless the newest current Runner re-export of the plan (or a fresh terrain stage) is on the terrain basis, which Hazard (2.18.63) then adopts. The log names the basis and how to get per-pixel depth: NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS=terrain set before the Runner's HEC-RAS stage runs (a resumed chain does not run it again) or ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport --depth-basis terrain when the stage's record decides (a resumed chain keeps the import as already done, so a re-export is used only when the import runs again: when NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS or HEC_TERRAIN changes, both in its resume check; unticking 'Skip stages already done' also runs the HEC-RAS stage again, which replaces the results the re-export was made from), else the variable (read when the import runs); a stage that was asked for terrain but found no terrain (hecras_stage.json depth_basis_requested 'terrain', depth_basis 'cell_min') is named as such, with the re-export and --terrain; the stage outputs record depth_basis (terrain stays, empty when not used) and Hazard's own depth_basis.json value (hazard_depth_basis). The import's resume fingerprint includes NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS: a resumed chain imports and classifies a dam again once after this update (its earlier import used the terrain) and whenever the variable changes.
    * An unknown RunSettings.hecras_depth_basis / NDBREACH_HECRAS_DEPTH_BASIS stops the HEC-RAS stage before the model is built (HecRasEngineError, so the run says the LISFLOOD-FP results are kept); it was found only after the whole compute, as a HecRasError run_hecras_stage did not catch. reexport.json records the results HDF it read (results_hdf_stat: size, mtime), so DamSafety Hazard 2.18.63 does not take a re-export for the results of a later model run. The recommended DamSafety Hazard is 2.18.63 (was 2.11.0; damsuite versions.py), as the chain's cell-minimum Hazard import relies on Hazard choosing the newest current Runner re-export; the minimum stays 2.9.1. Tests: test_v320 (Hazard 2.18.63 is current), test_chain_batch chain_hazard_import_depth_basis_follows_the_hecras_stage and chain_qgis_ops_hec_wait_and_import (default: no TERRAIN; it asserted TERRAIN before), test_hecras_depth_cellmin hecras_stage_unknown_depth_basis_stops_before_the_model_is_built and hecras_reexport_records_the_results_hdf_it_read.
    3.10.83 (2026-10-26; tests only: the real-Qt DEM-rows test finds DamSafety_Screening through a symlinked Runner, and says why when it cannot)
    * dem_download_rows_keep_their_height_with_real_qt_offscreen failed with 'AssertionError: 1' when the Runner suite ran from a nd_breach_runner -> DamSafety_Runner link outside the repo (or a Runner-only copy). tests/realqt_dem_rows.py looked for DamSafety_Screening only beside the unresolved path, so the Runner built the "Download a DEM (not available)" stub with one label and the test counted 1 row. It looked QGIS 4 only because QGIS 3.34 skipped the test in the same runs (env not activated). Now the test also looks beside the symlink-resolved plugin folder; with no Screening at all it prints SKIP locally and fails under CI; the laptop-screens check asserts the real section was built. No plugin code changed.
    3.10.85 (2026-10-26; HEC-RAS export: tighter solver and plan settings - 40 iterations, 3 s step cap with the variable time step at Courant 1.0 / 0.45, D1D2 3 iterations, cores by mesh size (physical cores only), 2D initial-condition ramp-up, sine breach progression, outflow clipped to the ANUGA mesh with normal-depth edges; every setting NDBREACH_HECRAS_*)
    3.10.82 (2026-10-25; HEC-RAS road/structure/breakline bands: overlapping and crossing bands pass the 8-face check, no near-duplicate seeds - #170)
    * Every band point was protected from the face fixer, so where the bands of nearby roads overlapped (a town's streets, DWR-2801 Absaraka) a cell of protected points among protected neighbours kept more than 8 faces and the HEC-RAS build stopped (4 cells at the default 3 x 20 ft, 1 at 2 x 50 ft). Now: (1) band points closer than 0.7 x the band cell (was 0.5) to an already kept one are dropped (inner rows win), so crossing and close parallel bands leave no near-duplicate seeds; (2) only the first row each side (the points whose shared faces lie on the line) is protected, and only where no other banded line is within 1.5 rows; the outer and graded rows, and the first rows where bands meet, may be moved or dropped by fix_faces, which now drops the neighbour across an over-faced cell's shortest face and splits a big coarse cell ringed by band cells; (3) the Voronoi pre-check, fix_faces and mesh_check run qhull on centred coordinates: at state-plane feet its round-off (about 1e-3 ft) decides near-cocircular faces of thousandths of a foot, so the DWR-2801 build passed 8 faces while its written .g01 had a 9-face cell (on a street grid jittered by 0.001 ft, raw qhull at state-plane coordinates passes all 4 cells over 8 faces). DWR-2801 dry build at the defaults: see PR. Tests: hecras_bands_dense_crossing_streets_pass_the_8_face_check (fails with every first row protected), hecras_bands_crossing_and_close_parallel_lines_no_near_duplicates_and_8_faces, hecras_centred_voronoi_is_the_diagram_at_the_origin (the centring), hecras_voronoi_precheck_is_stable_at_state_plane_coordinates (a stability check), hecras_breaklines_fix_faces_splits_a_big_cell_ringed_by_small_ones, hecras_breaklines_fix_faces_drops_the_neighbour_across_the_shortest_face (fails with FACE_DROP 'self').
    3.10.81 (2026-10-25; tests only: the self-test tests' no_models() also hides the remaining model-discovery settings)
    * Hardening on top of #180, which already hid NDBREACH_LISFLOOD / NDBREACH_ANUGA_PYTHON from these tests (selftest_algorithm_runs_and_writes_the_report passes with them set since #180; this release does not change that). tests/test_selftest.no_models() now also clears NDBREACH_GMSH_PYTHON and NDBREACH_HECRAS_WINEPREFIX / _WINDOWS_DIR / _LINUX_DIR; makes the suite store's lisflood_exe / anuga_python (project override, QgsSettings ndsuite/<key>) and the provider's Processing settings NDBREACH_LISFLOOD_EXE / NDBREACH_ANUGA_PYTHON return no model; makes shutil.which miss lisflood / Ras / RasUnsteady; and stubs core.nd_data's candidate lists (home folders, CONDA_PREFIX, conda env list) besides the detectors. Everything is restored afterwards, also when the block raises. Test no_models_clears_and_restores_the_model_environment sets each of these (fake executables on the PATH) and fails against #180's helper. No plugin code changed.
    3.10.80 (2026-10-25; LISFLOOD-FP gauge discharge: the gauge section is clipped to the grid)
    * A virtual gauge's section was as long as the domain's longest side, laid across the narrow side, so it ran off the grid. LISFLOOD-FP clips an E/W section against the column count and a N/S one against the row count (swapped), so it read past its flux arrays and the gauge discharge was garbage (seen as 1.8e297 m3/s, or a negative value clipped to 0) depending on the build and memory layout. The Runner now clips the section to the grid before writing the .gauge file (core/lisflood._gauge_line). Gauge discharges (gauge CSVs, cascade and gauge reports) of earlier runs may be wrong. Test: gauge_section_is_clipped_to_the_grid; found by the new model-tests CI job on LISFLOOD-FP 8.3.
    3.10.79 (2026-10-25; HEC-RAS export: depth from the terrain under each 30 m grid cell, not the HEC-RAS cell's minimum; re-export of a finished run - DWR-2801 NickReview QA)
    * HEC-RAS depth from the actual terrain: the export took depth = cell max WSE minus 'Cells Minimum Elevation' and rasterised it nearest-cell, so every 30 m grid cell of a 150 ft HEC-RAS cell got the depth of the cell's lowest ditch. Now each grid cell gets the max WSE of its nearest HEC-RAS cell (held flat over the cell, the same nearest-cell mapping as before) minus the run's terrain averaged onto the output grid (GDAL 'average'), clipped at 0 (core/hecras.terrain_depth, core/hecras_engine.terrain_on_grid / results(terrain=...)). A grid cell inside the mesh with no terrain falls back to the cell minimum (counted in the notes). DWR-2801 (nickreview_qa_20261023 REVIEW_HECRAS.md, common domain 51,387 ac): >=1 ft 5,060 -> 1,923 ac (ANUGA 1,960), >=0.1 ft 17,420 -> 8,843 ac (ANUGA 6,718), wet 18,727 -> 10,657 ac.
    * Wet mask and arrival on the same depth: WSE / velocity / time of max are kept only where the terrain depth is wet, and arrival (0.1 ft) is per grid cell, the first time the cell's WSE series minus that grid cell's terrain reaches 0.1 ft (interpolated); a grid cell that reaches it only between output steps (max WSE is kept every computation step) takes the cell's time of the max. hecras_stage.json records depth_basis = terrain (cell_min when the run has no terrain, with a warning).
    * Re-export without rerunning: hecras_engine.reexport_hecras_results(run_dir) and 'python3 ndbreach_cli.py hecras-reexport DAM_FOLDER [--out DIR] [--hdf --terrain --grid --t0-hr --arrival-depth-ft --overwrite]' read the existing .p01.hdf and the terrain and write the four rasters plus reexport.json (old cell-minimum vs new areas) to DAM_FOLDER/_hecras/reexport_<UTC time> by default; an existing output is never overwritten unless --overwrite. The HEC-RAS import algorithm has an optional Terrain input for the same depth.
    * Fix: per-point arrival with more output steps than one read block failed (a name clash in _first_reach).
    3.10.78 (2026-10-25; Windows HEC-RAS launcher: keep fresh RasUnsteady results over a stale file; COM compute marshalled into the watchdog thread - NOT TESTED ON WINDOWS)
    * run_headless promotion fix: a stale <name>.p##.hdf from an earlier run no longer stops this launch's fresh, verified RasUnsteady.exe results from being promoted. The old guard deleted the fresh .tmp.hdf whenever any results file already sat at the canonical name, so a re-run over an old result wrongly reported "no results". Now the fresh verified tmp always replaces the canonical file (os.replace advances the mtime, so the freshness gate still accepts it). Regression test tests/test_hecras_windows.py::hecras_windows_stale_results_overwritten_by_fresh_rasunsteady.
    * run_com watchdog (timeout) fix: when NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S is set, the blocking Compute_CurrentPlan runs in a worker thread. The worker now CoInitializes and receives the controller through CoMarshalInterThreadInterfaceInStream instead of calling the main-thread apartment's object directly (which fails with RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD on Windows). pythoncom is absent off Windows and under the test mocks, where the stream stays None and the worker calls the object directly as before. Windows verification still pending.
    3.10.77 (2026-10-25; the suite pack's water-body layers checked against its manifest.json)
    * Water-body mask (report figures): when no water-body file is used and the suite offline pack's manifest.json lists a water-body layer whose file is missing or unreadable, the run log says so (damsuite.packcheck, files only, read-only) instead of leaving the mask out silently. Suite package 1.17.5 (packcheck).
    3.10.76 (2026-10-25; HEC-RAS: bands of 20 ft cells along roads, rail, structures and breaklines; optional ANUGA line band)
    * HEC-RAS build_model: 3 rows of 20 ft cells each side of the roads, rail, culvert / bridge connection lines and breakline files (faces on the lines), graded out at 1.5:1 to the 2D cell, in the wet area + 500 ft; BreakLine blocks with CellSize Min and Near Repeats in the .g. NDBREACH_BAND=off gives the 3.10.75 geometry. The 2 x 50 ft option: NDBREACH_BAND_CELL_FT=50, NDBREACH_BAND_ROWS=2 (core/hecras_bands.py).
    * ANUGA gmsh line band (NDBREACH_ANUGA_BAND=on; off by default, about +58 %% triangles on DWR-2801): 6.1 m triangles within 18.3 m of the embankment lines, breaklines and culverts; the gmsh gate follows the smallest size line.
    3.10.75 (2026-10-25; HEC-RAS: RasUnsteady stack limit, structure lines on cell faces, connection names without '/' - DWR-2801 NickReview)
    * RasUnsteady gets a bigger stack: the Linux compute died with exit 174 (SIGSEGV in GridGeometry.f90:151, "Reading 2D Area(s)") under the default 8 MB stack on DWR-2801 (421,317 cells). compute() sets the child's RLIMIT_STACK soft limit to unlimited, or to the hard limit when unlimited is not allowed, in the child before exec (preexec_fn), so it holds through nice, a wrapper or Wine; the log and run_report.json (hecras_time_limit.stack_limit) say what was set. No change on Windows (core/hecras_engine.stack_limit_plan / raise_stack_limit).
    * RasUnsteady's OpenMP worker-thread stacks: with the stack unlimited, DWR-2801 still died (forrtl 174) at PROGRESS 0.50 in the Intel OpenMP worker threads, whose 4 MB default stack ulimit -s does not cover. KMP_STACKSIZE and OMP_STACKSIZE are now 1g in RasUnsteady's env (compute), in the Wine env (Ras.exe -c and the RasUnsteady.exe it starts) and for the Windows launcher's Ras.exe / RasUnsteady.exe. A value already set in the environment is kept; NDBREACH_HECRAS_OMP_STACKSIZE sets another default ('off' = unset). The log line 'OpenMP thread stacks for RasUnsteady' and run_report.json (hecras_time_limit.omp_stacksize) record them (core/hecras_engine.omp_stack_env).
    * Structure connection lines lie on HEC-RAS cell faces: the breakline point pairs along a culvert / bridge / weir line are now at most half the line's length apart (floor max(20 ft, 0.2 cell)) and run one spacing past both ends, so a short line gets two or more faces and facepoints at its ends. Before, a line shorter than the cell (DWR-2801: 97-257 ft lines at 150 ft cells) got ONE pair and HEC-RAS turned that face off the line ("The SA 2D Connection at OSM way_254643 2 has an error with the connection"). The gmsh seeds are graded down to that spacing along the short lines (new gmsh_mesher spec key size_lines, as #160's line sizing; ANUGA unchanged).
    * New per-connection check of the share of each line on the cell faces HEC-RAS will make (core/hecras_structures.aligned_share: the Voronoi faces with HEC-RAS's merge of very short faces): under 90 %% is a warning in the log and STRUCTURES_README; under 1 %% stops the build with the list (option structure_align_check = warn to only warn; warn-only when structure_breaklines is off). The old share_on_faces read 100 %% for every DWR-2801 line.
    * Connection names: '/' and other characters HEC-RAS refuses become '_' (letters, digits, spaces and ( ) . _ - are kept), at most 16 characters, unique without regard to case (' 2', ' 3' on a collision), the same name in the geometry, plan and report; STRUCTURES_README lists each renamed connection with its original name (the OSM / NBI id).
    3.10.74 (2026-10-24; batch-chain and Windows HEC-RAS launcher: correctness fixes from a static review)
    * "Cannot verify" is no longer "has results": with no h5py on the HEC-RAS/QGIS box, a partial, geometry-only or stale plan HDF is no longer accepted as a finished compute and handed to Hazard. A result is accepted only when it is positively verified, or (for a manual run) its size is stable over two polls - even when the wait is 0 minutes (core/hecras_windows._has_results, run_headless freshness gate; core/chain.wait_for_plan). A HEC-RAS box without h5py now waits for a verifiable result rather than classifying an unverified plan.
    * Windows HEC-RAS launcher: the COM compute runs under NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S (a watchdog quits RAS and falls back to the manual wait); a timed-out headless run kills the whole process tree, not just Ras.exe; a partial RasUnsteady output is promoted to the canonical .p##.hdf only when RasUnsteady succeeded and the file verifies (the partial is deleted otherwise); a stale pre-existing results HDF is rejected; an unrecognized NDBREACH_HECRAS_WIN_METHODS or a malformed NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S warns instead of silently reverting to the default pair / dropping the limit. Still mock-tested only (Windows verification pending).
    * Batch: a summary/manifest write failure (Summary.csv open in Excel, disk full) is logged and the run continues instead of aborting every later dam. Features dropped for a blank/NULL or a duplicate dam ID are reported in the preflight. Two dam IDs that collapse to the same output folder are a preflight ERROR, and a saved chain state stamped with a different dam is dropped before resume (no cross-attribution). Override cells are kept as raw text (leading zeros and decimals preserved), dams and overrides are matched by a canonical ID (123.0 = 00123 = 123), stage-tool override keys are case-normalized, and a per-dam override that needs an uninstalled stage is caught in the preflight.
    * Chain dialog: the Check preview counts dams with the same ID normalizer the run uses, so a QVariant NULL is not previewed as a real dam.
    3.10.73 (2026-10-24; read-only ANUGA slowdown checker: host freeze or time-step collapse)
    * New core/anuga_slowcheck.py (standard library only; python3 core/anuga_slowcheck.py RUN_FOLDER [--pid N] [--window N|Nm] [--state FILE] [--sample-s S] [--json]): one line VERDICT=normal|finished|host_freeze|timestep_collapse|stalled_unknown|not_running|insufficient_data with a confidence and the numbers (last model t, last line age, seconds the box was frozen since the worker started (wall minus /proc uptime), CPU, dsteps recent vs the window median, model s per wall s, the limiting triangle from timestep_diag.csv). Exit codes 0 normal, 1 finished, 3 insufficient data, 4 host freeze, 5 time-step collapse, 6 stalled, 7 not running. --state compares with the previous call (frozen seconds in between, CPU s per model s now vs the run's). Pre-3.10.70 logs: checkpoint times, the log's time, uptime and CPU, confidence medium or low. Reads only; writes only the --state file.
    3.10.72 (2026-10-24; culvert pre-check before a run)
    * New `ndbreach_cli.py culvert-precheck` (core/culvert_precheck.py, read-only, no model, no QGIS): for each culvert end of a structures layer, the lowest fine-grid ground within max(apron, 5 m) and what the run will do (3.10.71 rules via structures.reconcile_inverts + fine_ground_inverts): HIGH = given invert more than 0.3 m above that ground (the run caps it), FALLBACK = no fine-grid data there (the run uses the 30 m model DEM). Input: a dam folder (rundam.json, structures*.gpkg, *composite*.vrt) or --structures + --fine-dem; output: markdown, CSV and JSON per dam.
    3.10.71 (2026-10-24; ANUGA culvert inverts on the fine ground; enquiry points past the inlet triangles)
    * ANUGA culverts: with the fine terrain bed (ANUGA_ELEVATION=fine), an invert the structure does not give is the lowest fine ground within max(apron, 5 m) of the end, not the 30 m model DEM (DWR-2801: a median 0.69 m, up to 2.3 m, too high). No fine ground there: the model DEM as before, noted. A given invert more than 0.3 m above that ground is lowered to ground + 0.3 m with a WARNING; otherwise it is kept as given.
    * The head is read one triangle edge (1.3 x the equilateral edge of the culvert-end area: 9.9 m at 25 m2) past each inlet region (ANUGA enquiry_gap), not inside an inlet triangle; where the triangles at an end are larger (e.g. a road mesh line through the culvert's refined region), the gap is raised to 0.5 m past the furthest triangle over that inlet (noted). Not passed to an ANUGA without enquiry_gap, nor where the point would leave the mesh (noted). Inverts, sources and gaps: run_report.json anuga_culvert_inverts, the summary and the manifest. HEC-RAS packages take the same inverts.
    3.10.70 (2026-10-24; ANUGA time-step diagnostic, opt-in; wall clock on progress lines)
    * New opt-in ANUGA time-step diagnostic (anuga_dt_diag, or NDBREACH_ANUGA_DT_DIAG=1 and NDBREACH_ANUGA_DT_DIAG_EVERY=N; off by default): every N yield steps a row of _breach_model/anuga/timestep_diag.csv with ANUGA's internal dt (last, min, max, mean and the step count since the last row), the triangle limiting the last step (centroid x/y in the model CRS and lon/lat, area, depth, speed) and the nearest breach inlet or culvert when within max(3 inradii, 50 m); timestep_diag_summary.txt ranks the limiting triangles. Read-only on the domain: the results are bit-identical on and off. A resumed run appends after the checkpoint's time.
    * Every ANUGA PROGRESS line (always) appends utc=, steps= (cumulative), dsteps= (since the last yield), wall_s= (since the last yield) and rate= (model s per wall s), so a host freeze (a long wall gap, normal steps) can be told from a real slowdown (many small steps). The fields are appended; the line's start is unchanged.
    3.10.69 (2026-10-24; ANUGA gmsh far-field sizing, opt-in)
    * gmsh mesher: NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_GRADE_DIST_M (grading distance from lines; channel 2x, culvert ends 0.6x), NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_LINE_MIN_AREA_M2 (smallest triangle on lines, e.g. 200 m2), NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_MAX_AREA_M2, NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_REFINE_FOOTPRINT=wet (refine only inside the LISFLOOD-FP wet area + NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_REFINE_PAD_M, 200 m). Same keys in anuga_gmsh.
    * Unset = the mesh is exactly as before (pinned by tests/test_gmsh_sizing.py). Bad values stop the gmsh mesh with a clear message; the values used are in run_report.json anuga_mesh.sizing.
    3.10.68 (2026-10-22; Batch chain: HEC-RAS 6.6 launcher for Windows - NOT TESTED ON WINDOWS YET)
    * New core/hecras_windows.py: on Windows the chain's HEC-RAS 'Run' tries headless first (Ras.exe -c project plan, then RasUnsteady.exe for the .p##.tmp.hdf), then the HEC-RAS Controller COM ('RAS66.HECRASController' through pywin32: Project_Open, Plan_SetCurrent, Compute_CurrentPlan blocking, close). Both need the terrain made first: if the package has none the dam goes to 'waiting' with the RasMapper steps (CHAIN_HECRAS_TODO.txt). Any launcher failure also falls back to 'wait for a manual run'; a plan already computed by hand is taken without launching.
    * Settings: NDBREACH_HECRAS_WINDOWS_DIR (folder with Ras.exe), NDBREACH_HECRAS_WIN_METHODS (headless,com), NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S (time limit per method). Written from mocks; not run against HEC-RAS or on Windows (Mac + VMware Fusion test pending).
    3.10.67 (2026-10-22; Batch chain: a list of dams through the whole suite, dam by dam)
    * New 'Batch chain: dams through the whole suite…' window (the Runner window's Dam tab; Processing ndbreach:chainbatch). For each dam of a list (a point layer, its selected dams only by default; typed IDs; or an IDs CSV): the Screening steps for that dam only -> the Runner -> HEC-RAS 2D build (the Runner's culvert inventory; an existing ANUGA run's culverts and bridges when there is one, ANUGA is never launched) -> HEC-RAS run -> Hazard import (ndhazard:hecrasplan) -> Hazard classify (ND + TM 11), then the next dam. A dam that fails is a 'failed' row with the stage and reason and the list goes on.
    * HEC-RAS 2D stage: Run (where an engine is available) / Wait for a manual run (the chain writes the package and _chain/CHAIN_HECRAS_TODO.txt, then waits up to N minutes for the plan's results .p##.hdf; a dam still waiting is 'waiting' and a resume picks it up) / Skip (the Runner's LISFLOOD-FP or ANUGA rasters go straight to Hazard). Per batch and per dam (overrides CSV column HEC_MODE = run / wait / skip).
    * Settings: one suite settings file (damsuite format) with a section per stage (ndscreen|window, ndbreach|batch, ndbreach|hecrasexport, ndhazard|hecrasplan, ndhazard|classify) and the chain's own rows (ndbreach|chainbatch); 'Fill from the settings file' / 'Save to the settings file' in the window. An overrides CSV changes single dams (dam_id plus only the columns that differ, 'plugin.tool.KEY' or a chain key). 'Check' runs a preflight (missing plugins by algorithm ID, missing files, unknown dam IDs or override columns) before anything runs.
    * Run options: resume (finished stages whose outputs are still there are kept), stop after a stage, keep going, cancel. Each dam gets _chain/chain_state.json and _chain/chain_settings_used.json (values, overrides applied, software versions, input checksums); the output folder gets Chain_Batch_Summary.csv (after every dam) and chain_manifest.json.
    * Each classified dam's Hazard window state is saved as <dam folder>/Hazard_<ID>.qgz (ndhazard:savedamstate, Hazard 2.18.59): Project > Open it, open the Hazard window, use 'Add scenario' / 'Add from a folder…' and Classify again.
    3.10.66 (2026-10-22; the batch and the CLI run the HEC-RAS 2D stage; numbered after #155's 3.10.65)
    * ndbreach:batch with Models = 'LISFLOOD-FP then HEC-RAS 2D' gave each dam LISFLOOD-FP results only, without a word: the HEC-RAS 2D stage ran in 'Run breach model for one dam' only. The stage is now shared (core/workflow.run_hecras_stage) by the one-dam tool, the batch and the ndbreach CLI (run and batch). A dam whose HEC-RAS run fails is a 'failed' row with the reason (its LISFLOOD-FP results stay) and the batch goes on; 'skip finished dams' also needs a finished _hecras/hecras_stage.json (with its results HDF; a timed-out stage is not finished) for this tier. The CLI checks the HEC-RAS engine up front like the batch.
    3.10.65 (2026-10-22; HEC-RAS structure connections 2 cells inside the 2D area)
    * HEC-RAS culvert / bridge connections: both ends of each line stay at least 2 cells inside the 2D perimeter and the build stops otherwise (an end one cell from it stopped HEC-RAS at Absaraka KO-01 with "Check that the To: is set correctly"); an adverse culvert keeps its inverts with a note; add_to_package(exclude=) leaves named structures out
    3.10.64 (2026-10-22; Download DEM downstream bearing)
    * Download DEM 'Downstream bearing' (deg from north, optional): when set, the single-dam DEM box is shifted so the dam sits near the upstream edge and the box reaches downstream instead of being centred on the dam; same box size, so the download is no larger. Blank keeps the centred box. The Runner 'Model one dam' wizard's Terrain page carries the same field and passes it to the download. County and state areas ignore it.
    3.10.63 (2026-10-21; tool dialogs stay in front of the window and the wizard)
    * Tool dialogs opened from the window (DEM download, offline layers, the suite tools) are owned by the window and modal (new shared/ui/tool_dialog.py). Before, QGIS's execAlgorithmDialog owned them from its main window and showed them before exec_(), so they were not modal: a click on the plugin window raised it over the tool, and from the Runner's 'Model one dam' wizard (modal itself) the DEM download could not be clicked at all, the wizard popped up in front of it. No tool, parameter or default changed.
    3.10.62 (2026-10-21; Qt6/PyQt6 enum hygiene and a suite-wide CI lint for unscoped Qt enums)
    * Qt6/PyQt6 (QGIS 4.x) enum hygiene: core/qa_maps.py's no-matplotlib QImage fallback (QImage.Format_RGB32) and the suite_dams NID reply handler (QNetworkRequest / QNetworkReply attributes and errors), plus the NID-fill test's field type, now resolve the scoped Qt6 enum with a getattr fallback that keeps Qt5 working, instead of the unscoped names that raise AttributeError / TypeError on PyQt6. These paths were latent (no Qt6 test exercised them). No result changes.
    * New fast CI gate tools/ci_checks.py 'qt6 enums' (with tools/test_ci_checks.py): it forbids the unscoped Qt enum spellings and bare-int / QVariant QgsField field types that broke CI in #131 and #134, across the whole suite; strings and comments are masked and hasattr-guarded or '# qt6-ok' lines are allowed. run_all.py (Hazard and Screening) now prints a failing script's full output, so a traceback can no longer be hidden by the old 12 / 6 line tail.
    3.10.61 (2026-10-20; HEC-RAS 2D gmsh points time out on the wall clock; renumbered from 3.10.41)
    * HEC-RAS 2D gmsh points (core/hecras_seeds.py): the gmsh run in another Python now times out on the wall clock through core/wallclock.py, as the HEC-RAS engine and the ANUGA mesher do (#96). Elapsed time is the larger of wall and monotonic time, so a paused or resumed VM counts. On expiry the process group gets SIGTERM, 5 s to exit, then SIGKILL. Before, subprocess.run(timeout=) used the monotonic clock only and killed the interpreter alone, so a child it had started could keep running. Same 600 s timeout and messages; no mesh or result changes. tests/test_hecras_seeds_wallclock.py.
    3.10.60 (2026-10-20; gdal.Warp without source overviews; renumbered from 3.10.43)
    * DEM to model grid without the source's overviews (gdalwarp -ovr NONE; new core/warp_opts.py, the same file as Screening's). gdal.Warp's default (-ovr AUTO) took a coarser target from the source's nearest overview, so a 1 m lidar tile or VRT of tiles that carries overviews was resampled to a 5 m grid from its 4 m overview (10 m from its 8 m one) instead of the 1 m cells. gdal_io.GdalTerrain (DEM and Manning to the model grid), gdal_io.read_like, hecras_model._warp and hecras_model.write_terrain now pass overviewLevel='NONE' (the keyword in the 3.8.5 bindings of QGIS 3.34 here and in 3.10 / 3.13; '-ovr NONE' otherwise, GDAL 2.0+). Model grids built from a DEM WITH overviews can shift slightly (typically 1-2 cm mean |dz| for AVERAGE/CUBIC overviews, 5-12 cm for NEAREST ones; mean bias about 0), so result baselines may move; a DEM without overviews gives identical grids. dem_refill is unchanged (its 3DEP tile is fetched at the grid cell). Test: tests/test_warp_no_overviews.py.
    3.10.59 (2026-10-21; HEC-RAS package gets the culverts and bridges ANUGA used; terrain CRS guard; renumbered from 3.10.56)
    * HEC-RAS builder (core/hecras_structures.py): the culverts and bridges can come from another run of the dam (option structures_from = its dam folder or run_report.json; Processing 'Culverts and bridges from another run'), e.g. the ANUGA run when the package is built from a LISFLOOD-FP run. Culverts take the inverts ANUGA itself used (anuga/anuga_job.npz) when the report leaves them empty. A bridge or notch on the same road crossing as a culvert becomes an open gap in that culvert's road weir (it used to be left out: 'too close to ...').
    * The package stops if the terrain GeoTIFF lost its EPSG code or does not match Projection.prj, or proj.db is unreachable. Culvert connections inside the 2D area are routing type 1 (type 3 stopped HEC-RAS: "check the To:"). The connection lines are breaklines built into the computation points (option structure_breaklines, default on), with the 8-face and perimeter pre-checks on the points near them only. Before HEC-RAS, each structure weir face and culvert invert is checked against the lowest ground of the cells it connects and raised to it + 0.01 ft if below, and listed (option structure_cell_fit, default on); the terrain is never changed.
    3.10.58 (2026-10-21; run queue QA a_culvert_anuga: ANUGA outer edges are outflow-only; renumbered from 3.10.55)
    * ANUGA's outer edges were anuga.Transmissive_boundary, which copies the inside momentum to the edge, so water moving inward next to an edge (a pond against a clipped edge, an eddy, flow along the edge) was fed in from outside. The NDDOT culvert run (2026-10-21; its 1,000 m pass clipped by the LISFLOOD-FP grid at the SE corner) ended with 8.25 M m3 in the domain for 1.99 M m3 of breach inflow (4.15 x) and a 10 ft pond along the clipped edge; the 2026-10-15 structures run 1.51 x. Now the edges are transmissive where water leaves and mirror the normal momentum where it would enter (zero mass flux), so no edge gains water. NDBREACH job key edge_bc ('outflow' default, 'transmissive' = before). ANUGA results change wherever the flood reaches the mesh edge; a run that stayed inside is unchanged.
    * The worker records ANUGA's boundary flux integral (DE algorithms) and a volume balance (in domain / (initial + breach inflow)); above 1.02 the run gets a 'volume check FAILED' warning in Things to check. The run report's ANUGA lines show the ratio.
    3.10.57 (2026-10-21; run queue QA c4_hecras_v2e_gmsh_r: HEC-RAS compile errors reach the log; dam connection weir-cell pre-check; renumbered from 3.10.54)
    * Ras.exe -c that exits without writing the run inputs now raises an error with HEC-RAS's own Compute Messages (plan HDF Results/Summary/Compute Messages (text) and the Summary 'Solution', from <name>.p01.hdf or .p01.tmp.hdf, else <name>.p01.computeMsgs.txt; the ERROR lines first). Before, the 'wrote no ... within {:.0f} s' message could not format the None (no) time limit of 3.10.48, so a TypeError hid the real error (v2e_gmsh_r: 'SA 2D Connection at Dam has a weir elevation lower than the cells').
    * A None time limit reads 'no time limit' / 'no limit' in every HEC-RAS engine message (hecras_engine.within_text, _hm).
    * HEC-RAS builder: a CHECK-ONLY pre-flight of the cells along the dam connection where the weir is below the top of dam (the spillway notch) against their lowest ground on the written terrain (HEC-RAS refuses a weir lower than a cell it connects to). Option weir_cell_check: warn (default; note, site checks, run_report.json 'hecras_weir_cells'), fail or off. The terrain, the weir and the notch are never changed. A bad weir_cell_fail_ft is a settings error (raised before the check), not 'pre-check not run'.
