```text
    3.10.56 (2026-10-21; ANUGA thread-count checks, Docs & QA follow-ups to #132; numbered 3.10.49 on its branch until #137-#143 took 3.10.48-3.10.55)
    * NDBREACH_ANUGA_THREADS=inf raised OverflowError in resolve_threads and stopped the run; inf, nan and text are now ignored with a note (the run log, run_report.json["anuga_threads"]["rule"]) and the automatic count is used.
    * Decimal counts (2.7) were truncated (to 2); a count must be a whole number (6 or 6.0), anything else is ignored with a note. Same for the threads setting.
    * An explicit or NDBREACH_ANUGA_THREADS count above the CPUs this process may use (e.g. 64 on 8) is clamped to them, with a note.
    * ndbreach_cli.py --threads help: 0 = automatic (LISFLOOD-FP all cores; ANUGA NDBREACH_ANUGA_THREADS, else CPUs - 2, at most 4), no longer '(0 = all)'. No result changes.
    * Review (Docs & QA): an explicit negative count (threads=-3) is ignored with a note too (it fell through silently); cpu_count() notes that a cgroup CPU quota is not read.
    3.10.53 (2026-10-21; US / State highways at least Medium, REG_05.2026 5.2.1.2(b); arrival depth 0.1 ft; on #130; numbered 3.10.45 on its branch)
    * Arrival depth (ARRIVAL_FT) is 0.1 ft by default (was 1 ft): the ND hazard 'any depth', so every wet structure gets an arrival time (REG_05.2026 5.4(c); 9 of 10 wet structures in the DWR-2801 sample had none at 1 ft). One constant (core/units.ARRIVAL_DEPTH_FT) for Run a dam, the HEC-RAS import, Breach products from rasters, the CLI template, LISFLOOD-FP inittm_depth and ANUGA. The same depth is the 'flooded' depth of the run summary (flooded_acres_over_arrival_depth, receptors flooded, velocity at structures, flood extent / arrival zones), so those counts grow. The domain-extension edge tolerance (EDGE_TOL_FT, 1 ft) is unchanged. Results run before 3.10.53 keep 1 ft until re-run.
    * ND hazard classification (core/nd_hazard.py 1.2.0): an overtopped US or State highway is at least Medium whatever its AADT (5.2.1.2(b), p. 23); the private / AADT < 500 Low rule (5.2.1.2(a)) is left for local, county and private roads. Interstates and AADT > 2,000 stay High. A highway is told from the class field or the name: 'US 2', 'U.S. Highway 85', 'ND 23', 'ND-1804', 'State Hwy 22', 'Highway 23' / 'Hwy 2' (not 'County Highway 12'), TIGER route types U / S, OSM trunk / primary (nd_hazard.us_state_highway_from_text). The finding's reason cites 5.2.1.2(b).
    * A flagged structure (damsuite/structure_flags.py, DWR-3161) keeps its hazard of record in the ND hazard tool: class_to_use is the record's class, the report and log say the computed class is for information only.
    3.10.52 (2026-10-20; hazard tables against REG_05.2026; numbered 3.10.44 on its branch)
    * 'Hazard Classification Findings.csv' / .geojson (core/nd_hazard.py) have a danger_zone column after zone, with the zone names of REG_05.2026 Figures 5.1 / 5.2 (HIGH DANGER ZONE / JUDGMENT ZONE / LOW DANGER ZONE); zone keeps its values. The ND Fig 5.1 / 5.2 chart legend uses the same names. No class or default changes. 3.10.43 is #128's.
    3.10.51 (2026-10-20; REG_05.2026 zone colours; numbered 3.10.42 on its branch until #127-#139 took 3.10.41-3.10.50)
    * The ND Fig 5.1 / 5.2 review charts (core/nd_hazard.py chart_svg) and styles/acer_zones.qml / acer_zone_points.qml use the colours of REG_05.2026 Figures 5.1 / 5.2: High #EF9897, Judgment #F9F4A3, Low #C4E0AD (the judgment zone was peach, the low zone nearly white; the zone rasters were Material red / yellow / green at 0.75 opacity, now full opacity). Breakpoints and classes unchanged. 3.10.41 is not used here: it belongs to the open PR #127. Zone legend labels follow the standard (HIGH DANGER ZONE / JUDGMENT ZONE / LOW DANGER ZONE).
    3.10.50 (2026-10-21; #132 follow-up: the real OpenMP thread count, Windows ANUGA envs. 3.10.49 is taken by the open PR #138)
    * The run report's ANUGA thread count now comes from the OpenMP runtime itself (core/omp_probe.py: ctypes omp_get_max_threads of the libgomp / libomp / libiomp5 / vcomp the ANUGA process loaded). anuga.get_omp_num_threads() only echoes the count it was given, so anuga_reported could never show a run that stayed on one thread. stats: omp_runtime_threads, omp_runtime; a note when the runtime's count differs or cannot be read.
    * Windows: ANUGA conda environments are found (envs\*anuga*\python.exe, an active env named *anuga*, and %%USERPROFILE%%\miniforge3 / %%LOCALAPPDATA%%\miniforge3); before only bin/python was looked for. Linux and macOS search the same places as before.
    * .github/workflows/anuga-omp-scaling.yml (manual) with tools/anuga_omp_bench.py: ANUGA at 1/2/4 threads on Windows, macOS (Apple Silicon and Intel) and Linux, time and checksum.
    3.10.48 (2026-10-21; HEC-RAS 2D compute without a default wall-clock limit. 3.10.45 is #131's, 3.10.46 is reserved)
    * The RasUnsteady compute has NO wall-clock limit unless NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S is a positive number. 3.10.17-3.10.47 stopped it at max(2 h, 3 x the estimate), capped at 48 h, with the variable unset, which killed long runs. Unset, empty or 0 (also none / off): no limit. A positive value: a limit as before (SIGTERM to the process group, NDBREACH_HECRAS_KILL_GRACE_S, then SIGKILL; also each preprocess step). Text or a negative number: ignored with a note in the log.
    * The Wine preprocess steps (terrain.exe, mesh.exe, drv.exe, Ras.exe -c) have no limit either without a value (they had 1,800 s each): no limit anywhere. A Wine dialog waiting for a click then waits until someone deals with it (NDBREACH_HECRAS_TCU_HOOK, or set a positive value for unattended batches). The gmsh-points timeout is unchanged.
    * The limit in force ('none' or N s, why, and the run-time estimate) is in the Runner log (compute and preprocess lines), run_report.json["hecras_time_limit"], _hecras/hecras_stage.json and the run report ("HEC-RAS wall-clock limit"). No result changes.
    3.10.47 (2026-10-21; ANUGA OpenMP threads. 3.10.41-3.10.46 are taken by open PRs #126-#131)
    * ANUGA ran on ONE thread when the threads setting was 0 ("0 = all cores"): the Runner set nothing and ANUGA 4.0.1 defaults to 1 thread without OMP_NUM_THREADS (the refined 72 h Absaraka and 20x20 runs used 1 of 8 cores). 0 now resolves to NDBREACH_ANUGA_THREADS when set (> 0), else min(CPUs - 2, 4), at least 1; an explicit count is used as given. The job file, the worker's OMP_NUM_THREADS and anuga.set_omp_num_threads (also after a checkpoint resumes) get the count; runner.log, anuga.log, run_report.json["anuga_threads"] and run_report.md record it. LISFLOOD-FP and gmsh are unchanged.
    * Results do not change: 1 vs 4 threads bit-identical (real ANUGA, 589 and 87,749 triangles; a run killed at 1 thread and resumed at 4 is bit-identical too). About 2.6x faster at 4 threads on the 87,749-triangle check (62 s -> 24 s); Grok Bot's 262k-triangle benchmark: 2.0x at 4, 3.0x at 8.
    * A build without OpenMP, or without set_omp_num_threads, runs on one thread and says so in the report; never an error.
    * The per-step envelope update (max depth / speed / WSE, arrival) works on the deep triangles only and no longer builds full temporary arrays: the same numbers bit for bit (tests/test_anuga_threads.py), about 25-30 %% less time in that serial step.
    * Threads help text (provider setting and tool parameters) says what 0 means for each model.
    3.10.40 (2026-10-20, fixes of the 2026-10-19 audit, rebased onto current main)
    * R-H1: scripts/build_lisflood_linux.sh (and Setup's copy, kept byte-identical, checked by tools/ci_checks.py) applies patch_lisflood.py before cmake and refuses an unpatched build.
    * F-02 (tests): the Screening <-> Runner parity tests find the in-repo Runner and FAIL in CI when it is missing.
    3.10.39 (2026-10-20; #107)
    * Main window: the rows of 'Download a DEM' keep their height when the section opens. The window has an explicit minimum size (fit_window), so Qt did not raise it to the layout's minimum and squeezed the rows to 6-9 px, drawn on top of each other. The dam rows, Download a DEM, Advanced and the Run/Tools tabs now sit in a scroll area (the action bar, the Log and Close stay below it), the sections keep their own height, the Run/Tools tabs take the room that is left, and opening a section grows the window up to its screen. No change to any setting or result. Numbered 3.10.36 on its branch until #121 and #123 took 3.10.36 and 3.10.37; #117 claims 3.10.38.
    3.10.38 (2026-10-20; ANUGA checkpoints and partial results, #117; numbered 3.10.35 on its branch until #120 took it, 3.10.36 / 3.10.37 came with #121 / #123)
    * ANUGA checkpoints (ANUGA 4.0.1 domain.set_checkpointing) ON FOR LONG RUNS by default: a run of >= 12 h simulated or >= 50,000 expected triangles checkpoints every 30 wall-clock minutes (keep 2) and writes anuga/anuga_partial.npz every 10 minutes; a short run is unchanged. NDBREACH_ANUGA_CHECKPOINT_MIN=0 switches it off, > 0 forces that cadence; NDBREACH_ANUGA_CHECKPOINT_STEPS, _KEEP, _PARTIAL_MIN set the rest.
    * The hydraulics are unchanged: bit-identical depth, velocity, arrival and WSE with and without checkpoints on a real-ANUGA test domain (31 checkpoints written).
    * Partial results: anuga_partial.npz (the envelope grids so far, atomic write) after every checkpoint, periodically and on SIGTERM / Cancel.
    * Resume: NDBREACH_ANUGA_RESUME=1 (or the run's Resume with checkpoints on) continues the same ANUGA job from its newest complete checkpoint; a different job starts over. Recorded in run_report["anuga_checkpoint"].
    * An unpicklable domain or too little disk: the run goes on without checkpoints and says why.
    3.10.37 (2026-10-20)
    * Engine chips put the mark first ('✕ ANUGA'), like every other mark in the suite. A missing ANUGA is amber, not red, because only the ANUGA tier needs it; the tooltips say 'installed' / 'not installed'.
    3.10.36 (2026-10-20; GUI grammar, part 3)
    * The dam wizard is the suite's one per-dam wizard (docs/gui-grammar.md). DamSafety Setup's Start here… hands it the suite data folder's scan: its DWR dams are a 'Dams from' choice, with a County filter and 'Name (ID)' in the dam list. The dam page opens with a DamCard summary (name · county · height · storage) whose amber lines are the page's notes (no top of dam, no surface area, values from dam.json); the values are in two groups, Required and Improves the estimate, each row led by its mark (✓ / ○ / ✕). The terrain page says live whether the DEM covers the dam point; roughness is an Advanced fold whose header shows the values. With the suite's DWR dams (moved from Setup's dam page, suite_dams/): a County and Name search, 'Show all records' for the records that are not a dam to model, the NID layer of the folder filling a blank height or storage, 'Fill from NID (online)' (only on a click) and 'Estimate from DEM' (a screening estimate in a background task; a loaded DEM that covers the dam is used, else it asks for one). A value that is not the DWR record's is marked ⚠ and named in the card and on the Review page. A page's problems show in red on the page, not in a message box. 'Download DEM for this dam…' and 'Open the tool…' carry the ellipsis. tests/realqt_wizard.py checks the wizard in real QGIS (CI). No default changes.
    3.10.35 (2026-10-20; GUI grammar, part 2)
    * Main window: no title line inside the window (the window title names it); the ⚙ sits on the tab bar as in Hazard and Screening; Run this dam (no ▶) is at the bottom right of an action bar under the tabs, with the engine chips on its left. The settings window's legend is two lines: the notation (bold, [YOUR value]) and, quieter under it, the units footnote. No default changes.
    3.10.34 (2026-10-20; GUI grammar, part 1)
    * GUI grammar foundation (docs/gui-grammar.md): window colours from the shared tokens.py; the shared window files have one canonical copy in shared/ui/ (tools/sync_ui.py, checked in CI); tools/gui_lint.py opens every window in real QGIS and checks the rules.
    * Settings window: '&' in tab names shown, Value column in view, title 'DamSafety Runner — Settings'. Wizard title 'DamSafety Runner — Model one dam'. Processing provider named DamSafety Runner. Run this dam keeps Qt's look (no painted blue). Menu entry has the plugin icon. ui_fit.py is the canonical copy (this plugin's saved-size version, now in all four). No default changes.
    3.10.32 (2026-10-19; PR #94, Claude's audit-fix campaign)
    * M6 (default changes): top of dam from the 98th percentile of the DEM within 30 m of the dam line, not the single highest cell; a lower, less conservative crest where a road or hill spike was picked before.
    * M5 (opt-in, off by default): a built-in numpy local-inertial flood engine ('builtin' keyword / NDBREACH_USE_BUILTIN); labelled on every report surface; not validated against LISFLOOD-FP. The default still uses LISFLOOD-FP.
    * H6, H7, H8, M7-M9, M11-M16, L1: closed upstream edges wired into the fine FV1 run; HEC-RAS export units from the CRS; metre-DEM-in-feet-CRS check; DEM-estimate datum label; and the medium/low fixes listed in docs/Fix_campaign_report.md.
    3.10.31 (2026-10-19)
    * ANUGA bed from the finest terrain (ANUGA_ELEVATION, default "fine"; core/anuga_model.py prepare_elevation, core/anuga_worker.py): ANUGA's elevation was the 40 m LISFLOOD-FP model grid sampled at the triangle centroids, so a road or rail crown along an embedded gmsh line kept only 12-21 %% of its fill height (2026-10-19 benchmark, DWR-2801/2662/4441). The bed is now read from the DEM source at its own cell size (the same conditioning, refills and carve as the model grid; 2 m when the source's cell size is unknown, at least 0.5 m, coarsened only to stay under 256 million cells) in 2048-cell blocks into a memory-mapped float32 .npy beside the job (blocks off the mesh are skipped; the file is deleted after a successful run), and the worker sets it at the triangle VERTICES (bilinear). ANUGA makes each edge the mean of its two vertices and the centroid the mean of three, so a crown on an embedded edge keeps its full height on that edge and 2/3 of it in the triangles each side; an area mean of the fine cells would spread a 2 m crown over a 20 m triangle. Benchmark, gmsh with embankment lines: share of the fill kept 13-21 %% -> 56-63 %% at the centroids, 81-82 %% on the edges. Vertices without fine data use the model grid. LISFLOOD-FP keeps its model grid. run_report["anuga_elevation_source"] records the mode, source, cell size and sample counts; ANUGA_ELEVATION=model_grid (or NDBREACH_ANUGA_ELEVATION) restores the old bed and job file exactly; NDBREACH_ANUGA_ELEVATION_CELL_M sets the cell.
    * gmsh mesher, embedded lines just longer than the mesh size (core/gmsh_mesher.py short_edge_counts): gmsh cuts an embedded curve of length L into floor(L / h) + 1 pieces, so a 20.0 m embankment end segment at h = 19.76 m became two 10 m pieces and the triangles there (44.06 m2) failed the 45 m2 gate (DWR-4441). Edges shorter than 3 local sizes are now set transfinite with round(L / h) pieces (at least 0.75 h each); the gate is unchanged. DWR-4441 with embankment lines now passes (smallest triangle 62.3 m2).
    * gmsh culvert ends get the default mesher's 25 m2 (gmsh_spec passes culvert_area_m2 = min(fine area, 25 m2) when there are culverts; a gmsh setting still overrides it). They got the 100 m2 minimum before. DWR-2801: smallest triangle 50.7 -> 17.7 m2 with lines on (gate 11.25 m2), estimated time step 0.38 -> 0.22 s.
    * ANUGA worker: create_domain_from_regions' mesh_filename is passed only when its signature has it (inspect.signature; ANUGA 4.0.1 has none), instead of retrying after any TypeError, which also hid real TypeErrors in the mesh build.
    3.10.30 (2026-10-19)
    * ANUGA default mesher, culvert ends (core/anuga_model.py culvert_regions): the refined octagons (radius max(3 x width, 25 m), 25 m2 triangles) around the two ends of a culvert, or around the ends of culverts side by side, that overlap or come within one triangle edge of each other are merged into one region (the convex hull of their corners). Overlapping octagons crossed each other's edges and ANUGA's mesher left sliver triangles at the crossings: Absaraka (DWR-2801, two 36 m culverts) had 92 triangles under 5 m2, the smallest 0.15 m2, which set the ANUGA time step (about 0.02 s estimated; 0.13 s now, smallest triangle 8.7 m2, mesh-only check). Octagons clear of all others are unchanged, so meshes and job files without such culverts are as before (the default-mesher job-file pin is unchanged).
    3.10.28 (2026-10-19; PR #90 with #103, numbered 3.10.22 on its branch)
    * HEC-RAS model (build_model) breach timing, from the Absaraka (DWR-2801) HEC-RAS test: the sunny-day breach now starts at a set time (Breach Start=False,,<date>,<HHMM>, HEC's L40 format) after a quiet warm-up of breach_warmup_hr (0.25 h, 0 to 6 h) with the pool at its starting level, instead of a trigger 0.01 ft below the starting pool that fired at t = 0. Add the warm-up to the plugin's times to compare. breach_start="wse" keeps the old trigger; a flood run starts at the hydrograph's breach initiation time (set time) when it has one.
    * The plan's failure mode follows the plugin: the plugin's routed methods open a trapezoid that deepens from the crest, so the plan uses HEC's Overtopping mode (hec_breach_mode="match_plugin", the default); only the piping-orifice method keeps Piping. hec_breach_mode="failure_mode" writes the run's failure mode as before.
    * Spillway: a spillway crest and width (options or SPILLWAY_CREST_FT / SPILLWAY_WIDTH_FT, or dam.json spillway_crest_ft / spillway_width_ft) becomes a notch in the dam connection weir at the end away from the breach; during the warm-up a steady inflow equal to the spillway flow at the starting pool holds the pool level, then the inflow stops. HEC-RAS counts the breach flow inside the connection's Weir Flow (Absaraka v2: Total - Weir = 0 throughout), so the breach flow is HEC's own 'Breach Flow' (the connection's Breaching Variables; hecras.connection_timeseries returns it as breach_flow) and the spillway is Total Flow - Breach Flow (other_flow).
    * Solver tolerances: tolerances="tight" halves the water-surface and 2D volume tolerances and allows 30 iterations, to bring the volume error down; "default" writes what 3.10.20 wrote.
    * Runner_reference/Plugin hydrograph on the HEC-RAS breach.csv: the plugin's routed breach recomputed on the breach HEC-RAS builds (the raised breach bottom, the same width, formation time and side slope), so the two peaks compare like for like; PACKAGE_MANIFEST.json adds breach_start, hec_breach_mode, spillway, tolerances, mesh_seeds and plugin_reference.
    * Computation points seeded with gmsh (MESH_SEEDS / mesh_seeds / NDBREACH_HECRAS_MESH_SEEDS, off by default): gmsh places points finer along the dam (1/4 cell), the breach (1/4 cell) and the channel (a full cell; the channel strip gives it its alignment) and the 2D area keeps them as its Storage Area 2D Points; HEC-RAS still builds the cells. gmsh runs in this Python or another one (gmsh_python, NDBREACH_GMSH_PYTHON or NDBREACH_ANUGA_PYTHON); when it is not there the regular grid is used and the README and run_report.json (hecras_mesh_seeds: setting, used, gmsh version, point count) say so.
    * Inflow site check wording: the note no longer says the dam point is on the DWR dam line when it is not.
    * 2D breaklines (option breaklines, on by default; core/hecras_breaklines.py): the two sides of the breach cut and the two banks of a one-cell-wide row (a full cell) along the run's flow path (smoothed, from the end of the cut to two cells from the edge of the 2D area) are written as 'BreakLine' blocks (cell size near = the line spacing, far = the cell size), and the dam connection gets 'Conn CellSize Min/Max'. HEC-RAS keeps a given point layout exactly and does not enforce breaklines in it (Muncie gmsh test: 0 of 10,824 points moved, faces on 20 %% of the breaklines against 96-98 %% with HEC's own points), so the Runner builds them into the points the way HEC-RAS does: the points within a spacing of a line go and a pair is put across it every spacing (faces on the line); an even row of points half a spacing inside the dam line. With gmsh points the gmsh nodes on the lines are removed the same way. Absaraka: 98-100 %% of each line on a cell face.
    * No cell with more than 8 sides (HEC-RAS's limit; the Absaraka gmsh package failed HEC-RAS's property tables with "48 cell(s) with more than 8 sides"): a Voronoi pre-check of every cell (clipped to the perimeter where it reaches it) and every face point (at most 8 cells) before the .g01 is written; a bad point is nudged to the middle of its cell, then removed (a breakline pair stays and its busiest neighbour goes); a layout that still fails stops the build with the reason. The pre-check flags 58 cells on that package (HEC-RAS: 48), never fewer than HEC-RAS. README row 'Voronoi pre-check'.
    * gmsh points: Frontal-Delaunay (gmsh algorithm 6; Delaunay gave the nodes with 9 neighbours: 58 against 0 on Absaraka at the same sizes), a fixed random seed and one thread with the points sorted (the same points every run), size fields that grow at most 1.3 times from a point to the next, points closer than half the smallest spacing thinned, a point cap (max_seeds_ratio x the grid, at most max_points 2,000,000) with a clear message, and the 2D area's nominal cell size (Point Generation Data) set to the median point spacing.
    * Runner window, Advanced: a 'Refine HEC-RAS mesh with Gmsh points (optional)' checkbox (off by default; tooltip: needs gmsh, falls back to the regular grid) and the matching advanced HECRAS_GMSH parameter of Run a dam; when Models includes HEC-RAS 2D the HEC-RAS stage then asks for gmsh points. It does not change the flood run or its checkpoints.
    * Perimeter pre-check (Absaraka v2b_gmsh stopped in HEC-RAS with "1 face errors detected on the perimeter of the mesh"): every cell that reaches the 2D area's perimeter is also checked for being cut in two by it, for a perimeter face shorter than max(2 %% of the local spacing, 5 ft), and for a perimeter corner within max(0.5 %% of the spacing, 0.5 ft) of a cell edge. A corner near an edge nudges the nearer point about 1.5 ft; a split or sliver cell loses its point (a breakline point stays and its nearest neighbour goes); the build stops with the reason if any are left. The check runs for the regular grid and for gmsh points. On the failed v2b_gmsh geometry it finds the cell cut in two (54811) and the 0.6 ft perimeter face (54967); on HEC's own example meshes it finds neither. README row 'Perimeter pre-check'.
    * Channel strip one full cell wide (was half a cell): on Absaraka v2b the 50 ft strip held HEC-RAS's time step at half of v2's for most of the run (2,957 s against 1,628 s), with no effect on the downstream depths. gmsh's channel refinement (channel_frac) defaults to 1.0 to match.
    * Dam row finer across the breach: 4 cells over the breach bottom (20.2 ft for an 80.8 ft breach), one more each side, growing at most 1.3x a cell to the dam spacing; the breach lines start where it ends. On v2b only 2 low cells fronted the breach, so HEC-RAS's connection-segment tailwater (the average of the cells at a segment's ends) took in the dry embankment cells, and the pool stalled 2.3 ft high (1,049.2 against 1,046.9 ft).
    * After Ras.exe builds the mesh, hecras_engine.seed_check compares HEC-RAS's cells with the points (count, one-to-one, largest move, spacing) and writes run_report.json 'seed_check' in the project folder.
    * Breach-cell pre-check and a dam row that never puts a face on a breach corner (Absaraka v2c / v2c_gmsh: HEC-RAS warned 'SA 2D Connection at Dam has a weir breach lower than the cells they are connected to' at station 809.6, cells 62974 / 64879 at 1,049.26 / 1,049.78 ft against the 1,046.43 ft invert, and the runs had 38.7 %% / >= 12 %% volume error, against 0.5 %% in v2 / v2b): the v2c row of 20.2 ft cells put a face exactly on each bottom corner, so the embankment cell beside it, with no cut terrain, fronted the corner. The dam row now has cells centred on the breach centre and on both bottom corners (40.4 ft at Absaraka, BREACH_DAM_CELLS = 2, at least 0.4 x the dam spacing; a narrow bottom gets one cell that holds both corners), and the terrain cut runs a little past each corner (about one terrain cell, then the breach side slope) and from 1.5 terrain cells upstream of the dam line, so those cells reach the invert in HEC-RAS's interpolated terrain. After the points are final, every cell fronting the final breach bottom is checked against the invert (bilinear terrain over the cell; on v2c it finds HEC-RAS's cell, 0.35 ft higher); the result goes to the README, PACKAGE_MANIFEST.json site_checks.breach_cells and run_report.json 'hecras_breach_cells'; a failure stops the build (option breach_cell_check: auto / fail / warn / off). Breakline enforcement also clears points within 1 %% past a line's reach and any point inside the breach cut (gmsh left nodes 20-25 ft from the breach pairs and the dam row).
    * HEC-RAS dam line along the embankment crest (option dam_axis, 'crest' by default; core/hecras_crest.py; Runner window Advanced checkbox 'HEC-RAS dam line follows the crest (recommended)', Run a dam advanced HECRAS_CREST, HEC-RAS tool DAM_AXIS / BREACH_STATION_FT), from Absaraka (DWR-2801), where the straight line through the dam point across the flow ran 27-28 degrees off the bent crest at the breach, so the breach cut crossed the embankment obliquely and about 2 acres of pool lay inside the 2D area: the crest is traced from the DEM (from the dam point snapped to the ridge, a cross-section every terrain cell, the highest ground near the heading while it stays within max(3 ft, 10 %% H) of the top of dam and stands max(2 ft, 10 %% H) above both sides; gaps up to max(2 cells, 300 ft) are stepped over; it stops at the abutment tie-ins, the end of the embankment or the dam length), smoothed, simplified to about a cell between vertices and made straight across the breach. The SA/2D connection, the weir stations, the perimeter cut, the breach cut (a rectangle at right angles to the crest at the breach), the dam row, breach-side and channel breaklines, the gmsh refinement lines and every pre-check follow the polyline. A dam line of your own (option dam_line_xy, or the DWR dam line of the run, now kept in run_report.json crest_line_xy) wins over the trace; when the crest cannot be traced the straight line is used with a WARNING note. The breach centre goes to the deepest dam section along the crest (the lowest ground at the downstream toe, where the old channel crosses); breach_at ('toe', 'lowest_crest', 'inflow' = the old rule) or breach_station_ft override it. run_report.json 'hecras_dam_line' (and PACKAGE_MANIFEST.json dam_axis, README rows 'Dam line' / 'Breach location') record the trace, its ends and why each stopped, the skew, the breach station and how it was chosen. Absaraka: 1,143 ft along the crest (was 1,700 ft straight; the NID length runs onto natural high ground), breach 300 ft north-east at the deepest toe, downstream bearing 128 (was 101).
    3.10.27 (2026-10-19)
    * Run report metrics (issue #91; reporting only, the model and the rasters are unchanged; docs/Run_report_metrics.md): run_report.json summary, run_report.md / .html, the DOCX report, the batch CSV, the sensitivity and probabilistic tables and the per-pass lisflood_runs[] / anuga_runs[] stats get the flooded area at or above 0.1, 1 and 2 ft (flooded_acres_ge_0_1ft / _ge_1ft / _ge_2ft), the P95 / P99 velocity over cells at least 0.1 ft deep, the depth and position of the maximum-velocity cell (max_velocity_depth_ft, max_velocity_xy) and the max / P95 velocity at flooded structures and overtopped roads and rail. Existing fields keep their names and values; new CSV columns are appended.
    * Optional water-body mask (WATER_MASK setting, NDBREACH_WATERBODY_MASK, else the dam folder's or the suite's hydro waterbodies layer when there is one; 'off' turns it off): masked cells stay in the model; the report gives the masked figures (*_masked) next to the unmasked ones, the masked area, and 'Water Body Mask.geojson'. TIER 2 HEC-RAS figures go through adopt_final_engine the same way. The QA depth map's title gives the threshold areas, P99 and max velocity (with its depth) and outlines the mask.
    * The suite lookup also finds hydro/3DHP_*waterbod*.gpkg (for example 3DHP_ND_waterbodies.gpkg), after hydro/3DHP_waterbodies.gpkg and before hydro/NHD_waterbodies.gpkg. The mask still turns on by itself when one of these exists (whether it should be opt-in is open).
    3.10.26 (2026-10-19)
    * HEC-RAS timeouts on the wall clock (core/wallclock.py): the RasUnsteady compute, the Wine preprocess steps and the Ras.exe -c polling loop measured their timeout on time.monotonic(), which stops while the machine is paused or forked, so a 5 h (18,099 s) timeout fired after 34,749 s of wall time. Elapsed time is now max(wall, monotonic): a pause counts, a clock stepped back never makes it fire early, and every wall/monotonic disagreement over 60 s is logged. NDBREACH_HECRAS_TIMEOUT_S and the max(2 h, 3 x estimate) timeout capped at 48 h are unchanged.
    * On a RasUnsteady timeout the timeout is written to linux_run/run.log and the Runner log first; then the process group gets SIGTERM, NDBREACH_HECRAS_KILL_GRACE_S (default 60 s) to exit, then SIGKILL (it was an immediate SIGKILL). The half-written results HDF is kept as <name>.p01.partial.hdf (never used as results; copied to _hecras/), with linux_run/timeout.json, _hecras/hecras_stage.json (status timeout) and run_report.json 'hecras_timeout' (timeout, wall and monotonic elapsed, clock jumps, signals sent, the partial HDF and whether it is readable). run.log is opened for append, so RasUnsteady's last lines cannot overwrite the timeout line.
    * The ANUGA gmsh mesher's 600 s timeout is on the wall clock too (SIGTERM, 5 s, SIGKILL).
    3.10.25 (2026-10-19)
    * ANUGA gmsh mesher (core/gmsh_mesher.py): the outer polygon is checked before gmsh is imported, so a mesh spec with fewer than 3 outer points is reported as such ("outer polygon has fewer than 3 points") also where gmsh is not installed; it was reported as "ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'gmsh'". Meshing itself is unchanged. (3.10.24 is taken by the open #96, 3.10.22 by #90 and #92.)
    * Tests only: the default-mesher job-file pin is checked with the built-in outline tracer (contourpy, when installed, traces the polygons up to one cell differently); the Word report figures are expected embedded exactly when cairosvg renders here; the GUI tests find the other plugins through a symlinked checkout; the real-Qt window test skips with a reason in a Python without PyQt5.
    3.10.23 (2026-10-19)
    * Flagged structures (damsuite/structure_flags.py, docs/Flagged_structures.md): a record the suite lists as not a dam to breach is refused by 'Run breach model for one dam' and its sensitivity tool with the reason (DWR-3161 / ND00597 Sanish Bay WPA Dam 1: the ND-23 road fill over backwater, breach analysis not applicable, hazard Low from the DWR record). 'Run a flagged structure anyway' (ALLOW_FLAGGED_STRUCTURE, advanced), the CLI's --allow-flagged-structure or ALLOW_FLAGGED_STRUCTURE=1 runs it for information only, and the log and run report say so. The batch tool and the CLI batch skip it: Status 'flagged', the reason in Message and the new columns Structure_Flag and Hazard_Of_Record in Batch_Breach_Runs.csv (existing columns unchanged). The Dam suite home shows the flag and the class of record. (3.10.22 is taken by the open #90 and #92.)
    3.10.21 (2026-10-18)
    * Window sizing (ui_fit.py, the Hazard 2.18.36 fix): before it is shown the window is sized for its parent's screen, else the screen under the mouse (it fell back to the primary screen); it opens inside that screen, never larger than it (the cap follows the window to another monitor), and the 520 x 400 floor that could exceed a small screen is gone. The remembered size (save_size) is kept and still clamped.
    * One ellipsis character (U+2026) instead of '...' on the Run a dam… menu entry, the tool list of the Runner window, the wizard's Browse… button and timing line, the Processing labels / help, and the shared Dam suite home window (suite_home.py, identical in the three plugins).
    3.10.20 (2026-10-18)
    * Reservoir bottom carved from the storage curve (RESERVOIR_CARVE: Off by default, Auto, On; advanced; core/reservoir_carve.py, docs/Reservoir_carve_from_storage.md): for LiDAR that shows a water surface instead of the reservoir bottom (DWR-3161). The footprint is the DEM-connected ground at or below the top of dam upstream of the dam (the downstream side the inflow uses; the DWR dam line a wall), within 3 x sqrt(curve area) (600 m to 5 km). The cells under the water sheet are lowered into a bowl (depth ~ distance from the shoreline to a power, solved by bisection) so the volume at the top of dam matches the storage curve within 2 %%; never below the curve bottom / breach bottom or the upstream dam face, the shoreline and dry banks unchanged, nothing downstream. Auto carves only when the DEM shows the flat-water problem (pool volume or area under half the curve, or the floor less than H/2 below the top of dam).
    * The source DEM is never written: _breach_model/<dam>_dem_carved.tif, <dam>_carve_depth.tif and a PNG preview; the engines read the carved terrain (model_dem: LISFLOOD-FP, ANUGA, the near-dam refine) and the HEC-RAS builder lowers its terrain by the same depth raster, so the site checks see it. run_report.json reservoir_carve: method, footprint, target vs DEM vs carved volume and area at five elevations, max carve depth; the report warns "Reservoir bottom carved from the storage curve; the lidar showed a water surface" and says when the footprint cannot hold the storage.
    3.10.19 (2026-10-18)
    * ANUGA gmsh mesh (ANUGA_MESHER / NDBREACH_ANUGA_MESHER, hidden): the quality gate is 0.45 x the smallest intended triangle (min(min_area_m2, culvert_area_m2)); it was 0.5 x 100 m2 and rejected Absaraka's good 20 x 20 mi mesh (smallest 49.93 m2, 31.3 deg) without saying so. min_area_tol is configurable (anuga_gmsh or NDBREACH_ANUGA_GMSH_MIN_AREA_TOL).
    * ANUGA_MESHER=gmsh is strict: a rejected gmsh mesh, a gmsh error or the 600 s timeout stops the run with the reason. ANUGA_MESHER=gmsh_or_default falls back to the default mesh with a WARNING in the log and "gmsh mesh rejected: <reason>; used the default mesh" first in Things to check and in its own section of run_report.md. run_report.json records the mesher asked for and used (anuga_mesh, summary.anuga_mesher).
    * gmsh mesh: road (RECEPTOR_ROADS) and rail (RECEPTOR_RAIL) centrelines on an embankment inside the flood become mesh lines (crown >= 0.3 m above the ground 20 m to both sides, from the terrain at <= 5 m). Near-parallel duplicate lines are dropped, loose ends are joined, and edges are split at nodes passing close by, so real road and rail layers make no slivers. Option culvert_area_m2 refines culvert ends below min_area_m2. The default ANUGA mesh is unchanged (byte-identical job file).
    3.10.18 (2026-10-18)
    * Breach inflow on the downstream side: when no cell is half the dam height below the top of dam, the crest fallback (place_from_crest) now keeps to the downstream side the run already found (downstream_direction). Before, it took the steepest step from the crest, which at DWR-3161 (a low fill between two hydro-flattened water surfaces) went 40 m into the reservoir while the flow trace ran north; a trace that steps into the reservoir is started again a few cells out on the downstream side.
    * Side check: run_report.json inflow_point.side_check compares the inflow point with the flow trace from it and the downstream side; a disagreement (trace running back over the dam point, point behind the dam) is a 'CHECK THE DAM POINT' warning at the top of the report.
    * Dam point near a road (within 30 m, the receptor road layer): a warning that the embankment in the DEM may be the road's fill (DWR-3161 is on the ND-23 fill), unless the DWR dam line (DAM_LINES) was given; run_report.json dam_point_checks.
    3.10.17 (2026-10-18)
    * HEC-RAS model (build_model) site checks, from the DWR-3161 HEC-RAS test (36 %% volume error): the build stops with a plain-English reason when the breach bottom is more than 5 ft below the ground just downstream of the dam, the top of dam is less than half the dam height above that ground, the ground inside the reservoir outline is never lower than a quarter of the dam height below the top of dam, or the ground below the top of dam upstream covers less than 20 %% of the storage curve's area. ALLOW_BAD_SITE=1 (or the option allow_bad_site) builds it anyway and says the results are not meaningful; smaller mismatches are notes.
    * A breach bottom below the ground it flows onto is raised to that ground (the note gives the storage that stays behind), and the breach opening through the downstream half of the embankment is cut to the breach bottom in the terrain GeoTIFF, so the 2D cells at the breach reach it.
    * No dam length on record: the dam connection is the embankment crest (ground within max(3 ft, 10 %% H) of the top of dam around the dam point, stepping over gaps up to 300 ft such as a spillway), at least the breach width, at most 3,000 ft (was the whole edge of the 2D area across the valley, 4,612 ft over hills at DWR-3161).
    * Simulation window: the later of the end of the breach hydrograph (below 2 %% of its peak) and the run's latest arrival, plus 25 %% (at least 2 h) to drain, 6 to 48 h (option sim_hours / sim_cap_hr). The plan keeps HEC's adaptive (Courant) time step.
    * README_HECRAS.md, WINDOWS_README.txt and PACKAGE_MANIFEST.json say that HEC-RAS's piping breach (a pipe that grows, then collapses) is not the plugin's linear trapezoid, and list the site checks; the manifest adds site_checks, sim_hours/sim_how, dam_line_how and time_step.
    * HEC-RAS results: a time series is paired with the Time of its own output block (hecras.pair_time), and hecras.connection_timeseries reads a dam connection's flow and stages from the minute (DSS Hydrograph) block, never the per-profile table.
    3.10.16 (2026-10-18)
    * Export the breach to HEC-RAS now also builds a HEC-RAS 6.x 2D dam-breach model package (core/hecras_model.build_model) in <dam>/_breach_model/hecras/<DamID>_<date>/ plus a .zip, with WINDOWS_README.txt (steps for HEC-RAS 6.6 on Windows) and PACKAGE_MANIFEST.json. Not yet opened in HEC-RAS. The old skeleton writer hecras.write_ras_project (keywords HEC-RAS does not use) is retired.
    * Inflow point fix: when no toe was found below the dam, place_from_crest took the highest ground within 400 m as the crest and could put the inflow on a nearby hill above the top of dam (DWR-3161: 1916.7 ft vs top 1844.4 ft). The crest is now ground at the top of dam (within max(3 ft, 10%% of H)) and the inflow must be below the top of dam; build_model ignores an older run's inflow that is not.
    * HEC-RAS dam connection trimmed to the dam length (option, the run's crest length, or dam.json dam_length_ft) centred on the dam; run_report.json adds work_crs, dem_z_to_m and structures_used.
    3.10.15 (2026-10-16)
    * Window redesign: muted disclaimer banner, Dam -> Terrain -> Model run -> Products stage strip, prominent 'Run this dam' with LISFLOOD-FP / ANUGA found chips, DEM download and Advanced as collapsible sections (Advanced shows its values in the header), two tabs (Run: the dam card; Tools: a category rail), Settings in its own window behind the gear button, collapsible log. 'Open Hazard / Screening' now open DamSafety Hazard / DamSafety Screening. No tool, wizard or Processing change.
    3.10.14 (2026-10-15)
    * Cross-platform (#32): text files read/written as UTF-8, CSVs written with newline='' (no blank rows on Windows), Windows conda ANUGA env python found, subprocess output decoded tolerantly.
    * Culvert matches from NDDOT Center Line Pipes: the plan-datum end elevations and note are in run_report.md and run_report.json (reported only; the burn uses LiDAR inverts).
    3.10.13 3.10.13 (2026-10-15)
    * Culvert guesses take their real size (span, rise, barrels, shape) from an NDDOT bridge inventory or NBI culvert within 75 m (CULVERT_MATCH_M); source "NDDOT <BRIDGE_ID>", surveyed, match_m.
    * Optional: cut inventory-matched culverts into the DEM (CULVERT_BURN_MATCHED, off by default).
    * Guesses now run alongside a STRUCTURES layer; guesses near a STRUCTURES line are dropped.
    * NBI item 43B = 19 is read as a culvert, other types as bridges.
    * NDDOT Center Line Pipes accepted in CULVERT_INVENTORY (auto-detected; priority bridge inventory > CLP > NBI; plan-datum inverts reported, the burn uses LiDAR inverts).
    * Burned culverts follow the channel with LiDAR inverts so the cut reaches coarse model grids; run_report.md lists the culvert matches.
    3.10.12: installed from main c3d3d78 (spillway adequacy tool).
```

## Archived metadata changelog — DamSafety_Screening

Entries moved verbatim out of `DamSafety_Screening/metadata.txt` `changelog=` on 2026-11-07 (QGIS's Plugin Manager parses and renders the whole `changelog=` value of every installed plugin, so `tools/ci_checks.py` now caps it at 12 KB and keeps the newest entries there). Newest first; the indentation is the metadata.txt continuation indent.

```text
    0.18.8 - Browser connections: the suite's map services (WMS/WMTS, XYZ Tiles, ArcGIS REST Servers: USGS 3DEP / 3DHP / NHD / topo / imagery, NLCD, ND GIS Hub, DWR MapService, NID, NTAD rail / crossings / NBI, FEMA NFHL and USA Structures, SARP, USGS stream crossings, NAIP and the services the user added) are added to the QGIS Browser as 'DamSafety: ...' connections when QGIS starts, each once (a deleted or edited one is left alone); setting ndsuite/register_connections = false turns it off (shared damsuite 1.18.5, connections.py, pin v3). Test: DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_browser_connections.py.
    0.18.7 (2026-11-05; GUI check follow-up, Nick: "Fix": with PROJ_NETWORK=ON and the PROJ grid server (cdn.proj.org) unreachable, the 3DEP request box (to_5070), the ND DWR tile box (EPSG:2266), the USGS 1 m lidar VRT box and a dam folder's domain box failed with RuntimeError, so a DEM download stopped before it began. A box needs no network grid: core/proj_offline.box tries it as-is and, if PROJ fails or returns inf, again with the PROJ network off, which is then restored. Heights are not touched.)
    0.18.6 (2026-11-04; GUI check of 2026-11-04, Nick approved: Dam suite home no longer has a fixed 900 px minimum width; it opens at 960 x 600 where the screen has room and shrinks to an 800 x 600 screen; Hazard classification INCREMENTAL (was 950 px) and Runner hand-off REFINE_NEAR (765 px) have short Processing labels with the detail in the help)
    0.18.5 (2026-11-04; Nick: the self-test (tool 14) has an EXE parameter, as the nd copy 0.17.0: the LISFLOOD-FP executable for step 7 with the LISFLOOD engines (0 / 1). It was never passed, so step 7 with a LISFLOOD engine failed unless tool 7 found the binary itself (NDBREACH_LISFLOOD or PATH). Empty = not passed, tool 7's own fallback, as before. Tests: tests/test_selftest_exe.py; '3DHP flowlines' where the label means the current stream source (data package FLOW, Run breach sim streams, the package check and the workflow slot; setting keys unchanged); the county data package downloads its flowlines from USGS 3DHP (3DHP_all/MapServer/50, damsuite servers.py) and adds the NHD-style fields (hydro3dhp.normalise_flowlines), was the NHD service; shared suite_settings_tools: the settings grid carries the parameters' help text; a label several tools share names its tool.)
    0.18.4 (2026-11-03; audit fix E: core/storage.py: the unused conf_leak flag and the 'medium' known-height branch (the same as the default) removed; the confidence is unchanged - low when a value was defaulted / not found or the DEM storage-leak guard held the storage (its note says 'storage leak'), else medium, as the nd copy.)
    0.18.3 (2026-11-03; audit fix D: damsuite loss.py docstring judgment; core/handoff.py comment metres. No behaviour change.)
    0.18.2 (2026-11-01; option C, Nick: peak depth with the peak velocity over the wet cells, even from different cells - core/nd_hazard.py and core/hazard.py Grid polygon (dv = footprint max). Reverts db9c665's same-cell velocity.)
    0.18.1 (2026-10-30; tool 1 with its default DWR field boxes on the suite GeoPackage failed with KeyError: 'structure_' - the defaults are the DWR shapefile's 10-character names. A chosen field is matched ignoring case; one the layer does not have falls back to the alias it has (structure_id, max_dam_height, ...) with a warning, else is left blank - never a KeyError. Inventory changes the same. Tests: tests/test_dwr_default_fields.py)
    * One-dam chain (Crest ND00199, 2026-10-30): step 1 takes the DWR / NID layers the way the preflight finds them (Inputs choice, else the suite folder / pack) - with only suite_folder in the settings the preflight passed and step 1 stopped with 'Provide a DWR layer, an NID layer, or both'. The preflight disk estimate counts only the only_ids dams (it said 10088). Tool 15 writes its chain log line by line, so a killed run leaves one, and has DAM_ID (as the ND copy: sets only_ids). Tool 6 (triage) matches ONLY_IDS against the dam id or the NID id, as tool 1 does: Crest sends ND00199 and tool 1 names the dam DWR-2801, so tool 6 dropped it. Tests: tests/test_onedam_chain.py
    0.18.0 (2026-10-30; Nick: shared nd_hazard 1.3.0 - a habitable structure in the ACER judgment zone is ND MEDIUM by rule (tool 8: hz_lethal 'no (judgment zone, MEDIUM by rule)'), not potentially lethal; above the high line is still HIGH. classify_nd(judgment_zone_high=True) keeps the old behaviour)
    0.17.58 (2026-10-29; damsuite 1.18.4: the Runner's local-datum band is 0-500 ft, not 0-300. Screening converts no datum itself)
    0.17.57 (2026-10-29; the offline pack in format 1 is read too - Nick)
    * Vendored damsuite 1.18.3: the DWR dams / lines, NID and the NLCD land cover are found in a format-1 pack (nd_offline_pack.sqlite: data sets copied once to a GeoPackage cache outside the pack, NLCD from its manifest "rasters") as in a format-2 pack; format 2 is unchanged.
    0.17.56 (2026-10-28; Nick: tool 3 reads the crest at mid-crest of the DWR dam line (optional LINES, step 3 passes the dam lines) instead of the 98th percentile near the point; tool 4 writes the mid-crest point (mc_x, mc_y, mc_z_m, mc_moved_m; a highest point at the edge of the +-25 m profile gets a REVIEW note) and the Runner hand-off uses it as the dam point; damsuite 1.18.2 (0-300 ft always local, converted to NAVD88 by the Runner's DEM tie, no override; Nick 2026-10-29). Screening converts no datum itself: the 7b hand-off passes no DWR elevations, so the Runner reads the top of dam from the DEM)
    0.17.55 (2026-10-28; 7b can download the Runner's DEM for the chosen dams - Nick, Mac, QGIS 3.34.6: the 'DEM for the runner' box was empty and 7b had no way to get one)
    * 7b Detailed runs with DamSafety Runner: 'DEM for the runner' is optional. New 'Runner DEM source…': Use the DEM above (default, as before) / Download USGS 3DEP 10 m covering the chosen dams / Download USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles (native) covering the chosen dams. The download covers the box of the chosen dams' breach points plus 'DEM download buffer around the chosen dams (mi)' (default 10), is written to 'Folder for the downloaded DEM' (default <Dams folder>/_runner_dem) and reused on the next run for the same dams.
    * 10 m: the USGS 3DEP ImageServer pieces become one EPSG:5070 GeoTIFF in metres (band unit m, facts file), the same code as Download DEM. 1 m: the native 10 km TNM lidar tiles (never the 1.2 km ImageServer pieces) in the suite's shared tile folder, a VRT in metres with the 10 m file underneath when there is one. The DEM is sent to every chosen dam with the Runner's 'DEM elevation units' = Meters.
    * The log gives the request / tile count and the size before anything is fetched; over 'Largest DEM download for one run (GB)' (default 10) the run stops before downloading and says what to change. Validate only plans the download and fetches nothing. Cancel stops at the next request (Download DEM too: 'Cancelled. Nothing was written.').
    * No DEM and no download: the error names the 'Download DEM (dam, county, or state)' tool and the new option (it said only 'A DEM is required').
    0.17.55 (2026-10-28; damsuite 1.18.0 vendored)
    * Vendored damsuite 1.18.0 (vdatum / VERTCON ND grid used by the Runner). No Screening behaviour change: Screening uses only differences of inventory elevations, which a datum shift does not change.
    0.17.55 (2026-10-28; damsuite 1.18.1 vendored)
    * Vendored damsuite 1.18.1 (vdatum / VERTCON ND grid and local-datum detection used by the Runner). No Screening behaviour change: Screening uses only differences of inventory elevations, which a datum shift does not change.
    0.17.54 (2026-10-27; tool 16 on a statewide water-body layer no longer stalls or comes back empty without saying why - Nick, Absaraka DWR-2801)
    * Tool 16 Find ponds and dugouts reads only the water bodies it can use: with Terrain source = Local DEM folder, those over the DEM mosaic's extent (a statewide layer with a one-dam DEM gave one 'no terrain data' error per water body of the state - 95 000 in 2 minutes on the box, about 360 000 in all); with a County, those in the county's box. The log says how many of the layer's water bodies are read.
    * Tool 16 County: the county test takes the lon / lat of the water body. It took the centroid in the layer's own CRS, so a projected water-body layer (EPSG:5070, UTM) had no water body in any county and the run ended at once with 0 analysed. The summary always gives the count outside the county.
    * Tool 16 with the USGS 3DEP service: above 200 water bodies a warning gives the time the downloads may take (each water body is one or two requests of a few seconds; Cass County's ~1 600 NHD water bodies about 1.5 to 5 h, the state ~12 days) and how to make the run quicker. A progress line every 30 s (water body i of n, analysed / written / failed, seconds each, time left), where the progress bar alone looked frozen. Cancel says where it stopped.
    * Tool 16: when nothing is written, a warning says why (natural depressions in the 'Water bodies not written' layer and the 'Also write natural depressions' option, failures, size limits, nothing in the county). Test: tests/test_findponds_stall.py.
    * Tool 1 (Prepare dams): optional DWR dugout points / polygons (filled from the suite folder slots dugouts / dugout_polygons). A dam inside a dugout polygon or within the buffer (DUGOUT_BUF_FT, default 100 ft = 30.48 m, true metres in every CRS) of a dugout point is left out, logged 'DWR dugout <structure_id>' and listed in 1_dugouts.csv; a dam with a real DWR hazard class or an NID id is kept with a warning. Empty inputs = no change.
    * Tool 16 (Find ponds): a water body that is a DWR dugout gets dwr_dugout = structure_id (new last field) and is classed a dugout (only the water above the natural ground is released, from the LiDAR); DWR's storage_above_grade_acft is recorded in the notes and a clearly larger DWR value is a warning.
    * Suite folder auto-fill takes the DWR export's own names (DWR_Dugouts_Polygons.gpkg, the two-layer DWR_Dams.gpkg); a dugout polygon ring far too large (bad geometry) is skipped and said.
    0.17.53 - breach_hydrograph: setHelp on MODE, SUNNY and OUTDIR
    0.17.51 - runnerhandoff: per-parameter help, engine first in rn_inputs, and DRY_RUN validate-only mode (issue #35)
    0.17.50 (2026-10-26; Download DEM records the elevation unit of what it writes - a feet fallback under the 1 m lidar tiles is converted, ND DWR DEM_Imagine in a gft* folder is no longer taken for feet; the 1 m ImageServer request count is explained - Nick, Absaraka DWR-2801)
    * Download DEM's local GeoTIFF (write_local_dem) and a reprojected copy (warp_to) now carry a band unit: the one their source records (core/dem_zunit.py, the rule of the Runner's 'Detect from the raster', byte-identical there; build_vrts tags its ND DWR VRTs), else metres for the USGS 3DEP ImageServer, whose mosaic of tiles records none. Before, the downloaded DEM had none, so the Runner's 'DEM elevation units' = 'Detect from the raster' could not tell feet from metres. A source that records no unit gives an untagged copy (never a blind 'm'); a feet source gives a feet tag, UNITS and facts file. The warps keep the values as stored (core/warp_opts.no_vshift, gdalwarp -novshift): GDAL's default vertical shift converts the values when a CRS is compound, and the tag would then not be their unit.
    * 1 m lidar tiles (SOURCE 2): the VRT is tagged metres, and a FALLBACK_DEM in feet showed through it as metres (951 ft read as 951 m, and the Runner's Detect trusts the tag). lidar_tnm.build_vrt now converts a feet / US survey feet fallback (x 0.3048 / x 1200/3937 when its band unit or vertical CRS says so; a USGS 3DEP Download DEM record is metres) before it goes under the tiles, exactly once (the warps keep the values as stored: GDAL's default vertical shift had already converted a compound-CRS feet fallback, or a band-'ft' one under compound tiles, so it read 88.4 m for 951 ft), and does not use a fallback that records no unit (a warning; the VRT has the tiles only). The json records fallback_units / fallback_refused.
    * ND DWR LiDAR: build_vrts took every metre DEM_Imagine tile for a feet grid (x 0.3048, then labelled metres) when '/gft' was anywhere in the path - an output folder such as D:/gft_dems or C:/Users/gftaylor. Only the path below the download folder counts now (_product_of).
    * A reused local DEM from before 0.17.50 ('reusing ...'): a USGS 3DEP one is tagged metres now; any other is left as it is, with a warning to set the Runner's unit or delete it and download again - an ND DWR LiDAR one is never tagged blindly (older builds could write feet grids labelled metres). The warning names the unit by the file's source (review round 3: a USGS 3DEP file whose record is no longer whole was told Meters 'for a DEM_Imagine download'; dem_download.record_source). An ND DWR download that Screening before 0.17.50 wrote in a folder whose name starts with 'gft' (its DEM_Imagine metres were scaled x 0.3048 and tagged 'm') is not reused: the tool stops and says to delete it and download again, and the Runner's 'DEM elevation units' stops on it whatever the choice (core/dem_zunit.data_problem). Download DEM now records which build wrote a file (dataset metadata WRITER, facts file 'written_by').
    * ND DWR guidance: DEM_Imagine is UTM metres (EPSG:26914 / 3721), only ASCII_Grids_ft is ND State Plane feet (converted) - in the tool help, the IMAGINE label ('DEM_Imagine (1 m, metres)', was '1 m feet'), the facts file's native fields and the window's DEM lines (they said 'feet, often EPSG:3721').
    * USGS 3DEP (ImageServer) at 1 m: the log says how many export requests the box takes and that they are 1200 px (1.2 km) request chunks, not lidar tiles - a 10 mi buffer is a 32 x 32 km box, 27 x 27 = 729 - and, above 100, points at 'USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles (native, TNM)' (about 20 lidar tiles for that box) or a smaller buffer. For a county the note gives the W x H km box and the native tiles (no buffer); for the state 10 m, or a county or a box at 1 m (the native tile source refuses the state). The tool help says the same. Tests: tests/test_dem_download_units_chunks.py, tests/test_dem_units_review.py, tests/test_dem_units_once.py.
    0.17.49 (2026-10-26; tool 7b sends the inventory max-pool area with no curve folder; tool 1's normal-pool area no longer reads max_pool_area)
    * Tool 7b surface area with NO curve folder (CURVES empty): the Runner's AREA_AC is now the DWR inventory's maximum-pool area (inv_maxa_m2 -> acres), with a warning naming the dam; surface_area_source = inventory_max_pool_area in rn_area_src, the dam.json note, the log tally and the run manifest. No inventory value = no area, as before, now with a warning. Dams with a usable curve are unchanged; a curve folder whose curve is too shallow / missing falls back as in 0.17.46. An older tool-1 layer without inv_maxa_m2 is warned whether or not curves are given. With no curve folder (and in the shallow-curve fallback of 0.17.46) AREA_AC is the DWR max-pool area; where tool 3 put the max pool below the DEM crest (stor_anchor_m below est_crest_m, 0.17.47), the storage sent includes the DEM fill above it, so the Runner's storage exponent can come out somewhat low (a lower routed peak) - give tool 7b the tool-3 curve folder to use the curve's area at the crest.
    * Tool 1 inv_area_m2 (the normal-pool surface area): on the suite GeoPackage (DWR_dams.gpkg has both normal_area and max_pool_area) it was filled from max_pool_area, because the surface area aliases listed max_pool_area and the last alias found won. It is now normal_area (shapefile: normal_are), by priority; max_pool_area only fills inv_maxa_m2. The DWR shapefile export was not affected (max_pool_a was never an alias). A chain field map saved with max_pool_area for the surface area (1_fields.json) is replaced by the normal-pool field; picking a max-pool field by hand in tool 1 is kept but warned. inv_area_m2 is never sent to the Runner; the only reader is dam.json's null surface_area_ac note, which quoted the max-pool value as 'normal-pool' before. Rerun tool 1 for correct inv_area_m2. Tests: tests/test_inv_area_fields.py, tests/test_7b_area_fallback_watermask.py.
    0.17.48 (2026-10-26; preflight: a broken offline-pack manifest.json is a warning row, not "no pack" - #171 follow-up)
    * Preflight 'Offline pack vs manifest.json': a manifest.json that cannot be read (half written, not JSON, not an object, saved with a UTF-8 byte-order mark the pack readers refuse) is a WARN row saying FAILED, where it used to show no row. Suite package 1.17.6 (packcheck never raises; half-written GeoPackages named). Test: tests/test_preflight_packcheck.py.
    0.17.47 (2026-10-26; each inventory storage at its own elevation - DWR-2865 / DWR-3034)
    * Tool 3 (Estimate storage), Max storage: the inventory maximum is placed at the max pool, (DWR max_pool_elev - normal_elev) above the lidar water surface (the normal pool; only the difference is used, so records in a local datum work), the normal storage at the normal pool, the DEM fill scaled to join them, and above the max pool only the DEM fill above it is added. Before, the "normal" storage was always put at the water surface and the DEM fill to the spill or crest added on top, so a record whose normal box held the maximum (NickReview's maxstor field map: DWR-2865 167 ac-ft at the 1870 ft pool, 237.6 ac-ft at the 1875.9 ft spill; DWR-3034 97.5 vs 71.6) double counted the fill between the normal and the max pool. A max pool above the DEM crest (est_crest_m) puts the maximum at the crest, the fill from the pool scaled to reach it, never on a point above it: the storage at the top of dam (tool 5's Vw, td_vw_m3) is never below the inventory maximum, as before. It is set to the maximum, which can be less than the normal storage plus the DEM fill to the crest (the inventory difference replaces that fill, as it does between the two pools). New stor_anchor / stor_anchor_m fields and flags in stor_notes (warnings, except MAXPOOL-ELEV-MISSING): NORMAL-EQUALS-MAX (normal = maximum, not used at the normal pool), NORMAL-NOT-BELOW-MAX, MAXPOOL-ABOVE-CREST, FILL-EXCEEDS-MAX (without a usable normal storage, the DEM fill from the pool to the max pool is more than the maximum: the DEM curve is kept, its below-pool estimate at the normal pool, never 0, which failed tool 5's sunny day, and the storage at the top of dam is above the maximum; with a normal storage below the maximum the DEM fill between the two pools is scaled to fit between them, no flag), CURVE-ABOVE-MAX, MAXPOOL-ELEV-BAD (a max pool below the normal pool, or at it with a maximum above the normal storage). Without pool elevations (NID-only records, a DWR layer without normal_elev / max_pool_elev, a tool-1 layer older than 0.17.47) MAXPOOL-ELEV-MISSING, and with MAXPOOL-ELEV-BAD, the fallback of before (the maximum at the top of dam): only a normal storage equal to the maximum is then not used below the pool; one above the maximum stays at the pool as before, flagged NORMAL-NOT-BELOW-MAX. Normal storage and Larger of the two (when the normal is larger) are unchanged.
    * Tool 7b: the stage-storage surface area sent as the Runner's AREA_AC (rn_area_src stage_storage; dV/dz over the top 0.3 m of the tool-3 curve, 0.17.46) changes where tool 3 uses the maximum. With pool elevations and the max pool more than ~0.3 m below the crest (or no usable normal storage), the top of the curve is DEM fill, so AREA_AC is about the DEM's area at the crest; before, it was inflated where the old logic raised the last curve point to the maximum. With a usable normal storage and the max pool within ~0.3 m of the crest, or above it (MAXPOOL-ABOVE-CREST), the 0.3 m window takes in the scaled segment, so AREA_AC is the DEM's area scaled like the fill, by (maximum - normal) / (DEM fill to the max pool or crest): it can be much smaller, also for dams the old logic did not raise. Fallback dams (no usable pool elevations) with NORMAL-EQUALS-MAX (e.g. NID-only records with NORMAL_STORAGE = MAX_STORAGE): the normal is no longer used at the pool, so where the DEM curve tops out below the maximum ensure_top_of_dam now raises the last point to it and AREA_AC grows (synthetic curve: 15.8 -> 36.9 ac at normal = maximum = 250 ac-ft, 15.8 -> 87.7 ac at 300). Other fallback dams keep their 0.17.46 area. Rerun tools 3 and 7b to update a work folder.
    * Tool 1 (Prepare dams): new 'normal pool elevation' / 'max pool elevation' boxes (DWR normal_elev / max_pool_elev; the NID has none) and output fields inv_norm_elev_m / inv_max_elev_m, last, after inv_maxa_m2 (0.17.46).
    * tools/recompute_storage_anchor.py (repo, read-only): old vs new storage for dams of an existing work folder. Tests: tests/test_storage_anchor.py (also tool 1's output values by field name and tool 7b's area on an anchored curve).
    0.17.46 (2026-10-26; tool 7b: inventory max-pool area fallback, Runner report water-body mask)
    * Tool 7b surface area: when a curve folder is given but the dam's tool-3 stage-storage curve is too shallow (spans less than half the dam height), missing, or gives no area, the Runner's AREA_AC is now the DWR inventory's maximum-pool area (max_pool_area, acres; the DWR records max pool at the top of dam, the level AREA_AC means) instead of nothing, with a warning naming the dam. No inventory area (0 or blank) = no area, as before, with a warning saying so. Where the area came from (surface_area_source = stage_storage / inventory_max_pool_area / none) is in the new output field rn_area_src, the dam.json source note of surface_area_ac, the log and the run manifest (extra.surface_area_source per dam). Dams with a usable curve, and runs with no curve folder, are unchanged.
    * Tool 1 writes the new field inv_maxa_m2 (m2, from DWR max_pool_area / max_pool_a; empty for NID dams, which have no max-pool area). A dams layer from an older tool 1 lacks it: tool 7b warns and the fallback reads a max_pool_area / max_pool_a field kept on the layer, if any. Rerun tool 1 to use it.
    * Tool 7b water-body mask (advanced; the window: Settings, 'Step 7b: mask existing lakes and ponds in the Runner's report', off by default, uses the Inputs NHD waterbodies layer): sent to the Runner's WATER_MASK (Runner 3.10.27 or later), which gives the report's flooded area and speeds with and without the existing lakes and ponds; the flood model, rasters and class do not change. Default sends nothing. Recorded in rn_inputs and the run manifest (extra.water_mask). Test: tests/test_7b_area_fallback_watermask.py.
    0.17.45 (2026-10-25; preflight checks the offline pack against its manifest.json)
    * Preflight: a new row 'Offline pack vs manifest.json' when the Inputs read an offline pack (format 2 folder of GeoPackages, or format 1 .sqlite): every listed layer present, with its feature count, fields, geometry type and CRS (damsuite.packcheck, read-only). Missing or different layers are a warning naming them, instead of the layer silently not being found. Suite package 1.17.5 (packcheck).
    0.17.44 (2026-10-24; tool 1 writes the DWR/NID field map it never saved)
    * Tool 1 (Prepare dams) now writes the chosen DWR/NID field names to <work>/1_fields.json next to the standardized-dams output. The writer existed but was never called, so the reader (core/workflow.py step 1) looked for a file nothing produced and re-detected the fields on every chain run. The map is written on a normal run; a memory/virtual output (no real folder) is skipped without error. Test: tests/test_field_map.py.
    0.17.43 (2026-10-24; dam.json surface_area_ac and dam_length_ft)
    * Tool 7b's dam.json (core/damrecord_io.py) now has dam_length_ft: the length of the mapped dam line (tool 4's crest_len_m on the breach point), else the inventory's dam length (tool 1's crest_len_m: DWR dam_length / NID DAM_LENGTH) - the order tool 5 uses - with where it came from in the source note. Before, it was never written, so the Runner used its 400 ft default crest length. A run of an existing folder now takes this length for the crest-raise strip, the PMF overtopping weir and the HEC-RAS dam connection, so results can change on a rerun.
    * surface_area_ac (the area at the top of dam the Runner reads for AREA_AC) is still only the area tool 7b sends from the tool-3 curve. When there is none it is written as null with the reason in its source note (the shallow-curve note, or no curve), quoting the inventory's normal-pool area when there is one; that area is not written, since the Runner would read it as the top-of-dam area. dam_length_ft with no line and no inventory length is a null with a note too. A null never replaces a value another tool recorded, and a later value fills it with no conflict.
    * Existing dam.json files get the fields on the next tool 7b run. Tests: tests/test_handoff_record_fields.py, tests/test_suite09.py.
    0.17.42 (2026-10-22; Download DEM downstream bearing)
    * Download DEM 'Downstream bearing' (deg from north, optional): when set, the single-dam DEM box is shifted so the dam sits near the upstream edge and the box reaches downstream instead of being centred on the dam; same box size, so the download is no larger. Blank keeps the centred box. The Runner 'Model one dam' wizard's Terrain page carries the same field and passes it to the download. County and state areas ignore it.
    0.17.41 (2026-10-21; tool dialogs stay in front of the window)
    * Tool dialogs opened from the window (DEM download, offline layers, the suite tools) are owned by the window and modal (new shared/ui/tool_dialog.py). Before, QGIS's execAlgorithmDialog owned them from its main window and showed them before exec_(), so they were not modal: a click on the plugin window raised it over the tool; the Runner's wizard had the same bug, fixed in Runner 3.10.63 (from the Runner's 'Model one dam' wizard (modal itself) the DEM download could not be clicked at all, the wizard popped up in front of it). No step, default or number changes.
    0.17.40 (2026-10-20; Von Thun & Gillette parity; renumbered from 0.17.33)
    * Von Thun & Gillette (1990) breach width: Cb now steps at 1,000 / 5,000 / 10,000 ac-ft (a volume on a limit is in the larger class), the method's limits and the Runner's (core/breach.py vtg_1990). The rounded m3 limits used before (1.23e6 / 6.17e6 / 1.23e7 m3) put reservoirs of 997.2-1,000, 5,000-5,002 and 9,972-10,000 ac-ft in the next class, so their VTG-E / VTG-R breach was 12.2-24.4 m wider than the Runner's. Other volumes and the other methods are unchanged. New tests/test_vtg_parity.py compares the two plugins on both sides of each limit.
    0.17.39 (2026-10-20; gdal.Warp without source overviews; renumbered from 0.17.35)
    * Terrain downsampled without the source's overviews (gdalwarp -ovr NONE; new core/warp_opts.py, the same file as the Runner's). gdal.Warp's default (-ovr AUTO) read the nearest overview of a 1 m tile or VRT when warping to a coarser cell. TerrainLoader.load (the larger-clip terrain), Find Ponds' terrain warp and its local-DEM clip, the terrain cache's local-DEM clip and Download DEM's local DEM (write_local_dem) now read the full-resolution cells. Results from DEMs WITH overviews can shift slightly (typically 1-2 cm, up to 5-12 cm mean |dz| for NEAREST overviews); a DEM without overviews is unchanged. Test: tests/test_warp_no_overviews.py.
    0.17.38 (2026-10-20; US / State highways at least Medium, REG_05.2026 5.2.1.2(b); on #130; numbered 0.17.37 on its branch, which #133 and #134 also use)
    * Tool 8 and triage (core/nd_hazard.py 1.2.0, the Runner's rules): an overtopped US or State highway is at least Medium whatever its AADT (5.2.1.2(b), p. 23); the AADT < 500 Low rule is for local, county and private roads. The highway is read from the road class field and, new, the road's name field (name / fullname / road_name). A flagged structure (DWR-3161) still gets its Low of record (test).
    0.17.36 (2026-10-20; hazard tables against REG_05.2026)
    * 'Hazard Classification Findings.csv' / .geojson (core/nd_hazard.py) have a danger_zone column after zone, with the zone names of REG_05.2026 Figures 5.1 / 5.2 (HIGH DANGER ZONE / JUDGMENT ZONE / LOW DANGER ZONE); zone keeps its values. The ND Fig 5.1 / 5.2 chart legend uses the same names. No class or default changes. 0.17.35 is #128's.
    0.17.34 (2026-10-20; REG_05.2026 zone colours)
    * The ND Fig 5.1 / 5.2 review charts (core/nd_hazard.py chart_svg) and styles/acer_zones.qml / acer_zone_points.qml use the colours of REG_05.2026 Figures 5.1 / 5.2: High #EF9897, Judgment #F9F4A3, Low #C4E0AD (the judgment zone was peach, the low zone nearly white; the zone rasters were Material red / yellow / green at 0.75 opacity, now full opacity). Breakpoints and classes unchanged. 0.17.33 is not used: it is left for an open PR.
    * Zone legend labels follow the standard: HIGH DANGER ZONE / JUDGMENT ZONE / LOW DANGER ZONE.
    0.17.32 (2026-10-20; GUI grammar, part 2)
    * Main window: no header line (the window title names it); the caveat is the shared grey banner; Data → DEM → Screen → Report is the suite's pill strip, mark before the word ('✓ Data'); the ⚙ on the tab bar opens Settings, its arrow also holds How it decides…; Screen this county, One dam… and Stop sit at the bottom right (Run tab); the log is the shared ▸ Log fold above Close. Settings: Run options no longer scrolls sideways (the long engine list kept its combo 802 px wide and hid the Browse buttons). The settings window's legend is two lines: the notation (bold, [YOUR value]) and, quieter under it, the units footnote. No default changes.
    0.17.31 (2026-10-20; GUI grammar, part 1)
    * GUI grammar foundation (docs/gui-grammar.md): window colours from the shared tokens.py; the shared window files have one canonical copy in shared/ui/ (tools/sync_ui.py, checked in CI); tools/gui_lint.py opens every window in real QGIS and checks the rules.
    * The Settings window fits a 1080p screen (its tabs scroll; it asked for 1147 px and put Save and Close off screen); '&' in tab names shown ('Dams & data'); Value column in view. Window title 'DamSafety Screening 0.17.31'; 'Open DamSafety Hazard…'; layer groups 'DamSafety Screening - …'; Processing provider named DamSafety Screening; plugin icon. Banner and Screen this county use the shared colours (no painted blue). No default changes.
    0.17.29 (2026-10-19)
    * Download DEM has a third source, 'USGS 3DEP 1 m lidar tiles (native, TNM)' (core/lidar_tnm.py): the 3DEP 1 m lidar project tiles themselves (10 km GeoTIFFs at 1 m, NAD83 UTM metres, about 275 MB each) from The National Map Access API, not the resampled ImageServer image. Area: a box (BBOX), else a dam folder (DAM_FOLDER: the Runner's model bounds plus DOMAIN_PAD_M, else dam.json's point and the buffer), else the dam point and buffer, else the county; never the state. The first run lists the tiles (project, collection and publication dates, size), the total, what is still to download and the free disk space, and downloads nothing; LIDAR_DOWNLOAD downloads them (refused over LIDAR_MAX_GB, default 10, or when less than 2 GB would stay free). Tiles go to TILE_FOLDER, else <suite data folder>/dem/lidar_1m/, else <output>/lidar_1m (never the offline pack or a read-only folder); tiles already there are skipped, a cut download resumes from its .part (HTTP Range), each tile is retried 4 times and checked (size, GDAL opens it at 1 m with a CRS), and lidar_1m_manifest.json records tile, project, dates, URL, sha256 and bytes. Where two projects publish the same tile the newest collection is used and the log says so (LIDAR_KEEP_OLDER keeps the older one underneath). The result is <DAM_ID>_1m_lidar.vrt in the output folder (1 m, the tiles' UTM zone, newest collection on top, the coarser DEM underneath: FALLBACK_DEM, else <DAM_ID>_10m_5070.tif when it is there), <DAM_ID>_1m_lidar.json (projects, tiles, dates, fallback) and the _dem.txt facts. Cancel keeps finished tiles. Nothing downloads on open. damsuite 1.17.4: suitedata skips dem/lidar_1m, so the tile cache is never taken for your own DEM.
    0.17.28 (2026-10-19; PR #94, Claude's audit-fix campaign)
    * M18 (default changes): the storage-leak guard extends the stage-storage curve to the crest for known-height dams again, with a new cap that limits the crest to the spillway level.
    * H11 (default changes): the downstream side is judged along the dam, not only at its midpoint, and terrain wins over the waterbody vote when they disagree; can flip the breach direction on long dams.
    * H1-H3, H9, H10, H12, H13, L2, M17, M19, M20: GeoPackage writes without ST_ functions, self-test temp isolation and the other fixes in docs/Fix_campaign_report.md.
    0.17.27 (2026-10-19)
    * Tool 3 honours the window's storage choice: new parameter 'Storage to use' (STORAGE_CHOICE: Max storage, the default / Normal storage / Larger of the two; the window's labels and values). Max raises the storage at the top of dam to the inventory maximum (as before); Normal keeps the DEM-measured part above the normal pool; Larger uses whichever inventory value is larger. A missing or zero value falls back to the other one (Normal without a normal storage uses the maximum; Max without a maximum uses the normal) and says so as a warning; with neither the DEM estimate is kept. Dams without a water body use the chosen value for the full-height inventory curve. stor_notes and the log start with the storage used and any fallback (no new field: tools 16 and 19 write the storage fields by position). The window no longer sends STORAGE_CHOICE to tool 1, which never had it. A model or script without the parameter gets Max.
    0.17.26 (2026-10-19)
    * Flagged structures (damsuite/structure_flags.py, docs/Flagged_structures.md): tool 1 writes struct_cls and flag_note for a dam of the suite's list (DWR-3161 / ND00597: road fill over backwater); tools 5, 6, 7 and 12 skip its breach (brch_conf / sim_status 'flagged', triage clear with the note), 7b never sends it to the Runner, and tool 8 gives the hazard of record (Low, DWR record) with hz_conf 'flagged' and the note in hz_reason instead of computing a breach hazard; the reports list it with that note. Existing field names unchanged; struct_cls and flag_note are new.
    0.17.25 (2026-10-18)
    * Window sizing (ui_fit.py, the same file as the Hazard's since 2.18.36): sized for the parent's screen, else the screen under the mouse (it was always the primary screen); opens inside that screen, never larger than it (the cap follows the window to another monitor); no 520 x 400 floor larger than a small screen.
    * One ellipsis character (U+2026) instead of '...' on the plugin menu entries (Screen a county…, Dam suite home…, Suite settings…), the Processing labels / help, and the shared Dam suite home window (suite_home.py, identical in the three plugins).
    0.17.24 (2026-10-16)
    * ui_widgets.py: the window widgets the DamSafety plugins share, an identical copy in the Runner, Screening and Hazard (a test checks the copies). Settings buttons read 'Load…' / 'Save as…' (one ellipsis character); the settings grid's first-row combo box sits in its own column.
    0.17.23 (2026-10-15)
    * Window layout: title 'DamSafety — Screening (v...)', the disclaimer as a muted banner, one Workspace row (work folder, data folder, county) with a red 'not set yet' under an empty folder, a Data → DEM → Screen → Report strip (same step states as before; click to go to the tab), one Run tab (Simple + All steps; Inputs folded into 'Override inputs'), Settings behind the gear button (changed count shown), the log folded to one line with Show log, Close bottom right. No step, setting or run behaviour changed.
    0.17.22 (2026-10-15)
    * 7b engine (#38, QGIS Dev's #22 review): ENGINE is the one engine choice (legacy FORCE_ANUGA = 'LISFLOOD-FP then ANUGA'; only TIER is sent), engine status line in the log/window, culverts on a LISFLOOD-FP-only run warned as ignored, early stop when ANUGA is requested but not installed.
    0.17.21 (2026-10-15)
    * Cross-platform (#32): text files read/written as UTF-8, CSVs written with newline='' (no blank rows on Windows), Windows conda ANUGA env python found, subprocess output decoded tolerantly.
    0.17.20 0.17.20 (2026-10-15)
    * 7b: passes the Runner's engine choice (TIER, FORCE_ANUGA, near-dam FV1 refine) and a CULVERTS layer with its field mapping to ndbreach:rundam (#22).
```

## Archived metadata changelog — DamSafety_Data

Entries moved verbatim out of `DamSafety_Data/metadata.txt` `changelog=` on 2026-11-07 (QGIS's Plugin Manager parses and renders the whole `changelog=` value of every installed plugin, so `tools/ci_checks.py` now caps it at 12 KB and keeps the newest entries there). Newest first; the indentation is the metadata.txt continuation indent.

```text
    1.5.6 - Browser connections: the suite's map services (WMS/WMTS, XYZ Tiles, ArcGIS REST Servers: USGS 3DEP / 3DHP / NHD / topo / imagery, NLCD, ND GIS Hub, DWR MapService, NID, NTAD rail / crossings / NBI, FEMA NFHL and USA Structures, SARP, USGS stream crossings, NAIP and the services the user added) are added to the QGIS Browser as 'DamSafety: ...' connections when QGIS starts, each once (a deleted or edited one is left alone); setting ndsuite/register_connections = false turns it off (shared damsuite 1.18.5, connections.py, pin v3). Test: DamSafety_Hazard/tests/test_browser_connections.py.
    1.5.5 - Offline pack: new layer USGS_stream_crossings (barriers/), the USGS Database of Stream Crossings in the United States (Wieferich 2022, doi:10.5066/P9YX6KTB; TIGER 2020 roads x NHD HR flowlines, about 75,000 crossings in the ND box) - the modelled base of the ABI unsurveyed road / stream crossings. Shared damsuite (pin v2): gpkgpack files USGS_stream_crossings under barriers/.
```
